# Kṛṣṇa-vijaya \[Trans. Bhumipati dasa\]

## Introduction

By the unlimited mercy of my divine Gurudeva Srila Gour Govinda Swami Maharaja, and all the predecessor Vaisnava spiritual masters, this book, Sri Krishna Vijaya has being published for the pleasure of the devotees. The effect of hearing Krishna-katha or pastimes of Lord Krishna through the proper source is described in the Srimad Bhagavatam:

*nivṛtta-tarṣair upagīyamānād*  
*bhavauṣadhāc chrotra-mano-'bhirāmāt*  
*ka uttamaśloka-guṇānuvādāt*  
*pumān virajyeta vinā paśughnāt*

Glorification of the Supreme Personality of Godhead is performed in the parampara system; that is, it is conveyed from spiritual master to disciple. Such glorification is relished by those no longer interested in the false, temporary glorification of this cosmic manifestation. Descriptions of the Lord are the right medicine for the conditioned soul undergoing repeated birth and death. Therefore, who will cease hearing such glorification of the Lord except a butcher or one who is killing his own self? (SB 10.1.4)

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

The nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are the life and soul of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, transmitted by learned sages, eradicate one's sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent. (SB 10.31.9)

The Lord mercifully incarnated in this material world and performed innumerable pastimes for the benefit of suffering conditioned souls. Hearing of such pastimes produces the medicine that cures the material disease of forgetfulness of God. Sri Krishna Vijaya is one of such transcendental literature that describes the pastimes of Lord Krishna when He appeared on this earth more than five thousand years ago.

A person seeing this book for the first time may raise an objection as to the necessity of publishing yet another book on Sri Krishna lila when there are already innumerable books about this subject matter on the market. However, these types of objections are due to the nature of the age of Kali. In this age, people are mostly lazy about spiritual subjects, thus a new book depicting the pastimes of the Lord is always seen as a burden. Those fortunate souls, who are truly desirous of self-realization, are full of enthusiasm at receiving a new volume depicting the pastimes of the Lord. Even though the new book may contain a repetition of pastimes previously published in other materials, there are always new angles in the presentation of a given author which can shed new light on the Absolute Truth. Discourses describing the pastimes of the Lord are always dynamic; never stagnant. The Lord's body is ever new; similarly, literature describing His pastimes is ever new.

Sri Krishna Vijaya is an authentic document of Vaisnava literature depicting the pastimes of the Supreme Lord Krishna. This book was first published in its original Bengali language more than 150 years ago by Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura, and was then republished by his son, the great Vaisnavamaster, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura. It has been noted there are other few editions of sri Krishna Vijaya, however in this new English edition, we have used the materials from the original published works of Srila Bhaktivinoda Thakura.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu who expressed His appreciation of the book as follows:

*guṇarāja-khāṅna kaila śrī-kṛṣṇa-vijaya*  
*tāhāṅ eka-vākya tāṅra āche premamaya*

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu then said, "Gunaraja Khan of Kulina-grama compiled a book named Sri Krishna Vijaya, in which there is a sentence revealing the author's ecstatic love of Krishna." (Sri Caitanya-caritamrta, Madhya 15.99)

*“nandanandana kṛṣṇa—mora prāṇa-nātha”*  
*ei vākye vikāinu tāṅra vaṁśera hāta*

Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu said, '"Krishna, the son of Nanda Maharaja, is my life and soul.' By this statement I am sold into the hands of the descendants of Gunaraja Khan." (Sri Caitanyacaritamrta Madhya 15.100)

*tomāra ki kathā, tomāra grāmera kukkura*  
*sei mora priya, anya-jana rahu dūra*

"To say nothing of you, even a dog living in your village is very dear to Me. What, then, to speak of others?" (Sri Caitanyacaritamrta Madhya 15.101)

By these verses, the authenticity, greatness, and honorableness of this book is proven. It is obvious then that this book will merit great veneration from the Gaudiya Vaisnavas for having received such lavish praise by none other than Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

Srila A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada commented as follows: "Sri Krishna Vijaya is a book of poems considered to be the first poetry book written in Bengal. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura states that this book was begun in the year 1395 Sakabda (A.D. 1473). After seven years, it was completed (in 1402 Sakabda). This book was written in plain language, and even half-educated Bengalis and women could read it very clearly. Even ordinary men with little knowledge of the alphabet could read this book and understand it. Its language is not very ornamental, and sometimes the poetry is not very sweet to hear. Although according to the sonnet style each line should contain fourteen syllables, there are sometimes sixteen, twelve or thirteen syllables in his verse.

Many words used in those days could be understood only by local inhabitants, yet this book is still so popular that no bookstore is complete without it. It is very valuable for those who are interested in advancing in Krishna consciousness.

Sri Gunaraja Khan was one of the topmost Vaisnavas, and he translated the Tenth and Eleventh Cantos of Srimad-Bhagavatam for the understanding of the common man. The book Sri Krishna Vijaya was highly praised by Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu, and it is very valuable for all Vaisnavas. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta SarasvatI Thakura gives a genealogical table and family history of Gunaraja Khan. When a Bengali emperor named Adisura first came from Kanyakubja, or Kanauj, he brought with him five brahmanas and five kayasthas. Since a king is supposed to be accompanied by his associates, the brahmanas accompanied the king to help him in higher spiritual matters. The kayasthas were to render other services. In the northern Indian high country, the kayasthas are accepted as sudras, but in Bengal the kayasthas are considered among the higher castes. It is a fact that the kayasthas came to Bengal from northern India; specifically from Kanyakubja, or Kanauj. Srila Bhaktisiddhanta SarasvatI Thakura says that the kayasthas who came from Kanyakubja were highclass men. Of them, Dasaratha Vasu was a great personality, and the thirteenth generation of his family included Gunaraja Khan.

His real name was Maladhara Vasu, but the title Khan was given to him by the Emperor of Bengal. Thus he became known as Gunaraja Khan. Bhaktisiddhanta SarasvatI Thakura gives the following genealogical table of Gunaraja Khan: (1) Dasaratha Vasu; (2) Kusala; (3) Subhasarikara; (4) Hamsa; (5) Saktirama (Baganda), Muktirama (Mainagara) and Alarikara (Bangaja); (6) Damodara; (7) Anantarama; (8) Gunmayaka and Vinanayaka. The twelfth generation included Bhagiratha, and the thirteenth Maladhara Vasu, or Gunaraja Khan. Sri Gunaraja Khan had fourteen sons; of whom the second son, Laksminatha Vasu, received the title Satyaraja Khan. His son was Sri Ramananda Vasu; therefore Ramananda Vasu belonged to the fifteenth generation. Gunaraja Khan was a very well known and wealthy man. His palace, fort, and temples are still existing, and from these one can deduce that the opulence of Gunaraja Khan was certainly very great. Sri Gunaraja Khan never cared for the artificial aristocracy introduced by Ballal Sena."

Sri Maladhara Vasu or Gunaraja Khan was a great devotee of the Lord who desired greatly to benefit the world with his rendition of the accounts of the pastimes of the Lord. Thus, throughout the book are found the blessings he bestowed on the readers of this book. While narrating the Lord's pastimes, he inserted his own teachings of the Absolute Truth that separates reality from illusion. The reader will thus enjoy the benefit of hearing the accounts of the pastimes of the Lord and, at the same time, hear the philosophy of the Absolute Truth.

Sri Krishna Vijaya mainly deals with the subject matters described in the tenth and eleventh cantos of Srimad Bhagavatam. The author also included some accounts from scriptures like Mahabharata, Harivamsa, Brahma-vaivarta Purana and Bhavisya Purana. Additionally, he revealed at the end of the book that he was actually instructed by Sri Vyasadeva in his dream to compose the book.

Our prayer is that this book be accepted by the devotees of the Lord, and that they bless us so we may continue presenting literatures such as this to the world.

Isvara dasa  
Completed in Sri Mayapur Dham,  
West Bengal, India, March 2011

## Invocation

*nārāyaṇaṁ namaskṛtya*  
*naraṁ caiva narottamam*  
*devīṁ sarasvatīṁ vyāsaṁ*  
*tato jayam udīrayet*

Before reciting this Srimad-Bhagavatam, which is the very means of conquest, one should offer respectful obeisance unto the Personality of Godhead, Narayana; unto Nara-narayana Rsi, the supermost human being; unto mother SarasvatI, the goddess of learning; and unto Srila Vyasadeva, the author.

I offer my respectful obeisances at the lotus feet of Lord Narayana, who is beginning-less and is the ultimate cause of creation, maintenance, and annihilation. I fold my hands and offer my respects to Lord Hari. Krishna, the son of Nanda Maharaja, is the Lord of my life.

I offer my obeisance … Lord in His creation. I offer my obeisance to Ganapati, for he takes away all of one's obstacles.

Bowing down to all the demigods, I shall now begin to describe some of the characteristics of Lord Krishna.

My obeisance to the mother of the three worlds, known as the material nature, or Maha-maya. She helps maintain the cosmic manifestation. I offer my obeisance to her two daughters, LaksmI and Sarasvati. By her mercy, Indra became the king of the demigods, and great personalities like Brahma show respect to her. She protected all moving and nonmoving beings, as well as the demigods and sages, by killing demons like Sumbha. I have suddenly been favored by her mercy to describe the characteristics of Lord Krishna.

Who can actually describe the birth and activities of the Supreme Lord and His incarnations Who descend into this world? Even if the stars in the sky are counted one by one, even if the water in the ocean is measured by a pitcher, and even if the particles of dust are counted, it will still be impossible to describe the transcendental glories of Sri Krishna. It may be possible to count the raindrops, but it is impossible to properly and fully describe Lord Krishna's characteristics.

Srimad-Bhagavatam has made its appearance in this material world simply to benefit and deliver the fallen souls. I have heard the topics of Srimad-Bhagavatam from the mouths of the learned scholars. Now I will try to narrate its essence in a simple manner. Just hear with faith and feel the ecstasy. In order to benefit the general mass of people, I have written the topics of Srimad-Bhagavatam in simple Bengali verses. It is not easy to understand the topics of Srimad-Bhagavatam; hence, I describe them in the form of songs. People in this Age of Kali are naturally sinful, yet while singing these songs, they will gradually become delivered.

By hearing these topics, all the people of the world will become purified. O human beings, hear with attention and do not disregard. This is the boat for crossing over the ocean of material existence. Even if a person is full of desire for religiosity, economic development, sense gratification, and liberation, if he simply hears the topics of Srimad-Bhagavatam, he will certainly go back to Vaikuntha, the spiritual world. The lotus-eyed Supreme Lord is the life and soul of all within the entire creation, and He is the unlimited Lord and the strength of all.

## A description of the Lord's incarnations

In the beginning of the creation, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Sri Hari, became Brahma.

In His second incarnation, the Lord appeared as Varaha-deva and delivered the earth.

It is well known throughout the entire world that in His third incarnation the Supreme Lord appeared as Narada.

In His fourth incarnation the Lord advented as Nara-Narayana and performed severe penances at Badarikasrama. The whole world sings of Their glories.

The fifth incarnation was that of Lord Kapila, who performed mystic yoga, and also introduced the system of sankhya yoga.

The sixth incarnation of the Supreme Lord was the best of yogis, Dattatreya. By serving Him, Karttikeya became the ruler of the world.

In His seventh incarnation, the Lord descended as Yajna along with the sacrificial fire.

The eighth incarnation of the Lord was the deaf and dumb Jada Bharata. The ninth incarnation was the unlimitedly glorious Prthu, who milked the earth and supplied necessities to the living entities.

In His tenth incarnation, the Supreme Lord appeared as Matsya and protected the Vedas.

Dhanvantari appeared as the eleventh incarnation from the ocean of nectar.

In His twelfth incarnation, the Lord appeared as a charming woman MohinI, and bewildered the demons. She then distributed the nectar among the demigods.

In His thirteenth incarnation, the Lord appeared in a wonderful form as Narasimha-deva, killed the demon Hiranyakasipu, and drank his blood.

The fourteenth incarnation of the Lord was that of Vamana-deva, who deceived Bali and conquered the planet Rasatala.

In His fifteenth incarnation, the Lord appeared as Parasurama and completely destroyed the ksatriyas of the whole world twenty-one times.

The sixteenth incarnation was Vyasadeva, who divided the Vedas and propagated the religious principles for the living entities.

In His seventeenth incarnation, the Supreme Lord appeared as Rama, the son of Dasaratha. The one Lord Visnu then manifested Himself in four plenary portions. He built a bridge on the ocean, killed Ravana and his associates, and delivered Sita.

In His eighteenth incarnation, the Lord descended as Haladhara.

As the nineteenth incarnation the Lord advented as Krishna, who is celebrated throughout the universe.

Buddha appeared as the twentieth incarnation of the Supreme Lord.

In His twenty-first incarnation the Lord will manifest as Kalki to annihilate the mlecchas.

In this way, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Krishna, or Hari, personally appears in different incarnations. Narayana in Vaikuntha is His plenary portion. O living entities, hear the topics of Krishna with full attention, for by hearing such topics you will be relieved from the ocean of repeated birth and death.

The name of my father is Bhagiratha, and my mother is Indumati. It is by their mercy that I have developed faith in Krsnacandra. Offering due respects to all pious and impious living entities, I, Maladhara Basu narrate Sri Krishna Vijaya.

## Summary description of Krishna's pastimes

In the beginning of Krishna's pastimes, the wonderful birth of Krishna is described. Although He is unborn, He takes birth. He is the Supreme Lord who holds a disc in His hand.

Vasudeva took the child Krishna to the house of Nanda Maharaja. Goddess Maha-maya then took the form of a jackal and walked in front of Vasudeva while the serpent Vasuki sheltered them by spreading his hood like an umbrella above Krishna's head. Vasudeva exchanged the baby boy Krishna for Yasoda's daughter and took her to Mathura, thus deceiving king Kamsa.

When Lord Sri Hari was only a small child, He sucked Putana's breast milk along with her life air. By the kick of His feet, Krishna broke a bullock cart. The sound of this kicking traveled a long distance, and Kamsa became very frightened when he heard it.

Krishna also killed the demon Trnavarta by strangling him. He displayed the universal form to His mother after being accused of having eaten clay. The exalted priest Garga Muni awarded Him the appropriate name Krishna. Krishna, who is nondifferent from Narayana, ate yogurt, and broke all the yogurt's storage pots.

After Mother Yasoda bound Him with a rope to a grinding mortar, Krishna uprooted the two trees Yamala and Arjuna. Krishna's childhood pastimes are wonderful and unlimited. The two sons of Kuvera who, as a result of being cursed, stood as trees in the courtyard of Nanda, became relieved of their curse by Krishna's mercy and then returned to their own abode.

In exchange for grains, Lord Krishna took many fruits from a fruit seller and relished them. Upon returning to her home, the fruit seller found that the grains had turned into precious jewels. Krishna left Gokula with His family and resided in Vrndavana. There He effortlessly killed the demon Vatsasura in a playful manner.

Krishna killed the gigantic demon Bakasura by bifurcating his giant beak. Thereafter He killed Aghasura, and then bewildered Brahma. Krishna eliminated the demon Dhenukasura and then enjoyed palm fruits with His friends. After this He subdued the formidable serpent Kaliya.

It was because of Balarama that Krishna finally revealed Himself to the residents of Vraja. He then swallowed a forest fire, and Balarama killed Pralambasura. By swallowing the forest fire, Krishna saved the lives of His cowherd boyfriends.

Lord Hari pleased the gopis by allowing them to dress and decorate Him. He begged food from the wives of brahmanas who were attached to performing sacrifice. He lifted Govardhana Hill and saved the residents of Vraja.

Krishna then delivered Nanda Maharaja from the clutches of Varuna, and He enjoyed transcendental pastimes with the gopis in Vrndavana.

Krishna celebrated the festival of Katyayani in Vrndavana. Thereafter He destroyed Sarikhacuda and protected the inhabitants of Vraja. He then killed the demons Kesi, Arista, and Vyoma.

Then Akrura came to Gokula and took Krishna and Balarama to Mathura. In Mathura Krishna killed a washerman, awarded benediction to a florist, and fulfilled the desire of Kubja. He visited all the places of Mathura one by one.

He broke a sacrificial bow and spent the night in Mathura. The next morning, Krishna prepared to meet King Kamsa. Kamsa arranged a group of many demons in the wrestling arena to fight Krishna. Krishna killed the elephant Kuvalayapida immediately in front of the main gate of the wrestling arena.

When Krishna arrived in the wrestling arena, all the assembled people saw Him differently according to their mentality. Krishna, the killer of the Mura demon, then killed the two powerful wrestlers Canura and Mustika.

Thereafter Krishna pulled King Kamsa down from the throne and killed him in front of everyone. After this incident the wives of Kamsa lamented profusely. Krishna then exchanged pleasing words with Vasudeva and Devaki.

After installing Ugrasena as the king of Mathura, Krishna then revealed Himself to His father and mother, Vasudeva and Devaki.

## Lord Krishna's further childhood pastimes.

Krishna went to the ashram of His spiritual master, Sandipani Muni, and mastered the sixty-four branches of knowledge.

By the request of His teacher, Krishna brought back Sandipani's dead son from the abode of Yamaraja. He returned and wandered about within the city of Mathura out of His own sweet will. Krishna, who holds a club, went with Uddhava to the house of Kubja and fulfilled her desire.

He also visited the residence of Akrura. He then sent Uddhava to Vrndavana to pacify the gopis. Krishna fought with Jarasandha eighteen times and defeated him each time. He then demanded some space from the ocean and established the city of Dwaraka.

Thereafter, all the inhabitants of Mathura shifted to Dwaraka. Later I will describe the pastime of the killing of Kalayavana.

I will also describe how Mucukunda attained liberation and how Balarama, who carries a plough, married Revati, and how He shortened Revati's stature by pulling her neck with His plough. I will then narrate the wonderful pastimes of Rukmini's marriage, at which Krishna appeared as the king of kings.

I will recount the pastimes of how Krishna married Jambavati and Satyabhama, and the incident of the Syamantaka jewel. Then I will describe Krsna's marriage to Kalindi in the city of Hastinapura, and the svayamvara ceremonies of Mitravinda and Bhadra.

Krishna married Nagnajiti by subduing seven bulls, and married Laksmana by piercing the eye of a fish. Lord Hari then killed Narakasura, and thereafter simultaneously married 16,100 queens.

Krishna's son Pradyumna killed Sambarasura. Krishna defeated Indra and brought the parijata flower down to the earth. He then enjoyed conjugal pastimes with Rukmini. In the svayamvara ceremony of Usa, Aniruddha fought strongly with Banasura.

Then I will narrate how King Nrga was released from a curse and how Duryodhana's daughter was forcibly kidnapped. Balarama then pulled the river Yamuna with great force and also killed the Dvivida monkey with great prowess.

I will describe how Narada Muni visited the city of Dwaraka and found Krishna engaged in household activities in each and every house. Krishna then killed the imposter god Srgala Vasudeva. I will also tell how the city of Kasiraja was burnt to ashes.

Krishna, the Lord of the universe, then destroyed King Jarasandha and also killed Sisupala in the Rajasuya sacrifice of King Yudhisthira. Then I will describe the battle between Salva and the Supreme Lord, and also how the Lord fought with Mucukunda.

Thereafter, the killing of Dantavakra and Rukmitook place. The topic of Vajranabha's killing was simply amazing. Then the poor brahmana Sudama went to see Krishna in Dwaraka with rice particles. I will also write the glorious topic of how the solar eclipse took place.

I will then nicely describe the topics of Vasudeva's performance of sacrifice and how the great sage Bhrgu kicked Krishna in the chest to test Him. Also how the demon Vrkasura was killed and about the dead son of a brahmana resident of Dwaraka.

Krishna then brought back the dead son of that brahmana. He also brought back the six dead sons of his own mother, Devaki. Next comes the story of how Arjuna kidnapped Subhadra.

I will describe the glories of the chanting of the Lord's holy names and how Ajamila achieved perfection by chanting the name of Narayana. The demigods headed by Brahma came to Dwaraka and requested the Supreme Lord, who holds a club in His hand, to return to Vaikuntha.

A chaos was created in Dwaraka on the pretext of a brahmana's curse. Krishna then instructed Uddhava about the science of self-realization as well as other branches of spiritual knowledge. Then Hari showed His universal form to Uddhava. In the place known as Prabhasa, all the members of the Yadu dynasty fought among themselves and killed one another.

Krishna then heard about the disappearance of Balarama, and so He too returned to His abode of Vaikuntha. Then, one by one, all the Pandavas went to heaven. Arjuna felt impotent in the absence of Krishna.

Krishna actually descended in this world to diminish the burden of the earth. I will try to describe all these topics one by one. O human beings, listen with attention as I, Gunaraja Khan, narrate these transcendental topics, which deliver one from the clutches of material existence.

## The cause for the Lords advent

Once upon a time, Mother Earth became perturbed by the heavy burden of great demons such as King Kamsa. She trembled in fear and approached Brahma. She said to Prajapati Brahma that she was unable to bear the burden anymore and something should be done immediately; otherwise, she would soon sink into Rasatala, the lower planetary region.

When Mother Earth cried pathetically and appealed in this way, Brahma certainly realized that the destruction of the earth was imminent, and that if he did not find a remedy, the earth would fall to the lower planetary region. Brahma and all the demigods then sat down and began to discuss what to do. Finally, they came to the conclusion that only the Supreme Personality of Godhead would be capable of diminishing the heavy burden of the earth being caused by the formidable demons. Thereafter, they decided to approach the Supreme Lord Visnu and inform Him about all the mischievous activities of the demons.

After deciding in this way, all the demigods headed by Brahma, arrived on the shore of the ocean of milk where Lord Narayana resides. With great humility, they prayed to the Lord thus, "O Supreme Lord! Just see how the demons are creating havoc on earth. kamsa, Mustika, Canura, Trnavarta, Sakata, Putana, Arista, Dhenuka, Kesi, Aghasura (who lives in the forest), the wicked Jarasandha (the king of Magadha), the thousand-armed King Bana, the sinful miscreants Rukmi, Sambara, Salva, and Paundraka, Dvivida monkey, Srgala Vasudeva, the terrific kalayavana, as well as many other demons are constantly causing great disturbances on earth. O Lord of lords, we appeal to You to please do something soon and protect the earth."

After hearing the appeal of Brahma, Lord Narayana, who carries a disc in His hand, smiled and replied, "O Brahma. Do not be afraid. I know that the earth is suffering the tyranny of the demons, and soon I will certainly do something about it. For now, you should go back to your respective places. But I have one instruction, O Brahma—that all of you take birth on earth. You can manifest your own expansions and take birth in the family of kings on earth. All the heavenly demigods should quickly take birth in and around Vraja. Surasena is the king of the Yadu dynasty and Vasudeva is his subject. The wife of Vasudeva is Devaki. I will soon take birth from the womb of Devaki. The first six sons of Devaki will be killed by Kamsa, the seventh will be My plenary portion, and I will take birth as the eighth child of Devaki. This is My plan."

## The Glories of the Goddess

After assuring the demigods in this way, the Supreme Lord, who holds a club in His hand, then turned to the goddess Maha-maya and ordered her, "Listen, O wife of Siva, you bewilder the people of the three worlds. You create, maintain, and annihilate the entire universe. The material existence has come from you, for you are the basis of it. You destroy the living entity's distress, lamentation, and poverty. You destroy the distress of those who serve you, for you are the mother of the universe. Take My order: go to the abode of Bali and quickly bring the six embryos. Let them take birth one by one from the womb of Devaki. Then, after their birth on earth, you should quickly take them back to the abode of Bali. You should also make arrangements for the transfer of Devaki's seventh embryo into the womb of Rohini to appear as if a miscarriage by Devaki. You yourself should take shelter within the womb of Yasoda in the house of Nanda; in that way, you may continue to bewilder Kamsa. After deceiving Kamsa, you may return to your own abode. There is no doubt that your fame will be spread throughout the world."

After Lord Sri Hari instructed all the demigods in this way, they returned to their respective abodes while contemplating the orders of the Supreme Lord. Meanwhile, Kamsa brought his sister Devaki to his own residence and arranged an auspicious day for her to be married. He conducted the marriage ceremony with great pomp and gave many gifts in charity.

After marrying Devaki, Vasudeva happily prepared to depart. Kamsa offered to drive Devaki and Vasudeva to their home on a new chariot. At that very moment, a voice from the sky declared, "O Kamsa! The eighth child of your sister Devaki will be the cause of your death."

As soon as Kamsa heard these words, he became frightened. He became angry and decided to immediately kill his sister. But Vasudeva, realizing the imminent danger, pacified Kamsa by saying, "O King! It is no use killing your own sister. She is not a threat to your life, but her sons will be. So as soon as a child is born to her, I will give it to you. Please control your anger and spare her life."

When King Kamsa heard these heart-rending words of Vasudeva, he had a change of heart. He displayed a little compassion and forgave her. Without saying anything further or showing any more respect, Kamsa returned home with a dry face.

Remembering the lotus feet of Lord Hari, I, Gunaraja Khan joyfully narrate His pastimes. O living entities, simply hear the glorious activities of Krishna. There is no other alternative in this Age of Kali than to hear, chant, and remember the topics of Hari.

## The advent of Lord Krishna

Feeling shocked and frightened, Vasudeva returned home with devaki. Realizing the sinful mentality of Kamsa, Vasudeva secretly married Rohini as well. In due course of time, Devaki became pregnant, and soon thereafter the news reached Kamsa.

When the first child was born, Vasudeva took him to Kamsa as promised. But on seeing the beautiful child, Kamsa felt compassion and did not kill him; so Vasudeva took him back home. Kamsa thought that he would be killed by Devaki's eighth child, so there was no use in unnecessarily killing this one. Devaki became very happy upon seeing her child alive.

At that time, the great sage Narada arrived at the palace of Kamsa. Kamsa quickly got up and properly greeted the sage. After duly worshiping him, Kamsa discussed a variety of topics with Narada.

Then suddenly, Narada said, "O king, I want to speak to you privately." Kamsa became astonished and eagerly waited for the sage to speak. Narada said, "Mother Earth complained so much about you to Brahma that he in turn informed the Supreme Lord about her laments. The Lord has made a resolution to kill you.

"He will personally take birth as the eighth child of Devaki. He also ordered all of the demigods to take birth on earth. They are already doing so, and as such, they will be the cause of your certain death. Therefore do not sit idle. All the demigods are determined to kill you."

After Narada Muni left, Kamsa began to deeply contemplate what he'd learned from the sage. He summoned all his associates and ordered them to find a means to foil the demigods' plan. He had full faith in the words of Narada.

It was then decided to harm God and all godly people in any way possible. The sinful Kamsa proceeded to kill all the six sons of Devaki as they were born. He instructed his followers to put Vasudeva and Devaki into prison and bind their hands and legs with iron chains.

Thereafter, Kamsa's followers began to blaspheme and torture the Vaisnavas, brahmanas, and cows. They stopped pious people from giving charity and worshiping Visnu.

When the seventh pregnancy of Devaki reached the seventh month, the goddess who was lying in Yoganidra suddenly transferred the child from the womb of Devaki to the womb of Rohini. A message went out that Devaki had had a miscarriage, though Kamsa found it difficult to believe.

The Supreme Lord assumed a form with a white complexion and appeared as Baladeva, the sustainer of the universe Who assists Krishna in His pastimes. Rohini was sent to the house of Nanda Maharaja; however, Vasudeva and Devaki remained in the prison house of Kamsa.

Vasudeva secretly sent a message to Nanda: "You are my most dear friend and well-wisher. I shall keep my wife in your house, so please look after her. I am very unfortunate; hence such a thing has happened to me. When the child is born, kindly take care of him."

In this way, Rohini secretly resided at the house of Nanda for some time. In due course, she gave birth to an attractive male child full of all transcendental qualities and symptoms. The mother and the son resided in Nanda's household without being noticed by anyone.

The order of Krishna cannot be reversed, so eventually, Devaki again became pregnant within the prison. Vasudeva and Devaki performed the required rituals for the well-being of their soon-to-be-born child.

## Krishna's appearance in the womb of Devaki

All glories to the womb of Devaki, wherein the Supreme Lord Hari lived! Mother Devaki held within her womb the enchanter of the three worlds. Soon the servants of Kamsa informed their master that Devaki was again expecting, and was looking very effulgent. As a matter of fact, she was two months into her pregnancy.

As soon as Kamsa heard this news, he immediately went to see Devaki. Upon seeing her, he became rather frightened. He cursed the child in the womb and ordered his servants to guard the couple very attentively. He also told them to remind him about this impending birth each month, for he knew perfectly well that this child would bring about his ruination.

Kamsa returned to his residence with a fearful mind. He began to think of Krishna as the cause of his death. Meanwhile, Kamsa's servants regularly informed him about Devaki's pregnancy.

Realizing that the Supreme Personality of Godhead had entered the womb of Devaki, whose complexion looked bright and shiny, all the inhabitants of the earth and heaven began to glorify her womb. They said, "The unlimited Supreme Lord Hari, who has no material form, has accepted a human body and is now residing within the womb of Devaki." It was indeed very wonderful throughout the entire creation. The demigods, headed by Brahma, came to see Devaki. After seeing her effulgent womb, Brahma offered his obeisance to the Supreme Lord by falling flat upon the ground. Then he and all the demigods prayed as follows:

## Glorification of the womb

"You are the unlimited Personality of Godhead. You are the original progenitor. You are the supreme controller and the ultimate goal of all. You are the moon, the sun, the stars, and the ten directions. You are Indra, Varuna, Agni, Vayu, and the cause of all. You are the days and nights, the moments and the hours. You are sacrifice, the chanting of Vedic hymns, austerity, and charity. You are the cause of creation, maintenance, and annihilation. You are Lord Narayana. You are the founder of mystic yoga. You are the provider of all enjoyments and the source of spiritual knowledge. When You sleep, the entire creation sleeps, and when You awaken, the whole creation awakens. Although You are free from material qualities and are fully aloof, still You have entered within the womb of Devaki. This is simply because You are affectionate to Your devotees. You have appeared in a human form to kill ferocious demons like Kamsa and thus diminish the burden of the earth."

After praying to the Lord within the womb, the demigods returned to their respective abodes.

## The birth of Lord Krishna

During the tenth month of Devaki's pregnancy, King kamsa doubled the number of the security guards just to keep everything under control. It was the auspicious month of Bhadra, the period of the waning moon, and the eighth day. Everything was perfect and auspicious. As the sun set and the darkness began to increase in the sky above, the whole city of Mathura became covered with clouds. As the night progressed, the darkness grew thicker and thicker.

By the supreme will of the almighty Lord, all the guards and doorkeepers of the jail fell into deep sleep. They were as if unconscious due to the depth of their sleep. The moon rose in the sky at about midnight. All the planetary positions were perfect and full of auspiciousness. Jupiter was in the house of lagna, the moon was in the sign of Taurus, the son of Bhumi was in the sign of Capricorn, Saturn was in the house of Libra, Mercury was in the house of Virgo, and in all respects, the time was most auspicious. Everybody looked pleased. All directions appeared peaceful; the stars were happy, the rivers were joyful, and the ocean was satisfied. At that time, the moment called Mahendra-yoga—which is considered the most auspicious of all auspicious moments—arrived. A loud sound of "Jaya! Jaya!" filled the whole universe and the entire atmosphere was filled with transcendental ecstasy.

The Supreme Personality of Godhead first appeared in His four-armed form as Narayana. He held a conch, a club, a disc and a lotus in His four hands. His ears were decorated with earrings the shape of a fish. He was adorned with a garland of forest flowers. He wore a crown of diamonds, jewels and rubies on His head. His fingers were decorated with golden rings, and His arms with armlets. His lotus feet were decorated with anklets. He had the mark of srivatsa on His chest. On His right stood Laksmi and on His left stood Sarasvati. His associates were constantly offering prayers to Him from all sides.

When Vasudeva and Devaki saw this form of the Supreme Lord, they were struck with wonder. They could not utter a word. They trembled and remained stunned on seeing the Narayana form of the Lord. They did not know what to do or say.

Nevertheless, having received the Lord of the universe, the reality of the world, and the ultimate cause of creation, maintenance and annihilation as their son, both Vasudeva and Devaki became extremely happy. Devaki immediately folded her hands and began to offer prayers to Lord Govinda with undivided attention.

She prayed, "It is certainly most amazing that You have actually taken birth from the womb of a human being. But O Lord, as soon as the wicked and sinful Kamsa will hear about You, he will kill You. Therefore, please tell me what I should do so that Kamsa does not come to know about You." When Lord Hari heard these words of His mother, He smiled and spoke to her in the following way.

O human beings! … Krishna Vijaya.

## Krishna's assurance to Devaki and Vasudeva

With an enchanting smile, Lord Govinda then spoke to Devaki, "O mother! Hear about your previous births as I describe them to you. Previously, you performed a great penance with faith and devotion. You offered many prayers to Me. You had performed austerity for twelve celestial years by not eating anything. Being pleased by your austerity, I appeared before you in this same form, and requested you to ask for a benediction. Instead of praying for liberation from material existence, you wanted a son like Me. To fulfill your request, I appeared twice as your son; as Prsnigarbha and as Vamana. In My first appearance, you both were Prsni and Sutapa; in the second appearance, you were Aditi and Kasyapa, and now you are Devaki and Vasudeva. By the arrangement of Yogamaya, you do not remember any of your previous births. Now give up your awe and reverence and treat Me as your son. Do not worry. Do not be afraid of kamsa. I will kill all these demons. Take Me to the house of Nanda whose wife Yasoda has just given birth to a baby girl, Mahamaya. Keep Me there and bring Mahamaya to the palace of kamsa, thus deceiving him. I will diminish the burden of the earth by executing transcendental activities."

After speaking in this way to His father and mother, Lord Mukunda suddenly transformed Himself into a small two-armed baby boy. The iron shackles on Vasudeva fell apart, and all the doors and gates opened up as the guards and doorkeepers continued their deep sleep.

## Vasudeva's departure to Gokula

Thereafter, Vasudeva placed the child Krishna in a basket, put Him on his head, and left for Gokula. He looked as if he was carrying the moon. Goddess Mahamaya walked ahead of Vasudeva in the form of a she-jackal and the serpent Vasuki spread his hood over Krishna. Vasudeva became very frightened when he saw the huge waves of Yamuna. Just then a voice from the sky said, "Do not be afraid. Look how even a jackal is crossing the river." This assurance made Vasudeva somewhat pacified and so he slowly entered into the waters of Yamuna. At that moment, Lord Govinda remembered something from the past and suddenly slipped out of Vasudeva's hands and fell into the water. In a frantic state, Vasudeva began to search for his beloved child in the water while condemning his luck. He lamented profusely and condemned the creator.

Meanwhile, Krishna satisfied the river Yamuna and again became captured by Vasudeva. Vasudeva carefully crossed the river and went to the house of Nanda. It was well past midnight and no guard or doorkeeper was awake.

Yasoda, having given birth to a baby girl, was in deep sleep, so much so that she did not know whether she gave birth to a boy or a girl. Vasudeva slowly entered within that room where Yasoda was sleeping. Laying Krishna by her side, he took the baby girl and left the room.

Silently, he left Gokula and went back to Mathura the same way as he had come. He placed the daughter by Devaki's side, and explained to her everything that had happened. Soon they were chained like before and all the doors became locked again.

Just then, the girl child began to cry loudly so as to wake everybody up. All the guards immediately shouted, and hurriedly informed the king that a child had been born to Devaki. King kamsa ran with his hands upwards and was shocked to find that a daughter had been born to Devaki instead of the dreaded son. Still, the wicked kamsa snatched the daughter away from Devaki who immediately began to cry and beg kamsa, rolling on the ground.

Devaki said, "O dear brother! What are you doing? Even a dog-eater would not do that! You have already killed six of my moon-like sons without any mercy. You have not even left a family heir or descendant of mine in this world. Although you are my brother by relation, by actions, you are more than an enemy—you are like personified death. This child is only a girl; she poses no threat to your life. O dear King kamsa, kindly do not kill this daughter of mine!"

The sinful kamsa, however, did not pay any attention to Devaki's words. He grabbed hold of the baby's legs but, when he tried to smash her against the floor, the baby girl slipped out of his hands and assumed her real form in the sky as Goddess Mahamaya. She began to speak in a deep voice that filled the air.

She said, "O sinful one! How dare you cause distress to me? You wretched fellow! The jewel-like personality who will kill you has already taken birth. He took birth this very night in Gokula. O King Kamsa! Your days are numbered." After speaking these words, the goddess returned to her own place. Kamsa fell to the ground unconscious out of fear of his impending death.

## Instigation by Kamsa

When he regained his senses, King kamsa called all his ministers and trusted stalwarts and began to address them, "O Canura and Mustika! O Kesi, Dhenuka and Baka mahasaya! O sister Putana! O brother Aghasura!

O Trnavarta, Arista and Pralamba! Today, Goddess Mahamaya has predicted my death at the hands of a mere human being who has just taken birth in Gokula. We had better kill Him early; otherwise, it will be very difficult to kill Him when He grows up. Besides, He will be a constant cause of worry to us."

After kamsa spoke in a grave voice like this, his ministers and relatives replied, "O dear king! Do not worry at all. He is not invincible. Any one of us can kill Him. As Hari has taken birth as a human being, He must have power like that of a human being, so what can He do with such a limited power? We are capable of killing and eating any human body. We will attack Him in Gokula one by one. First, let us send Putana with her breast smeared with poison. She can go to Gokula and kill that child by feeding Him her poisonous breast milk."

After conspiring with his associates in this way, the wicked kamsa left. His sister Putana was the first demon to try to kill Krishna. Meanwhile, kamsa called Vasudeva and had him released from his shackles. He then said to him, "O dear one, I have uselessly given you so much trouble.

I have killed so many of your sons for no fault of yours. Therefore, kindly forgive me. Goddess Bhagavati had just declared to me that the person who will kill me has already taken birth in Gokula. Please do not take offense. O brother-in-law, be happy and go home without any anxiety."

Kamsa spoke to Vasudeva and Devaki in this manner, and so they returned to their residence.

Meanwhile, Yasoda had woken up from her deep sleep and had seen a beautiful moon-like child by her side. She became extremely happy and the entire house of Nanda plunged into a pool of bliss.

Nanda was especially happy because he had been blessed by a son at his advanced age. He immediately called qualified brahmanas and had them perform the required rituals for the child's birth. In great ecstasy, he donated twenty thousand cows to the brahmanas.

Both male and female inhabitants of Gokula joyfully celebrated the auspicious birth of Nanda's son. Soon, the household of Nanda Maharaja began to prosper even more than before. Nanda decided to visit Vasudeva and give him some gifts, so he took ghee, yogurt, and milk, put them on a bullock cart, and went to see Vasudeva.

As soon as Nanda met Vasudeva, they embraced each other and began to shed tears of joy. Vasudeva said "I have heard you've got a son at your old age. You are indeed fortunate. As far as I am concerned, all my sons were killed by King Kamsa. Please take care of your boy. You and your good wife should give Hirn love and affection. Now you should return home quickly; otherwise, your son may face some kind of trouble."

After speaking these words, Vasudeva gave Nanda a warm embrace, and then Nanda left. In the meantime, the witch PGtana had entered Gokula and was living there incognito. One day, she dressed herself in such an enchanting manner that simply by her mere glance, she would have been able to attract the mind of anyone. She decorated herself with various ornaments and flower garlands and pretended to be friends with the neighboring ladies.

Unable at first to find the newborn child anywhere, one day Putana suddenly arrived at the house of Nanda. Meanwhile, Lord Govinda in His form as a child, smiled; knowing full well that Kamsa had sent Putana to kill Him. He decided to kill the witch in such a manner that Kamsa would be struck with wonder when he heard about it.

Putana slowly approached the child Krishna, and sat down next to him. Externally, she upheld her farce and spoke engagingly about various topics with Yasoda, while internally she was waiting for an opportunity to execute her plan.

After some time, she said to Yasoda, "This child of yours is really beautiful. Let me see Him better; let me put Him on my lap. I have never seen such an attractive child before." Taking the boy as she spoke these words, the wicked Putana then thrust her breast nipple into the mouth of baby Krishna.

## The killing of Putana

Lord Govinda simply smiled sweetly as He drank Putana's breast milk. Apparently sucking the breast milk, Krishna also began to suck out her life air. Upon comprehending what was happening to her, the formidable witch Putana started growing and growing as she displayed her real, horrible, demon form and began to scream. "Leave me, leave my breast! Look, Yasoda, look what your child is doing. He is sucking my life out!"

Putana screamed loudly while assuming her original form, and baby Krishna simply sat comfortably on her chest and kept drinking her milk. When the inhabitants of Gokula heard this loud shouting, they quickly came to the house of Nanda and saw the gigantic witch Putana lying dead on the ground.

Meanwhile, Nanda Maharaja returned home after giving gifts to Vasudeva. He too heard this commotion and rushed to the spot where everyone had gathered around Krishna and the witch Putana. He immediately took his beloved Krishna on his lap, and asked the onlookers what had happened. He quickly realized that whatever Vasudeva had told him was certainly true.

The dead body of Putana fell over an area of twelve miles, breaking the trees and houses of Gokula. Her gigantic body looked terrible with its width extending over a two-mile span. The rows of her teeth were as sharp as plow-stilts. Her formidable nostrils resembled mountain caves. Her cheeks appeared like two mountains, and the color of her hair was copper. Her navel looked as deep and fearful as a dark well. Her hands and legs looked like the banks of a huge lake and her belly resembled a dry pond. At first, everyone ran away out of fear, but later on, realizing that she was dead, all the residents of Gokula joined together to cut her huge body into pieces and burn them to ashes. The aroma from the burning of her body matched the aroma of deer musk, for Lord Narayana had personally sucked her breasts.

In this way, the sinful witch Putana was killed by Krishna but, before her death, was awarded the position of mother by the Supreme Lord. Simply by the touch of Krishna, she obtained such an exalted position even though she had fed Krishna milk from breasts smeared with poison. What then can be said of Mother Yasoda, who constantly fed Krishna her pure breast milk with great love and affection? I am unable to describe the glorious position of Nanda and Yasoda who served the Supreme Lord, life after life. Both of them had performed severe austerities and, as a result, Lord Narayana gave them this unique benediction.

Try to understand the topics of Krishna with faith and conviction as I, Gunaraja Khan, narrate His wonderful transcendental topics.

## Arrangements for Krishnas protection

Mother Yasoda and RohinI would always fondle Krishna with great affection. Sometimes they would put Krishna on their laps, and embrace Him with utmost love. They would wish for Krishna a long duration of life, as long as the duration of the great sage Markandeya's life. They would sprinkle Ganges water on Krishna, and pray in various ways to protect Him from all types of danger. They prayed thus, "May the spiritual master of the demigods protect Your two legs and knees, may Lord Acyuta protect Your thighs, may Your waist, belly and heart be protected by Lord Kesava, may Brahma save Your entire body, may the sun god protect Your throat, may Visnu protect Your arms, may Indra protect Your head, and Lord Madhusudana give You a long duration of life. May Hrsikesa protect You from all sides. May Siva protect Your back, may the six-headed demigod protect Your mouth, and may Pavana save Your ears. May the unlimited Lord protect Your hands and legs. May Brahma, Visnu, and Siva give You full benediction so that Your prosperity enhances day by day. May Lord Govinda protect You when You play, and Madhava when You take rest. May the husband of Laksmi protect Your right side, and Parvati Your left side. Let all demigods protect Your whole existence."

In this way, the ladies of Vraja prayed for Krishna's protection. They would then happily put Him on a cart. Mother Yasoda then put black ointment on Krishna's eyelids as an extra measure of protection. The ladies of Gokula surrounded Mother Yasoda and sang songs in glorification of Krishna.

## The breaking of the cart

Once, Lord Hari was sleeping under a cart when suddenly, in the mood of a child, He kicked the cart. The cart broke and fell apart. There was a loud noise, as all the utensils which were kept on that cart scattered all over the place. Hearing the loud noise, mother Yasoda came running to where Krishna had been sleeping. She cried, "O my dear child, O my dear child!" and immediately picked up Krishna and put Him on her lap to comfort Him.

Seeing the broken cart, mother Yasoda asked the boys who were playing nearby if they had done something, but they informed Her that it was the child Krishna Who had broken the cart by the powerful kick of His leg. "Not only has your child broken the cart, but even the container kept on the cart has flown all over the place," they said. When Mother Yasoda heard this, she became frightened and remembered the evil intentions of the wicked Kamsa.

Mother Yasoda remembered what Mahamaya had predicted about her child. She was amazed at how a tiny child could kill the gigantic witch, Putana, by sucking her life air along with her breast milk, and how a mere child could break a huge cart. Amazed about all these deeds, she breathed heavily; thinking about what would be in store for Krishna in the future.

Meanwhile, Kamsa began to look for opportunities to kill Krishna at any cost. He quickly called Trnavarta and said to Him, "O brother! I know your strength and what you are capable of. In fact, everyone knows your prowess. Go to Gokula and kill the child Krishna somehow or other. He is not an ordinary child, I tell you; this son of Nanda is a great obstacle that must be removed."

## The killing of Trnavarta

On the order of Kamsa, the formidable demon Trnavarta assumed the form of the air and went to the house of Nanda in Gokula. He created a powerful dust storm that filled the whole city of Gokula. The entire atmosphere became so dark that nothing could be seen. All the residents grew very frightened as their eyes filled with the dust. Lord Damodara, however, sitting on the lap of His mother, simply smiled thinking, "Another demon has come to kill Me." Suddenly, Krishna made Himself heavier than the earth, so Yasoda slowly took Him off her lap and placed Him on the floor.

When Yasoda put the child Krishna on the floor, the demon Trnavarta, in the form of a powerful wind picked up Krishna and carried Him up to the sky. Putting Krishna on his shoulder, he whirled around ferociously in the sky. The wicked demon created a powerful whirlwind in order to kill Krishna, but with ease, Krishna, who was sitting on the shoulder of Trnavarta, tightly grabbed hold of the demon's neck. The demon soon gave up his life as he suffocated; choked by the strong hands of baby Krishna. To the amazement of all the residents of Vraja, the demon Trnavarta fell down dead to the ground with a loud noise, while Krishna still sat on his shoulder; weeping just like an ordinary child.

Mother Yasoda was shocked to see the gigantic demon. She immediately snatched Krishna away from the demon's body, and embraced Him with great affection.

She caressed baby Krishna with emotion and began to cry. She said, "O dear child! You came back from certain death. O beautiful Murari! Your absence made all of us completely helpless. Why did the creator write so much trouble? It is only Your luck that has saved You! The formidable whirlwind took You up in the sky and then dropped You, but nothing has happened to You, my beloved child. Instead, the miscreant has lost his own life. It is said that Lord Hari destroys one who violates the principles of religion. Just see how Lord Hari has saved You and killed that demon." After speaking these words, mother Yasoda gave her beloved child Krishna a bath and then sprinkled Ganges water on Him while chanting mantras for His protection. She placed Krishna on her lap and kissed Him repeatedly. She gazed at His face with great affection. Just then, with a desire to display some of His own opulence, Krishna suddenly yawned with a smile, and Yasoda saw within His mouth the entire creation with all of its variety.

Yasoda exclaimed, "What did I just see? Was it a dream?" But, before she could guess or analyze anything, Krishna covered her with His own internal potency. O readers! Krishna's topics are simply amazing and full of nectar. Just go on hearing as I, Gunaraj a Khan, narrate them.

## The Name-giving ceremony of Krishna

After some days, Vasudeva invited the great sage Gargamuni to his house and spoke to him in a solitary place. He said, "O respected sage. You know my son is at present living in Gokula. Kindly go there and conduct the name-giving ceremony of the child."

When Vasudeva made a request in this way, Gargamuni thought, "The Supreme Personality of Godhead Who carries a disc in His hand, has appeared in this world to diminish the burdens of the earth. He has advented as the eighth child of Devaki, yet made all the arrangements to let Kamsa know that Devaki gave birth to a girl child. I know He is at present residing in the house of Maharaja Nanda in Gokula." Thinking in this way, Gargamuni happily went to Gokula.

Gargamuni was extremely happy to be able to see Krishna directly, and he considered his life to be successful because of this chance. Soon he arrived at the house of Nanda, who immediately stood up with respect, and offered him a sitting place. Nanda then washed the sage's feet and worshiped him properly. He then gently spoke to the sage, "O Lord! I am very fortunate to have you in my house. Please command me; tell me what I can do for you."

Gargamuni replied, "I have come here to name Your child."

With great jubilation, Nanda folded his hands and said, "O great sage! It's my pleasure that you have come here to name my beloved child."

First, Rohini devi came with her child, and the sage gave Him the name Rohineya. He declared, "Because He possesses unlimited power, He will be celebrated as Balabhadra. He will have all the qualities of Lord Ramacandra, hence He will also be called Balarama. Since He attracted the embryo of His mother and transferred it to Rohini, He will also be renowned as Sankarsana.

The sage then said to Yasoda, "Your son has all the qualities of Narayana. Therefore His name will be Narayana. But He will have many other wonderful and glorious names by which He will be celebrated throughout the three worlds."

After speaking in this way, the great sage Gargamuni left, and Nanda, Yasoda and Rohini became jubilant.

## Krishna's childhood pastimes

As a child, Krishna began to enjoy various pastimes in Gokula. All the inhabitants of Vraja were in ecstasy. Whenever Krishna played some mischief, Balarama and other cowherd boys would immediately complain to mother Yasoda.

One day, Balarama complained to Mother Yasoda, "Come and see! Krishna has eaten clay. When I tried to stop Him, He did not listen to Me." Mother Yasoda quickly came and politely spoke to Krishna. "O dear son! Why are You eating clay? What do You not have at home?" Krishna replied, "No mother! I did not eat clay. Balarama is telling a lie. If you do not believe Me, come and look in My mouth."

As soon as Krishna said this, He opened His mouth. Yasoda was astonished to see the three worlds inside there. She saw the upper, middle and lower planetary systems within Krishna's mouth. She saw the sun, moon, oceans, and mountains; day, night, heaven, and hell; the earth, rivers, lakes, and many other wonderful things as well within the mouth of Krishna. Yasoda was puzzled at seeing such a sight. She forgot where she was standing, whether it was day or night, and whether she was dreaming or if some magical feats had occurred.

In this way, Lord Sri Hari displayed His universal form to mother Yasoda, and then vanquished all her illusions in the very next moment. Afterward, Yasoda took her child Krishna home as if nothing had happened. Such are the wonderful pastimes of Krishna.

## Krishnas pastimes of stealing butter

Lord Sri Hari enjoyed various blissful childhood pastimes in Gokula. Sometimes He would crawl, and sometimes He would walk slowly to the neighboring houses and play with other children. His whole body would be covered with dust. Both Krishna and Balarama would play different sports with the cowherd boys of Gokula.

One day, Mother Yasoda engaged all her maidservants in different household duties while she herself churned butter. She sang of the glorious activities of Krishna while churning butter. Both Mother Yasoda and Mother Rohini used to sing of the activities of Krishna with ecstasy. They described how they should not take Krishna to milk the cows because He was too naughty, how He eats yogurt and then breaks the pots, and how He would hold on tightly to the churning rod. They'd also sing about how He threw yogurt and butter around, and then gave it all away to the monkeys, and how they were now going to keep all the milk products hanging from the ceiling, and so on.

The naughty child Krishna heard all these tales and simply smiled. He thought of enacting some other wonderful pastimes for the pleasure of the Vrajavasis. He stacked wooden boards one above the other and then placed a grinding mortar on top of it. In this way, He could reach the ceiling and be able to slowly take away the pots of butter.

But then. Mother Yasoda turned and saw Krishna stealing butter. She took a stick in her hand and came running, but Krishna immediately got down off His perch out of fear of Mother Yasoda, who then chased Him everywhere. The Lord smiled as He ran swiftly from Mother Yasoda who was chasing Him from behind, her hair loosened. Though she was very tired and perspiring, still she was determined to catch Krishna.

When Krishna, who is very compassionate, saw the distress of His mother, He agreed to be caught by her. Mother Yasoda decided to bind her child with rope, and Krishna began to cry profusely. She chastised Him saying, "You are very mischievous. You eat butter and yogurt and break the pots." Yasoda thought for a while about what to do with her naughty boy, and then decided to tie Krishna, the Lord and master of the three worlds, to a grinding mortar.

Krishna is not one to be humbled so easily, so every time Mother Yasoda tried to bind Him, the rope fell short by a few inches. She brought more ropes but the result was the same, and she soon became very exhausted from her efforts. On seeing her in such a condition, Govinda became compassionate to His mother, and finally allowed Himself to be bound by the ropes.

Yasoda felt somewhat relaxed after binding Krishna. She said to Him, "O Kanai! Now I will see how You eat yogurt and butter! Just stay here like this. I have other duties to perform. When I return, I will teach you more lessons." After Mother Yasoda left Krishna with His belly tied to a grinding mortar, Krishna began to devise some new mischief. Looking around, the Lord espied the two yamala-arjuna trees standing in the courtyard of His house.

## Krishna uproots the Yamala-Arjuna tress

Two demigods had previously been cursed by the great sage Narada to stand as trees. Today their curse will become nullified by Krishna's mercy. Now hear with full attention the tales of those trees.

Once upon a time, Nalakuvara and Manigriva, the two sons of Kuvera the treasurer of the demigods were fully intoxicated, and began to wildly enjoy water sports with young girls in the waters of Yamuna. Both brothers and the young ladies had no clothes on as they sported in the water. While they were sporting in the water like madmen, the great sage Narada, who is enriched with austerity, happened to pass by.

As soon as the young ladies saw Narada, they immediately came out of the water with great awe and reverence, put on their clothes, and offered their respects to the sage. The two inebriated sons of Kuvera, however, completely ignored Narada, and went on enjoying their games.

The great sage Narada became very angry at this irreverent behavior and chastised the two brothers, "How dare you play naked like madmen? You are the sons of the exalted Kuvera, yet you have developed such a low class mentality. You are so intoxicated that you have no sense of discrimination. You openly play with young naked ladies and have become too puffed up with pride even to feel shame in the presence of a witness to your debauchery. There is no one more sinful than both of you in the whole world!"

The great sage Narada then cursed the two brothers to become trees and stand still in the courtyard of Nanda Maharaja in Gokula. In reply to their appeal for liberation, Narada said, "At the end of Dvapara Yuga, Lord Hari will appear in this world in a human form to diminish the burden of the earth. He will enjoy His pastimes here in Gokula. By His mercy, both of you will be delivered from your curse. Now stay here as trees for the next one hundred celestial years."

After cursing Kuvera's sons in this manner, the powerful sage left for outer space. Since then, those two brothers had been standing in the courtyard of Nanda as two trees Yamala and Arjuna. Krishna knew of this incident, and so He approached those two trees with the desire to deliver the brothers. As baby Krishna crawled right in between the two trees, the mortar to which He was tied got stuck. Krishna then forcefully pulled and pulled, yanking the mortar through the trees, and as a result of His actions, the two trees fell to the ground completely uprooted. They began to roll on the ground and make a loud noise that frightened everyone in Gokula. The people thought there must have been some catastrophe; the sky must have fallen to the earth or something similar. Slowly, the two brothers emerged from the two uprooted trees, looking even more beautiful now than they had before, due to the touch of Krishna. They immediately folded their hands, paid their obeisances, and began to offer prayers.

## Prayers by the two sons of Kuvera

"O Supreme Lord! You are the original Personality of Godhead, master of all mystic powers. You are the supreme controller of creation, maintenance and annihilation of the entire cosmic manifestation. You are the proprietor of millions of universes. We have no power to glorify You properly. It was auspicious that the great sage had cursed us. Because of his mercy, we have become qualified to see You, O Lord, who carries a cakra in Your hand. Words which glorify Your holy name are considered auspicious. We have become indeed fortunate by the grace of Narada Muni. Glorious are those hands which are engaged in Your service, glorious is that head whicn bows down before You, glorious are those eyes which see Your beautiful form. Glorious is that mind which meditates upon You, glorious are those feet which visit holy places related to You and glorious is that tongue which relishes Your remnants."

Hearing the two sons of Kuvera pray in this way, the Supreme Lord smiled and spoke to them as follows, "O Nalakuvara! Now you may go back to your residence. O Manigriva! By My mercy your mind will always remain fixed on Me. Anyone who gets My darsana (audience) never returns empty handed. Whatever anyone desires to attain, he receives by My mercy."

After receiving this benediction from Krishna, the two brothers offered their obeisances to Him, circumambulated Him, and returned to their own abode.

Such are the wonderful pastimes of Krishna. O readers! Hear these topics with full attention as Maladhara Basu narrates them falling at the feet of Govinda.

As soon as theyamala-arjuna trees fell to the ground, making a sound like a thunderbolt, a storm commenced, and rain fell even without any cloud. All the inhabitants of Gokula, headed by Nanda and Yasoda, quickly went to that spot, beating their breasts as they ran. On seeing the two trees fallen to the ground, they were very astonished. Yasoda picked up her beloved child and embraced Him tightly with affection. She inquired from the cowherd boys about how the two great trees had fallen. The cowherd boys explained that it was the child Krishna who had uprooted the two trees with the help of the grinding mortar to which He was bound. Nanda Maharaja smiled to hear the extraordinary abilities of his son, and jovially asked, "Why are you blaming my son?" Thereafter, Nanda Maharaja took Krishna on his lap and embraced Him, and then holding Him tightly, he returned home. Once back in their house, Mother Yasoda gave Krishna a nice bath and chanted various mantras for the protection of her beloved son.

One day, while Krishna was playing in the courtyard, He suddenly heard a voice saying, "Who wants to buy some fruit?" As soon as Krishna heard this, He took some grains of rice in His hand as He had seen the adults do, and ran out to get some fruits. Enchanted by the charms of this beautiful boy, the fruit vendor filled His small hands with fruit. Krishna took a large quantity of fruits from the fruit seller in exchange for a small quantity of rice. Soon afterwards, the fruit vendor found that all of those grains of rice had been somehow transformed into precious jewels. By the mercy of Krishna, the fruit vendor became very rich simply because she had satisfied the Lord by offering Him some fruits.

Early in the morning, Rama and Krishna go out to play with the other cowherd boys. Lord Damodara enjoys various sporting activities with His friends till noon. Once Nanda Maharaja, realizing that it was time for lunch, asked Yasoda to call Rama and Krishna for lunch. Yasoda went to the bank of Yamuna and saw Krishna playing with His friends. She called out, "Come on, my dear son. It is already noon. Why are You still playing and not coming to take Your meal? You left home early in the morning and till now have neither eaten anything nor drank any breast milk. Therefore, please stop playing and come home to take Your meal. Your father is waiting for You. All your friends have already had their meals. And just look at you! Why are You two all covered with dust?" She then turned to Balarama and said "O Balai! Bring Kanai home immediately. After You finish taking lunch, You may go back and play some more." In this way, mother Yasoda brought Rama and Krishna home and fed Them with great affection. Thus, Krishna and Balarama used to daily enjoy wonderful pastimes with Their cowherd friends.

## Krishnas Vrndavana pastimes

The elderly cowherd men assembled together at the instruction of Nanda Maharaja. They began to discuss among themselves how to stop great disturbances in Gokula being created by the demons. Nanda Maharaja said, "The witch Putana came to kill Krishna, but instead she herself was killed by Krishna. Then suddenly that huge cart was broken into pieces in my courtyard. We also saw how the fearful demon Trnavarta was killed very recently. Just consider how the two large trees Yamala and Arjuna fell down without any storm. It appears that everyone is envious of my son Krishna. How long can we expect to escape dangers? In order to live peacefully, I think we should leave this place Gokula, and shift our residence to Vrndavana."

When Nanda Maharaja put forth this proposal, all the cowherd men of Vraja readily accepted it. Soon, they began packing and preparing to move to Vrndavana. They loaded all their belongings on bullock carts and departed towards Vrndavana in a joyful mood while playing buffalo horns. They arrived on the bank of Yamuna and established their residences in places like Vrndavana and Govardhana. They built their homes in various designs, planted trees and plants, and began to live peacefully in Vrndavana.

## The killing of Vatsasura

The duty of Krishna and Balarama was to look after the calves. One day, Krishna and Balarama along with the other cowherd boys went to the pasturing ground to tend the calves. Meanwhile the news of Krishna's uprooting the two giant trees had reached kamsa who promptly began to contemplate how this child Krishna could be killed. Thinking in this way, Kamsa immediately called Vatsasura and ordered him as follows, "This fellow Krishna has become my greatest enemy. He looks after the calves in Vrndavana and plays with His friends. You go there and kill Him by hook or by crook."

Having received the order of the king, Vatsasura quickly went to the bank of Yamuna, assumed the form of a calf, and entered the pasturing grounds. Despite this disguise, Krishna could immediately recognize him and told His brother Balarama about this demon. Krishna said, "O brother! Look at this demon Vatsaka. He is very sinful. Kamsa has sent him to kill Me. He has come here to kill Me, but instead he himself will be killed just now. Just watch the fun as I annihilate him."

After speaking in this way, Govinda took His yellow upper cloth and made a noose out of it. He tightened it around His waist and approached Vatsasura who was grazing along with the other cows. Krishna quickly caught hold of his hind legs and tail, whipped him around forcibly, and threw him up into a tree. The demon lost his life and fell down to the ground from the top of the tree. As soon as demon Vatsasura was killed, everyone became very happy. Flowers started raining down from the sky onto Krishna, and the demigods played drums. All the inhabitants of Vrndavana however became frightened at seeing this incident.

## The killing of Bakasura

King kamsa was very surprised upon hearing the news of Vatsasura's death. Realizing that Krishna was becoming a more and more powerful enemy day by day, he constantly devised new plans to kill Krishna. One day, he called his brother Bakasura and said to him. "Listen, O brother Baka, do not ignore these words of mine. The son of Nanda has become my greatest enemy. His business is to look after the cows on the bank of Yamuna. You go there quickly and destroy Him forever." Bakasura immediately went to the bank of Yamuna and assumed the form of a mighty duck. Meanwhile, Krishna had become very tired looking after the cows, so He went to drink some water in the Yamuna. As He approached the river, Bakasura suddenly grabbed Krishna and swallowed Him. All the demigods in the sky began to lament with great fear, but when the Bakasura demon tried to swallow Krishna, he felt a fiery sensation in his throat. The demon could neither swallow Krishna nor spit Him out of his mouth. His whole body began to burn. With great difficulty, he finally threw Krishna out. The demon then assumed his original form and looked very formidable, his giant body extending up to sixteen miles in length. Even the demigods were afraid of him. Bakasura gathered his forces and prepared himself to attempt to swallow Krishna again. Krishna simply smiled and said to him, "Now you have come under my grip. You will not escape this time. Fearing you, the demigods hide themselves, but today Yamaraja will certainly take away your life. I will satisfy all the demigods by killing you which will also make king Kamsa even more frightened." Saying this, Govinda wrapped His yellow upper cloth around His head and tightened His waist band. Sri Hari then caught hold of Bakasura's beak with His two hands and wrenched it apart, killing him. The demigods smiled and joyfully chanted, "Jaya! Jaya!" when Krishna, the son of Nanda killed Bakasura by splitting his whole body into two pieces. The demigods happily played drums in the sky and showered flowers on the Lord. After killing Bakasura, the son of Nanda returned home a hero. The inhabitants of Vrndavana had become very afraid when they saw Bakasura swallowing Krishna, but instead of Baka killing Krishna, Krishna killed him. All the cowherd boys returned to their respective homes with great joy and narrated this incident to their mothers. Who other than Krishna can perform such wonderful activities? While describing this pastime of Govinda, Gunaraja Khan, who is a servant of Govinda, becomes filled with fear.

## The killing of Aghasura

King Kamsa grew very unhappy upon hearing that Krishna had killed Bakasura on the bank of Yamuna. He asked his messenger repeatedly to explain exactly how Krishna had killed Baka. He thought Baka possessed great strength and that everyone in the world knew of his terrible prowess. "But how could Krishna defeat him alone then? How could a mere child like Krishna have killed him so effortlessly?" Thinking like this, Kamsa became more and more afraid.

One day, he summoned the demon Agha to his side, and ordered him, "Listen, O Aghasura, do you remember how Krishna killed my sister Putana, how He killed the powerful Trnavarta, and how He killed the giant Baka while drinking water in the Yamuna? Although He is but a small boy, He performs all these wonderful acts. He has taken birth in Gokula in order to kill me. I know you have immense power, and no one can rival you in the three worlds. So I request you to quickly go and kill Krishna."

When Aghasura heard these pleading words of kamsa, he assured him that his request will be fulfilled. kamsa, feeling greatly reassured by Aghasura's words, got down from his throne and embraced the demon with joy. At the order of the king, Aghasura assumed a form of a python and entered the vicinity of Vrndavana.

Meanwhile, one morning, Krishna along with his friends had gone to the forest to tend the cows. All the boys had taken their lunch along with them to have a picnic in the forest. They were to take their meal together on the bank of Yamuna at noon.

After arriving at the pasturing ground, Krishna and Balarama let the cows graze in the field while they themselves enjoyed blowing buffalo horns. Suddenly, Krishna and His cowherd boyfriends espied a huge serpent lying on the ground. This python was the formidable demon, Aghasura. His body was one hundred and sixty miles long and twentyfour miles wide.

When he opened his mouth, his lower jaw touched the ground while his upper jaw touched the sky. His mouth was red like fire. Everyone within the three worlds was afraid of this terrible demon. Unaware that this large red cave-like hole was in fact the mouth of a demon, all of the innocent friends of Krishna playfully entered Aghasura's gaping jaws. The demon had kept his mouth open in order to swallow up Krishna and His friends, but Krishna did not follow the other boys into the mouth. Instead, He remained outside contemplating how to save his friends from what appeared a certain death. The demon Aghasura however continued thinking that as soon as Krishna entered his mouth, he would swallow Him up along with His friends. Krishna thought for a while about how He could kill the demon and at the same time save the boys and the calves. After some deliberation, Krishna bravely entered the mouth of Aghasura. All the demigods, led by Brahma, had gathered to see the fun while hiding themselves within the clouds. When Krishna entered the demon's jaws, Aghasura slowly shut his mouth. Krishna, the king of all mystic powers, used His strength to obstruct the demon's breathing by expanding Himself. Although the demon had a gigantic body, he was choked by the expanding body of Krishna. Unable to breathe any longer, his big eyes swelled violently and burst out of their sockets as he suffocated. His life air could not find a way to come out from any source, so ultimately it burst out of a hole on the top of his skull. Krishna then came out of the demon's body and held the mouth of the demon wide open so that His friends could come out as well. Soon all the boys and calves came back out of the gigantic body of the demon. In this way, Krishna killed that sinful demon who thereby attained liberation as all his sinful reactions were destroyed simply at Krishna's auspicious touch. The denizens of heaven began to shower flowers on Govinda. King Kamsa lost his patience and felt annoyed and aggrieved simultaneously at the news of Aghasura's death. Thus Maladhar Basu narrates the topics of Krishna.

## Brahma steals the calves and the cowherd boys

Lord Krishna, who wears a garland of forest flowers, killed Aghasura; much to the delight of His friends who promptly hugged Him with joy. The boys then informed Krishna that they were hungry. Krishna immediately let all the calves and cows grazing on the banks of Yamuna to drink water from the Yamuna while He and His friends sat down together to take lunch. All the boys sat around Krishna who sat down in the middle. Krishna personally distributed rice mixed with yogurt to all His friends. Some of them took it in their hands, some on a plate, and some on leaves. Some of them kept their food in baskets and placed the baskets on their laps. In this way, Lord Krishna, in the company of His cowherd boy friends, enjoyed a picnic on the bank of the river Yamuna. Brahma watched this pastime of Krishna from the heavenly planets and became struck with wonder. He immediately came to Vraja in order to test Krishna's supremacy.
