Brahma came and stole all the calves that were there on the bank of Yamuna. Turning to Krishna, the cowheard friends inquired, "Listen, O Govinda! What happened to the cows? We don't see them any more?" Krishna immediately told His friends to continue enjoying their meals while He Himself set out to find the lost cows. Searching for the cows, Krishna could not find them anywhere. After some time He returned to the picnic spot, and was surprised to see the whole place empty. He could not find His friends either. Immediately realizing this to be the mischief of Brahma. Krsna thought, "Brahma did this to test My greatness." Krishna then with His inconceivable potency instantly created the same number of calves and cowherd boys who looked exactly like before in age, nature, qualities, dress, activities, and bodily shapes. These cowherd boys took charge of their own calves and returned home in the evening. At home, they drank their mothers' breast milk and stayed in their houses just like the original boys. In this way, Krishna completely bewildered Brahma, who had stolen the calves and boys of Vraja and kept them captive for one full year. When there were only two or three days left in the year, both Krishna and Balarama went to the forest to tend the calves. Brahma returned at this moment to see what was going on, and was amazed to find that Krishna, along with His cows and cowherd boys, were enjoying pastimes in the forest of Vrndavana as before. He thought, "I stole all the cows and cowherd boys. From where did Krishna get all these calves and cowherd boys again? Is it that they have deceived me and run away from where I am holding them captive?" Brahma went back to the cave where he had hidden Krsna's friends and found that the calves and boys were still here in captivity.

It was then that Brahma came to his senses and realized that the Supreme Lord had bewildered him. Lord Narayana had bewildered him by His illusory energy. So Brahma returned to the banks of the Yamuna, and when he approached Krishna in Vraja, Krishna was alone. Soon afterward, Krishna was joined by Balarama and then the calves and the cowherd boys came along. Brahma saw how all the calves and the cowherd boys were in fact four-armed forms of Narayana, each of them holding a conch, a disc, a club, and a lotus in their hands. Prajapati Brahma also saw that each of these expansions of Narayana was being glorified by separate Brahmas.

He became struck with wonder and fell into a dilemma. He realized that he had certainly committed an offense at the feet of the Supreme Lord and became afraid that the Lord would be upset with him.

## Prayers by Brahma

Brahma got down from his swan carrier and offered his respectful obeisances at the lotus feet of Krishna by touching all his four heads to the ground. Tears began to flow from his eyes as he folded his hands and began to offer prayers to the Lord.

"O Lord! Why do You bewilder me like this? I am a very insignificant person. You can create millions of Brahmas in a moment's time. People call me aja or unborn and because of this, I have become blinded with pride. That is why I failed to recognize Your greatness. O Lord! I was manifested from the lotus which sprouted from Your navel. Are you therefore not my father? I am not unborn, but rather it is You who is unborn and the husband of Laksmi. You are the beginning and end of everything. You are Narayana. You are the cause of the innumerable universes. The three material modes goodness, passion, and ignorance are working under Your direction. O Lord Hari! You have created me. Who has the power to describe Your glories? Millions of universes constantly emanate from the pores of Your skin. I am only an insignificant Brahma among millions of Brahmas. The measurement of my body is about eight feet, yet You can create millions of such Brahmas within the blink of an eye. You can create millions of universes within moments and annihilate them simply at Your will. You are the essence of the whole cosmic manifestation and the cause of all universes. You have no beginning, middle, or end, therefore, You are addressed as Narayana. Only out of great good fortune one obtains the association of Your devotees. O Lord Govinda! Please remove Your illusion. Please do not bewilder me in this way. A child lives within the womb of his mother. In the course of his stay in the womb, he hurts his mother by kicking her, but the affectionate mother never takes it seriously or becomes angry. Similarly, there are millions of Brahmas within Your body, and if one tiny Brahma has offended You, You cannot possibly become angry at him. Listen, O Damodara, I will be ruined if You do not bestow Your mercy upon me."

After speaking these words, Brahma began to cry. When Lord Hari heard these pathetic words of Brahma, He became compassionate and immediately withdrew His illusory energy. Brahma joyfully offered his obeisances at the feet of Krishna and Balarama, and immediately returned all the calves and cowherd boys that he had previously stolen. Then, after circumambulating Rama and Gopala, Brahma happily returned to his abode. Although this incident took place within a few moments, the inhabitants of Vraja realized it only after one full year.

Meanwhile, Krishna's cowherd friends, who were still holding rice and yogurt in their hands, called their beloved Gopala to come and enjoy the meal with them. Such are the wonderful pastimes of Krishna that took place in Vrndavana!

After spending their days enjoying various sporting activities in the forest, Krishna, Balarama, and their friends returned home in the evening. They played buffalo horns as they walked back home. On returning home, all the boys informed their mothers about how Krishna had killed the terrible demon Aghasura. All the Vrajavasls began to think that their beloved Krishna was certainly not an ordinary human being. His activities were uncommon. He had appeared at the house of Nanda to perform activities which are impossible even for the demigods. All of those demons who came to kill Krishna got themselves killed just as flies rush to the fire only to lose their fragile lives. Thus by killing Aghasura, Krishna protected all the inhabitants of Vraja. Anyone who hears these pastimes will be victorious over his enemies. In each and every house of Vraja there is only discussion about the topics of Krishna. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Krishna's pastimes with great happiness.

## Blissful sporting on the bank of Yamuna and the killing of Dhenukasura

Early one morning, Rama and Damodara, accompanied by the cowherd boys and calves, went to the forest of Vrndavana while playing their flutes and buffalo horns. They enjoyed various sporting activities in the waters of Yamuna. Some of them jumped like monkeys, and some of them danced like peacocks while others imitated the voice of the cuckoo. Rama and Damodara relished all these pastimes, and They encouraged their friends to enjoy more and more. Sometimes the two brothers would follow the shadow of the birds flying in the sky with great joy. Sometimes Murari would pluck flowers from the forest and decorate His chest, ears, and head. In this way, Krishna and His cowherd friends enjoyed many wonderful pastimes in Vrndavana. One day, Krishna's friends said to Him, "My dear Krishna we are very hungry. We are unable to walk any further. We can see some palm trees in front of us. This forest is known as Tala. It belongs to King kamsa, and Dhenukasura is in charge of this place. Therefore, if You can somehow kill that demon, we will be able to eat tala fruits."

On hearing the request of His friends, Gopala smiled and thought of how His friends wanted to eat some palm fruits. Taking His friends with Him, Krsna entered the tala forest. Balarama went ahead of the group, grabbed a palm tree, and shook it vigorously. As soon as He did that, many ripe palm fruits fell to the ground. The cowherd boys hurriedly picked up the ripe palm fruits and began to eat them. Seeing this fun, Krishna laughed with ecstasy. Then Balarama again caught hold of another palm tree and shook it. As a result, many ripe and unripe palm fruits fell to the ground. A short distance away from the boys, Dhenukasura heard the unexpected sound of someone shaking the trees. He shouted, "Who is breaking my palm trees?" and ran to find out. From afar, the demon saw that Balarama was picking up all of the fallen palm fruits and feeding them to His friends.

Dhenukasura came rushing over and caught hold of Balarama's throat. Balarama kicked the demon forcefully out of anger and surprise. Falling down from Balarama's kick made the demon angrier and more frustrated. He got up and growled at Balarama, challenging Him with great force. Balarama caught hold of the demon's hind leg and threw him up into the air above the palm trees. Dhenukasura fell to the ground with a great crash and died on the spot, all his bones smashed to pieces. In this way, Balarama killed that demon and sent him to the abode of Yamaraja. Dhenukasura was killed by the kick of Balarama, who in the course of killing the demon, collected many palm fruits for His cowherd friends as well. After the demon's demise, the cowherd boys peacefully enjoyed the ripe, sweet tala fruits. The cowherd boys had been amazed to see how Balarama effortlessly killed that gigantic Dhenukasura.

In the evening, Rama and Kanai, along with the cowherd boys and calves, returned home with great joy. Soon a messenger went to King Kamsa and informed him that Kanai had killed Dhenukasura and had taken away all of the palm fruits, too. On hearing about this incident, Kamsa started to breathe heavily. His heart began to tremble, and he became extremely anxious as he kept on making plans to kill Krishna in his mind. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya remembering the pastimes of Govinda.

## Krishna jumps in the Kaliya lake

On another occasion, Krishna, in the company of His cowherd friends, went to the forest of Vrndavana to tend the calves. On this particular day, Krishna did not take Balarama with Him. Krishna was adorned with a garland of forest flowers. With great fun and joy, He slowly approached the particular spot where the poisonous serpent Kaliya lived. His friends, who were very thirsty, began to drink the waters of Yamuna. As soon as they drank this water, all of them fell down dead because the waters of Yamuna had been poisoned by Kaliya. Krishna looked around and saw that all His friends were lying dead. He immediately understood that it was an act of Kaliya. Bringing His friends back to life by casting His nectarean glance on them, Krsna watched the boys get up as if nothing had happened and began to sing His glories. Krishna thought that this was not a fit place for Kaliya to live. He thought, "I will be enjoying pastimes and sporting activities with My friends at this place, therefore I must remove Kaliya from this place and make all the inhabitants of Vraja free from fear. Otherwise, anyone who will drink water from this lake will die instantly. Kaliya must leave this place at once so that everyone in Vrndavana can drink Yamuna water and live peacefully."

After contemplating in this way, Krishna climbed up on a nearby kadamba tree, and from there, jumped into the middle of the lake within the Yamuna. Krishna began to sport within the waters of the lake which had been inhabited by Kaliya and the other serpents.

Sensing someone had intruded upon his space in the Yamuna, Kaliya became very angry. He immediately went before Krishna and bit His chest. Some time before, as soon as Krishna had jumped in the waters of Yamuna at Kaliyadaha, all the cowherd boys ran home to tell Nanda and Yasoda what was happening.

## The cowherd boys narrate this incident to mother Yasoda

The cowherd boys hurried to Mother Yasoda and said, "Listen, O Mother Yasoda and Mother Rohini! Listen, O all the cowherd men of Vrndavana! Wherever you are, listen to this! This morning, we went to the bank of Yamuna with our calves. We became very thirsty, and so we went to Yamuna and drank its water. As soon as we drank the water of Yamuna that had been made poisonous by the demon Kaliya, we became unconscious, but your beautiful Gopala brought us back to life. After that, He jumped into the water of Yamuna and up onto the serpent Kaliya. The formidable and gigantic serpent coiled around your son and bit Him on His chest. That is all we saw, but we are sure that Krishna has died by now because we saw that the water of Yamuna had turned red from the blood of Krishna's body. There was a tumultuous sound and many bad omens were visible there. The earth trembled, a body of fire fell from the sky, and many other inauspicious symptoms could be seen. Because of all of these symptoms, we are sure that Krishna is no more."

As soon as Mother Yasoda heard their report, in a frantic state she began to beat her chest as she rushed towards the bank of Yamuna crying profusely. Rohini, Nanda Maharaja, and all the other inhabitants of Vraja followed close behind, running to the bank of Yamuna.

When Mother Yasoda reached the Yamuna, she could not find Krishna there on the bank so, crying pathetically, she fell to the ground.

## Yasoda and others cry in lamentation

"This water of Yamuna is full of sin. Moreover, it is mixed with the deadly poison of the serpent Kaliya. Therefore, who can tolerate its poisonous effect? All of the creatures who live on either bank of Yamuna die because of the poisonous breath of Kaliya. O my beloved Gopala! Where are You? Please come out. This Kaliya is so sinful that not even a bird flies over the spot. Even the sun and moon avoid Kaliya's proximity. O my lotus-eyed son! Who asked You to jump in the water of Yamuna? O child! Come and see Your brother Balabhadra waiting for You. Look at all of Your friends standing here. See, all the inhabitants of Gokula are waiting here for You. Look, my son, at this buffalo horn, this stick, these clothes all of these things are Yours. Take them and let us go back home. You are the life and soul of each and every resident of Vraja. You always deliver them at the time of danger. If You live, then all of us live. Listen, O my son Damodara, if You don't come back we will also not return home, but instead we will give up our lives here on this very spot. What is the use of our wealth and followers? Without You, the entire city of Gokula is empty. O son of Nanda! Look, it is already noon time. Please come out of the water and drink the breast milk of Your mother. Come and sit on my lap. If I don't see You even for a moment, I feel that the ten directions are empty. Putana came to kill You, but then You survived. You survived when Sakata fell on You. You survived when the great demon Trnavarta took You up into the sky. O Damodara! You killed Vatsasura in the pasturing ground. You also annihilated Bakasura by tearing apart his beaks. O Krishna! You easily killed the wicked Aghasura, whom even the demigods fear. You and Balarama killed Dhenukasura and enjoyed palm fruits along with Your friends. You are not even seven years old, so how can You leave us like this?"

Lamenting in this way, Yasoda and Rohini rolled on the ground as they cried profusely. Maharaja Nanda cried loudly, and all the cowherd men of Vraja became despondent as if they had lost their lives. All the men and women of Vrndavana ran to Yamuna and, not seeing Krishna there, cried in desperation. They lamented, "O dear child! There is no doubt that You are our life and soul. Now who will save us from danger? From today onwards, the whole Gokula will be empty. Your father and mother especially will lead a pathetic life."

As all the residents of Vraja cried profusely on the bank of Yamuna, they continued to anxiously look in the waters of Yamuna hoping that Krishna would soon come out. Everyone except Balarama cried in lamentation for He alone was aware of the glories and power of Krishna. Balarama slowly walked to the shore of the river Yamuna and spoke these words in a soft tone. "Krishna, You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You are Narayana Himself. You are the cause of the creation, maintenance and annihilation of the entire material world. By the request of Brahma and the other demigods, and to diminish the burden of the earth as well, You have descended in Gokula. Actually, You are the life and soul of the inhabitants of Gokula. Now without You, they will not survive. Therefore, keep My word and give up Your illusion. Please deliver this terrible serpent Kaliya."

In order to keep the words of His brother, and to stop the pathetic crying of His mother, Lord Sri Hari smiled and emerged from the lake of Kaliya in Yamuna dancing on the hood of the serpent Kaliya. The serpent Kaliya almost lost his life underneath the burden of Krishna's body as Krishna had made His body extremely heavy. Soon the pride of Kaliya was completely smashed. On seeing the condition of their husband, the wives of Kaliya came forward and began to offer prayers to Krishna with folded hands. O readers! In this Age of Kali there is no better way for deliverance than to remember Lord Hari. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the glories of Krishna while fixing his mind on His lotus feet.

## Prayers by the wives of the serpent Kaliya

The wives of Kaliya prayed to Krishna as follows: "O my Lord! You are identical with Narayana. You are the proprietor of the whole universe. By Your direction, creation, maintenance, and annihilation take place. You are the unlimited Lord and the cause of all causes. You are the almighty. You have created all living entities including the demigods, the human beings, the birds and the beasts. If You desire to kill someone, nobody can save him. You have created us with snake bodies. We do not know what is good and what is bad; we kill as soon as we get the opportunity. Still, we must have performed some vows or other pious activities that have made it possible for us to achieve Your lotus feet. Even if we take birth millions of times, and perform the severest austerities, we will not be able to understand Your potency. Laksmi had undergone severe austerities for many lifetimes; then only was she able to touch Your lotus feet. This Kaliya must be very fortunate for he received the touch of Your lotus feet on his own head. It is good that Kaliya took birth in this world, and it is good that he lived in this water of Yamuna. Today is the most auspicious day for Kaliya, and for all of us, because today we got Your darsana."

After speaking in this way, the wives of Kaliya specifically requested Krishna to spare the life of their husband. When Krishna heard this heartfelt appeal of the wives of Kaliya, He felt compassion for them. Thereafter, Kaliya felt somewhat embarrassed, and he too began to offer prayers to Govinda. "O Hari! You have given me this serpent birth. By nature I am envious, and I cannot avoid it. Due to my own faults I am suffering. Please forgive me for my offenses. O Lord Gadadhara, kindly instruct me; tell me what I should do."

The Lord Sri Krishna who wears a garland of forest flowers then ordered Kaliya to immediately leave the river Yamuna and go somewhere else so that the inhabitants of Vrndavana would no longer die of poisonous effects from drinking the water ofYanuma.

## Appeal by Kaliya

When Krishna ordered Kaliya to leave in this way, Kaliya thought for a while and then began to speak as follows, "O Lord! There is no one within the three worlds who can reverse Your order. Now let me tell You something about my previous history. You know very well that I had a quarrel with Garuda a long time ago. Garuda used to eat snakes wherever he found them. Soon the snakes were on the verge of extinction. Concerned about the fate of my race, one day, I took the chance to consult my friend Kasyapa about this situation. He suggested that every day we offer one snake to Garuda as his food, and then he will not destroy all of us. We agreed to try his suggestion, and for a long time indeed this procedure went on. Finally the day I had dreaded arrived and it was my turn to go to Garuda with some gift. I became very afraid thinking that day would be my last. Suddenly I remembered that Garuda had been cursed that he would die if he ever visited the lake in the Yamuna. A long time ago, the great sage Saubhari was performing austerities in the waters of Yamuna. By his side, a fish lived along with its family members. Just as the sage was in the middle of his puja, Garuda arrived on the spot, and ate that fish and its family members. The sage had compassion for the fish and, out of anger, cursed Garuda that if he ever came to Yamuna again to eat fish he would die immediately. That is why if any bird comes to this place and touches this water, he dies instantly. Since then, I have been residing in this place peacefully and happily. But, O Lord! Now that You have ordered me to leave this place, how will I escape the wrath of Garuda?"

After hearing Kaliya's appeals, the Lord smiled and reassured him, "O Kaliya, do not be afraid of Garuda. He will not touch you because My lotus feet have been imprinted on your head. As soon as he will see that, he will leave you respectfully."

Blessed by the order of Lord Sri Krishna, Kaliya circumambulated Him, and left the river Yamuna happily with his family. Before departing, he offered Krishna many gifts like jewels, diamonds, and so on. After Kaliya left Yamuna, Govinda came out from the water fully decorated with various jewels and ornaments. As soon as Krishna came out, all the inhabitants of Vraja regained their lost lives. Mother Yasoda came running to embrace her child Krsna, and held Him on her lap. Nanda and other cowherd men danced in jubilation.

Anyone who hears this pastime of Kaliya-damana will not have any fear of snakes in this world. By hearing of Krishna's pastimes, one is sure to become delivered from this material world. This is the opinion of Gunaraja Khan.

## Kaliya leaves Yamuna

After the great serpent Kaliya left the river Yamuna along with his family and associates, the inhabitants of Gokula became somewhat worried about Krishna. They thought that Krishna could not be an ordinary child, but that He must be the incarnation of Narayana who had descended in this world in the form of a human being. Whatever Krishna did was beyond the imagination of the residents of Vraja. They could not even stand near the bank of Yamuna because of the burning sensation produced from the poison of Kaliya, but Krishna had not only entered within that poisonous water of Yamuna, but He had also made Kaliya leave Yamuna forever. At that time, millions of other large and poisonous snakes left the waters of Yamuna as well, and took shelter in the dense forests and mountain caves. What to speak of the human beings, even the demigods grew afraid at the sight of those snakes.

Yasoda and Rohini openly expressed their feelings of fondness and attachment for their beloved child Krishna. They embraced Him with affection and cried, "O Kanai! You left us almost dead. It is due to our good fortune that the Supreme Lord has saved You."

In this way, the day passed and the evening slowly approached. The inhabitants of Vrndavana ate whatever they could find there fruits, roots, milk, and yogurt and took rest on the bank of the river Yamuna. When finally they were fast asleep, all of a sudden, a devastating forest fire erupted. It was the month of Jaistha, or May, so the atmosphere was already hot, Therefore, the fire began to spread quickly.

The blazing fire soon burnt the entire forest into ashes and started to work its way towards the banks of Yamuna. The cowherd boy friends of Krishna heard the noise made by the blazing forest fire, and woke up in astonishment. They all ran to Krishna and exclaimed, "O Krishna! O Balarama! Look at this forest fire! Please do something to save us. Not only us, but even Your parents are suffering the wrath of the blazing fire. You are the life and soul of us all and we completely depend on You. O Narayana! It is You alone who can save us from this danger. It is not good that we lose our lives when You are present before us."

As soon as Krishna heard these touching words, He assumed the universal form and swallowed the entire forest fire then and there. In this way, Krishna saved all the inhabitants of Vraja by personally devouring the blazing fire.

Early the next morning, all the cowherd residents led by Nanda happily returned to their respective homes. They simply talked about Krishna's glorious activities day and night. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the pastimes of Krishna.

## The killing of Pralambasura

Soon the wicked Kamsa heard how Krishna had chastised the serpent Kaliya and driven him out from Yamuna and that Krishna had saved the residents of Vraja from the devastating forest fire by personally swallowln g it. On hearing about these extraordinary acts, Kamsa almost fainted with dismay. Being unable to tolerate Krishna's presence anymore, the evil minded Kamsa called for Pralambasura and said to him, "O dear brother Pralamba! I have a request. I have a great enemy in the city of Gokula. I want you to create some kind of trick and kill Rama and Damodara. Do not tak e Them lightly thinking that They are just small boys."

Having received this order, the powerful Pralamba immediately took the form of a human being and secretly lived among the residents of Vrnavana.

One morning, Krishna gathered all His cowherd friends and said "O friends! It is very hot today. Let us all go to Yamuna and enjoy water sports."

Krishna, along with His friends and calves, soon started for the bank of Yamuna while playing His enchanting flute. This pastime took place when Krishna was only seven years old. He looked exquisitely attractive and charmed the minds of everyone, even Cupid. Although it was summer, by the grace of Krishna, everyone felt the pleasant climate of spring. By now, the demon Pralamba had assumed the form of a small boy and entered the midst of the cowherd boys. Krishna however understood the intention and ruse of the demon, and began to think how to kill him. After some time, Krishna called all His friends and said, "Let us all go to Bhandiravana, and play there."

So, the boys happily went off to Bhandiravana and began to enjoy transcendental pastimes. It was decided that they would wrestle each other in pairs and whichever pair lost would have to carry the victorious members on their backs. The cowherd boy Sridama won the duel, and Krishna had to carry him on His back, which He did. Seeing this as his golden opportunity, the demon Pralamba intentionally accepted defeat and was very glad to carry Balarama on his back. The demon had a wicked desire to attack Balarama, so he took Balarama on his shoulder and carried Him to a faraway place. As soon as the demon and Balarama were out of sight of the rest of the cowherd boys, Pralamba assumed his original form. His gigantic body stretched and grew to touch the sky as he carried Balarama towards Mathura. Krishna had immediately understood what was going on and took action. "So!" He exclaimed, "O Baladeva! Why are You pretending to be an ordinary boy? Assume Your original form and kill the demon quickly." As advised by Krishna, Balai squeezed the demon's throat with His two legs and hit his head with His powerful fist. Struck by Balarama, Pralambasura started bleeding profusely and fell down to the ground dead. All the denizens of heaven began to shower flowers on Baladeva. After this incident, Krishna and Balarama returned home with Their friends and cows. The sinful kamsa was shocked when he heard about the death of Pralambasura. He fell off his throne in astonishment.

O readers! Please hear these wonderful pastimes of Krishna and Balarama narrated by Gunaraja Khan.

## Krishna's pastimes of tending the calves in the pasturing ground

The inhabitants of Vraja were amazed to hear how Balarama had killed the formidable demon Pralamba. They thanked Krishna and Balarama for timely saving them from different kinds of disturbances and calamities. As usual, as if nothing extraordinary had happened, the two brothers peacefully took Their dinner and went to bed. The next morning, Krishna took the calves and departed towards the pasturing ground. These fortunate calves grazed happily in and around the banks of Yamuna; drinking water from the sacred river whenever they felt thirsty. When the calves were taking rest under the shade of the trees at noon, all of a sudden a forest fire broke out. The cowherd boys were unable to move as the blazing fire swiftly moved towards them from the four directions. In great anxiety, they shouted, "Listen, O Rama and Krishna! Please come and save us from the fire! This devastating fire is about to swallow us. You are the Lord and savior of all the Vrajavasis. Therefore we take shelter of You. Those who chant Your name even once become delivered from all dangers, and do not take birth again in this world. What can the fire do in the face of Your immense potency?"

When Krishna heard this appeal from His friends, He simply smiled. In a fraction of a second, Krishna drank the entire blazing fire. The cowherd boys witnessed Krishna, who is nondifferent from Narayana, swallowing the forest fire in one great gulp. The boys began to dance in jubilation with their arms held high. Krishna then took His cowherd friends and wandered about from one forest to another.

All the inhabitants of Vraja including the birds, beasts, aquatics, insects, and trees, joyfully served Krishna in their own capacity, just as a devotee serves his beloved Lord. After the summer season, the rainy season arrived. The whole Vrndavana forests became green and nourished by the rainfalls. Heavy rain showered from the clouds, accompanied by lightning. Peacocks began to dance with joy in the forests of Vrndavana. In this way, Krishna, Balarama, and the cowherd boys of Vrndavana enjoyed many blissful pastimes.

Damodara used to relish various foodstuffs like sweets, yogurt, and butter with His friends on the bank of Yamuna. Gradually the rainy season er)ded, and the autumn season came. People became very happy as the sky cleared, and the muddy roads dried to hard-packed earthen thoroughfares again. The aquatics were very happy in the abundance of water, and they found no trouble taking care of their young. The farmers joyfully tilled their land with ploughs in the cool autumn breezes. By and large the residents of Vraja enjoyed a sense of relief at the passing of the summer heat.

The autumn nights were especially pleasing because the moonlight cooled the whole atmosphere. Various types of flowers began to bloom, and the air carried the sweet aroma everywhere to the tune of the son of Nanda playing His flute with great happiness. The sweet sound of Krsna's flute completely bewildered the young gopis of Vraja. Krishna decorated His head with a peacock feather, wore earrings made of flowers on His ears, and dressed in yellow garments like a dancer. The damsels of Vraja became overwhelmed and could not remain steady seeing Krishna's enchanting beauty. It is impossible for the ordinary human beings to properly describe Krishna's beauty in words.

## The gopis of Vraja worship Goddess Katyayani

At the end of the autumn season, the weather in Vraja slowly changed. A slight chill in the air cooled the days. The damsels of Vraja had a habit of going to take bath in the Yamuna. Once there, they would take off their garments on the river bank and slide into the refreshing waters of Yamuna. Every day after taking their bath, they would worship goddess Candi, or Katyayani. They had made a deity of Katyayani on the bank of Yamuna and worshiped her by offering Yamuna water and flowers. They then prayed to her to have Nanda's son Krishna as their husband. In this way, they regularly came to Yamuna and worshiped goddess Parvati after taking their bath.

One day, after leaving their garments on the shore, the young gopis entered into the waters of Yamuna and began to enjoy water sports. Meanwhile, Govinda secretly went to that place, stole all of the garments and ornaments of the gopis, and climbed on the top of a kadamba tree there. When the gopis came out of the water, they were surprised to see that they could not find their garments and ornaments. They were shocked and became frightened; how would they return home? They wondered who could have possibly taken their garments away. They thought, "We play in the waters of Yamuna all the time, but such a thing as this has never happened before. Although Kamsa is a powerful king, still there are many thieves around." Looking up as they considered their situation, they saw Kanai sitting high in the branches of the kadamba tree. The garments of the gopis were neatly folded by His side. Seeing Krishna's mischievous behavior, the gopis became very angry and said, "O son of Nanda! Why do You act like this? O pampered child of Nanda! We are feeling cold because we have been standing in the water for a long time. Please give us our garments and ornaments back so that we can quickly go home. If You do not give our clothes back, we will go straight to the court of Kamsa and complain about You. Then You will be arrested as a thief and be punished. So it is better for You to return our garments and let us go home. O Son of Nanda! We beg You to kindly return our dresses and ornaments."

Hearing the requests of the gopis, Krishna began to laugh. He picked up the gopis' clothes and came down from the tree top to the ground. He then addressed the gopis, "Listen, O young girls! What can your Kamsa do to Me? Even if you complain about Me to Kamsa, he cannot do anything to Me. Kamsa has already sent many powerful demons to kill Me, but I killed them all and sent them to the abode of Yamaraja. I will return your clothes provided you beg for them. O young girls! Your vow will become futile as a result of taking bath in the Yamuna without any clothes on. If you want your vow to be fulfilled, come out from the water and take your garments from Me after offering your obeisances."

After hearing this proposal of Krishna, the gopis of Vraja bent their heads in shame and could not decide what to do, although at the same time they could no longer remain suffering in the freezing cold water of Yamuna. They thought if they ignored Krishna's words, their lives would be ruined. Finally, giving up their shyness, the gopis came out of the water covering the upper and lower parts of their bodies with their two hands. Slowly all the gopis went before Krishna to get their garments and ornaments back from Him. Krishna stood there with the garments of the gopis on His shoulder, and said, "Come here and take your clothes. You all spoke so many things with pride. Now you fold your hands and beg forgiveness from Me, otherwise I will not return your garments."

The gopis realized that Krishna was very determined and would not compromise in any way, so they came forward with folded hands and offered their obeisances. Krishna became very pleased by the behavior of the gopis, and He happily returned their garments to them one by one. After getting back their dresses and ornaments, the gopis felt very satisfied, and returned home singing of the wonderful characteristics of Krishna on their way. The gopis had nothing in their hearts other than Krishna.

Gunaraja Khan, following in the footsteps of the gopis, remembers the lotus feet of Krishna.

## Begging of food from the sacrificial Brahmanas

After returning the garments and ornaments to the gopis, the son of Nanda went back to the Bhandiravana forest and joined His friends. After they had played different sports, the cowherd boys said, "O Rama and Krishna! We are very hungry. Can you arrange some food for us to eat?" Krishna thought for a minute about where to get some food and then He told His friends to go to the sacrificial arena where some brahmanas were engaged in performing austerities, and beg food from them. These brahmanas were attached to performing ritualistic activities like the ahgirasa sacrifice. Krishna, knowing the intentions and mood of those brahmanas, asked His friend Sridama, "My dear friend, go to the place of the sacrificial brahmanas and beg some food for all of us. You may give My name as a reference while begging. I am sure they will give you a large amount of food."

Eager to follow Krsna's instructions, the boys quickly went to the sacrificial arena and offered their obeisances to the brahmanas. They folded their hands and requested, "O great brahmanas\\ The sons of Nanda, Balarama and Krishna, offer Their respects to you and ask us to beg some food from you. They are busy taking care of the cows on the banks of Yamuna. Both of them are very hungry and, realizing that you were performing sacrifices nearby, They have sent us to beg some food from you." To their great surprise, the brahmanas did not pay any attention to the words of the cowherd boys and promptly refused to honor their request. These brahmanas had no respect and devotion for Krishna because they did not serve His lotus feet. When the brahmanas refused to give any food to Krishna and Balarama, the astonished cowherd boys returned to the Lord of the universe, Krishna. They informed Krishna that the brahmanas would not give them any food. Krishna and Balarama simply smiled at the predictable behavior of the brahmanas.

## Begging of food from the wives of the sacrificial brahmanas

Krishna then said to His friends, "O friends! Listen to Me. Go now to the wives of those brahmanas as at this time of day, these pious ladies must be engaged in cooking lunch. You go and tell them that the two brothers Rama and Kanu have requested that they give some food. I am sure if you give My name as a reference they will certainly give you an abundance of foodstuffs."

Being thus ordered by Krishna, the cowherd boys again went to the house of the brahmanas, but this time they approached the wives. The boys said, "O respected Thakurams, Rama and Krishna are tending cows in the forest. They are very hungry, and They have sent us to you to get some food. Kindly give us some delicious foodstuffs so that Krishna will be very pleased."

## The wives of the brahmanas come to see Krishna with plates full of foodstuffs in their hands

As soon as the wives of the brahmanas heard this, they immediately thanked their good fortune. They thought, "Today is the most auspicious day in our lives. Rama and Krishna have incarnated in this world to diminish the burden of the earth. Although Krishna is the proprietor of the three worlds, He begged food from us. We should personally go with food and see His lotus feet."

After thinking in this way, the wives of the brahmanas took different kinds of palatable foodstuffs and proceeded towards the forest. As they began to move, their brothers, friends, mothers, fathers, father-in-laws, mother-in-laws and husbands tried to stop them in various ways, but they did not bother to listen to any of them. Their hearts were fully intoxicated by ecstatic love for Krishna. They quickly went to fulfill their lifelong dreams of seeing the lotus feet of Krishna directly. These brahmanis arrived before Krishna with plates of fruits and other foodstuffs in their hands, and stood patiently in a row. They fixed their minds at Krishna's lotus feet as they stood silently awaiting His attention.

Krishna then addressed them with a smile, "O brahmanis, why did you leave the sacrificial arena and come here? You are mere women; how could you take such a step? Your husbands and friends will soon reject you."

Hearing the words of Govinda, the wives of the brahmanas replied smilingly, "What will we do with husbands, children, and friends? Simply by Your remembrance, all of one's bondage and affections are destroyed. It is good that we defied the order of our husbands so that we are able to receive the darsana of Your lotus feet. Actually, You are our true husband, son, friend, worshipable Lord, and relative. You are Lord Narayana Himself. What is the use of home, children, and family? These are all illusion. You alone are the Supreme Truth, and everything else in this world is illusion. Ordinary people do not know You. Even Lord Ananta is unable to describe Your glories properly. Personalities like Siva, Sukadeva, Narada, Prahlada, Sanaka, Sanatana and Dhruva only know You partially. Even the great sages and demigods like Brahma do not know Your limits.

"Your devotees always take pleasure in singing Your glories. You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead, yet You have appeared in this world in a human form and enjoy various pastimes in Vrndavana. We were always thinking how we could get Your darsana. We must have performed many austerities in our past lives, and that is why we have become qualified to receive Your darsana. We are fortunate because today the Supreme Lord has personally asked food from us. Our lives are successful, for today we have seen You face to face. O Lord! Please bless us so that we can see Your lotus feet birth after birth."

When those pious women spoke like that, Krishna became compassionate and said, "You have taken a brave step that most women would not dare to take. You did not care about the criticism of others. You have displayed intense attachment for Me. Despite opposition from your own family members, you came here with food. Do not worry. None of your friends, husbands, or relatives will reject you. By My mercy, all of you will attain the supreme destination, and your minds will always be fixed on Me."

After speaking these words, Krishna bid the wives of the brahmanas farewell. In this way, those fortunate women returned home after supplying food to Lord Govinda.

## The repentance of the sacrificial brahmanas

When the wives of the brahmanas returned home and narrated everything to their husbands, the brahmanas condemned themselves and considered themselves most unfortunate. They lamented, "What is the use of our undergoing penance? What is the use of our studying Vedic literatures? We have not been able to develop a mentality like that of our wives. The Supreme Lord Govinda asked for food from us, but we were so puffed up and bewildered by the Lord's illusory energy that we did not pay any attention to His request."

The brahmanas continued to lament profusely and cursed themselves, but out of fear of Kamsa, they could not bring themselves to go to see Krishna. Ultimately, the brahmanas attained their respective destinations according to their mentality and activities. This is the result of neglecting the service of Lord Govinda.

Meanwhile, Rama and Damodara sat down with Their friends on the bank of Yamuna and had a sumptuous meal. After the feast, the son of Nanda began to play with His cowherd friends with great joy.

O human beings! Just hear these wonderful activities of Krishna with undivided attention; then you are sure to go back to Vaikuntha at the end of your life. Moreover, by hearing these topics, all your distress and lamentation will be vanquished. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Krishna's pastimes.

## Krishna advises the cowherd men of Vraja to worship the Govardhana hill

Krishna and Balarama passed Their days enjoying Their transcendental childhood pastimes in Vrndavana, while, the cowherd men of Vraja, under Nanda Maharaja's direction decided to perform a sacrifice to please Indra. King Nanda made an announcement throughout Vraja that there would be a festival dedicated to the worship of Indra. On the advice of Nanda Maharaja, all the residents of Vraja took various ingredients and foodstuffs like yogurt, milk, sweets, and grains, and rushed off to the bank of Yamuna to offer worship to Indra. When Krishna saw all these arrangements, He smiled and inquired from His father in a gentle manner, "My dear father, whom do you worship? Please tell Me. Where are you all going? To whom are you going to offer your worship?"

Being asked by Krishna, Nanda Maharaja replied, "My dear child, we are cowherds by caste. Our duty is to maintain cows. If there is an abundance of green grass in the field, then the cows and calves will live peacefully. Without the rainfall, there is no question of any green grass. Therefore, O Gadadhara, we worship Indra to get sufficient rain because he is in charge of water. We have been worshiping Indra for many years, and he has been sending sufficient rainfall for our benefit. That is why we regularly worship Indra on the bank of Yamuna. The cows live peacefully by his mercy. O child! Just sit down peacefully, and watch as we worship Indra."

Upon hearing these statements of His father, Krishna smiled and replied, "I have never heard anywhere that Indra showers rain. Whatever is destined by Providence will certainly happen. Who has the power to reverse the order of the Almighty? I do not know who has given you this idea of worshiping Indra. If you can try not to consider Me a mere child, then please listen to a few words about this matter. You live here in this world, and Indra lives in some other planet. So, how does he accept your worship, and how does he benefit you? Whoever has given you this idea certainly has no sense. Actually, no one knows what is beneficial for them. We are cowherds by caste, and we live in the forest. We take support from the great Govardhana Hill. Because of its mercy, the cows eat green grass and live peacefully. They take rest at the foot of Govardhana Hill. Govardhana Hill has thousands of peaks and it is full of minerals and jewels as well as all of the necessities of life. Therefore, why should you worship Indra and not Govardhana Hill? If the Govardhana Hill wants to harm you, can the king of demigods save you?"

When Krishna spoke these reasonable words, all the cowherd men became convinced and decided to stop worshiping Indra. Very soon all the inhabitants of Vraja led by Nanda gave up the worship of Indra and decided to worship Govardhana Hill instead. They collected yogurt, milk, butter, and many other ingredients and began to worship the great Govardhana Hill under the guidance of Krishna and Balarama. Then Krishna expanded Himself into two forms. One form remained with the cowherd boys, while the other form went to the top of the Govardhana Hill and transformed into Govardhana Hill. Thus everyone saw Krishna as nondifferent from Govardhana. Krishna, in the form of Govardhana Hill, ate all the foodstuffs that the inhabitants of Vraja had brought with them. They were struck with wonder and thought that the son of Nanda has indeed given them very good advice. They thought, "We have never seen anything like this before; a hill assuming the form of a person and accepting all of our offerings. We did not see this ever when we worshiped Indra. He never ate our offerings directly. How fortunate we are to live in Gokula under the shade of Govardhana Hill!" Then all the cowherd residents of Vraja circumambulated the Govardhana Hill and returned to their respective homes in the company of Krishna and Balarama.

## Indra becomes angry at Nanda Maharaja

Thus the tradition of worshiping Indra was broken by Krishna's advice, which made Indra very angry. He thought, "This Nanda and his associates have stopped worshiping me at the instigation of Krishna. Nanda Maharaja is now worshiping the Govardhana Hill instead, and letting Krishna eat all the offerings. The son of Nanda has certainly insulted me. It is true that He has appeared in Gokula to diminish the burden of the earth, but He has broken the custom of my worship out of pride. Therefore, I will destroy Gokula. Let me see how the son of Nanda protects Gokula and its inhabitants."

In an angry mood Indra, the king of heaven, called all the clouds and wind, and ordered them as follows, "All of you go immediately to Gokula and inundate the entire place with water. Take water from the ocean and shower incessant rain all over Gokula. I order all the sixty-four clouds headed by avartta, samvartta, drona and puskara to go immediately to Gokula. I also give forty-nine types of wind to assist you in this work."

As per Indra's orders, all the clouds and wind gathered together and proceeded to Gokula. Within a short time, they covered the entire sky of Gokula. The wind began to blow as violently as if the time for annihilation had come. The wind was so forceful that it broke the doors, gates, and windows of the residences of Gokula. The whole area was covered with dust. The dust cloud was so intense and dark that it was difficult to make out whether it was day or night. Soon there was an incessant shower of rain accompanied by a storm. It rained continuously day and night in Gokula. All of the cowherd men of Vraja, headed by Nanda, were surprised to see such heavy rainfall, the likes of which they had never experienced before. Soon all of Vrndavana became flooded, and the rivers and ponds overflowed with rainwater. In fact, the whole of Vrndavana began to float in the water. Naturally the inhabitants of Vraja began to feel miserable because of the extreme cold and wind. Moreover, hundreds of thunderbolts struck the land of Gokula and burnt many parts of it into ashes. Indra did all of this because he was angry that Krishna had stopped the inhabitants of Gokula from offering him his usual sacrifice. Unable to find any respite from this sudden calamity, the residents of Vraja took shelter of Krishna. They cried, "O Krishna! Look what is happening to us. Because we listened to You, we are now suffering. You advised us to stop worshiping Indra. Now Indra is angry with us, and is causing this unusual rain. Today the whole of Gokula will be submerged in water. You are the savior of everyone and the Lord of Gokula. Please tell us what we should do to save our lives! We are shivering in the cold. The cows and the calves are also shivering and standing with their heads bent down. Already many cows and calves have died because of the forceful wind and heavy rains. Because of You, now all Vrndavana has been ruined. There is complete chaos in Gokula. "When Krishna heard these words of the cowherd residents of Gokula, He began to contemplate how to save them from their imminent distress. "How dare Indra challenge Me! Today I will teach him a lesson!" thought Krishna.

## Krishna lifts Govardhana Hill

Krishna then went to the Govardhana Hill, slid His little finger underneath it, and lifted it effortlessly high up into the air. He lifted the great mountain with His left hand, and it became like a huge umbrella for the residents of Gokula. Krishna called out to all the inhabitants of Vraja, saying, "Do not be afraid. Just come over here underneath this hill. All of you and the animals can take shelter under this hill. Do not fear that the mountain will fall from My hand. I assure you that you all will remain safe and dry under this hill."

All the inhabitants of Gokula and their treasury of cows and calves immediately took shelter underneath the great Govardhana Hill. Relieved from the distress of the heavy rainfalls and storms, they stayed there joyfully. Meanwhile, sitting on his carrier elephant on the top of Govardhana Hill, Indra continued to shower heavy rain on the hill. All the trees and plants growing on the hill broke when hit by the thunderbolts. Although King Indra kept on causing heavy rainfall in Vrndavana, Krishna protected all of the inhabitants by lifting the Govardhana Hill. Even though Indra showered rain continuously for seven days, all the inhabitants of Vraja were completely safe underneath the hill. When the clouds and wind grew tired of showering rain and blowing, they approached their master Indra and said, "Listen, O king! We do not know what is happening. Although we tried our best, we could not succeed. Krishna lifted the Govardhana Hill and spoiled all our efforts. This Krishna is certainly extraordinary. He seems to be just a small boy, yet He easily lifted the gigantic mountain. All our attempts to disturb the inhabitants of Vraja were in vain, and now we are left with no power and no water."

When Indra heard their words, he suddenly came back to his senses and his anger subsided immediately. He realized that Krishna is the Supreme Lord Who had descended into the house of Nanda with a particular mission to fulfill, and that He is in fact the only worshipable Lord in the whole universe. Indeed, all of the inhabitants of Gokula were struck with wonder at seeing Krishna's greatness. They discussed among themselves, "This child of Nanda is not ordinary, even though He looks like one of us. Otherwise, how could a boy of seven years old possibly lift such a huge mountain? Lord Hari has personally incarnated in Gokula. After concluding in this way, all the inhabitants of Gokula returned to their respective homes.

## Indra offers prayers to Krishna

The inhabitants of Vraja returned to their respective homes, and then, Indra , the king of heaven, approached Lord Krishna. After offering his respectful obeisances, Lord Indra began to offer prayers, "O Lord! You are Narayana , the creator of the entire material manifestation. You are capable of annihilating millions of Indras like me within a moment. You are the ultimate cause of creation, maintenance, and annihilation. No one within this material word is able to surpass the influence of Your illusory energy. Even if we perform penances for millions of lifetimes, still we will not be able to understand Your potency. O Narayana! Please give up Your anger. I take shelter at Your lotus feet. Please bestow mercy on this fallen soul."

As Indra offered prayers to Krishna in this way, tears flowed down from his eyes and soaked the lotus feet of the Lord. He continued to pray, "O Lord! The child lives within the womb of his mother, and the mother has to tolerate the child's kicking, but the mother never takes offense. Similarly, kindly be merciful upon me, Your insignificant child. I was proud of being the king of the demigods, hence I failed to recognize You. I was bewildered by material enjoyments and forgot You completely. Please forgive me this time. I surrender unto You."

After hearing Indra's prayer, Lord Hari forgave his offenses and instructed him to return to his own abode. Later on, Indra brought water from the celestial Ganges and milk from the Surabhi cow, and performed a grand abhiseka for Krishna. Following the abhiseka, Indra declared, "From today onwards, You will be celebrated as the lifter of the Govardhana Hill." Indra then circumambulated Krishna and returned to his own heavenly residence. As soon as King Kamsa heard the news of Krishna's lifting the Govardhana Hill, he became unconscious and fell to the ground.

Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the pastime of Krishna's lifting of the Govardhana Hill.

## Krishna enters the waters of Yamuna to search for Nanda Maharaja

Krishna saved the inhabitants of Gokula by lifting the Govardhana Hill. Then, Indra performed a grand abhiseka for Krishna. These activities surprised the cowherd residents of Gokula and now they were convinced that Krishna was not an ordinary child. In each and every house people discussed Krishna's childhood pastimes and His extraordinary activities.

Once on DvadasI, Krishna's father Nanda Maharaja went to take bath in the Yamuna at an inauspicious time. As soon as Nanda entered within the waters of Yamuna, a servant of Varuna immediately arrested him and brought him before his master. Varuna thanked his servant for bringing Nanda because he realized that now he would have the opportunity to meet his worshipable Lord Krishna face to face. He thought, "Krishna has descended in Gokula to purify the earth of sinful elements, and He will certainly set out to look for His father, and end up coming here. Consequently, we will get a chance to see the Lord." Thinking in this way, Varuna happily kept Nanda Maharaja in his palace.

Some time earlier, though, someone had seen Nanda entering into the waters of Yamuna and now went to Krishna and Yasoda, saying, "O Queen Yasoda! O Krishna! Listen to this wonderful incident. I saw Nanda enter the Yamuna to take bath, but he did not come out of the water. I am sure that he has been eaten by a crocodile. I saw him taking a dip in the water, but he never came out. Surely he has died within the waters of Yamuna. O Kanai! You should immediately go and look for Your father!"

When Yasoda heard this news, she felt like she had been struck by a thunderbolt. She lamented, "Alas! I observed many vows life after life, but all of those vows have gone in vain. O my dear child, Murari! Your father has died in the waters of Yamuna. Now I have become a widow and all my prestige is at stake." Queen Yasoda began to roll on the ground, crying Piteously, "O my child! Just now Your father went to take a bath, but alas!

He did not come out of the river. O Krishna! You are the essence of this material world, and yet Your father was killed by a crocodile in Yamuna. Now tell me what can be done about it!"

As soon as Krishna heard the pathetic cries of His mother, He immediately tightened His dhoti, and ran to the Yamuna. In order to retrieve Nanda from the clutches of Varuna, Krishna, the Supreme mystic, entered within the waters of Yamuna. Once there, however, although He looked for Nanda everywhere within the water, He could find neither His father nor any crocodile.

## Krishna goes to the abode of Varuna

Krishna thought for a while, and came to understand that a servant of Varuna had arrested His father and taken him to the abode of the water god. Krishna, who is non-different from Narayana, immediately went to Varuna's watery palace. As soon as Varuna saw his worshipable Lord, He offered obeisance to the Lord and worshiped Him by offering Him padya and arghya. He then began to offer prayers to Krishna with folded hands and undivided attention, "O Supreme Personality of Godhead! You have incarnated in this world to diminish the burden of the earth. I had a great desire to see Your lotus feet. In order to entice You to personally come to my abode, I kidnapped Your father, Nanda. I could not find any other way to bring You here. O Lord, here is Your father. Please take him back. O Madhusudana! You are the controller of creation, maintenance, and destruction. O Lord Hari! You alone bestow liberation. Today, my life has become successful for I have directly seen Your lotus feet. Please take Your father and return to Your own residence."

After speaking to Krishna in this way, Varuna presented Him with various gifts like jewels, diamonds, and different ornaments. Krishna and Nanda then joyfully returned home from the abode of Varuna.

Hearing the news that the presumed dead Nanda had instead miraculously come back to life, all the inhabitants of Gokula came running to his house to see him. When they heard the entire story from Nanda Gosai, the cowherd men danced in ecstasy. They said, "Listen, O Nanda! O Yasoda ! O Rohini! Your son Krishna is not an ordinary child. He is the Supreme Lord who has appeared in your house in the form of a human being. We are certain that even the demigods are unable to do what your small child does."

Nanda then acknowledged the words of his fellow cowherd men by saying, "I know my son Kanai is not an ordinary human being. He is Narayana Himself and has descended in a human form. He will destroy the sinful demons of this world and thus diminish the burden of the earth. This fact was disclosed to me by the great sage Gargamuni and the words of the sage cannot be false. And now the truth is very much evident. By Krishna's mercy, we have avoided many dangers. Although Kamsa has sent many of his followers to kill Krishna, they have all been annihilated by Him. Just recently Indra even showered heavy rain on us, but Krishna effortlessly lifted the Govardhana hill and saved the lives of the Vrajavasis."

Remembering the lotus feet of Krishna, Gunaraja Khan firmly declares that anyone who surrenders to Krishna will have nothing to fear.

## Description of Krishnas beauty

Krishna's mercy, all the cowherd men and women of Vraja lived happily. They knew nothing of disease, lamentation, or fear. They were the most fortunate souls. They received the constant association of Krishna, and thus all their sinful reactions accumulated from many, many births were completely vanquished. As time went on, Krishna grew up and entered into adolescence. At that age, He looked most attractive. Even Cupid was enchanted by His divine beauty; especially that of His lotus face which resembled the full moon. His two eyes defeated the beauty of the lotus flower, and the curly locks covering His head were made even more captivating by decorations of peacock feathers. From His shell-like ears hung earrings made of diamonds and jewels, and His curvy neck was adorned with a garland of different colored forest flowers. His fingers were decorated with golden rings and His wrists with golden bracelets. His two lotus feet were decorated with anklets and tinkling bells. On His head sat a beautiful crown. Looking like this, Krishna, the Lord of the universe, tended the cows and calves in the company of His cowherd friends.

Krishna, who wore a garland of forest flowers, dressed Himself in yellow lower garments and a top cloth. His yellow cloth appeared like lightning in the midst of a dark cloud. His charming face looked like a mirror of blue sapphire. There were dots of perspiration on His face. On seeing Krishna's exquisite beauty, the young damsels of Vraja could not keep their minds under control. They became afflicted with lust and constantly remembered Krishna's lotus feet. Their hearts were pierced by the arrows of Cupid. Even though some of them were already married, they gave up all fear of their own relatives, and their natural shyness, and fixed their minds on the lotus feet of Govinda. They were so much attracted to Krishna that they gave up all of their household duties and simply thought meeting Krishna somehow or another. Their only interest was in finding a way to attain the lotus feet of Krishna. Thus they remembered Krishna day and night.

Lord Krishna, who is the indwelling Supersoul of all living entities, understood the intention of the gopis. He decided to enjoy rasa-lila pastimes with the gopis with the support of His own internal potency, yogamaya. Thinking like this, Krishna made His way towards the bank of Yamuna where there were many beautiful trees, creepers, and flowers.

O human beings! Hear this rasa-lila pastime with full attention. This is the request of Gunaraja Khan.

## The beauty of Vrndavana

The forest of Vrndavana is the most sanctified and beautiful spot within the three worlds. The land of Vrndavana is bedecked with many beautiful trees and flower plants such as tulasi, malati, yuthi, amalaki kunda, marubaka, campa, nagesvara, adila, bakula, mali, gandha, jhinti, ketaki, kesara, asoka, vasaka, keya, kimsuka, cuya, pakadi, tala, coconut, tamala, beautiful ramaguya, simali, jackfruit, petal, olive, kamarahga, red sandalwood, arjuna, dates, ksiri, navadi and hetala. It is decorated with various trees, plants, and creepers of different colors. It is saturated with the sound of the humming bees, he and she-parrots, and peacocks. The beauty of Vrndavana is beyond description. There are countless groves decorated with = and parula flowers. The lakes and rivers of Vrndavana are decorated with many flowers like blue lotuses, pink lotuses, water lilies, and siyali.

## The rasa-lila pastimes of Krishna

Krishna desired to enjoy the rasa dance with the gopis in the transcendental abode of Vrndavana, which is decorated with flowers, trees, and creepers. As the full moon night of Sarat arrived, a fragrant and cooling breeze began to blow and the whole atmosphere in Vrndavana became most pleasing. Cuckoos began to sing in five different tunes. The honey bees became intoxicated and flew around humming. All the ten directions were filled with fully blossomed fragrant flowers. Such is the beauty of Vrndavana at the arrival of autumn. The trees were full of new leaves and twigs which looked even more beautiful when the moonlight fell on them. Hari, Who is the transcendental Cupid, slowly began to play on His enchanting flute. The gopis of Vrndavana became almost unconscious upon hearing the sound of Krishna's flute and instantly realized that Krishna was playing His flute in the forest, so they decided to go and meet Him there. When the flute's transcendental notes reached their ears, the gopis had been engaged in different activities at their homes. Some of them were resting with their husbands, some chatting with their friends, some were engaged in cooking, and some were taking their meals or feeding breast milk to their children. Some were lying on their beds, some were serving food to their husbands, some were engaging in household activities in the company of their mother-in-laws, and some were enjoying pleasantries with their husbands. Some were combing their hair, decorating their foreheads with tilaka and dots, or smearing their eyes with black ointment. Others were decorating themselves with necklaces and earrings. Some were chewing betel nuts mixed with camphor, while others were engaged in decorating themselves with deer musk, or putting vermillion on their foreheads; yet as soon as they heard the sound of Krishna's flute, they left everything and immediately departed to meet Krishna, the son of Nanda.

Some of the gopis were forcibly stopped by their husbands and relatives from going to see Krishna. Since they were so much absorbed in thoughts of Krishna, they could not tolerate even a moment of separation from Him. As a result of being deprived of seeing their Lord, they left their bodies on the spot while thinking about Krishna's lotus feet. Immediately all of their bondage to the material world was vanquished, and they attained Krishna's eternal association.

The rest of the damsels of Vraja quickly went before Krishna and surrounded Him from all sides and stood there still like a picture. None of them said anything, overcome by embarrassment and fear, although at the same time, their hearts were burning with the fire of transcendental passion. Realizing their intentions and intense love for Him, Krishna turned to them with a smile, saying,

"O gopis! Why have you come here at the dead of night? And to this dense forest? Did you not feel afraid of anything? There are many jackals and other wild beasts in the forest. How dare you come here and leave your husbands at home? What have you come here for at this time of the night? Listen to Me carefully. Your husbands and relatives must be searching for you, therefore you should quickly go back home. You should not stay here any longer. Your husbands must be full of anxiety and feeling distressed. No wife lives happily in this world if she gives up her husband. It is said that by serving the husband, the wife becomes delivered from any hellish condition. For a wife, her husband is the religious principles, heaven, liberation, and everything else. If the husband becomes displeased, then the wife goes to hell. Why did you abandon your husbands, children, and friends and come here? O cowherd women! Quickly return home and serve your husbands and children!"

When Govinda advised the gopis like this, they bent their heads and began to cry. Tears flowed down over their chests and onto the ground. Their garments became soaked by their tears. Unable to decide what to do and what to say, the gopis slowly spoke to Krishna while marking the ground with their toenails. The hearts of the gopis were overwhelmed by lust, and they could not speak as they felt great embarrassment and distress. They breathed heavily and offered their obeisances to Krishna. Then they spoke,

"O dear Krishna! Why do You speak like a merciless person? O Lord! We have given up our husbands, children, friends, and relatives. Day and night we simply think about Your lotus feet. What is the use of home and household? What is the use of having husbands and friends? We are ready to die right now just to see You. Even if our husbands leave us, we will not be as afflicted as when You advised us to go back home. Your indifference hurts us more. O carrier of the disc! Why are You so cruel? We will die here thinking about Your lotus feet. Our only desire is to obtain Your lotus feet birth after birth. You are our husband, children, friend, and everything else. We will not go back home at any cost. O dear Murari! Please save us by giving us nectar from Your lips. Otherwise, You will be responsible for killing women, and people will call You the killer of women. We took a heavy risk, and now You ignore us. We had so much hope within our hearts, but O Lord, You did not fulfill our desires, rather You have cheated us. O Hari! You are an ocean of mercy. If You do not show mercy upon us, what can we say? If You hate us, then You may reject us. But know that it is a fact that we always think of You with our bodies, minds, and speech. O Lord! Is it possible that You still have no mercy to spare?"

When the gopis spoke these heartfelt words to the Lord, the kindhearted Krishna became compassionate. The most attractive Krishna who conquers millions of Cupids then obliged the gopis and fulfilled their desires. As the thirsty female cataka birds become jubilant as soon as they see clouds in the sky, similarly the gopis of Vrndavana became completely satiated by the shower of the dark cloud in the form of Syamasundara. When Krishna wandered about within the forest of Vrndavana, it looked exactly like the moon surrounded by the stars. While all of the gopis were enjoying Krsna's intimate association, suddenly Krishna disappeared; taking with Him one particular gopi. With that special gopi, Krishna went to the bank of Yamuna and there He carefully plucked many fragrant flowers. Then, resting His left hand on the shoulder of that gopi, Kanai blissfully decorated Her to His full satisfaction. As Krishna decorated Her body, She felt a little proud, and in that mood, She said to Krishna, "My dear Krishna, I am unable to walk any further. If You want to enjoy intimate pastimes with Me, then carry Me on Your shoulder. I am sitting down right here, I can no longer walk."

Krishna replied, "O young cowherd girl! All right then, if You cannot walk, sit on My shoulder. O You, the most beautiful lady within the three worlds! I have no objection to carrying You."

As soon as the gopi gave permission to Krishna to carry Her on His shoulder, Krishna immediately disappeared. The gopi, unable to find Krishna anywhere in the four directions, rolled on the ground in an almost unconscious state.

## Lamentation by Sri Radha

"Why has Providence written such an ill fate in My destiny? Why has the creator deceived Me in this way? I have lost My beloved Lord after capturing Him within My grip. I had the jewel in My own pocket, but now I have lost it. My intelligence has become polluted; that is why I became puffed up with pride. I spoke like a mad woman, that is why the son of Nanda has left Me. O Hari! Why am I still alive? What can I do now? Where can I go to find My Govinda? Who has taken away the Lord of My life? O Lord of the universe! We are innocent by nature, and our intelligence is immature. I have said something which I should not have said. For this I have gotten the proper punishment."

Speaking these words, Sri Radha cried profusely while meditating on Syamasundara within Her heart. Meanwhile, when the other gopis did not see Govinda with them, they began to search for Him all over the forest.

O readers! Falling at the feet of Krishna, hear attentively as, I, Maladhara Basu narrate Krishna's pastimes.

## Display of madness by the gopis due to separation from Krishna

The gopis of Vrndavana became almost like mad. Their condition was pathetic. They were fully absorbed in thoughts of Krishna as they looked everywhere, searching for Him. They searched for Krishna in the trees, and then they went to the bank of Yamuna to look for Him. They suddenly saw Tulasi on the way, sat down around her, and began to speak, "We know you are the dearest to Krishna within the three worlds. O Thakurani! Can you please tell us which way Krishna has gone? We beg at your lotus feet. Please help us, and tell us the truth. Do not ignore us by considering us as co-wives. You have controlled Krishna by your service and fragrance. That is why we can see so many bumblebees flying around you. Please do not lie to us. We are ready to serve you as your maidservants."

In this way the gopis looked for Krishna everywhere. They inquired from anything and everything they saw before them. They asked the jati, yuthf, and malatf flowers, "Have you seen our Govinda anywhere? Have you seen Him pass by this way? Murari is very affectionate to all of you. Can you tell us where we can see Kanai?"

The more the gopis lamented, the more their feelings of separation intensified. After going a little further, they saw a kadamba tree. The gopis asked, "Krishna likes you very much. He always enjoys pastimes under kadamba trees. He wears a garland of kadamba flowers. He decorates His head with kadamba flowers. So you must know in which direction the Lord of our hearts has gone. We are all very unfortunate women. That is why the Lord has deceived us. O kadamba tree! Why don't you disclose to us the whereabouts of Krishna. Our hearts are burning due to separation from Krishna."

Lamenting in this way, the gopis looked upwards towards the sky and saw the beautiful moon there. Thinking the moon to be Krishna's face, they became jubilant. But when they realized that it was not their beloved Kanai, but that it was in fact the moon surrounded by the stars in the sky, they folded their hands and asked the moon, "O husband of the night! Please give us some answer as to where the Lord has gone after leaving us behind?" Then they addressed the stars, "O stars! You all know how painful the separation from a beloved is, so please be merciful to us, and give us some information about Krishna."

In this way, the gopis walked around the forest of Vrndavana and inquired about Krishna from whomsoever they met. Although they asked each and everyone, none could tell them anything about Krishna. Finally, being unable to tolerate the intense separation from Krishna, they almost became mad, and began to imitate Krishna's activities. One became Putana while another acted like Krishna, killing Putana while grabbing her tightly by the neck. Another became Krishna and sat on the chest of Putana and then killed her. Yet another became Yasoda and tried to churn butter while another acted like Krishna and stole butter from her. Someone imitated Krishna and ate the butter while someone ran after her shouting, "Catch the thief! Catch the thief!" Someone among the gopis acted like Damodara and cried out of fear of mother Yasoda. Some gopis tried to bind Krishna with a rope while someone rolled on the ground acting as the two yamala-arjuna trees. Someone became the demon Vatsasura while another gopi acted like Krishna and killed the demon. One of the gopis imitated Bakasura while another gopi imitated Krishna and killed him. Someone became Aghasura and was killed by another imitating Krishna. One of the gopis pretended to be the serpent Kaliya and someone acted like Krishna and punished Kaliya. One danced on the head of Kaliya just like Krishna had done, and another offered prayers to Krishna just like Kaliya's wives had. Someone acted like Indra and showered torrents of rain and another acted like Krishna and lifted the Govardhana Hill saying, "Do not worry. I am the Supreme Lord. I will protect you from all dangers."

In this way, after enacting various pastimes of Krishna, the beautiful gopis of Vrndavana paraded along the bank of Yamuna.

As they went near Yamuna, they suddenly saw another gopi in an even more pathetic condition. She was crying and lamenting, "O Krishna! Where are You? Why did You leave Me like this?"

Seeing this gopi, all the other gopis approached Her and inquired about the cause of Her lamentation. The single gopi then began to describe things about Govinda in this way, "Krishna took Me to a solitary place and enjoyed all kinds of intimate pastimes with Me. After enjoying a great deal, My mind became polluted with pride. So I told Him that I was unable to walk. Krishna then asked Me to ride on His shoulder so that He could carry Me. But Alas! As soon as I gave Him permission to do so and was about to climb on His shoulder, Krishna disappeared from there. He is certainly a cheater."

After hearing Her descriptions, all the gopis together began to search for Krishna everywhere with full attention. After a while they all sat down on the bank of Yamuna and began to discuss Krishna's wonderful characteristics. They said, "When the son of Nanda beautifully plays on His flute while standing in a three-fold bending form, then all the women of heaven, the female vidvadharas, and the other exalted women forget themselves being pierced by the arrows of Cupid. When this Krishna plays His flute in Vrndavana, all the trees and plants become filled with fully blossomed flowers even if it is out of season. Whenever Krishna goes to the forest or comes back from the forest after tending the cows and calves, playing on His flute, He enchants the hearts of all the inhabitants of Gokula. We remember when Krishna played His flute on the banks of Yamuna how the waves of Yamuna began to flow in the opposite direction. The stones melted after hearing the sound of Krishna's flute. The sages stopped their penances upon hearing the sound of Krishna's flute, and when Krishna played His flute under the kadamba trees, the peacocks immediately began to dance in jubilation. Even the dried-up trees and plants of Vrndavana were revived, and became filled with fruits and flowers as soon as there was the sound of Krishna's flute playing. All the birds of Vrndavana attentively hear the sweet sound of Krishna's flute. Why is Krishna not playing on His flute anymore? Where can we go so that we can see Nanda's son? O Hari! What has the creator written in our fate? We had the jewel in our grip, but now we have lost it. Krishna is not an ordinary human beins. He is certainly the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He incarnated in this world at the request of Brahma to diminish the burden on the earth. Krishna annihilates the demons and protects the devotees. But, O Krishna! Why are You killing us this way? If we do not see You for a few moments, we consider that time to be longer than twelve years. When Krishna goes to the forest in the company of His cowherd friends and cows playing His flute and buffalo horn, He looks most enchanting. We will meditate on that particular form, and give up our lives. O Krishna! Where are You now? We are dying due to separation from You. We fall down at Your lotus feet and beg that, O Lord of our heart, You please come back and give us Your darsana again."

## Krishna reappears before the gopis

Seeing the damsels of Vraja crying pathetically and rolling on the ground, out of compassion Krishna appeared before them. As soon as the gopis saw Krishna, they felt as if life had come back into their dead bodies. They became very pleased and, out of joy, tears began to flow from their eyes. The gopis immediately surrounded Krishna from all sides and stood with folded hands. All the symptoms of ecstatic love manifested on their bodies. Their bodily hairs stood on end, they breathed heavily, they shivered, and were stunned.
