They gazed at Krishna with full attention as each gopi focused her mind on a particular limb of Krishna's body. Krishna was standing in the midst of all the gopis, and it looked as if the moon was shining in the midst of the stars. Krishna then expanded Himself into as many forms as there were gopis. It was a wonderful sight; like corals in the midst of pearls, or a golden necklace mixed with blue sapphires. The gopis were decorated with dots of vermillion while Krishna was dressed in yellow garments. This scene appeared like the rainbow in the midst of the blue sky.

In this way the son of Nanda enjoyed amorous pastimes with the gopis who were afflicted by transcendental passion. He embraced them, kissed them, and satisfied every one of them according to each gopi's own desires.

After enjoying the rasa dance pastimes with the gopis, Krishna desired to enjoy sporting in the waters of Yamuna. In Yamuna, Krishna and the gopis enjoyed various water sports to the fullest extent. After that, Krishna said goodbye to all the gopis who, with great pain, left Krishna's association and returned home. They slowly went back to their houses and lay down beside their husbands. Under the influence of Krishna's illusory energy, their husbands thought that their wives had been by their sides all the time. None of them understood anything about the pastimes Krishna regularly performed with the women of Vrndavana. Such are the activities of Govinda who is supposed to protect the principles of religion. Although He is the Lord of the universe, He enjoyed the intimate company of other people's wives. As a matter of fact, there is nothing independent of Krishna. Nothing exists separate from Krishna. Everything belongs to Him and is meant for His satisfaction. Everything that we see within the universe is inseparably related to Krishna. He is not influenced by mundane piety and impiety. He is beyond all rules and regulations.

For example; fire burns everything into ashes including good and bad elements, but the fire itself never becomes contaminated as a result of this combustion. In the same way, everything becomes purified by the association of Krishna because He is the almighty Lord of the universe. For an ordinary person, enjoying the association of other men's wives would certainly lead to hell. There are eighty-four thousands different regions in hell, and if a person enjoys another man's wife then he is made to suffer in each and every one of these different hellish conditions. O human beings! Never indulge in sexual intercourse with someone else's wife for by doing so you will certainly have to go to hell.

Krishna enjoyed rasa-lila in the forests of Vrndavana with the gopis. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Krishna's pastimes while fixing his mind at His lotus feet. Sri Vyasadeva wrote srimad-Bhagavatam in Sanskrit. Gunaraja Khan composed these pastimes of Krishna as described in the tenth and eleventh cantos of srimad-Bhagavatam as a simple narration. He named his book sri Krishna Vijaya. This book is very dear to everyone as it is written in a sublime language. Only one who is favored by Krishna will keep this book in his house.

## Sri Govindadeva performs rasa-lila with the gopis in the Yogapith a of Vrndavana

Listen, O human beings! Listen carefully as I describe Krishna's rasa-lila pastimes in Yogapitha. From the time Krishna reached adolescence, He began to enjoy transcendental pastimes in the company of the gopis. Gradually He grew up and turned fourteen years old. He was situated in the prime of His youth and looked most attractive. His beautiful form enchanted the minds of everyone within the universe. One day He sat down under a desire tree in Yogapitha in Vrndavana. There He appeared as effulgent as the full moon. There were sixteen principal gopis around Him. Among these sixteen, Radharani was the topmost. Krishna was sitting on a throne within a temple made of gems and jewels. On His left was Radhika, and on His right was Candravali. There were different groups of gopis standing in the four directions. This transcendental temple had four gates, and the throne of Krishna was placed facing the west. There were four gatekeepers on the four gates. These gate keepers were cowherd boys. They looked as beautiful as Krishna as they were dressed like Him. The cowherd Sridama guarded the western gate, and Sudama guarded the eastern gate; Dama guarded the northern gate while Kinkinika guarded the southern gate. In this way, Krishna joyfully engaged in pastimes in the forest of Vrndavana.

There were millions of cowherd boys in and around that divine temple. Boys like Subala guarded the temple. These boys were decorated with different ornaments and garlands of forest flowers. They dressed like Krishna and were expert in all kinds of arts. Some of them were black in color, some of them were fair, but all of them were in the prime of their youth. Their bodies shone brightly and their heads were decorated with peacock feathers. Some tucked buffalo horns in their waists, and some held flutes while others carried sticks in their hands. All of them were decorated with waistbands and dressed in fine silken cloth. They were very happy to make Krishna happy. They sang in sweet tunes for Krishna's pleasure. Their minds were fully absorbed in thoughts of Krishna. They circumambulated the divine temple while singing Krishna's glories.

Within that temple, the son of Nanda enjoyed transcendental pastimes. His attractive body was decorated with sandalwood pulp and a garland of forest flowers; His head was adorned with a peacock feather, and He held His favorite flute in His hand. He sported an enchanting smile. Krishna, who is the crest jewel among all paramours, was surrounded by the young beautiful gopis of Vraja.

## The rasa-lila pastime

While Krishna was standing in the midst of the young gopis, He directly pleased each and every one of them. These gopis are compared to lotus flowers because their bodies release the sweet aroma of a lotus flower. They were completely merged in the ocean of transcendental bliss as they enjoyed intimate pastimes with their beloved Lord Krishna. Krishna would hold the hand of one gopi, and then sometimes He would stand in the three-fold bending form while leaning on another gopi. Sometimes He would embrace one gopi, and sometimes He would play His flute together with another.

The sound of Krishna's flute was so attractive that it enchanted the minds of all of the inhabitants of the whole universe. This sound melted even stone and caused Yamuna to flow in the opposite direction. The gopis became intoxicated by the sound of Krishna's flute; so much so that they even forgot who they were. When Krishna played His flute loudly, all of the inhabitants of Gokula including the birds, beasts, trees, and creepers became filled with ecstatic love for Krishna.

Krishna did not disappoint anyone. He personally went to each gopi

and enjoyed her company and thereby gave her full satisfaction. Sometimes Krishna would embrace a gopi and listen to her sweet whispering. Sometimes He would kiss one gopi and give her His own chewed betel nuts. Sometimes He would press the cheek of a gopi with His own cheek, and sometimes He would caress the chest of a gopi with His own hand. Sometimes He would decorate a gopi with parijata flowers. He would sit down and chat with one gopi, while with others He would enjoy joking and laughing. In this way, the gopis of Vrndavana floated in an ocean of bliss. Sometimes Krishna would touch a gopis breast, and sometimes He would smell their bodies. Krishna is expert in the art of amorous pastimes. Sometimes He would bite the lips of a gopi and sometimes He would arouse a gopi s passion by embracing her tightly or by pinching her breast. Sometimes He would kiss a gopi while grabbing her braided hair, and sometimes He would kiss the gopi by holding her cheeks. In this way, Krishna enjoyed conjugal pastimes with the young gopis of Vrndavana.

Sometimes when a gopi pretended to be angry at Krishna, Krishna would solace her by giving her a sweet glance. Sometimes He would pull a gopi to Him and make her sit on His lap. Sometimes Krishna would talk with them in a strange manner, and sometimes He would converse with them in a hilarious manner. The gopis were not envious of each other, even though all of them enjoyed the company of Krishna together. There was no envy and no animosity among the gopis; rather there was complete harmony among them. Actually, they were all one in their unanimous, singleminded devotion; there was no difference between them, and their only object of love, Krishna.

Krishna was standing in the middle while Radhika and Candravall were standing on His left and right. The sixteen principal heroines were standing surrounding these three. Innumerable groups of other gopis surrounded these sixteen principal gopis. The beauty of these gopis is beyond description. They were charming and their bodies were smeared with sandalwood pulp. They were adorned with transcendental qualities. The beauty of the gopis of Vraja certainly defeats the beauty of Rambha, Menaka, Rati, Saci, Urvasi and Parvati. There is no comparison to the gopis of Vraja within the three worlds. They are the only source of their own beauty and qualities. Their walking is dancing, and their conversation is singing. Krishna became fully captivated by their beauty and transcendental qualities. Among all the gopis, Radha and Candravall are the most dear to Krishna. Sasirekha and Citrarekha were equally glorious, too. Apart from those, the other gopis among the sixteen principal gopis were Srimati, Madhumati, Madhavi, Kadambini, Navaranga, Ratilekha, Kuntlni, Srimantini, Priya, Vanapriya, Rama and Madana-manjari. The sixteen main gopis are very dear to Krishna and are very expert in giving pleasure to Krishna. Among these sixteen gopis, Radha and Candravall are the topmost. They possess beauty and qualities equally. Radha and Krishna are one, but they have accepted two separate forms. Actually Radhika is celebrated by the three extraordinary names vrnavanavilasini, Radha, and Krishna-priya. These three names were hidden in the tantras, but now these names have been revealed to all. Radhika is the best of all the gopis; in fact, all of the other gopis are parts and parcels or expansions of Radha.

In total there are eighteen principal gopis, including Radha and Candravali. Eight of them belong to the group of Candravali and the other eight belong to the group of Radhika. These are very confidential topics described in the tantras. Each of these sixteen gopis have their own expansions. These expansions are also glorious in their beauty and qualities. The beautiful Lalita who applies deer musk on her breasts is the foremost among them. The names of the rest of the gopis are Syamala, Dhavala, Rati, Bhadra, Padma, Haripriya, Visakha, Indumukhi, Sumukhi, Vallabhi, Candrika, Catura, Madura and Apsara; in this way ,all the gopis are either plenary portions or portions of the plenary portions of Sri Radha. Krishna eternally enjoys rasa-lila pastimes with all these gopis.

## Krishna's water pastimes

Being absorbed in the pleasure of transcendental mellows, Krishna slowly went to the bank of Yamuna and began to enjoy water sports in the waters of Yamuna. Sri Hari surrounded by the damsels of Vraja enjoyed pastimes within the waters of Yamuna. There were various groups of gopis in the water, and Krishna was in the middle of all of them. The gopis splashed water at Krishna and Krishna also reciprocated by splashing water at them. Some of the gopis had scented oil and sandalwood pulp in different small boxes, which they opened and applied on Krishna. Some gopis splashed water on Krishna's forehead, some splashed water on Krishna's ears, some splashed on His body and some splashed on His eyes. Although Krishna tried to stop them with His two hands. He was unable to do so. In this way, the gopis all splashed water on Krishna from the four directions. Suddenly, Krishna caught hold of Radha's hand and splashed water on her ears and head. Radha asked Krishna with sweet words, "My dear Krishna, I surrender to You, please do not splash water any more."

Krishna smiled at this request of Radha, and went over to Candravali who immediately ran away in fear. While running away, one of her anklets was lost in the waters of Yamuna. Even though she searched for it, she could not find it. Meanwhile Krishna had already found the missing anklet and had hid it in the corner of His chadar, yet He too joined Candravali and the other gopis in their search for the anklet. Krishna said, "Who has stolen this anklet? It is not good to steal the property of a princess. Are you not ashamed of yourself for stealing someone else's property?"

When they could not find the anklet anywhere, Krishna said to them with a smile, "I have an idea. Each one of you should take off your clothes and check to see if it may be hidden somewhere. If it is not there, then you are not guilty of stealing."

Following Krishna's request, the gopis did as Krishna had told them, but the anklet could not be found. Then the four gopis named Catura, Para, Apsara and Madhumati surrounded Krishna from four sides while Sasirekha, Citrarekha, Kamala and Madana manjari took off Krishna's dhoti and chadar to see if He had hidden it somewhere on His body. When the gopis were engaged in undressing Krishna, all the celestial women from outer space watched with great joy. Suddenly, the gopis found the anklet tied to the end of Krishna's chadar. They then began to laugh loudly and spoke as follows, "O Krishna! You are a thief. You have been a thief since Your birth. Now You are grown up, but still You have not given up Your habit of stealing. We know mother Yasoda bound You once for stealing butter. We also know You once stole the garments of the gopis, and now You have stolen the anklet while playing in the waters of Yamuna. Not only once or twice, but for a third time, You have been caught stealing. You will be renowned as a thief in the society of Your beloved devotees."

The gopis came out of the water carrying Krishna's dhoti and chadar, while Krishna remained without any clothes in the waters of Yamuna. The gopis said, "O Krishna! How do You feel now? How do You enjoy remaining like this in the water?" In this way, the most fortunate gopis of Vraja teased and joked with Krishna. Thereafter Krishna begged the gopis with great humility to return His clothes. Finally, Radha personally took Krishna's dhoti and chadar from them and returned the clothes to Him. Krishna then got dressed and returned home. Such are the wonderful pastimes of Krishna. Even the demigods become struck with wonder after seeing such extraordinary pastimes.

O human beings! Forget everything and just hear these wonderful characteristics of Krishna. Listen to the blissful pastimes of Krishna as Gunaraja Khan narrates them.

## Krishnas pastimes of killing the demons

## The deliverance of Nanda from the clutch of the great serpent

The gopis of Vrndavana lived happily in the company of Krishna. In due course of time, the auspicious day of the worship of Katyayani arrived. As usual, each and every household in Vraja celebrated this festival with great pomp. They collected various ingredients for worship. They exchanged various gifts with one another. The women of Vraja dressed up in beautiful garments and decorated their bodies with different ornaments.

The gopis went to a forest near Govardhana to worship goddess Katyayani. There they duly worshiped the goddess with love and devotion. They stayed up all night as a matter of respect for the goddess. In the course of their worshiping, they danced, sang, and played musical instruments. They plucked colorful and fragrant flowers and offered them to goddess Katyayani, begging for her mercy.

One day, Krishna's father Nanda Maharaja was suddenly attacked by a huge python in Vrndavana. The snake captured Nanda within its coils as Nanda shouted loudly in fear. He exclaimed, "O Krishna! O Hari! Where are You? Although I have a son like You, still I am dying in this way."

As soon as Krishna heard the cry of His father, He immediately went to him and tried to drive away the formidable python. The snake was ferocious; it did not go away, rather it came charging with its mouth wide open. Krishna became so angry at this that He kicked the head of the python. As soon as Krishna kicked him, the snake left his old form and assumed his original form as a Vidyadhara.

## The deliverance of the Gandharva named Sudarsana

After the python returned to his original form, he mounted his chariot and began to offer prayers to Krishna before leaving for his own abode. He prayed as follows, "O Supreme Lord! You have delivered me from the curse of the sage Angira. My name is Sudarsana, and I am the leader of Gandharvas. Once when I was enjoying the intimate association of the young girls in my abode, the great sage Angira passed by. The sage was enriched with penance and had matted hair on his head. Upon seeing his strange appearance, I laughed at him. This made the sage very angry and he immediately cursed me by saying, 'You are puffed-up because you have a beautiful Gandharva body. How dare you ridicule me like this? You will take birth as a python in Vrndavana and live there. When Krishna appears in this world to fulfill His mission, you will be relieved form this curse after being touched by Krishna." O Krishna! The curse of the sage was actually a blessing in disguise. Now as the sage predicted, I have become relieved by Your kick."

After glorifying Krishna and narrating this story, Sudarsana offered his respectful obeisances to Krishna and returned to heaven. All who were present there were amazed to see this wonderful pastime of Krishna. They concluded that Krishna is certainly Lord Narayana. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the festival of Katyayani and the deliverance of Sudarsana Gandharva.

## The killing of Sankhacuda

Lord Damodara enacted many amazing pastimes in Vrndavana. At fourteen years old, He looked most enchanting. Krishna and Balarama used to enjoy pastimes in the forest of Vrndavana along with Their own sets of gopis. Krishna would go to the dense forest and enjoy unlimited wonderful pastimes in the midst of His beloved gopis. He appeared just like the moon surrounded by the stars in the sky.

One day, as Krishna was passing His days joyfully in Vrndavana, a great demon named Sankhacuda suddenly arrived there. This demon was expert at creating illusions. He was a follower of Kuvera, and his business was to kidnap the cowherd girls. One day he came to Vraja, and suddenly took away one gopi. The gopi cried loudly saying, "O Govinda! Please protect me from this demon!"

As soon as Krishna heard this loud screaming, He immediately rushed to save that gopi and gave the charge of protecting the other gopis to Balarama. In an angry mood, Krishna tightened His dhoti and ran towards the demon. He shouted, "O wicked demon! How dare you kidnap another man's wife? Today is certainly the last day of your life. Yamaraja must be happy for he knows that I am going to send you to his place."

Speaking these words, Krishna caught hold of the demon by his hair and pulled him to the ground. Krishna then sat down on Sankhacuda's chest and squeezed his throat till he died. The assembled young women of Vraja became jubilant seeing that Sankhacuda had been killed. The gopis who had been captured by the demon were thus released by Krishna's mercy. After killing the demon and enjoying Himself sufficiently in the forest of Vrndavana, Krishna returned home. O human beings! Hear Sri Krishna Vijaya with full attention, then you will become happy in this life and go back to Godhead in your next life. Do not neglect to hear these pastimes with love and devotion. This is the prayer of Gunaraja Khan.

## The killing of Aristasura

When King Kamsa heard about the killing of Sankhacuda, he became really worried. So, he quickly called his trusted hero Aristasura and said to him, "My dear Arista, have you heard anything about this Krishna? The son of Nanda is a strange person. Whatever He does is out of this world. He performed uncommon activities even when He was only a child. When He was only seven years old, He lifted the Govardhana Hill. He delivered the Gandharva called Sudarsana. Now He saved the gopis by killing Sankhacuda. I know that I am destined to be killed by Him. It is a fact that there is no superior hero within the whole universe than Him. You too are a great hero in our society, but I feel ashamed that I will die even when there are great heroes like you around."

When Kamsa pathetically spoke this way, Arista began to laugh and reassured Kamsa saying, "O King Kamsa! Do not worry at all. I will only have to kill a few boys. This is not a big job for me. Why did you send other demons to kill Krishna when I am here? See the result; they could not conquer Krishna and instead have given a bad name to all of us. It's only a matter of time before I meet Him in Gokula. I will kill both Rama and Krishna and send them to the house of Yamaraja."

After speaking these heroic words, Aristasura bowed down to kamsa and quickly left to kill Krishna. This demon assumed the formidable form of a bull and looked frightening to everyone. He expanded his body to almost eighty miles in length, and his hump was as high as a mountain peak. When he walked, all the trees and plants were smashed by the touch of his heavy body. The entire earth shook, and the houses on his left and right fell apart simply at the touch of his body. When this most frightening demon entered Gokula, all the cowherd men and women became extremely afraid. When the demon lowed loudly, the sound pierced the ears of the inhabitants of Gokula. Many cows gave up their bodies just from hearing his loud lowing, and pregnant cows had miscarriages. The cowherd boys quickly ran to Krishna and informed Him of the arrival of the formidable demon.

Krishna rushed to the pasturing ground and saw the great demon Aristasura, in the form of a bull, creating havoc there. Lord Hari, the protector of the pious, then said to the demon, "O wicked demon! You have come here to die by My hands. I will reduce the burden of the earth by killing you." Then Krishna jumped in front of the demon and caught hold of the demon's two horns with His two powerful hands. Krishna pulled the demon around and around in a circle and then dashed him forcefully to the ground. The demon fell to the ground at a distance of about ten feet from where he had been standing. Aristasura shook himself off, regained his valor, and charged Krishna again, pointing his sharp and powerful horns at Him.

Angrily, Krishna snatched his horns and tore them from the demon's head. Swinging them like weapons, Krsna then struck Arista on his head with his own horns. After being hit so powerfully by Krishna, the demon fell down to the ground, yet he had not lost heart, so he again got up and charged at Krishna, still trying to kill Him. This time, Krishna grabbed his tail and smashed him to the ground. The gigantic demon lost his life then and there. The denizens of heaven began to shower flowers on Krishna for this wonderful act. All the inhabitants of Gokula danced in joy. They were amazed by this extraordinary activity of Krishna and spoke about the incident with everyone they met.

Soon the news of Aristasura's death reached King kamsa and he almost fainted at hearing it. kamsa then called his friends and associates to discuss how to eliminate Krishna.

## Narada instigates Kamsa so that he would be killed as soon as possible

Kamsa summoned his ministers and associates to have a discussion, but at that moment, the great sage Narada arrived in his assembly. As soon as Kamsa saw Narada, he immediately stood up to greet him. Kamsa worshiped Narada by offering him padya and arghya, and then he offered his respectful obeisances to the sage. After the great sage was thus worshiped, he was very pleased and spoke to Kamsa in a polite manner, "O king! You look free from anxiety, but this is not the time for you to remain tension-free for your killer has already been born from the womb of Devaki. Lord Hari has already incarnated as her eighth child. Don't you remember, Vasudeva kept Krishna and Balarama secretly at the house of Nanda? He then brought Yasoda's daughter and deceived you. Don't you remember all these things? O great king! Your enemy Krishna has become really powerful. You should immediately do something about it. I am telling you this for your own benefit."

The sage Narada advised Kamsa in this way, so the king then began to discuss the matter with his ministers and friends. Finally, he decided to eliminate Vasudeva and Devaki. Kamsa had them brought before him. Taking a chopper in his hand, he grabbed their hair and attempted to sever their heads. Seeing what was happening, the great sage Narada who was standing near by, immediately stopped Kamsa by catching hold of his hand and saying, "O king! This behavior is not proper for you. Why do you act like this? I have never heard of a brother killing his sister's husband. It is better that you kill your own enemy. Vasudeva and Devaki are not your enemies. Moreover, by killing them you will violate the principles of religion, and by violating the principles of religion, you will die even sooner. It is better that you do not kill them, but simply keep them shackled in the prison house. You should now focus all your attention on destroying your main enemy, Krishna."

## The demon Kesi disturbs the inhabitants of Gokula

When the great sage Narada advised Kamsa like this, Kamsa managed to control his anger. He decided to send for another of his demoniac friends, Kesi, and order him to go to Gokula. He said to the demon, "O sir! O Kesi! Help me to get rid of Rama and Krishna. You go to Gokula and kill these two brothers for my satisfaction. If you fail to kill them, then I will have Akrura bring Krishna and Balarama here." kamsa was full of anxiety. He had no peace of mind. Therefore, he also called Akrura and said to him, "O Akrura! You should not waste any more time. Go to Gokula quickly. The son of Nanda has become my biggest threat. Somehow or other I have to kill Him. If Kesi fails in his endeavor to destroy Krishna, then you have to bring the two brothers here on some pretext. You may tell them like this, 'You two are expert wrestlers. The king has invited both of you to a wrestling match that he is arranging in Mathura. Therefore he has sent me to bring both of You.' O Akrura! Somehow you should convince Krishna and Balarama and bring them here. Then I will have them killed by my most powerful and trusted wrestlers. Let the brahmanas perform a fire sacrifice to help me fulfill my desire, and let a flag of victory be hosted in each and every house of Mathura."

## Preparing for the wrestling competition

Kamsa instructed his associates, "Invite all the kings to watch this fun. Erect a raised platform and decorate it with gold. This platform is to be used as the wrestling arena. Arrange to keep the elephant Kuvalaya in the middle of the gate so that as soon as the son of Nanda enters through the gate the elephant can kill him. We have to get rid of the two brothers. When Krishna and Balarama are destroyed, I will have no enemy within the three worlds. I am sure all the powerful kings like Jarasandha will support me in this attempt. Thereafter, I will rule and enjoy my kingdom with full satisfaction and happiness." After kamsa had made his plans, he returned to his residence.

Meanwhile, the demon Kesi had entered the city of Gokula. The earth trembled as he walked. He tore parts of the houses, uprooted the trees and plants, and killed many cows and people of Gokula. Krishna's friends ran to their only life support, Krishna, and informed Him about the mischief created by the demon Kesi. They said to Krishna, "O Krishna and Balarama! What are You doing here? There is a great demon in Gokula ruining the entire city. He has assumed the form of a horse and is as big as a mountain. He is turning the whole city upside down. There is no way to get relief from him. We fear that after all this time, Gokula is going to be destroyed. This wicked demon will finish off everything in Gokula. We the inhabitants of Gokula have taken shelter of You. O Hari! Kill the demon and save us."

As soon as Krishna heard this news, He promptly went over to challenge Kesi. The demon who had taken the form of a horse was about to destroy the entire earth by pressing it with his hooves. The residents of Gokula were greatly frightened by the loud sound of the horse-demon's neighing. Krishna stopped and thought for a moment how to kill the demon Kesi who was ready to devour Him and devastate Gokula.

## The killings of Kesi and Vyomasura

Realizing the mentality of Kesi, Lord Hari skillfully caught hold of his tail and began to whirl him around. Effortlessly, Krishna threw the demon to the ground with great force. The demon fell about a hundred yards away from Krishna. After being hit by Krishna, the demon became more dangerous and angrier. He stood up and charged towards Krishna, ready to swallow Him. This time Krishna inserted His right hand within the mouth of the demon. Krishna's hand obstructed the demon's breathing. As a result, the demon started choking as he tried desperately to breathe. Unable to come out of the demon's body through his mouth, the trapped air finally burst out of the demon's stomach. The demon Kesi fell to the ground and began to kick wildly in the air. The entire earth shook vigorously because of his kicking. In this way the sinful demon Kesi was killed by Krishna. The body of the demon burst into many pieces. The whole world watched as the fierce demon Kesi was killed by Krishna. Because of killing Kesi, Krishna became celebrated as Kesava. The inhabitants of the heavenly planets became very happy, and they offered lavish prayers to Krishna before returning to their own residences.

Rama and Damodara thus performed many wonderful pastimes in the company of the cowherd boys. They enjoyed innumerable pastimes on the banks of Yamuna. Krishna, who wears a garland of forest flowers, sometimes enjoys the sport called 'The King and the Thief. In this game, one player becomes the king while another becomes the thief.

One day, as they were enjoying their sport, suddenly the great demon Vyomasura appeared. The demon came there incognito and kidnapped many cowherd boys one by one. He confined these boys within a mountain cave and closed the gate of the cave with a giant chunk of stone. After the demon had seized a number of Krishna's friends, Krishna looked around and was surprised to see only a few friends there. He began to consider the situation, thinking, "I came here with so many of my friends to play. But where have they gone? Who has stolen them away?" Being the almighty Lord Krishna, He could immediately understand that it was the act of the demon Vyomasura. Krishna quickly decided to annihilate the demon. Thereafter a fierce battle took place between Krishna and Vyomasura. Both of them were very powerful so they uprooted trees and threw them at each other. Finally, Govinda threw the demon to the ground with great force. He captured him just like an expert wrestler would, and grabbed his throat tightly. This grip was enough to make the demon choke and die. After Krishna had skillfully destroyed the demon, He went to deliver His own friends from the cave of Vyomasura. Damodara, who is non-different from Narayana, removed the huge piece of stone from the mouth of the cave and released His friends from the confinement. After this pastime, Krishna and His friends went to Yamuna and enjoyed water sports. Later on, everyone returned home and told this tale to their parents.

So, King kamsa received no respite from his distressed and fearful condition; instead he had to hear the sad news of the death of Kesi and Vyoma. Thus Gunaraja Khan joyfully narrates Krishna's pastimes after offering his obeisances to Gopala Krishna.

## A conversation between Krishna and Narada

The great sage Narada visited Krishna in Vrndavana and informed Him about the evil motives of the wicked kamsa. He said, "The wicked kamsa was about to kill your father Vasudeva, but somehow I stopped him. Now he is planning to take You and Balarama to Mathura with the help of Akrura. This kamsa is always up to something. O Krishna! I think it's time that You brothers kill him. After all, due to kamsa's wicked nature, Your parents are suffering in a prison house."

Upon hearing kamsa's plan, Krishna smiled and replied to Narada Muni. "O great sage! Let Akrura come, I am ready to go to Mathura. I will participate in the upcoming wrestling competition, and then I will meet Kamsa face to face and do the needful."

Reassured by Krishna in this way, Narada Muni returned to his transcendental travels, and Krishna resumed His sporting activities with His childhood friends.

## Akrura goes to Gokula

After kamsa had ordered Akrura to go to Vrndavana and bring Krishna and Balarama back to him, Akrura returned home and spent the night in a joyful mood. He was happy because now he would have the opportunity to meet the Supreme Lord Madhusudana, his very life and soul. He also thought about how the coming day would be the day in which all the reactions of his sinful activities committed for millions of lifetimes would be vanquished. Early the next morning, Akrura started for Vrndavana. He mounted his chariot and happily began his journey. He considered himself fortunate to be able to see his worshipable Lord and thanked Kamsa for giving him this job. He thought, "Great demigods like Brahma and others cannot see the Supreme Lord face to face. But today, I will have the opportunity to meet my Lord directly. I will make my life successful by offering my humble obeisance to the Lord. Then, Krishna will pick me up from the ground and embrace me. He will inquire from me about my wellbeing. Only then my life will become successful." Akrura continued his journey while thinking in this way, and finally in the evening, he arrived in Vrndavana. Upon arriving in Vrndavana, Akrura saw that Krishna and Balarama were engaged in playing with Their friends. Akrura immediately got down from his chariot and offered obeisances at the lotus feet of Krishna by falling flat to the ground. He also offered his respectful obeisances to Baladeva, Nanda and Yasoda. Nanda and Yasoda greeted Akrura with humility and duly worshiped him with padya and arghya. They fed him with sweets, grains and drinks. When they inquired about the cause of his visit, Akrura replied in the following way.

"King Kamsa has arranged for a Dhanuryajna and a wrestling competition. That is why I have come here to invite all of you to participate in this grand occasion. Please quickly load your daily needs like yogurt, milk, ghee, and grains on bullock carts and go to Mathura along with your two sons Krishna and Balarama. The king has also advised you to bring your tax money. He especially requested you to bring Krishna and Balarama because he is keen to see their wrestling skills. He has heard that your two sons are very powerful, so he wants to arrange a wrestling competition between Krishna and his own wrestler. O cowherd Nanda! This is the order of the king. Get ready quickly and come to Mathura."

When Akrura spoke like this, Nanda turned to his beloved child Krishna and asked him what to do. Krishna simply smiled, and said, "Very good, let us go. I will wrestle and then meet Kamsa. Let us pack our bags on bullock carts and go to Mathura."

Soon Nanda Maharaja made an announcement all over Vrndavana that they should get ready to go to Mathura to watch the fire sacrifice and the wrestling competition.

## The pathetic condition and the statements of the gopis on hearing about Krishna's departure for Mathura

As soon as the announcement had been made, all the inhabitants of Vrndavana, especially the gopis, felt terribly dejected. They couldn't believe that Krishna would ever leave them and go elsewhere. Upon realizing that Krishna would go to Mathura, the gopis almost fainted and began to cry pathetically. They lamented, "Alas! We took birth in Gokula as a result of our good fortune accumulated from many lifetimes. That is why we were able to receive the association of Nanda's beloved child. O friends! How can we tolerate this? The treasury of our lives is now leaving us. What is the use of remaining alive in this world and accumulating wealth? Without Krishna everything is useless for us. Krishna is our very life, so if He leaves us then where is the question of our survival? O Sakhis! If we do not see our beloved Kanu even for a moment, we will die. If we do not see Krishna even for a fraction of a moment, we consider that time to be longer than twelve years. We cannot tolerate even a moment's separation from Krishna. We know nothing but Krishna day and night. We have transgressed all social etiquette including our past, modesty, fear, family traditions, husbands, households, friends, and relatives simply for the sake of Madhusudana; in fact, for Him, we did not mind abandoning all worldly moralities. But now, our most dear Krishna will go to Mathura. O friends! We will try to keep Krishna here by any means. If the elderly persons stop us or chastise us we will not regard their words. We will give up everything including our lives for the sake of Krishna."

After discussing the matter in this way, all the gopis returned to their respective homes. Early the next morning, Akrura awoke, got up from his bed, and took his bath in the Yamuna. He also performed his morning rituals on the bank of Yamuna. Thereafter, he took Nanda Maharaja, Krishna and Balarama on his chariot. There were many bullock carts loaded with yogurt, milk, ghee and other necessary items. As Krishna and Balarama sat down on the chariot the young gopis of Vrndavana stood on the pathway and began to cry profusely. As Akrura was about to move his chariot, the gopis fell down on the ground and cried pathetically. They lamented, "Who says you are Akrura, or gentle? Actually you are Krura, or cruel, because you are taking away Krishna from us. Whoever has kept your name Akrura is certainly a sinful person. We have never seen a crueler person than you. Krishna is not only our Lord but also the Lord of the universe. And now you are taking away that Krishna from all of us. Today the entire city of Gokula will become empty without Krishna. Today the jewel of Gokula is going to Madhupuri. From today, the blissful Vrndavana will look deserted. Who will take care of the cows and calves of Vrndavana? Today all the inhabitants of Vraja have become like orphans. The creator has taken away all our happiness and given us immense distress. O Sakhis! We will no longer go to the banks of Yamuna and enjoy Krishna's intimate association. We will no longer embrace Krishna. We will not see Krishna's moon like face anymore. We will no longer kiss His beautiful face anymore. We will not go to the garden and pluck flowers. We will not prepare flower garlands anymore for Krishna. No more will Krishna decorate our heads and bodies with flowers. Krishna will no more give us His chewed betel nuts."

In this way, the gopis cried and lamented pathetically because of the intense separation from their most beloved Krishna. "O Sakhis! We will surely die when Krishna leaves us. What is the use of dying after He leaves us? It is better we die now in front of Him so that He will feel embarrassed. It is easy to give up a small amount of wealth, but it is very difficult to give up a large amount of wealth. So, how can we give up our most coveted treasure, Krishna? How can we let Him go? With whom will we go and enjoy pastimes on the banks of Yamuna? Who will solace us when we become afflicted by separation? How will we survive without seeing Krishna? Moreover, if Krishna goes to Mathura, He will not return. There are so many beautiful young girls in Mathura, and we are sure Murari will enjoy their company and forget us. Besides, we are only innocent village girls and the girls of Mathura are civilized."

Even though the gopis of Vraja tried their best to stop Akrura from taking Krishna and Balarama away from them, they were unable to do so. Finally the chariot rolled on and Krishna left for Mathura. The condition of the gopis became even worse as Krishna's chariot began to move. They cried and lamented loudly without caring for the elders. They kept on looking in the same direction where Krishna's chariot was going. They stood there motionless for as long as they could to see Krishna's chariot. Soon the chariot disappeared from their eyesight, and they could only see a cloud of dust, nothing else. Still, they kept looking without blinking their eyes even once. As Akrura took away Krishna and Balarama to Madhupurl, the cowherd damsels of Gokula simply cried while remembering their beloved Lord Krishna.

## Akrura sees the four-armed form of the Lord

Akrura's chariot began to move quickly towards Mathura. By noon they were midway to Mathura, and they stopped on the bank of Yamuna. Akrura got down from his chariot and went to take his bath in the waters of Yamuna. Just as he entered the water, he suddenly saw Krishna and Balarama already within the water. He was surprised because he had just left Krishna and Balarama sitting on the chariot. Despite his confusion, seeing Them there made his happiness know no bounds. He saw Balarama in the form of Anantadeva with thousands of heads. He was glorified from all sides by the kings of the Nagas. Lord Ananta was nicely decorated with armlets, earrings and necklace. He had one thousand hoods. He held a conch, a disc, a club and a lotus in His four hands. He also saw Laksrm and Sarasvati on either side of Lord Ananta. Seeing the two brothers within the waters of Yamuna, Akrura smiled and thought, "Just now they were sitting on the chariot, so how they have come here?" In order to remove his doubts, he went back to his chariot and saw that both Krishna and Balarama were sitting there as before. He returned to the waters of Yamuna and found Krishna and Balarama there as well. He was puzzled, yet fully satisfied. He contemplated, "Today is the most auspicious day of my life, for today I have seen the four-armed form of the Supreme Lord. Now all my contamination that I have had for millions of births has been vanquished. Lord Narayana has become merciful to me."

After completing his bath, Akrura sat down on his chariot and proceeded towards Mathura. Meanwhile all the other cowherd men of Gokula headed by Nanda had already reached the outskirts of Mathura and were waiting for Krishna and Balarama to arrive. After a short time, Akrura arrived there and requested that all the inhabitants of Vrndavana stay at his house that night. He said, "O Rama! O Damodara! Please come to my house. Please make my house purified by the dust from Your lotus feet. It is because of Your lotus feet that the Ganges has become renowned as the giver of liberation within the three worlds. Anyone who leaves his body even near the Ganges attains liberation. O Lord! May those glorious lotus feet of Yours make Their appearance in my house so that myself along with my family members become purified."

When Akrura requested that Krishna be his guest, Krishna told him that He will visit Akrura's house after meeting king kamsa. Then, Krishna and Balarama walked down the main road in the city of Mathura. In the meantime, Akrura mounted his chariot and went to meet kamsa. Upon reaching there, he offered his respects to Kamsa and said, "I have brought Krishna and Balarama here. Today they will take rest in the city and tomorrow morning they will come to meet you. After giving this news to Kamsa, Akrura returned to his residence."

## The killing of the Washerman

Meanwhile, Krishna and Balarama were wandering about the streets of Mathura. After walking a short distance, the son of Nanda met a washerman. Krishna said to him, "O washerman, give Me some nice clothes to wear." The washerman first laughed and then replied in a strong voice, "O sinful cowherd boy! How dare You speak like that? Don't You know how strict King Kamsa is? I am his servant, and I wash his clothes. Who are You? You live in the forest and look after cows. You don't understand anything. Aren't You afraid of death? O son of Nanda! You had better go away from here quickly. If the king comes to know about Your audacity, nobody will be able to save You. Don't speak like that again."

When the washerman spoke like this, Krishna laughed loudly. He pushed the washerman aside, and took away all the clothes. Krishna then caught hold of the washerman's hair and forcefully threw him to the ground. As a result of the fall, his bones broke into pieces, and he died instantly. This is how Krishna killed the washerman as soon as He had entered the city of Mathura. Krishna also took away the clothes from the other washermen, and slapped them hard. He wore some of those clothes and gave some of them to the boys of Mathura. The rest He simply threw around the streets of Mathura. The inhabitants of Mathura happily picked up those garments and returned home.

Soon a messenger went to Kamsa and informed him that Krishna had killed the washerman and taken away all of his clothes. Kamsa became livid. Out of frustration, he began to cry and lament his great misfortune. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the topics of Hari and assures that anyone who remembers Krishna will not take birth again.

## Krishna removes Kubja's hump and makes her beautiful

When Krishna and Balarama dressed themselves with these clothes, they looked most attractive and their beauty defeated the beauty of Cupid. After walking a little further, Krishna met a florist. Krishna asked the florist, "Give Me a garland of fragrant flowers, and you will make so much profit." Saying this, Krishna sat down next to the florist. As soon as the florist turned and saw Krishna sitting by his side, he immediately stood up in awe and reverence, and offered his obeisance to Him. He faithfully worshiped the two brothers by offering padya and arghya. He offered Them excellent garlands of fragrant flowers and opulent dresses. He offered sandalwood paste, varieties of foodstuffs, and then betel nuts.

Krishna became very pleased by the florist's hospitality. The Lord gave him the benediction that the florist would enjoy unlimited happiness in this world. As a matter of fact, the florist became very famous by the benediction of Govinda. People henceforward started accepting food and water from him. After giving this benediction to the florist, Krishna continued to walk down the main road of Mathura. He was accompanied by Balarama and other small boys. Suddenly, Krishna saw Kubja whose body was twisted in three different places. Upon seeing her crooked form, Krishna began to laugh. Krishna asked the woman who she was and what her name was. The woman replied with a smile, "O Govinda! My name is Trivanka, and I am a maidservant of Kamsa. My duty is to supply sandalwood paste, kumkum, and deer musk to King Kamsa. O son of Nanda! How can I serve You? You two certainly look as beautiful as Cupid. I think this sandalwood paste will suit You the best. I wish to apply this sandalwood paste on Your body. I don't care what Kamsa will do."

Kubja offered sandalwood paste to Krishna and Balarama, who decorated Themselves with sandalwood paste to Their full satisfaction. When bluish Krishna smeared Himself with kumkum, He looked like the blue sky decorated with a rainbow. When Balai, whose complexion is like crystal, applied deer musk, He appeared just like the peak of the Kailasa Mountain with a black spot on it. As Murari became fully satisfied by the service of Kubja, He placed His left hand on the back of Kubja, and with His right hand, He grabbed Kubja's chin and gave it a jerk. The hump of Kubja immediately disappeared. In this way Krishna removed Kubja's crookedness. She was transformed into a beautiful Vidyadhari, and became the most beautiful woman in the three worlds. Kubja fell at the feet of Krishna and displayed her gratefulness. At Krishna's touch, Kubja became afflicted by lust, so she said "My whole body is burning with the fire of lust. Please enjoy my company and satisfy me. O Lord of the universe, I have lost my heart to You. My mind is restless and my heart is burning due to passion. Kindly embrace me and keep me at Your lotus feet. If You refuse then I will certainly give up my life, and You will be responsible for killing a woman."

When Kubja spoke like this, Krishna began to laugh. He was embarrassed because His elder brother Balarama was by His side. Quickly He said to her, "For today you should go home, but I will certainly fulfill your desire. I will visit your house before leaving Mathura. Now you let go of My dhoti as I have to go meet the king immediately." In this way, Krishna and Balarama tactfully pacified Kubja and continued to wander about in the streets of Mathura.

All the residences in Mathura were made of crystals and decorated with pearl garlands. The doors were plated with gold and flags flew on the top of each house. Everything was opulent in Mathura. There were many varieties of beautiful trees, and the roots of all these trees were covered with stone slabs. At the entrance of each house, there were betel nut and coconut trees. The capital of kamsa was thus decorated with all opulent ingredients, looking exactly like the abode of Indra.

The son of Nanda walked slowly in His own style. As Vanamali Krishna walked on the main road, He performed many wonderful pastimes.

## The pastime of breaking the bow by Krishna

Within a short distance, Krishna saw that some brahmana's were performing the Dhanuryajna. They were guarded by kamsa's followers. Out of curiosity, Krishna entered within the sacrificial arena, and Krishna addressed the brahmanas, "O twice born! Who is the beneficiary of this sacrifice? Tell Me who carries this wonderful bow?"

Without waiting for the reply, Krishna Himself picked up that bow with His left hand and effortlessly pulled its string. Upon doing so, the tumultuous sound of the bow filled the ten directions. Then, to everyone's surprise, He broke the bow into two pieces. The sound was so loud and deafening that the people could no longer hear anything. Those who stood as guards in the sacrificial arena were hit by the broken pieces of bow and fell down dead. All the other followers of Kamsa ran away out of fear.

After breaking the bow, Krishna slowly walked down the road. Krishna and Balarama wandered about within the city of Mathura like this, until the evening when they returned to take rest with Nanda Maharaja.

They spent the night in a charming flower garden located outside the heart of the city. After having Their dinner with Nanda, They went off to sleep.

Meanwhile, Kamsa had heard from his many different messengers everything that Krishna had been doing throughout the day. Out of fear of Krishna, Kamsa could not sleep at night. He was afraid of his impending death. He began to experience bad omens and inauspicious symptoms. Even in his dreams, he saw strange things like ladies wearing red garlands, and rains of blood falling in the four directions. King Kamsa woke up abruptly and could not sleep any longer. He was greatly frightened. The next morning, he ordered his followers to quickly arrange for the wrestling competition.

## The killing of the elephant Kuvalaya

King Kamsa had called everyone to participate in the Dhanuryajna. He brought Vasudeva and Devaki specially to be present at the event, and sat them near the wrestling arena, so that they could see the death of their son with their own eyes. He ordered his men to keep elephants, horses, and chariots ready. He instructed his servants to keep the mad Kuvalaya elephant at the gate so that as soon as the son of Nanda passed through the gate, the elephant would kill Him either with its tusks or by crushing Him under its feet. "If somehow the two brothers still survive, then we will have them killed by our powerful wrestlers." After imparting orders to his followers in this way, Kamsa sat down on his throne with his weapon in his hand.

In the meantime, Krishna and Balarama had awoken from Their sleep and gone down to the Yamuna to take Their bath. They then dressed beautifully like dancers and decorated Themselves with various ornaments. After dressing, the two brothers along with Their friends went to meet King Kamsa.

Nanda Maharaja duly offered respects to kamsa and gave him his tax payment. King Kamsa invited Nanda and his men to sit down at the wrestling arena to watch the wrestling competition. A little later, Krishna and Balarama arrived at the gate to the courtyard of Kamsa with smiles on Their faces. Just as Krishna was about to step through the gate, He saw an elephant standing there in an awkward position. Krishna requested the elephant driver to give Him way so that He could go in and meet the king. He also threatened the elephant keeper with dire consequences if He was not allowed inside. These words made the elephant driver mad. Instead of giving way to Krishna, he drove his elephant forward in a way to attack Krishna. The mad elephant charged at Krishna in an angry mood. Krishna quickly jumped back to avoid being hit by the elephant's trunk, then caught hold of the elephant's tusks with His hands and pulled him forcefully sideways, giving the mad elephant a strong jerk. With both His hands, Krishna grabbed the elephant's tusks, pressing them down to the ground with great force. The elephant began to make a loud noise and could not move either his body or his trunk. Then, with great force and power, Krishna tore apart the elephant's trunk from its body. Lord Hari then jumped on the back of the elephant, and put great pressure on it. Soon the elephant lost control and fell to the ground dead, his heavy body making a huge noise as it fell. After killing Kuvalayapida in this way, Krishna and Balarama removed the two tusks of the elephant, and using the tusks, killed the elephant's driver as well as all the bodyguards sent by kamsa.

After the killing of Kuvalayapida, Krishna and Balarama slowly entered the wrestling arena carrying the tusks of the elephant on Their shoulders. By nature, Krishna is beautiful, but now He looked even more beautiful because His whole body was covered with the elephant's blood.

## Krishna the reservoir of ail rasas

When Krishna joyfully entered the sacrificial arena He manifested many different forms according to the qualifications of the spectators. The wrestlers saw Him just like a thunderbolt, the pious kings saw Him as the beautiful Krishna of Vrndavana, and the women saw Him as the most enchanting transcendental Cupid. The cowherd men headed by Nanda saw Him as their most beloved child. The sinful kings saw Him as death personified. Vasudeva and Devaki saw Him simply as their darling boy. King Kamsa saw Him as Yamaraja, the yogis and perfect beings saw Him as the embodiment of mystic perfection, and the members of the Yadu and Vrsni dynasties saw Him as the most obedient and responsible descendent. Thus according to their respective qualifications, all the assembled spectators at the wrestling arena saw Krishna differently. Although Krishna is one, He manifested different forms according to people's different degrees of understanding.

As Krishna made His way towards the wrestling arena, the assembled spectators began to murmur amongst themselves about the activities of Krishna. "This is the same Krishna whom Vasudeva transferred to the house of Nanda. Vasudeva instead brought back with him the daughter of Nanda to deceive Kamsa. When He was only a child, Krishna killed Putana. He killed the demon Trnavarta and broke the bullock cart in His childhood. This Krishna once uprooted the twin trees known as yamalaarjuna, and then killed the demon Vatsasura in the pasturing ground. This is the same Krishna who killed Aghasura, Bakasura and Dhenukasura. When He was a small boy, He swallowed a blazing forest fire. Once, He saved all the cows and calves by killing Pralambasura. He is the one who drove away Kaliya from the waters of Yamuna. This is the same Krishna who saved the lives of the Vrajavasis by lifting the Govardhana Hill. Don't you remember how He recently killed Aristasura and KesI? He saved His father Nanda from the clutch of a python. He also enjoyed rasa-lila pastimes with the young ladies of Vraja. He killed Vyomasura in Vrndavana. Just recently when He came to Mathura, He killed Kamsa's washerman, transformed the hunchback Kubja into a beautiful girl, and broke the giant bow in the sacrificial arena. Just now while entering this wrestling arena. He killed the mad Kuvalaya elephant." The spectators discussed Krishna's activities in this way, but suddenly there was a huge commotion.

## The wrestlers Canura and Mustika killed

With great fanfare, Kamsa's trusted wrestlers Canura and Mu§tika arrived in the wrestling arena. Canura teased Krishna by speaking in the following manner, "O son of Nanda! You are no match for a wrestler like me. You live in the forest and take care of cows. You seem to be very happy about the wrestling competition. It is the duty of the subjects to please their king. If the king is satisfied, then everyone will become satisfied. The king is personally going to watch this competition. I will satisfy the king by wrestling with You. Get ready to wrestle with me, and let everyone watch the fun."

When Canura foolishly spoke like this, Krishna, the darling of the residents of Vraja, replied with a smile, "What you have said is true. Every citizen is duty bound to please his king. I will certainly wrestle with you. But before that, I wish to remind you of something. If someone challenges another to fight, it is the duty of the opposition to oblige. I am only a small boy and you two are great wrestlers. So two against one is not fair.

When Krishna said like that, Canura began to laugh sarcastically saying, "You seem to be a mere boy. But how did You kill so many powerful heroes in Your childhood? Just now, You killed the elephant Kuvalaya who possesses the power of ten thousand elephants. If You are really a small boy, then how did You accomplish all this? O Son of Nanda! Do not play any more tricks. Come and fight. As far as the fairness of the competition is concerned, You may wrestle with me, while Balai wrestles with Mustika."

Krishna simply smiled and began to get ready for the competition. He tightened His waist band and chadar. Krishna stretched His two hands and began to wrestle with Canura. A fierce fight took place between the two powerful wrestlers and Krishna and Balarama. The spectators were very frightened. They said, "Look at Krishna and Balarama. Their bodies are so soft whereas the bodies of these two wrestlers are as hard as thunderbolts. Therefore this wrestling is unlawful. The king is not justified when he ordered Krishna and Balarama to fight with his two most powerful and experienced wrestlers. I think we should all leave this place immediately because it is sinful to even watch such a one-sided competition."

Vasudeva and Devaki began to cry and lament seeing their beloved child fighting it out with the two experienced wrestlers. When Madhusudana saw that His parents were extremely worried and distressed, He decided to kill the enemies at once. After enjoying the wrestling for some time, Krishna suddenly grabbed Canura's two legs and smashed him to the ground. He then caught hold of Canura's throat with His left hand and punched his face with His right hand. At this powerful punch, Canura's teeth fell out and blood began to flow from his mouth and nose. It was a terrible sight, yet people were amazed to see how Krishna, although a small boy, was able to beat such a powerful wrestler. The immensely powerful Canura somehow tolerated this blow from Krishna. He slowly got up and then pushed Krishna to the ground. This time, he grabbed Krishna's hair and started to pummel Him with his fists. Undaunted, Krishna became so angry that He picked Canura up over His own head and forcefully smashed him to the ground. This time Canura had no hope but to go to the house of Yamaraja. As Canura fought with Krishna, similarly Mustika had a great fight with Baladeva. Finally, Balarama threw Mustika to the ground and sat down upon his chest. The wrestler died instantly under the heavy weight of Balarama. People of the entire universe began to shout "Jaya! Jaya!" When Canura and Mustika were thus killed, Kamsa called his other wrestlers to come and fight, but out of fear, they ran away from the wrestling arena. Although Kamsa was greatly frightened in his heart, he presented a brave face to everyone and spoke in a steady voice as follows.

## Krishna kills Kamsa and his associates

"O great heroes! Listen to my order; take these two brothers out of this arena. Take away Nanda and put him behind bars. Beat him up and take away all of his wealth. Drag Vasudeva and DevakI out from\*here, cut off their heads and throw their dead bodies in the graveyard. Even my father is my enemy. So take him out and severe his head. How dare he envy me despite being my father? Stop playing all the musical instruments at once. There is no need for that."

In this way Kamsa spoke like a mad man because he knew that his death was near. However, Krishna began to think after hearing Kamsa spoke like a crazy person. It was out of utter frustration that Kamsa had ordered his own servants to kill indiscriminately. Krishna, realizing the urgency and the mentality of Kamsa, quickly jumped onto the raised platform where the king was sitting. As Krishna approached Kamsa, Kamsa immediately stood up in great fear, looking as if he knew that death personified had come to catch him. The wicked Kamsa picked up his sharp sword and charged towards Krishna like a maddened lion. Krishna faced him powerfully. With His left hand, Krishna grabbed Kamsa's throat and with His right, He snatched away his sword. Krishna then pushed Kamsa hard, and the demon, unable to keep his balance, crashed to the ground. Krishna took this opportunity and quickly jumped on his chest. By the supreme will of Krishna, Kamsa felt the burden of the whole earth upon his chest. As a result of this great weight, the sinful Kamsa left his body. On seeing Kamsa's pathetic condition, the entire community of demons cried and lamented in disbelief. They could not tmst their eyes. The inhabitants of the heavenly planets showered flowers on Krishna and Balarama. Soon, by the order of Krishna, Vasudeva, Devaki, Nanda and all the other cowherd inhabitants of Vraja were released. They became jubilant, embraced Krishna and Balarama and thanked Them profusely. The two brothers had not only killed Kamsa, but also eliminated his other demoniac friends and associates who had come to fight with Krishna. They were killed just as flies get killed when rushing into the fire. In this way, kamsa and his associates were killed by the Supreme Lord Krishna in front of all the inhabitants of Vraja. O brothers! Listen to these topics of Krishna with full attention as Gunaraja Khan narrates them.

## Lamentation by the wives of Kamsa

The news of kamsa's death quickly spread all over Mathura. When the wives of kamsa heard this news, they became shocked and rushed to the place where their husband was lying dead. They picked up kamsa's dead body and put him on their laps and began to cry loudly. "O Lord of our heart! From today onwards we will have no guide and protector. We will be shelterless. Where did you go? Why have you left us in this condition? The moment you began to torture the cows, brahmanas, and godly personalities, we understood that your intelligence had become polluted. It is said that anyone who gives trouble to a brahmana faces untimely death. From now on, we will live the lives of widows. O great king, although you are the controller of the three worlds, you are lying dead on the ground like an ordinary person." Speaking thus, the wives of kamsa rolled on the ground and lamented profusely.

When Krishna saw the condition of these women, He became compassionate and tried to solace them with sweet words. Krishna said, "This was arranged by providence. O wives of the king! I will try my best to make sure that all of you live peacefully." He then instructed relatives of Kamsa to take kamsa's dead body away and perform the last rites.

Thereafter, Krishna unshackled Vasudeva and Devaki and sent them to their own residence. Anyone who hears the description of how Krishna killed kamsa will have no fear from enemies. O human beings! Hear the glories of Krishna with full attention. Then you will easily cross over the ocean of material existence. O brothers! Do not neglect to hear these wonderful topics for this is the only means by which to surpass the material ocean. O brothers! I, Gunaraja Khan repeatedly request all of you to hear Krishna's topics.

## Krishna installs Ugrasena as the king of Mathura

Krishna was still a young boy when He killed Kamsa. The residents of Vraja could not believe how such a small boy could kill the most sinful and wretched Kamsa. The inhabitants of the three worlds felt relieved and shouted "Jaya! Jaya!" But the followers and friends of Kamsa became frightened. Krishna effortlessly killed Kamsa without any help from anyone. Thereafter, Krishna decided to give the charge of ruling the kingdom to Kamsa's father Ugrasena. Krishna approached Ugrasena and respectfully said to him, "From now on you are the king of Madhupuri. You will sit on the royal throne and rule your kingdom, and We will serve you as your obedient subjects. This is the most reasonable decision, as members of the Yadu dynasty have no right to become king. Since you are My maternal grandfather, I entrust this responsibility to you. As your servant, I will always assist you in conquering your enemies."

So, Krishna completed the formalities of installing Ugrasena as the king of Madhupuri. Then, Krishna and Balarama went to see Vasudeva and Devaki.

Upon arriving at their residence, Krishna and Balarama offered Their obeisances to Their parents and cried like ordinary persons. They said, "We are useless sons because We did not take care of Our parents. You both had to go through a great deal of trouble because of Us. Therefore Our lives have no meaning in this world. We are so unfortunate that We were even bereft of drinking Your breast milk. In our childhood, We could not sleep on Your lap." As Krishna and Balarama spoke like this, Vasudeva and Devaki became extremely emotional and as a result they began to cry loudly and roll on the ground. Vasudeva and Devaki affectionately hugged their beloved children Krishna and Balarama and shed incessant tears of love.

## The sacred thread ceremony of Krishna and Balarama

Krishna and Balarama joined Their parents and lived happily in Mathura. One day, Vasudeva called the family priest to perform all the required rituals that he had not been able to complete earlier due to his confinement in kamsa's prison. The brahmana priest came to the house of Vasudeva and conducted the brahmana thread ceremony according to the rules and regulations prescribed in the scriptures. Vasudeva now got the opportunity to fulfill his desires, and he used it to his full satisfaction. He gave twenty thousand cows in charity to the brahmanas. He had wanted to donate these cows at the time of Krishna's birth, but due to his circumstances, he had not been able to do so. He also pleased all the other residents of Mathura by giving them various gifts. Krishna thereafter called all His relatives and friends who had run away out of fear of kamsa, and gave them full reassurance of their safety. Then, after making Ugrasena in charge of ruling the kingdom, the two brothers Krishna and Balarama left for Avantipura to study.

## Krishna and Balarama study in Avantipura

There lived in the city of Avanti a pious brahmana named Sandipani Muni. He was well versed in all the scriptures and was enriched with humility and penance. He was equal to the great Vyasadeva. Krishna and Balarama studied under the care and guidance of the great sage Sandipani. In sixty-four days, the two brothers learned the sixty-four varieties of knowledge. This amazing feat made their teacher suspicious. He thought that these two students must be some demigods in disguise. After completing Their studies, Krishna and Balarama offered Their respectful obeisances to Their teacher and prayed to him, "O teacher! O great brahmanal Please tell Us what should We offer you as daksincP. We have learned everything by your mercy. Now if you permit Us, We can return home, but before that, We must give you your due." Upon hearing Krishna's request, Sandipani thought that his students must be some demigods who had come there to deceive him.

## Krishna gives daksina to His spiritual master

That night, Sandipani Muni spoke with his wife about what to ask from Krishna as daksina. The next day, he called Krishna and Balarama and said, "If You must give me daksina, then bring my dead son back to life. My son died in the waters of the ocean. You bring him back and this will be Your daksina." Being thus ordered by His spiritual master, Krishna immediately went to the shore of the ocean and demanded, "O predominating deity of the ocean! Return the dead son of My spiritual master at once."

As soon as Krishna said these words, the personified ocean immediately appeared before Krishna and offered his respectful obeisances. He then said, "O my Lord! I have not killed the son of Your spiritual master. There is a sinful demon who lives in my waters. He is so powerful that I cannot prevent him from living in my waters." When Krishna heard these words, He immediately dived into the waters of ocean and went straight to that demon whose body was shaped like a conch. Krishna tore apart the demon's body, but could not find the child of His spiritual master anywhere inside. When the demc n was killed, Krishna took away the Pancajanya conch and went to the abode of Yamaraja.

As Krishna entered the abode of Yamaraja, He powerfully blew the conch. The sound of that conch was so frightening that even Yamaraja himself became struck with wonder. He realized that it was his worshipable Lord Narayana. Dharmaraja's happiness knew no bounds. He considered himself to be fortunate for today he was going to meet the Lord of his life. As soon as he saw Krishna, he offered his respectful obeisances to the lotus-eyed Lord and worshiped Him with suitable ingredients. With folded hands, Yamaraja prayed to Krishna, "O Supreme Lord! You have descended to the earth in order to diminish her burden. O Lord of the universe! You have killed many sinful demons and thereby removed the burden of the earth. My life and activities have become successful because today I have touched Your lotus feet. O Lord Hari! Please order me, what should I do for You? My whole abode has become purified by the dust from Your lotus feet."

When Yamaraja prayed to Krishna in this way, the Lord, who carries a disc in His hand, smiled with joy and ordered him to return the dead son of His spiritual master to life. Upon hearing this order, Yamaraja became afraid and said to the Lord, "O Lord Narayana! Actually You are the supreme creator and the proprietor of everything. I have no power to bring any soul to life, nor can I truly punish anyone. According to one's own karma, each individual living entity is subjected to enjoyment or punishment. Unless, and until one's karmic reactions are over, one cannot be delivered. O Hari! You alone may help the son of Your spiritual master exhaust the reactions of his karma and take him back to life."

Pleased by Yamaraja's words, Krishna and Balarama listened gladly to him, then They took the son of Their spiritual master and left the abode of Yamaraja to return the son to Their spiritual master. After offering the son as guru-daksina, Krishna and Balarama took permission from Their spiritual master to leave. Sandipani Muni could understand that these two boys were not demigods, Gandharvas, or ordinary human beings but the Supreme Personality of Godhead in human forms. Otherwise, who could possibly have the power to bring a dead person back to life from the abode of Yamaraja? The sage stood up respectfully and bid farewell to Krishna and Balarama. In great happiness, Krishna and Balarama returned to Mathura.

## Uddhava goes to Gokula

Upon arriving in Mathura, Krishna and Balarama suddenly met Uddhava. Uddhava was a great friend and well-wisher of Krishna. Krishna affectionately caught hold of Uddhava's hand and requested him to go to Gokula, saying, "My dear Uddhava! Take a chariot and quickly go to the city of Gokula. All the male and female inhabitants of Gokula are hardly living, they are almost dead due to their great sense of separation from Me. My dear father and mother, Nanda and Yasoda are constantly thinking of Me. They cannot live peacefully without Me. The gopis of Vrndavana are especially very distressed because of their intense love for Me. You are to go there and pacify everyone." When Krishna requested Uddhava in this way, Uddhava offered obeisances to Krishna and immediately left.

Uddhava started off on his journey, and arrived in Gokula in the late afternoon. First, he went to the house of Nanda Maharaja. Realizing that he was a messenger sent by Krishna, Nanda Maharaja greeted him respectfully, and honored him according to proper etiquette. Nanda Maharaja embraced Uddhava with great affection and cried profusely while speaking Krishna's name. After a short while, he controlled himself somewhat and said, "How are my beloved Krishna and Balarama doing? How are Vasudeva, Devaki, Rohini, and the others? Is Krishna happy with all of them? Our beloved Krishna has left us. There is no greater sinner than me within the three worlds."

After Uddhava heard these words, he replied to Nanda, "Please do not cry. It is a fact that there is no one within the three worlds who is more fortunate than you. The unlimited Lord Sri Krishna is the Absolute Truth and your mind is fully absorbed in Him. Even if one performs penance for millions of births, it is not possible to become attached to the Supreme Lord Krishna. O king of Vraja! You are a liberated soul. By your association, others can obtain liberation."

After solacing Nanda and Yasoda with sweet words, Uddhava ate fruits, roots, and grains at their house and spent the night there. By the next morning, the news of Uddhava's arrival in Gokula had already spread very fast. The gopis gathered together and went to the house of Nanda. When they saw the chariot outside Nanda's house they thought, "Has the sinful Akrura come again?" But later they realized that Akrura had not come.

Meanwhile, Uddhava completed his morning duties and met the gopis. Uddhava looks more or less like Krishna, so as soon as the gopis saw him, they could not make out whether he was Krishna or someone else. The gopis greeted Uddhava with great respect. Uddhava then conveyed the message he had brought from Krishna to the gopis.

## Gopi-gita

"O gopis! Do not worry. Please pacify your minds. The lotus-eyed Krishna will soon return here and see you all."

The gopis understood that this gentleman had been sent by Krishna with a message for the inhabitants of Gokula. The condition of the gopis was pathetic. They were overwhelmed due to separation from Krishna. They spoke like madwomen because of their intense separation from Krishna. As they were speaking to Uddhava, they suddenly saw a bumblebee flying around. They immediately turned their attention to the bumblebee, and spoke to her as follows, "We know you have come from Krishna's place. We know Krishna is enjoying the company of the young girls of Mathura and enjoying His life. You have come here to deceive us. By nature, Krishna captures the hearts of the women. We know He is a cheater. In His previous incarnation, He cut off the nose of Surpanakha for the sake of Sita. There is no one more duplicitous than Him. Once He deceived Bali and sent Him to Rasatala. Yet in spite of all these acts, we cannot forget Him. But, see His behavior! He left us. The whole world knows about His duplicitous nature. Anyway what is the use of telling all these things to you? Our hearts are burning. It is also a fact that one who has given his life to Krishna will die thinking about Him."

The gopis continued, "We have no means of survival. Krishna must have forgotten us. Anyway, we live in the forest and are ugly. That must be why He left us. O messenger! By the way, how are Krishna and Balarama? Are They in good health? How are Their parents and friends? Does He ever remember us? Or is He simply taking pleasure in killing demons and enjoying other men's wives? Why would Krishna remember us for we are uncivilized and ugly?" Speaking in this way, the gopis cried profusely and lamented. They rolled on the ground, and their whole bodies became soaked by the tears from their eyes. They chanted "Krishna! Krishna!" over and over with intense attachment and love.

When Uddhava saw the extraordinary condition of the gopis, he became struck with wonder. He could not believe his eyes and ears. He realized that the gopis' devotion to Krishna was matchless. Uddhava immediately offered his obeisances at the feet of all the gopis and spoke to them respectfully. "O gopis I There is no one within the three worlds who is more fortunate than all of you. Your undivided attachment for Krishna is the evidence of your unique position. Please do not lament. Your beloved Lord will come to see you. The lotus-eyed Krishna will fulfill all of your desires. Kindly pacify your minds and wait for Him."

After solacing the gopis, Uddhava mounted his chariot and returned to Mathura. There he narrated each and every incident he experienced during his stay in Gokula to Krishna. He also handed over the tax which he had brought from Gokula to King Ugrasena. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the pastimes of Krishna.

## Krishna goes to the house of Kubja

Sri Krishna, Who is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and Whose eyes resemble lotus flowers, suddenly remembered Kubja while in Mathura. Krishna took Uddhava with Him and set out to see Kubja. In a short time, Krishna happily arrived at the house of Kubja, who became overwhelmed with passion as soon as she saw Krishna. She lost her external consciousness and fell down to the ground. Krishna gently picked her up and made her sit down by His side. Kubja was extremely happy thinking about Krishna's association. Krishna fulfilled all of Kubja's desires to her full satisfaction. Krishna never disappoints anyone who wishes to serve Him in any capacity. He does not discriminate in this regard. Uddhava stood guard in the house of Kubja as Lord Govinda enjoyed amorous pastimes in the association of Kubja. After fulfilling her desires, the Supreme Lord left Kubja's house in the company of His dearmost friend, Uddhava.

## Krishna goes to the house of Akrura

While enjoying the beautiful scenery of Mathura, Krishna and Balarama visited the house of Akrura. As soon as Akrura saw Krishna and Balarama at his doorstep, he greeted them with respect and embraced them affectionately. Akrura worshiped the two brothers with love and devotion, and gave Them comfortable seats to sit upon.
