He washed Krishna's lotus feet with fresh water, and then sprinkled that water not only on his own head, but also on the heads of all of his family members. Akrura prayed to Krishna, "Today my life has become successful, for I have seen Your lotus feet in my house. O Supreme Lord! You have delivered me by bestowing upon me the dust from Your lotus feet. You have advented in this world to decrease the burden on the earth. We will become delivered from the material ocean by Your merciful glance. O Lord! You are the cause of the creation, maintenance, and annihilation. Without Your mercy, no one can cross over the ocean of material existence."

When Akrura prayed to Krishna with great devotion, Krishna became very compassionate. He offered His obeisances to Akrura and spoke with folded hands, "You are a respectful personality and My paternal uncle. I am like your son. Therefore, why do you My elder pray to Me like this?" In this way, Krishna pacified Akrura with sweet words and said to him further, "O uncle! Quickly get ready and go to Hastinapura to meet the sons of Pandu. King Pandu died at an immature age, so his young sons must be in great difficulty. King Dhrtarastra is very old now, and I know his mentality about the sons of Pandu. I don't know how he is maintaining his subjects. You go there and check up on the mentality and behavior of all of them." Being advised by Krishna in this way, Akrura mounted his chariot and left for Hastinapura.

In due course of time, Akrura of the Yadu dynasty arrived in Hastinapura and met his family members there face to face. He saw King Dhrtarastra, the sons of Pandu, and their mother, Kunti. Kunti especially became emotional upon seeing Akrura. Akrura exchanged greetings with everyone according to the relationships he had with each one, and gathered information about their well-being. Thereafter, he returned to Mathura and informed Krishna about everything in Hastinapura. He informed Him of the ill treatment of King Dhrtarastra towards Kunti and her five sons. He disclosed to Krishna that Kunti was in great distress. He also advised Krishna to do something quickly because after Dhrtarastra, his son Duryodhana was to become the king of Hastinapura. At hearing this news from Akrura, Krishna simply smiled and assured Akrura that the Pandavas need not be afraid of anyone. Krishna and Balarama continued their days in Mathura in great happiness.

## The wives of Kamsa lament to their father

The two wives of kamsa, namely Asti and Prapti, were the daughters of Jarasanda, the king of Magadha. After their husband was killed by Krishna, they lamented to their father as follows, "O father! You are the emperor in-charge of Magadha. Out of fear of you, the serpent Vasuki shivers in the Patalaloka, and the inhabitants of the earth and the heavenly planets are also afraid of you. All the kings of this world are subordinate to you and they live by your mercy. Look at the two brothers, Krishna and Balarama, They are the sons of Nanda who lived in Vrndavana and looked after cows. When Krishna was a very small child, He killed the witch Putana by sucking away her very life. He killed Trnavarta, broke a bullock cart, and uprooted thqyamala-arjuna trees. When He was seven years old, He lifted the Govardhana Hill and saved the inhabitants of Gokula from the wrath of Indra. He killed Pralambasura, Bakasura, and drove away the serpent Kaliya from Yamuna. He killed Dhenukasura, and enjoyed palm fruits with His brother Balarama. O father! It is hard to believe how He killed powerful demons like Kesi and Aristasura, and how He annihilated the Kuvalaya elephant, who was as powerful as Yamaraja. Listen to this! He killed Canura, Mustika, and King kamsa. O father, because of Him, today we have become widows even during your lifetime. Although Krishna is a small boy, He performed all these activities with perfection. Now He has made Ugrasena the king of Mathura."

## The fighting between Jarasandha and Krishna

Jarasandha became furious when he heard from his daughters about the death of kamsa. He resolved to eliminate Krishna and Balarama forever. He sent a message to all his well-wishers and friendly kings to support him in his mission to defeat Krishna. He pacified his daughters and sent them back to Mathura. Within a few days, Jarasandha took twenty-three aksauhim soldiers with him and surrounded the whole city of Mathura, ready to attack Mathura from the four directions. Soon this news reached Krishna and Balarama. They took weapons in Their hands and came outside of the city of Mathura. The Lord of the universe, Krishna, took His Vaikuntha weapons like the conch, club, disc, and lotus, while Balarama wielded His favorite plough. Balarama sat on a chariot with a flag marked by the picture of a palm tree, and Krishna sat on His chariot decorated with a flag marked by the picture of Garuda. The two brothers bravely marched forward to have a look at the opposition army. Krishna then said to Balarama, "Dear brother! It is good to see so many sinful kings on the other side because it will give us an opportunity to diminish the burden of the earth by killing them. Listen, O noble-hearted brother, kill everyone except King Jarasandha. Then he will come back again with another army, and We will have more opportunity to remove the sinful elements from the surface of the earth."

After discussing strategies, Krishna and Balarama entered within the enemy camps. When King Jarasandha saw Rama and Krishna, he said, "O children, You have come here to die. O cowherd boys! You had better go away from here and look after the cows. Since You have come to fight, I will certainly send You to the house of Yamaraja!" After hearing Jarasandha speak like this, Krishna smiled and moved His chariot inside the opposition army. Krishna and Balarama stood in the midst of the oceanic army and killed all the sinful soldiers one by one. It is very difficult to give an estimate of how many rathis, maharathis, elephants, horses, and heads rolled on the ground. Krishna and Balarama gradually eliminated all the twenty-three aksauhinidivisions of soldiers. Some kings like Sisupala, Dantavakra, and the king of Kasi left out of fear of Krishna. The emperor Jarasandha finally left his chariot behind and started to run away. As the king was running away, Balarama chased him from behind with His plough in His hand. Balarama put the plough on Jarasandha's neck and pulled him to the ground. When He was about to hit Jarasandha's head with His plough, a voice from the sky declared, "Do not kill him. He is not to be killed by You." Although sad, Balarama spared Jarasandha's life. In this way, Jarasandha survived, but he felt humiliated. The two brothers fought a fierce battle, and in that battle killed millions of soldiers whose blood made a huge river. Many sinful kings and warriors were removed from this earth by Krishna and Balarama. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the topics of Krishna regarding the defeat of Jarasandha.

## Jarasandha makes a plan

After defeating Jarasandha in battle, the two brothers returned home to a hero's welcome. Everyone greeted them with ecstasy. People played musical instruments and chanted "Jaya! Jaya!" The demigods showered flowers from the sky and the ladies of Mathura sprinkled auspicious ingredients on the two brothers, making auspicious sounds in joy. Krishna and Balarama blissfully offered Their obeisances to Their parents, who embraced Them with great affection. After this reception, They were fed various kinds of sweets and pleasant drinks.

Meanwhile, Jarasandha called his ministers and friends, to discuss with them various ways and means to avenge his defeat. He said, "This is very insulting that I had to face defeat from two ordinary young boys. We must take revenge. I had twenty-three aksauhim divisions of soldiers, yet the two brothers have killed all of them. They fought so bravely that I had to leave my chariot and run for my life, so I cannot tolerate this insult." Jarasandha once again took another twenty-three aksauhim division of soldiers of the best quality and resolved to fight with determination to kill Krishna and Balarama. He led his army to Mathura and surrounded the whole city from all directions. Once again, Krishna and Balarama defeated all the soldiers of Jarasandha, killing each and every one of them. This time also, Jarasandha left the battlefield out of fear for his life. Jarasandha went back to his capital Magadha to gather more soldiers. Once again, he attacked Mathura with even more soldiers, but this time as well he was defeated by Krishna. In this way, he was defeated by Krishna repeatedly, seventeen times. His entire body began to burn out of humiliation, but he did not lose hope. He regained his strength once more and decided to attack Mathura for the eighteenth time. This time, he took help from his trusted friends like Kalayavana, Salva, and others, telling them, "I will personally attack Mathura from the east with a vast army. I will make a cakravyuha and kill all my enemies. O Salva! You take three billion mlecchas with you and attack Mathura from the south. Let Paundra and Kasiraja attack the city of Mathura from the north, while Vana and Bhauma can attack Mathura from the west. In this way, we can attack Mathura simultaneously and kill Krishna and Balarama. If we defeat Krishna and Balarama, then the whole earth will be under our control, and we will feast on human flesh." When the sinful kings heard this plan of Jarasandha, they agreed happily. All of the sinful kings collected their armies, weapons, and ammunitions, and prepared to attack Mathura under the guidance of the most wretched King Jarasandha, happy to help him conquer Mathura.

## Krishna builds His fort in the ocean

When Krishna and Balarama heard this plan of Jarasandha, They sat down and discussed among Themselves about Their future course of action. Finally, They decided to leave Mathura and live within a palace in the sea where no one could disturb Them and Their family members. The two brothers then went to the shore of the ocean and called the predominating deity of the ocean. Within no time, the controlling deity of the ocean appeared before Krishna and Balarama with various gifts in his hands. After offering his obeisances to Krishna, the personified ocean inquired about what he could do for Them. The Supreme Lord ordered, "Give Me about one hundred square miles space within your waters so that I can construct My residence there. I will build this house in such a way that no sinful king will have access to it."

Having secured the said amount of space within the ocean, Krishna built a beautiful palace and established the city of Dvaraka. The Supreme Lord Krishna invoked Visvakarma to immediately appear before Him, and ordered him to make the most beautiful and opulent palace, saying that the whole city of Dvaraka should look enchanting; in fact, His desire was that it be even more attractive than the abode of Indra. Being thus ordered by Krishna, Visvakarma began to construct a beautiful palace and the whole city of Dvaraka as well. The personified ocean helped Visvakarma by supplying him with various precious stones and jewels that he had in his possession.

The entire city was covered with gems and jewels. There were raised altars all over the city. The palace of Krishna was so high it appeared to touch the sky. There were theaters, schools, beautifully curved walls, kitchens, cowsheds, and everything else required to make the whole city perfect. Uddhava and Akrura had their own residences, and King Ugrasena also had a separate residence. In fact, Visvakarma built houses for all the friends, relatives and associates of Krishna. The whole city was protected by a raised wall followed by a ditch. There were arrangements for all castes of people to live peacefully. By and large, it was nothing less than the spiritual abode of Vaikuntha. After building a palace and a city of His own choice, Krishna returned to Mathura. He instructed all of His family members, relatives, and associates to immediately go to Dvaraka and reside there while He Himself and Balarama stayed back in Mathura.

## Jarasandha surrounds Mathura

Jarasandha was looking for an opportunity to attack Mathura. He increased the strength of his army by encouraging them and then went on to Mathura. He took with him twenty-three aksauhim division of soldiers and was supported by other powerful kings like Kalayavana and Sisupala. When Krishna and Balarama saw the huge army of Jarasandha, They purposely hid Themselves within a cave in Gomantha Mountain. The sinful Jarasandha searched for Krishna everywhere in each and every house, but could not find Krishna anywhere. He destroyed the whole city, cutting down trees, and smashing mountains, yet he could not find a trace of Krishna and Balarama.

Somehow Jarasandha knew that Krishna and Balarama had hidden Themselves somewhere in the mountains, so he ordered his followers to gather dry wood and spread it around the mountains, and light blazing fire. His followers did exactly what they were told, and very soon a fire was raging. The fire devoured everything. All the creatures who lived in those mountains were burnt to ashes. Birds, beasts, insects, and many sages began to call out for Krishna to save them. When Krishna heard their pathetic cries, He immediately devised a plan to save everyone. The Lord assumed a gigantic form and soon, from the weight of His heavy form, the mountain sank within the earth and the waters from beneath the earth rose to the top of the mountain. In this way, the blazing fire was extinguished. Krishna then returned to His original form and the huge mountain also emerged out of the ground and stood in its original place. Thereafter, the two brothers took up Their respective weapons, jumped from the top of the mountain, and vanished. In the meantime, Jarasandha, unable to find Krishna and Balarama anywhere, left the place. All the other sinful kings also left for their own kingdoms. Krishna and Balarama left the battlefields and went straight to Dwaraka, where They began to live joyfully in the association of Their relatives and friends under the rule of King Ugrasena.

## A messenger of Kalayavana arrives in Dwaraka

Once, when Krishna was residing in Dwaraka, a messenger of Kalayavana arrived there. First, he met with the doorkeeper and asked him to inform Krishna to allow him in. Krishna allowed Kalayavana's messenger to enter the royal court. The messenger went there and read out the following message sent by Kalayavana, "I am the undisputed king of the whole world. All the kings are under my subordination. I am the proprietor of the entire earth. You are a plunderer and are very mischievous. You challenged us to fight, and now You are running away from the battle like a jackal. If You have real courage, then come forward and fight with us. Otherwise You should leave Dwaraka for good."

After the messenger had read out the message, Krishna simply laughed and said to the messenger, "I have a gift for your master Kalayavana." Krishna put a black snake within a clay pitcher and wrapped it with a fine piece of cloth. He gave this to the messenger to give to his master. In due course of time, the messenger returned to King Kalayavana and told him everything about his encounter. He handed over the present to the king who immediately went to a solitary place and opened the gift. To his surprise, he saw a poisonous black snake inside a pitcher. The sinful king could immediately understand that Krishna had cheated him. He understood that Krishna considered Himself a black snake. Kalayavana became very angry at this behavior of Krishna. He decided to reciprocate, and put thousands of ants within that same pitcher to send to Krishna as a gift from him. The messenger again went to Dwaraka and offered Krishna the gift. The messenger also gave a small message to Krishna that said that if the snake survived in the midst of all these ants then Krishna too would survive.

Curious, Krishna secretly opened up the pitcher and saw that the snake had indeed died. Only the bones were scattered around within the pitcher and the ants were healthy and happy. The Supreme Lord Krishna understood that Kalayavana possessed a huge army. Moreover, Gargamuni had previously performed a great sacrifice to create innumerable Yavanas. Krishna thought, "This wicked Kalayavana is not meant to be killed by Me. He is destined to be killed by the great King Mucukunda, who is the son of King Mandata. At present, this Mucukunda is sleeping within a cave. In Treta-yuga, he helped the demigods fight with the demons and because of this, the demigods became very pleased with him and wanted to give him a benediction. The demigods asked Mucukunda, "O noble king! We are very happy with you. Ask for any benediction except liberation." Mucukunda replied, "I did not sleep for twelve years of the celestial time. I was engaged in fighting day and night. Therefore I am extremely tired. Please give me the benediction that I may be able to sleep peacefully for a long time. If anyone disturbs my sleep, they will be dead just by my gaze." The demigods bestowed this benediction on Mucukunda and since then he had been sleeping peacefully. Krishna contemplated like this, and understood the actual situation.

## Krishna kills Kalayavana

Krishna told the messenger, "O messenger! You go and tell your master that I challenge him to a battle. Tell your king to come forward well prepared to fight with Me." The messenger went back and delivered this message to the king. Kalayavana prepared himself nicely, and with a huge army, he went to accept the challenge given by Krishna. Meanwhile, Krishna entrusted the responsibility of looking after the inhabitants of Dvaraka to His elder brother Balabhadra while He Himself mounted His chariot and left for the battlefield. The fierce battle that took place between Krishna and Kalayavana is beyond description. At one point, Krishna suddenly began to retreat as if He were afraid of Kalayavana and his army. As Krishna ran away from the battlefield, the sinful king followed Him from behind exclaiming, "Don't run away! Stop, You coward!" He chastised Krishna with many harsh words. When he saw that Krishna had left His chariot and begun running away on foot, Kalayavana too left his own chariot and chased Krishna on foot. Krishna ran fast and suddenly entered within a cave. Kalayavana also arrived there and went inside the cave shouting, "Catch Him! Catch Him!" This cave belonged to Mucukunda, who had been taking rest there since Treta-yuga. Krishna went inside and slowly took off His own chadar and put it on the body of the sleeping Mucukunda while He Himself remained hidden. In no time, Kalayavana also entered inside the cave and saw someone was sleeping there. He thought it must be Krishna hiding Himself.

The wicked Kalayavana ridiculed Krishna, "O sinful living entity! You ran away from the battle. You are a coward. You are pretending to sleep. It is said that one should not disturb a sleeping person. But since You are feigning sleep, You must be punished. O cowherd fellow! Just now, You ran away from the fight, and so soon You have fallen asleep?" Speaking like this, Kalayavana forcefully kicked that sleeping person. As soon as he kicked King Mucukunda, Mucukunda woke up from his sleep. He was furious, wondering who had had the courage to disturb his sleep. He opened his eyes in anger and saw Kalayavana standing before him. Within moments, the wicked Kalayavana was burnt to ashes by the fire emanating from Mucukunda's eyes.

## Mucukunda offers prayers to Krishna

As the body of Kalayavana burnt to ashes, there was a tumultuous sound of "Jaya! Jaya!" everywhere. Mucukunda was very surprised, and he looked around within the cave. He was struck with wonder as he suddenly saw a beautiful blackish personality standing before him. He could immediately understand that this personality was none other than his worshipable Lord. The Lord held a conch, a disc, a club, and a lotus in His four hands. He was adorned with a garland of forest flowers. His head was decorated with peacock feathers, and He wore a necklace with Kaustubha gem. His arms were decorated with armlets, and His fingers were adorned with golden rings. He also wore a garland of parijata flowers. The Lord appeared exactly like the full moon. King Mucukunda immediately stood up with great awe and reverence and offered his respectful obeisances to the Lord with folded hands. He then began to offer prayers as follows.

"O Supreme Lord! I am the son of Mandhata. I was sleeping here for a long time by the benediction of the demigods. After fighting with the demons on behalf of the demigods, I was very tired, and I came to this cave to take rest. I was eagerly waiting for You. I knew that You would incarnate on earth in order to relieve the earth of its burden. O Lord, I can see Your effulgent transcendental form. Please tell me, how have You come here?" Krishna smiled and replied, "Brahma approached Me on the shore of the ocean of milk and offered prayers along with the other demigods. By his request I appeared in this world. I have killed many demons in Gokula, including Kamsa. Thereafter I have shifted My residence from Gokula to Dwaraka. Here also I have killed many demoniac kings. Just see, in front of you lies Kalayavana."

When Krishna explained what had happened, King Mucukunda became extremely jubilant. He was stunned upon meeting the Lord of his life face to face. He again fell down at the lotus feet of Krishna and began to shed tears of ecstatic love. He folded his hands and glorified the Lord saying, "You are Brahma, You are Rudra, You are Narayana, You are the cause of the creation, maintenance and annihilation. You are the land, the water, the air, fire. You are Indra, You are Vayu and You are the sky. You bring about the total annihilation in due course of time. One who remembers You does not die. O Lord! Be merciful to me for I am Your eternal servant."

After hearing this prayer of Mucukunda, Lord Krishna smiled with pleasure and asked him to take any benediction he desired. Mucukunda replied, "O Lord! I do not want any benediction. I have got Your darsana, and this is more than sufficient to me." Speaking these words, the king cried profusely out of love for Krishna. Krishna pacified Mucukunda with some sweet words as follows, "O king, you have fixed your mind in worshiping Me. Although I tried to divert your attention by giving you some benediction, you rejected it completely. Now do as I say. Go north to Badarikasrama where the sages Nara and Narayana live. After you give up your present body, you will attain liberation and return to the spiritual world."

Mucukunda departed for Badarikasrama as Krishna had ordered, and then Krishna returned to Dwaraka. On His way, He took away all the wealth of the Yavana king and brought it back to be stored in the city of Dwaraka. The inhabitants of the earth felt relieved by the killing of the sinful miscreant Kalayavana. Krishna lived happily in Dwaraka in the association of His relatives.

O human beings! Just hear these wonderful pastimes and you will no longer have to take birth in this material world. Think of Krishna, glorify Krishna, and worship the lotus feet of Krishna. This is the request of Gunaraja Khan.

## The marriage of Lord Balarama

O human beings, now hear about the glories of Lord Balarama with undivided attention. Listen as I explain how Balarama married Revati. There was a great king named Revata in Treta-yuga. He was so powerful that he defeated all the contemporary kings by his own strength. Killing many demons and miscreants, he helped the godly personalities living then. His glories were spread throughout the three worlds. King Revata lived a very happy life ruling his kingdom in a pious manner. He had a beautiful daughter named Revati. When Revati had grown to the proper age, the king began to think of a suitable groom for her. As beautiful as Goddess Parvati, Revati had all good qualities and auspicious symptoms. Therefore, the king could not find a suitable husband for her anywhere within the three worlds. Finally, after a long search, the king took his daughter with him and went to the assembly of Brahma in Brahmaloka.

Upon arriving in Brahmaloka, King Revata offered his obeisances to Brahma and spoke to him as follows, "O Prajapati! You are the controller of the universe. You know everything. I have been looking for a suitable husband for my daughter, but unfortunately I have not been able to find one within the three worlds. So, I have come to seek your advice. Kindly tell me who would be the ideal husband for my daughter."

When King Revata spoke these words, Brahma replied to him, "Just wait here for a moment, and I will certainly arrange some suitable groom for your daughter." As Brahma had ordered, King Revata waited patiently with his daughter. After a short while, Brahma returned and the king asked him for his news. Brahma joyfully said to him, "O king! You may return to the earth. The Supreme Personality of Godhead Hari has appeared on the earth to diminish the burden of the earth. He has taken birth at the house of Vasudeva. His elder brother is called Balarama, and He is supremely powerful. He is the most suitable husband for your qualified daughter. Your life will be successful if you can arrange the marriage of your daughter with Balarama. O king, you have already stayed here for a long time — an entire yuga has already passed by on earth. Many kings have come and gone during this time. It is almost the end of Dvapara-yuga on earth. So quickly return, get your daughter married, and go to the forest for spiritual cultivation. After you give up your present body, you will return to Kailasa."

After hearing these words of Brahma, King Revata circumambulated him and returned to the earth with his daughter. He was feeling very happy, but as he returned to the earth, he saw that all of the kings, human beings, and animals were now much shorter than before. He was surprised by this development. As soon as he entered Dwaraka people rushed to him, amazed to see him and his daughter. They were met by all the elderly residents of Dwaraka like Ugrasena, Krishna, and Balarama. King Revata was eager to see Balarama, and when he finally saw Him, he proposed that Balarama accept the hand of his daughter. Informing Balarama that this was the verdict of Brahma, he explained, "After I hand over my daughter to You, I will leave home and go to the forest."

Soon the grand marriage ceremony took place in Dwaraka. Revati was given in charity to Balarama. King Revata, his daughter Revati, and all the members of the Yadu dynasty were extremely joyful. Everyone enjoyed this function and glorified Balarama and Revati. The inhabitants of Dwaraka decorated themselves with aguru, sandalwood pulp, kumkum, and deer musk. And thus, Revati was married to the Supreme Lord Sarikarsana. The whole city of Dwaraka plunged in an ocean of bliss, with everyone dancing and singing in ecstasy. However, the bride was too tall for Balarama. So, Balarama took His plough and used it to pull Revati down by her neck. As a result, her stature became less, and now shorter, she looked extremely beautiful. Her beauty was incomparable for she was the consort of the Supreme Lord. After marrying Revati, Balarama lived happily in Dwaraka.

Thus Gunaraja Khan briefly narrates the marriage of Balarama.

## The marriage of Rukmini

O human beings! Just hear this Sri Krishna Vijaya with full concentration. The story of Krishna's marrying Rukmini is as follows. There was a pious king named Bhismaka in the city of Vidarbha. He had a beautiful daughter by the name Rukmini. When Rukmini attained the marriageable age, her father arranged for a svayamvara for her marriage. The king joyfully issued orders to his ministers and associates to arrange a grand festival, inviting all the neighboring kings and princes to participate. He had his men build many beautiful houses and quarters, all decorated with flags and golden pitchers. The whole city was adorned with colorful drawings. There were banana trees and betel plants on each and every doorstep.

The svayamvara arena was bedecked with gold, and had two rows of seats encrusted with jewels. Quarters for the invited kings were constructed with gold and silver, and there were also arrangements for lodging and boarding for their huge armies. Upon hearing the news of Rukmini's marriage, all the kings from different provinces gradually arrived in Vidarbha. The powerful king Jarasandha came there with his huge entourage. Many kings like Sisupala, Dantavakra, Kasiraja, Banasura, Bhauma, Salva, the one hundred sons of Dhrtarastra headed by Duryodhana, the five Pandava brothers, Dronacarya, and Karna arrived for Rukmini's svayamvara ceremony.

As the kings arrived in Vidarbha, King Bhismaka duly greeted them, offering sweets, grains, and drinks. They were decorated with sandalwood paste and flower garlands. When the kings were seated comfortably, King Bhismaka folded his hands and said, "My daughter is young, and I have made an arrangement for her to find a suitable groom. If you do not mind, I would like to say something. I have decided to hand over my daughter to Vasudeva's son, Krishna, who lives in Dwaraka. Krishna is very handsome, and He is in the prime of His youth. I think He is the suitable husband for my daughter."

As soon as King Bhismaka made this proposal before all the kings, his son Rukmi became curious and protested loudly, "This fellow is a cowherd man from a village. He is a spy of King Ugrasena. How dare you think of giving your daughter to Him? He lives in the company of the dog-eaters by the side of the ocean. He is a coward and runs away from the battlefield. Therefore, He is no match for my sister. Listen, everyone! I have got a suitable groom for Rukmini. He is very expert at fighting, and specifically at releasing weapons. He comes from a noble family and is full of good qualities. His name is Sisupala, the son of Damaghosa. He is a famous warrior and is better than all of us."

After Rukmi made this announcement, Jarasandha looked very happy. He thought, "This is exactly what I thought. Krishna is a cowherd by caste. Cowherd is considered a low caste in this country. Who knows about His real identity? Sometimes He acts like a cowherd and sometimes He acts like a ksatriya." Jarasandha then turned towards Bhismaka and said, "O noble king! Why do you want to marry your daughter to that fellow? In my opinion, Sisupala is the suitable husband for your daughter. Accept him as your son-in-law and give him many gifts. Find an auspicious day and get your daughter married to King Sisupala. Forget this Krishna — He is mischievous and is a number one thief! He plunders things from others. We suspect that He may even try to kidnap your daughter, but we are ready, if He tries to do that, we will kill Him." In this way, Jarasandha and others jointly requested Bhismaka to hand over his daughter to Sisupala, and he agreed to do so wholeheartedly.

## Rukmini Devi's feelings of unhappiness

Rukmini thought deeply for a long while and was extremely disappointed upon learning that she was to marry Sisupala. She almost lost her external consciousness. She breathed heavily and lamented profusely, "O Lord Hari! I am really disappointed. I have heard about Krishna since my childhood. In order to get Krishna as my husband, I have been worshiping Siva and Parvati with devotion. Krishna is the Lord of everyone, and is therefore the only suitable husband for me, yet my father thinks otherwise. He has now found someone else to be my husband. If I meditate on the lotus feet of goddess Candl with undivided attention, then I will certainly succeed in my attempt to have the lotus-eyed Krishna as my husband."

Rukmini devi thus pacified her mind to some extent. She then secretly called an old and trusted brahmana. Offering her obeisances, she said, "O respected one! Please take this message, and go to Dwaraka to give it to Krishna. Tell Him about my impending marriage to Sisupala and everything else. I have heard from people that Krishna is worshiped throughout the world. He is very attractive and His beauty enchants even Cupid. All the women of the world are bewildered by His beauty. The lotus-eyed Lord is the essence of the universe. He is non-different from Lord Narayana, and I have accepted Him as my only husband. I have surrendered my body, mind, and speech to Him. I do not know why my father wants me to marry someone else? I have tried My best to convince him, but it is of no use. O brahmana! Go quickly and tell Krishna to kidnap Me. If He asks how he can do that in the midst of all the kings, then tell Him that there is a way to do it. In my family tradition, there is a custom that the bride should visit the temple of goddess Durga on the day before her marriage. So, when I go to worship goddess Candl in the company of my friends, my Lord Damodara should then take me away by force. O brahmanal Do not waste any more time. Go and bring my Lord Krishna as soon as possible!"

## The Brahmana messenger goes to Dwaraka

Being thus ordered by Rukmini, the brahmana immediately left for Dwaraka in search of the Lord of the three worlds. There are no restrictions for brahmanas to move freely in the abode of Krishna. Therefore, the brahmana quickly crossed all barriers, ditches, and gates, and arrived at the palace to find Lord Krishna sitting on His throne. As soon as the brahmana approached the Lord, Krishna quickly stood up respectfully, making the brahmana sit down comfortably on the couch. After satisfying the brahmana in every way, Krishna enquired, "O brahmana, where do you live? What brings you here? Have you been insulted by your king or his men? Does your king treat everyone equally?"

The brahmana, who was more relaxed by now, replied "I have brought a secret message for you. Noble-hearted Bhismaka, the king of Vidarbha, has a beautiful daughter named Rukmini. She is charming like Parvati and equals Laksmi in qualities. She has chosen You as Her only husband. Her body, mind, a.id speech are fully dedicated to You. She thinks of no one but You as her husband. In every way, she is a qualified bride for You. Her father agreed to give her hand in marriage to King Sisupala at the request of his son Rukmi. O Lord! Tomorrow is the date of her marriage. Please go to Vidarbha at once, and do something about it. If You are neglectful and do not go, then she said she will give up her life while thinking about You."

When Krishna heard these words of the brahmana, He immediately decided to go to Vidarbha and save His would-be queen Rukmini. "Rukmini eternally belongs to Me. I am her only suitable husband. Who other than I can marry Her?" Krishna contemplated in this way as He prepared to leave for Vidarbha.

## Krishna goes to the city of Vidarbha

Krishna thus assured the brahmana, "I will go to Vidarbha at once and kidnap Rukmini. O crest jewel among the brahmanas\\ I give you My word that I will certainly marry Rukmini." Thereafter, Krishna ordered His servant Daruka to arrange His chariot quickly. When the charioteer brought the chariot, Krishna immediately mounted it with the brahmana and started towards the city of Vidarbha.

Meanwhile, Bhismaka began to perform the rituals performed on the day before a marriage ceremony. His family priest conducted different devotional procedures to satisfy his worshipable Lord. The king gave away many gifts and wealth in charity. He invited all the inhabitants of Vidarbha to participate in that ceremony. People came and enjoyed to their full satisfaction, dancing and singing. The whole city was joyful.

On the other side, Damaghosa, the king of Cedi, also performed the necessary rituals for the marriage of his son Sisupala. He invited many qualified brahmanas and had them conduct the ceremonies properly. Early in the morning on the day before the marriage, Damaghosa had his son take a special bath and perform different auspicious rituals for his benefit. When Rukmini saw and heard all these developments, she began to cry profusely. She fixed her mind on Krishna and lamented, "Alas! Why has the creator made such a fate for me? I belong to Krishna. How can Sisupala marry Krishna's queen? Even though I worshiped Siva and Parvati with love and devotion still I could not attain the favor of Lord Narayana. I must be very ugly; that is why the son of Nanda has not come. Pemaps He does not like me. Maybe my brahmana messenger could not reach Dwaraka in time. Maybe he is stuck somewhere on the way. Maybe Krishna hasn't got my message. Why has the Lord of my heart not come to marry me? O Hari! I am still alive. I belong to the lion and not to a jackal." While lamenting in this way, Rukmini lost her external consciousness and fell to the ground murmuring, "Let me die, let me die."

Meanwhile, as Krishna left on His chariot for Vidarbha, Baladeva started to feel somewhat worried. He immediately understood what was going to happen. He realized that Krishna had left to kidnap Rukmini from the assembly of many different kings. Thinking that Krishna was all alone, Balarama took Gada, Satyaki, and a large number of soldiers and followed Krishna to the city of Vidarbha. After some time, the two brothers Rama and Krishna met up with each other in the city of Vidarbha. A messenger immediately went and informed the king of Their arrival. Upon hearing the news, the king immediately came out of his palace and respectfully greeted Rama and Damodara. He worshiped Them with devotion and offered Them comfortable seats.

When Jarasandha, who was sitting in that assembly, saw Krishna and Balarama, he bent his head in anger and began to contemplate, "These two fellows are here again! I attacked the city of Mathura with twenty-three aksauhim divisions of soldiers but these two brothers, though They were only small boys, defeated us easily. I could not stand before Them and had to leave the battlefield. And now These two brothers are here and most probably will take away Rukmini just as a lion takes away its booty from the grip of a jackal."

After thinking in this way, Jarasandha thought it wise to speak to King Bhismaka. "O king! You are old and experienced. You are also very respectable. Therefore we tolerate all your misbehavior. We are ashamed to tell you that you have invited these ordinary cowherd boys in the assembly of great kings and warriors like us. This Krishna played some trick and killed kamsa, and foolish people glorify Him without knowing anything. He is a cowherd. He runs away from the battlefield because He is afraid to face powerful warriors. Nowadays He lives within the ocean in the company of the candalas. How dare you invite such low class fellows to this assembly? You are worshiping the servants instead of the masters. We will not stay here any longer. We will return home. If you wish, you may marry your daughter to the cowherd boy."

When Rukmini's father King Bhismaka heard this blasphemy against Krishna, he became extremely pained in his heart. He lowered his head in shame and did not utter a word. Then, the two great kings named Kratha and Kausika embraced Krishna and Balarama with great affection. They performed abhiseka to Krishna by pouring upon Him waters from different holy rivers. King Kratha held an umbrella above Krishna's head while King Kausika fanned Krishna with a camara. In this way, Krishna was officially installed as the king of Vidarbha.

## Indra performs abhiseka to Krishna

Just then, Indra, the king of heaven, and his wife Sacidevi arrived and began to perform abhiseka to Krishna with the milk from the Surabhi cow. After duly performing the ceremony, Indra enthroned Krishna as the king of kings. Sacidevi along with many celestial women worshiped and glorified Krishna with auspicious songs. In this way, after worshiping Krishna and showering flowers upon both Krishna and Balarama, Indra returned to his own abode. Being very happy, the demigods in the heavenly planets also showered flowers on Krishna and Balarama. They were struck with wonder at seeing Krishna's influence and prowess. All the assembled kings became frightened and sat with their heads bowed. O human beings! Hear Sri Krishna Vijaya attentively as Gunaraja Khan narrates Krishna's pastimes.

## Rukmini worships Durga and begs for benediction

After being enthroned as king, Krishna appeared like a lion in the midst of elephants.

In the meantime, Rukmini devi began to make preparations to worship goddess Candika. She was surrounded by female dancers, singers, wives of the brahmanas and so on. There were many musical instruments, which made pleasing sounds as Rukmini sat down on a chariot to go to the temple. In the course of her journey, she simply remembered Krishna. After riding on the chariot for a while, she got down and started walking. Once inside the temple of goddess Durga, she worshiped the goddess in the company of many chaste women by offering her various ingredients. She offered incense, ghee lamp, foodstuff, camphor, betel nuts, and many other articles. At the end of her worship, she folded her hands and prayed, "O goddess! I fall down at your lotus feet. My only wish is that I may get the lotus-eyed Krishna as my husband. Please give me this benediction that Govinda will become my husband."

After worshiping Siva and Durga with faith and various ingredients, the beautiful Rukmini remembered Sri Hari as she prepared to leave the temple. Just then she saw some auspicious signs. Her left thigh, left eye, and left arm all began to shake. Then she saw the old brahmana messenger whom she had sent to Krishna. Rukmini hurriedly went before the brahmana and said, "O brahmana! Has the Lord of my life come? Has my Lord Damodara arrived?" "Yes," the brahmana replied, "Krishna has come. The crest jewel of all the kings has arrived here. He is sitting in the assembly. Today your life has become successful for today the lotus-eyed Krishna will become your husband." As soon as Rukmini, the enchantress of the universe, heard these words, she became so happy that she did not know what to give to the brahmana to please him. Unable to find any suitable gift for the brahmana, Rukmini felt very unhappy, but within her mind she felt extremely grateful to the brahmana.

## A description of Rukmini's beauty

The daughter of the king of Vidarbha was extremely beautiful. Her hair was black, her two breasts were raised and round, and her lotus-like navel was deep. She wore a beautiful necklace, which resembled a conch. The beauty of her face defeated the beauty of the full moon in autumn. Her forehead was decorated with vermillion and her teeth resembled pearls. Her walk resembled the walk of a swan, and her two lotus hands were decorated with bangles that tinkled softly as she moved. She had a crown on the top of her head, and her curly hair was braided and draped with many necklaces studded with jewels. On her forehead were dots of sandalwood pulp and deer musk. It appeared as if the full moon had been swallowed by Rahu. Her two eyebrows certainly charmed even the mind of Cupid. She was dressed opulently and wore bangles made of conch shells. She also had a number of golden bangles. Her arms were decorated with armlets studded with precious stones, and her fingers were adorned with gold rings. Her two palms defeated the beauty of the lotus flower, and her nails were shining like the moonstone. Her two eyes were very restless. She decorated her ears with beautiful earrings, and from her nose dangled a nose ring studded with pearls. Her lips looked like the red bimba fruit. Her throat appeared as beautiful as a conch.

Rukmini was decorated with different necklaces made of diamonds, jewels, pearls, and coral. The hairs on her body were black like a bumblebee's. She was adorned with a garland of forest flowers on which many honey bees hovered around. She had three lines on her throat that made her exquisitely beautiful. Looking like a golden idol, it appeared as if the lightning itself had come down in the form of Rukmini. Her waist was thin just like that of a lion. The beauty of her entire body was beyond comparison. She rested her left hand on the shoulder of her companion. Her thighs and legs were thin like trees and she wore anklets and tinkling bells on her feet. As she walked slowly like a maddened elephant, she enchanted the minds of the whole universe as she is the incarnation of Laksmi Herself. She was only sixteen years old at that time. When Krishna saw Rukmini, He was struck with wonder. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Krishna's pastimes.

## The kidnapping of Rukmini

As Rukmini slowly went to the svayamvara arena, she held a beautiful garland of jasmine flowers. Anyone who saw her became faint. Being struck by her beauty, they were transfixed by the various parts of her body that happened to catch their glances. Just as all the assembled kings stood spellbound and overwhelmed by Rukmini's beauty, Krishna suddenly arrived in His chariot and snatched Rukmini away from the vision of everyone. Placing her on His left side, Krishna drove His chariot away roaring like a lion. Lord Govinda went first with Rukmini, and then Lord Balarama followed with a huge army.

## The fighting of the kings

When the assembled kings saw Krishna kidnap Rukmini, they became very angry, and chased after Him brandishing various weapons. First amongst them were Rukmi and Sisupala followed by Jarasandha with his vast army. They exclaimed, "You coward! Where are you going? You are like a deer in the midst of the lions. Don't run away. If You have guts, then stand and fight." Krishna and Balarama accepted their challenge and halted Their journey. Balarama, along with some of the army, fought vigorously with the kings. In great anger, shame, and frustration, Sisupala aimed his arrow towards Krishna and Balarama.

But before he could shoot his arrow, Balai cut his bow into three pieces by shooting three arrows from His own bow. When Sisupala again took up another bow, Balarama broke it into pieces once more. Balarama showered a rain of arrows upon the king. Unable to match Krishna and Balarama's prowess and fighting skills, Jarasandha addressed his fellow warriors as follows, "O kings! Quit fighting. There is no point fighting with these useless fellows. If we are defeated, we will be greatly ashamed. They have a vast army, and Garuda is also with Them, so it is very difficult for us to defeat Them now. Moreover, the time is not quite ripe for us to kill the two brothers. When the right time comes, we will certainly destroy Them."

Hearing Jarasandha speak like this, all the dejected kings began to retreat. Rukmi however refused to quit. He took courage and attacked Krishna. He made a promise in front of all the kings that he would not return home without killing Krishna. He mounted his chariot and went past Balarama to catch Krishna, roaring, "Why are You taking my sister and running away? You have stolen a girl from the society of gentlemen. You must be punished for this." As Rukmi proudly marched forward to fight with Krishna, Rukmini's heart began to tremble in fear. The four-armed Krishna then held Rukmini with two hands and with the other two He took up His bow and arrow. Krishna swiftly shot three arrows and broke Rukmi's bow into pieces.

Krishna shot three more arrows and killed Rukmi's charioteer. He then killed the four horses with eight arrows. Rukmi was desperate. He got down off his chariot, took up his bow and, in one stroke, shot ten arrows at Krishna. Four of these arrows struck Krishna on His chest, another four arrows killed Krishna's four horses, and the last two arrows destroyed Krishna's bow. Krishna was furious with Rukmi. As Rukmi again tried to attack Krishna with his bow and arrow, Krishna shot four fiery arrows that smashed Rukmi's bow into hundreds of pieces. Krishna quickly ran towards Rukmi, grabbed his hands, tied him with a rope, and pulled him up onto His own chariot. When Rukmini saw the pathetic condition of her brother, she passionately begged Krishna, "O Madhusudana! Please spare him. You are the essence of the whole world, yet he dared to fight with you. I beg a pardon on his behalf." Krishna listened to Rukmini's plea and simply smiled. He let Rukmi go, but only after shaving off his hair and beard. Rukmini wept profusely when she saw her brother's strange, unksatriya-Mke appearance. Balarama scolded Krishna mildly, for he knew such an insult was graver than death. He also pacified Rukmini with philosophical words. Krishna and Balarama then returned to Dwaraka after successfully kidnapping Rukmini. Meanwhile, remembering his promise, Rukmi did not return to Vidarbha, but rather lived in a makeshift kingdom called Bhojakata as an enemy of Krishna for the rest of his life.

## The marriage of Rukmini

After teaching Rukmi a lesson, Krishna returned to Dwaraka with Rukmini. When the inhabitants of Dwaraka heard the details of this wonderful pastime of Krishna, they floated in an ocean of bliss.

There was a festive mood among the citizens of Dwaraka. The entire city was decorated with flags, pitchers filled with water, banana trees, and betel plants. People got together and began to sing and dance in joy. Sri Hari duly married Rukmini with great pomp. In due course of time, King Bhismaka came to Dwaraka with his priest. He presented unlimited gifts to both the bride and the groom. He happily handed over the hand of his daughter to Krishna and then returned to his capital, Vidarbha. O human beings! Forget everything and just hear these wonderful topics of Krishna. In this way, Krishna brought Laksmi, the goddess of fortune, to Dwaraka.

## King Satrajit performs austerity and obtains the Stamantaka jewel

O readers! Hear with attention about the pastimes of Sri Krishna. Now hear about the topics of how Krishna married Satyabhama. King Satrajit was a friend of Krishna's. Once upon a time, he went alone to the seashore and worshiped the sun god for twelve years. He observed strict vows and fasted. He satisfied the sun god by his severe penances, so much so that one day the sun god personally appeared before him and said, "O king! I am pleased with you. Now ask me for any benediction you wish. I will grant you anything you want." When the sun god spoke like this, the king offered his obeisances, and turning to him with folded hands, he said,"O lord! If you are really pleased with me then give me that necklace from your neck." The sun god then without any hesitation gave Satrajit the brightly shining Syamantaka jewel he was wearing.

Filled with joy, the king returned to Dwaraka. Everyone who saw him became amazed to see his effulgence. Because of the jewel, the king was as bright as the sun. Soon people went to Krishna and inquired from Him, "O cause of the universe! I think the sun god has personally come to see You. We are unable to look at his effulgent form. Kindly speak to him quickly and send him off." At that time Krishna was playing chess with Rukmim. He paused for a moment and then replied, "Do not worry; it is only King Satrajit. He has been blessed by the sun god with a shining jewel. Let him come and live peacefully in the city of Dwaraka."

King Satrajit returned home, placed the Syamantaka jewel on a sanctified altar, and began to worship it properly. The most wonderful feature of this jewel was that it produced eight pitchers of gold every day. As a result of this feat, there was no disease, lamentation, hunger, thirst, immature death, distress, or unhappiness. One day, Krishna sent Uddhava to King Satrajit, after telling him to ask the king to give the Syamantaka jewel to Krishna. Unfortunately, the king was very miserly and wicked due to having been bewildered by the Lord's illusory energy. Despite his conditioning, with due respect, the king greeted Uddhava, and said with folded hands, "O Uddhava! I tell you the truth. I did not know before that Krishna would ask for the jewel. I have a younger brother named Prasenjlt. Out of affection for him, I have put that jewel on his neck. You explain this to Krishna nicely." Uddhava returned home and informed Krishna about the whole thing. Krishna simply smiled and went about His own business.

## The stealing of the jewel

Satrajit's brother, Prasena, was very happy and proud to possess the jewel. He regularly went to the forest to hunt wearing the jewel on his neck. One day as he was hunting deer in the forest, a lion suddenly appeared before him, killed him instantly, and took away the jewel, carrying it off to his den. By the arrangement of providence, however, the powerful gorilla king Jambavan saw the lion run off with the jewel. He chased after the lion, fought with him, and swiftly killed him. Jambavan took away the jewel from the dead lion and gave it to his son to play with, at his house in Patalaloka. Meanwhile, King Satrajit had begun to search for his missing brother. Even though he looked everywhere, he could not find a trace of him. He concluded that his brother must have been killed. The king cried profusely though his fellow citizens tried to console him. The king concluded that Krishna must have killed his brother and taken possession of the Syamantaka jewel.

## Krishna is falsely accused of stealing the jewel

The people of Dwaraka spread a rumor that Krishna had stolen the jewel. They said, "Krishna had previously wanted this jewel, but Satrajit did not give it to Him; rather he gave it to his brother, Prasena. That is why Krishna must have killed Prasena and took away the jewel." Soon this rumor reached Krishna. He contemplated why He had been implicated in this false accusation. Finally, after looking at the moon, He concluded that since it was the fourth day of the month of Bhadra, the effects of that particular moment must have been the cause of the false accusation. Hereafter, Krishna called all His friends and relatives and said to them, "This Prasena went to the forest one day while wearing the jewel on his neck. Someone killed him in the forest, and now people are accusing Me of this. I have to clear this misconception from the minds of the people. Therefore I will set out to find the truth."

Aong with some friends, Krishna mounted His chariot, and rode off towards the forest. Krishna found and followed the footprints of the horse which was used by Prasena. After following the footprints for some distance, Krishna saw markings in the ground which led Him to understand that at one point a lion had killed Prasena and most likely had made off with the jewel. Krishna then began to follow the footprints of the lion. Soon the Lord found out that the lion had been killed by a gigantic bear who had taken away the jewel and gone to the lower planets. He also found a tunnel through which the bear had made his escape. Krishna then addressed His friends, "My dear friends, I have to remove the false accusation from My head. Therefore, I will descend into Patalaloka through this tunnel. You wait for Me here in the entrance of the tunnel for twelve days. If I don't return in twelve days, you may return to Dwaraka and know for certain that I am no more. You may perform my sraddha ceremony according to the rules and regulations of the scriptures. Take care of Rukmini devi in My absence. Pay My obeisances to Vasudeva and Devaki. Tell them that I will serve them again if I come back."

After speaking these heart-rending words, Krishna entered the tunnel. After walking a while, He saw a beautiful palace with gates, courtyards, and gardens. As Krishna entered that palace, He saw a woman sitting there with a child on her lap. She was speaking sweetly to her child thus, "O dear child! Do not cry. Take this Syamantaka jewel and play with it." As soon as Krishna heard the name of the jewel, He quickly went and snatched away the jewel from her. The woman immediately ran and informed King Jambavan, "Listen, O king of the gorillas. A human being has sneaked into our palace. He beat me up and took away the jewel. I saw Him about to leave the palace with the jewel in His hand."

## Krishna fights with Jambavan

The moment Jambavan heard the words of the babysitter, he immediately started chasing Krishna. As he ran, he angrily exclaimed, "O mischievous fellow! You are stealing the jewel from my son. You do not know me. Today will be the last day of Your life. I am a man-eater. Today I will have a feast with Your body." When Krishna heard these words, He laughed. Krishna stopped running, turned, and challenged Jambavan to a fight. A fierce battle then raged between the two for a long time. Neither one of them could defeat his opponent. As the fight went on nonstop, Krishna's friends who were waiting for twelve days at the entrance of the tunnel concluded that Krishna must have died at the hands of someone. They wept profusely and returned to Dwaraka completely dejected.

## Lamentation by Devaki and Rukmini

The news of Krishna's death spread like a forest-fire throughout Dwaraka. Ugrasena called Vasudeva and Devaki and said, "Krishna is no more. He went into a tunnel but n;ver returned. It has been fifteen days since He entered the cave. I think He must have left His body inside the cave. Before He went in, He gave me a few instructions. He said, 'If I don't return by the twelfth day, all of you go back home and perform My last rites. Take care of Rukmini and give solace to My parents. Make sure that all the inhabitants of Dwaraka are happy.'"

As soon as Devaki heard these inauspicious words, she began to beat her chest. She rolled on the ground and wept continuously. Devaki embraced Rukmini and they both began to cry loudly while calling out the name of Hari. They lamented, "From today onwards the whole city of Dwaraka will be empty. O daughter-in-law! Why has the creator written so many obstacles in your fate? He did not die in Gokula despite being attacked by many demons. He survived the wrath of the sinful Kamsa. Jarasandha tried to kill Him eighteen times but failed each time. During your marriage, He fought with many powerful kings. At that time also He was saved by the goddess Bhavani. This sinful king Satrajit had polluted His mind; that is why He decided to enter the tunnel's depths only to meet death. Arrange a funeral pyre and make a blazing fire. I will certainly give up my life by entering into the fire." In this way Devaki cried and lamented profusely.

The condition of Rukmini was pathetic. She was unable to control herself. She cried and lamented saying, "O Lord Hari! The whole city of Dwaraka is empty today. O Madhusudana! I have been thinking of You since my childhood. Only out of good fortune did I find Lord Narayana to be my husband. O Lord of my life! I have now lost my husband in an untimely fashion. I will burn my beauty and youthfulness into ashes and go to Rasatala." As Rukmini cried relentlessly, suddenly some auspicious symptoms appeared on her body. Her left thigh began to shiver all of a sudden. As soon as this happened, Rukmini checked her crying and said with firm conviction, "My lotus-eyed husband is alive. He has not died. The vermillion on my head and forehead is still intact. My bangles, earrings, bracelets, and armlets are shining brightly like a blazing fire. This proves that my husband is still alive and healthy. The conch bangles on my wrists shine even more brightly which proves that wherever He is, He is safe." Rukmini then fixed her mind on goddess Candika the wife of Siva. Devaki too worshiped the goddess by placing a golden pitcher filled with water before her. She prayed, "O goddess! You are the cause of the creation, maintenance and destruction of the material world. You take away everyone's distress and remove everyone's danger. I beg you to return my son Govinda. Please bless me so that I can surpass the ocean of lamentation."

In this way, Devaki and Rukmini offered worship and prayers to goddess Durga. In the meantime, Ugrasena approached Vasudeva and pacified him with sweet words. They then went to the seashore and according to the worldly custom performed the last rites. According to the procedure prescribed for the ksatriyas, they offered ten oblations. They performed the sraddha ceremony on the thirteenth day. Meanwhile Krishna and Jambavan continued their wrestling for seventeen days without any food or drink, but when Krishna's relatives offered oblations in His sraddha ceremony in Dwaraka, Krishna suddenly regained His lost strength in Patalaloka. With extra vigor, Krishna wrestled with Jambavan nonstop for three more days and then finally defeated him. As the Lord sat down on the chest of Jambavan, Jambavan suddenly recognized his worshipable Lord Ramacandra whom he had served previously. Jambavan saw Krishna in the form of Rama and his illusion was dispelled. He understood that he was fighting with none other than Lord Hari. Jambavan then folded his hands and offered prayers to his beloved Lord, "O my Lord! You are the one who built a bridge over the ocean and killed Ravana in Lanka. At that time, I helped You in fighting Your enemies. At that time, You gave me a benediction that I would be unconquerable. Since then I have been residing in Patalaloka and, by Your mercy, nobody has been able to cause any harm to me. But O Lord, why did You deceive me by giving me such a benediction? What have I done?" Krishna simply smiled at these words of Jambavan. He got up from the chest of Jambavan and stood there in front of him with a smiling face.

## Krishna marries Jambavati and obtains the jewel

Jambavan the king of the apes stood up with folded hands and offered prayers to Krishna with undivided attention, saying, "O Lord! You are all in all within the whole material creation. O lotus-eyed Supreme Lord! Creation, maintenance and annihilation take place by Your order. O Lord! Please pacify Your anger and come to my house. Please deliver me by giving me the dust from Your lotus feet." Jambavan then brought Krishna to his palace and gave Him a jewel-studded throne to sit on. He faithfully worshiped Krishna by offering Him padya, arghya, incense, a ghee lamp, deer musk, and sandalwood paste. Jambavan had a beautiful daughter named Jambavati who was equal to Parvati in beauty and quality. He then handed over this beautiful daughter to Krishna as His queen. He also gave Him many gifts and the most coveted Syamantaka jewel. In this way, Krishna retrieved the jewel and got Jambavati as a bonus. He mounted on the chariot of Jambavan and came out of the tunnel. Soon Krishna and Jambavati arrived near the city of Dwaraka. With great joy, Krishna blew His Pancajanya conch loudly. All the inhabitants of Dwaraka became jubilant on hearing this sound. They came rushing out to meet Krishna and forgot all their lamentation. Krishna and Jambavati went home, looking as beautiful as Indra and Saci together. Mother Devaki joyfully greeted her new daughter-in-law and took her inside the palace.

O human beings! Just hear these wonderful topics of Krishna narrated in this book Sri Krishna Vijaya. Gunaraja Khan assures that by hearing these topics one will become free from the fear of death.

## Krishna returns the jewel to Satrajit and marries Satyabhama

After Krishna brought back the Syamantaka jewel, He called Satrajit in the royal assembly and returned the jewel to him, thus relieving Himself of the false accusation. Krishna narrated the whole incident of how He had retrieved the jewel. All the assembled personalities condemned Satrajit for falsely accusing Krishna. The king hung his head in shame and slowly left the assembly. Satrajit was very upset and began to think, "I have made a terrible blunder. How will Lord Hari ever be pleased with me again? The Lord is the life and soul of the universe. He is the wealth of all. What kind of wealth could I possibly give Him to make Him happy with me once more?" He considered his situation for a moment, and then suddenly realized that he had indeed a unique wealth in the form of his daughter. "Satyabhama is exquisitely beautiful, and her beauty is known throughout the whole world. She enchants the minds of everyone. I will offer Satyabhama to Krishna and thus make Him happy."

So Satrajit along with his associates happily went to meet Govinda and with folded hands he said, "O Hari! I know You sent Uddhava to get the jewel from me, but I disobeyed Your order by giving the jewel to my brother Prasenjit. As a result of this action, I committed an offense at Your feet. It is also a fact that if an unqualified person keeps that jewel, he will die. This is exactly what happened with Prasena. He was killed by a lion in the forest. O Lord, You have incarnated to destroy all the miscreants. I fall down at Your feet. Please forgive me." When Satrajit begged pardon in this way, Krishna smiled and said to him, "Do not feel sad. I forgive you. Besides, I know you are My well-wisher. Now you may go back home." The king again folded his hands and said, "O Lord of the universe! If You are really pleased with me, then I have a request. I have a beautiful daughter, and I beg You to please marry Her. O husband of Laksmi! My daughter is very talented, and I think You are the most suitable husband for her."

On hearing the king's words, Krishna smiled and replied with pleasure, "O respected king, you belong to a noble family and your behavior is exemplary. Therefore I will certainly marry your daughter." The king then fixed an auspicious day for the marriage ceremony of his daughter. The news spread throughout Dwaraka and people from each and every home became jubilant. Everyone plunged into a pool of happiness. There were flags, banana trees, pitchers, and other auspicious articles everywhere in the city. Professional reciters, singers, and players began to perform their respective duties. The dancers danced in ecstasy. People celebrated this festival with great pomp. All the pious kings of the neighboring countries came to watch Krishna's marriage with Satyabhama.

On the day before the marriage, the priest conducted the required rituals. On the auspicious day of the marriage ceremony, Krishna went to the house of Satrajit to marry his daughter Satyabhama. The bride was naturally beautiful. She was charming, and her body was decorated with various ornaments. She could easily defeat the beauty of Cupid. There was no substitute for Satyabhama's beauty within the three worlds. As she was beautiful, so too was her husband attractive. At the most auspicious moment, sapphire blue Krishna and golden Satyabhama married each other. King Satrajit gave many gifts in charity. He also donated many elephants, horses, chariots, and the Syamantaka jewel to Krishna. He requested his son-in-law to take care of Satyabhama nicely. Thereafter, Krishna took leave of the king and returned to His own palace in the company of His newly married queen, Satyabhama.

## Krishna returns the jewel to Satrajit

After Krishna returned home with Satyabhama and the jewel, He called Vasudeva, Devaki, and Baladeva together. He said, "This jewel is not fit for all of you. As you know, Prasena lost his life because he was not qualified to handle the jewel. Therefore I think it better that we return this jewel to King Satrajit." When they heard this proposal, they immediately agreed. Krishna then invited Satrajit to His palace, and handed over the jewel to him. He said, "O king, take this jewel and keep it with you. This is the opinion of everyone. Let the jewel stay in your house. You should worship the jewel daily and live happily. This will ensure the well-being of the inhabitants of Dwaraka." King Satrajit did not hesitate a second about taking the jewel. He happily grabbed the opportunity and returned home with the jewel. In this way, Krishna married Satyabhama and lived happily in Dwaraka.

## Krishna departs for Hastinapura after hearing bad news about the Pandavas

Krishna was living happily in Dwaraka when suddenly He received word that the five Pandava brothers had died. Someone informed Krishna, "O cause of the universe! O Krishna! The five Pandavas along with their mother have been burnt to ashes by Duryodhana. The sinful King Duryodhana sneakily drew them into a house of lac and, once they had settled in there, he promptly set the house on fire when they were fast asleep at night." As soon as Krishna heard this shocking news, He immediately realized that the sons of Pandu were very much alive. He also understood that the five brothers and their mother had escaped the burning house of lac somehow and were now healthy and safe. In fact, He knew that at that moment, they were living in the forest. Krishna then departed towards Hastinapura in search of the Pandavas. After arriving in Hastinapura, Krishna saw Bhisma, Drona, Krpa, Dhrtarastra, Duryodhana, Karna, Salya, Vidura, and Satyavati living in their respective houses. They were afflicted with lamentation for the Pandavas. Krishna stayed there for a few days and pacified everyone with sweet words.

## The death of Satrajit

Meanwhile back in Dwaraka, one day Krtavarma, Satadhanva, and Akrura had a discussion together. They concluded that King Satrajit was violating the principles of religion, and therefore, he had to be killed as soon as possible. They considered how he had promised to marry his daughter to Satadhanva. He did not keep his promise; instead, he gave his daughter to Krishna. The Syamantaka jewel was also in his possession at that time so, the three thought they should quickly kill him and take away the jewel before Krishna returns to Dwaraka.

After deciding in this way, Satadhanva disguised himself as a thief and entered the confines of the palace of Satrajit in the dead of night. The king was fast asleep on the couch. Satadhanva slowly walked up to the king, chopped off his head, and took away the jewel. Suddenly, a loud cry filled the entire palace. The guards screamed, "Some thief killed our king and stole the jewel." The whole palace was instantly plunged deep into an ocean of grief. Satyabhama cried pathetically and rolled on the ground. All the relatives and well-wishers of the king also cried profusely. After a while, Satyabhama controlled her grief and decided to inform Krishna about this unlawful act. She placed the dead body of her father in a pond of oil and immediately left for Hastinapura on a chariot. Upon arriving at Hastinapura, she informed Krishna in a choked voice.

"O Lord of the universe! You are the controller of the whole world; yet in Your presence, my father has been killed. My father was sleeping on the couch and some thief killed him while he was asleep and took away the Syamantaka jewel." When Krishna heard this, He was shocked. He did not waste any more time but returned to Dwaraka immediately with Satyabhama. He employed spies to find out who had killed Satrajit. It is very difficult to hide a sinful act, especially if it is committed through cheating. Very soon the spy informed Krishna that it was the act of Satadhanva. Krishna then spoke with Baladeva and Uddhava and made a resolution to immediately eliminate Satadhanva for this heinous crime.

## Satadhanva runs away

When Satadhanva understood about Krishna's plan to kill him, he at once called Krtavarma and Akrura to his side and said, "I killed Satrajit on the advice of you two. Now Krishna is after me. Tell me how can I defeat Krishna. If you two help me then I can surely conquer Krishna." On hearing Satadhanva speaking like this, Akrura called out loudly and said, "Don't speak like that to me anymore, you fool! Kamsa was the emperor of the whole world, but when Krishna was only a child. He killed him effortlessly. This Krishna defeated Jarasandha in battle eighteen times. He disfigured Rukmi only recently. Everyone knows how Krishna stayed Kalayavana. He is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Hari, otherwise how could He lift the Govardhana Hill at the mere age of seven? Therefore, who can conquer Him in battle, He who is unconquerable? O king! Run away with your life and never even think of fighting with Krishna." King Satadhanva somewhat became pacified and trusted the words of Akrura. He said, "Akrura, you are a pious person and I have full faith in you. Keep this Syamantaka jewel with you and let me go peacefully to the forest." After speaking these words, King Satadhanva handed over the jewel to Akrura and left for the forest riding on the back of his horse.

## The killing of Satadhanva

Recognizing the threat of an impending attack by Krishna, Satadhanva wasted no time. He left his wife and children behind and quit his home out of fear. Krishna and Balarama together surrounded the house of Satadhanva, but it was too late as he had already left. They traced his footprints and soon found him in the forest. When the king saw Krishna, he got off his horse and ran away into the trees on foot. Krishna turned to Balarama and said, "Let Me get down from the chariot and chase him in the forest on foot. He is escaping on foot, so it is the duty of a ksatriya to chase another warrior in the same manner." Krishna ran after the king shouting, "Catch him, catch him." Satadhanva became so tired from running in fear that he suddenly dropped to the ground and gave up his life. Krishna then cut the body of Satadhanva into pieces in search of the jewel. But to His surprise, Krishna could not find any jewel. Krishna returned to Balarama and said, "There is no jewel with Satadhanva. On the contrary, he has been killed unnecessarily."

Baladeva displayed a little anger at this, and He spoke to Krishna in a grave voice. "Why do You deceive Me for the sake of a woman? I am not interested in the jewel at all. You give it to Your beloved wife. Forget about the jewel now, and let Us go home. I have to go to Mithila to meet the sages." After advising Krishna in this way, Balarama returned home and then set out for the city of Mithila, where He taught Duryodhana how to fight with a club.

Meanwhile, Krishna felt embarrassed that He had participated in the killing of Satadhanva. He slowly returned to His palace. He went before Satyabhama and said to her with folded hands. "O dear devl! I killed Satadhanva in the forest. Although I cut his body into pieces, I could not find the jewel. Please believe Me." Satyabhama began to cry at this and replied in an angry mood," I know Your intentions. You want to give the jewel to Rukmini. All right, be happy with her. I am going to my father's house." Krishna felt very sad about the whole incident. He was again falsely accused. He began to think about what had gone wrong. He returned to His own quarters completely dejected, thinking about the jewel and nothing else.

## The city of Dwaraka faces famine due to the absence of Akrura.

Meanwhile Akrura decided to leave Dwaraka and go to the city of Bhojaraja. In due course of time, Akrura left Dwaraka. The moment he left the city, inauspicious signs appeared everywhere. There was no rainfall in the city for twelve years. There was a scarcity of food all over Dwaraka, and people were afflicted with various diseases. There was lamentation, disappointment, and worry in the human society. The inhabitants of Dwaraka became extremely worried. The elderly people gathered together and discussed among themselves various causes of their strife. Finally, they came to a conclusion, "This present condition is due to the absence of Akrura, the son of Svaphalka, and Gandini. Ever since he left Dwaraka, we have been facing various difficulties. We also know that when Svaphalka was still inside his mother, he did not come out of the womb for twelve years. His father performed many sacrifices and gave charity. He gave away one golden deer daily to the brahmanas; only then after many years of such donations was he born. At about the same time, there appeared a beautiful daughter at the house of Kasiraja. Immediately after her birth, in this place as well there was no rainfall. All the people of Kasi advised the king to arrange for his daughter to marry Svaphalka and thus bring an end to the drought.

## Akrura is invited to Dwaraka

In this way, on the advice of his subjects, Kasiraja gave his daughter in marriage to Svaphalka. Immediately there were torrents of rain in the kingdom, and everyone felt jubilation. The fanners harvested a bumper crop and poverty gradually disappeared." Krishna thought, "Akrura is the son of such an exalted personality. We must bring him back to improve our luck." Many elderly people were thus sent to bring Akrura back. Indeed, as soon as Akrura arrived in the city of Dwaraka, Indra began to shower heavy rains and people heaved a sigh of relief. They felt satisfied and thanked Akrura. Krishna however understood that it was the Syamantaka jewel in Akrura's possession that had made all the difference.

A few days passed by joyfully in this manner, and then one day, Krishna invited Akrura for lunch. When Akrura went to Krishna's house, he was fed varieties of foodstuffs and sweets. After lunch, Krishna caught hold of Akrura's hand and gently said to him, "Tell Me the truth. I think the jewel of Satrajit is in your house. I have a feeling that Satadhanva gave it to you." To this, Akrura replied with a smile, "Yes, Satadhanva gave it to me before his death. I have it in my residence. If You desire, I can bring it to You." Krishna then requested Akrura to bring Balarama to Dwaraka from Mithila. Following His words, Akrura went to Mithila and met Baladeva. He requested Balarama, "O Haladhara! Please return to Dwaraka. Krishna has invited all the great personalities for a grand festival. He wishes to feed everyone sumptuously." Upon hearing Akrura's request, Baladeva returned to Dwaraka.

On a fixed day, all the important people gathered at the palace of Krishna. Krishna sat down along with Rukmini, Satyabhama, and Jambavati. Krishna then folded His hands and offered His obeisances to Akrura who was seated next to Him. He said, "I think you have Satrajit's jewel in your house. King Satadhanva left it to you. Please explain to everyone assembled here how you received the jewel so that the doubts in their minds will disappear." Akrura then folded his hands and narrated the whole incident of how he had received the jewel from Satadhanva and so on. He then brought out the jewel to show to everyone. At this, Balarama, became especially embarrassed and did not say a word to anyone.

## Krishna explains the cause of the false accusation

Krishna tried to solace Baladeva by saying, "Do not feel embarrassed. I have been falsely implicated because of this jewel. I will tell You why this happened to Me. Once I looked at the moon on the fourth day in the month of Bhadra, hence I had to face such an awkward situation. Therefore, I advise all of you not to look at the moon on the fourth day of the month of Bhadra. You must be careful on that night. If you see the moon on that day by mistake, then remember this pastime of Mine, and you will be relieved of the inauspicious effects." Krishna then picked up the jewel in His hand and said to Baladeva, "O brother, You are always intoxicated with pride, thus this jewel does not befit You. If Satyabhama keeps it, then she will forget Me. Therefore I think Akrura is the best person to keep it." Krishna then turned to Akrura and told him, "My dear Akrura, you keep this Syamantaka jewel in your house. Worship it daily and then, by the grace of this jewel, all the people of Dwaraka will live happily."
