O readers! This story of the Syamantaka jewel is wonderful. Anyone who hears it with faith attains the abode of Vaikuntha. Such person achieves happiness in this life, and a higher position in his next life. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the topics of Satyabhama and Jambavati's marriage ceremony.

## The marriage of Kalindi

O human beings! Hear with full attention as I narrate the topics of Sri Krishna Vijaya. Now hear the topic of how Krishna married Kalindi. Krishna was residing in Dwaraka happily in the company of Rukmini, Satyabhama, and Jambavati. Once after properly destroying the enemies, Krishna was peacefully relaxing on His couch. Suddenly, He remembered the Pandavas and how they had faced many difficulties in the forest, how they had killed Hidimba, Baka, and become victorious in the svayamvara of Draupadi. He thought of how they had married the daughter of King Draupada and how Duryodhana had invited the Pandava brothers to the palace and tried to solace Yudhisthira. While thinking like this, Krishna became anxious to see the Pandavas. So, in an auspicious moment, He set out on His chariot with Daruka towards Hastinapura.

In due course of time, Krishna arrived in Hastinapura and met all His friends and relatives. He first met Bhisma and Dhrtarastra and offered them obeisances. He then offered His obeisances to Dronacarya, Krpacarya, Satyavati, Kunti, and Yudhisthira. When Krishna met with Arjuna, He gave him a warm embrace. He then showered blessings on Nakula and Sahadeva. He bowed down to Bhimasena as well. After duly showing respects to everyone, Krishna sat down on a throne. All the inhabitants of Hastinapura became joyful in Krishna's presence, and they offered Him various palatable foodstuffs to eat. In this way, Krishna spent a few days in a joyous mood.

One day, Krishna and Arjuna set out on a chariot for a pleasure ride on the banks of Yamuna. Riding along, they came upon a young girl performing severe penance on the waters of Yamuna. She was beautiful and had a slim figure. She appeared like the incarnation of Laksmi. She was fasting and observing severe vows while standing in the water. After looking at the charming lady, Krishna said to Arjuna, "O friend, look, at that beautiful girl. See how she is practicing austerity without any food or water while looking upwards. She is an innocent young woman. She must be undergoing penance to obtain a qualified husband. Let us stop here for a while. Let Me wait here while you go and find out why she is doing such a serious penance."

On the request of Krishna, Arjuna got down from the chariot and approached the woman. Arjuna respectfully asked, "Who are you? Why are you performing such a severe penance? I don't see any inauspicious signs on your body. You are as beautiful as a Vidyadhari. Do not deceive me; tell me the truth." On hearing Arjuna's words, the woman opened her eyes and broke her silence. She folded her hands and spoke in a soft voice. "I am the daughter of the sun god," she said. "My name is Kalindi. I have been practicing severe penance on the order of my father. As I grew up, my father told me to go to Hastinapura, stand in the waters of Yamuna, and perform penance while looking at the sky. He also said that Lord Narayana will incarnate in this world to diminish the burden of the earth by killing the demons, and He will be a suitable husband for me. Ever since then, I have been practicing this penance."

Thereafter, Arjuna returned to Krishna and explained to Him everything in a happy mood, "O friend! There is no woman in the three worlds more beautiful and talented than the daughter of Surya. She is performing penance to have You as her husband. Do not waste any time. Go and get that most charming woman of the three worlds." Then Krishna and Arjuna happily moved their chariot towards Kalindi and picked her up on the chariot. After returning to the palace, Krishna narrated this incident to King Yudhisthira, who became very happy and quickly made all the arrangements for Krishna's marriage with Kalindi. Soon after, a grand festival to mark this auspicious ceremony took place. The entire Hastinapura was plunged into an ocean of bliss as Govinda married the daughter of the sun god. After the marriage, Krishna continued to stay in Hastinapura for some more days with His dear friends and lovely new consort.

## The burning of the KhSndava forest

One day while He was still there, Vayu and Agni came to meet Krishna at Hastinapura. They folded their hands and prayed, "O Govinda! We are suffering from indigestion due to consuming an excessive amount of ghee in the sacrificial fire. We have to consume some flesh to get rid of the illness. There are many wild animals in the Khandava forest. If You permit us, we will burn the forest and fulfill our desire. But there is one problem. We know that as soon as we try to burn the forest, Indra will send torrents of rain out of anger. If You can somehow check the rainfall by spreading a network of arrows in the sky then we will be able to accomplish our mission."

Krishna listened to their request and agreed to help, so He immediately departed with Arjuna for the Khandava forest. Arjuna took his bow and arrows and happily accompanied Krishna. Soon they arrived at the forest and watched as Vayu and Agni set it on fire. A blazing forest fire erupted, and in no time began to burn the entire forest into ashes. As predicted, this made Indra furious, and he in turn began to immediately shower heavy rains on the Khandava forest. On the order of Krishna, Arjuna spread a network of arrows that checked the clouds from raining. In this way, after fulfilling the wish of Vayu and Agni, Krishna and Arjuna returned to Hastinapura. Krishna and Kalindi spent a few more happy days in Hastinapura, and then returned to Dwaraka.

## The marriage of Mitra Vinda, Bhadra and Nagnaj it i

Krishna enjoyed kingly comforts in Dwaraka in the company of Rukmini, Satyabhama, Jambavati, and Kalindi. One day, Krishna heard the news of the svayamvara ceremony of Mitravinda. Mitravinda was the daughter of the king of Avanti. She was very beautiful and appeared just like a celestial lady. Her father had made a resolution that his daughter should be married to the son of Vasudeva, but her two brothers Vinda and Aravinda protested with tooth and nail. They argued with their father and said, "O father, why are you speaking like a madman? You think this son of a cowherd is a suitable husband for our sister? It is better that we arrange a svayamvara for her, and let her choose her own husband." The king had no option but to agree to that proposal. Somehow the news of the Mitravinda's svayamvara reached Damodara. Krishna immediately mounted His chariot and went to the city of Avantipura. Krishna then forcibly took Mitravinda away in the midst of many powerful kings. A fierce battle took place, and as predicted, Krishna easily defeated all the kings. Thereafter, Krishna proudly returned to Dwaraka with His new queen, Mitravinda. All the inhabitants of Dwaraka became jubilant. On an auspicious day and in an auspicious moment, Krishna formally married Mitravinda with great pomp. After marrying her, Krishna and His new bride lived together happily in Dwaraka.

## The marriage of Bhadra

Time passed by as the inhabitants of Dwaraka spent their time in joyful and festive moods. There was a king named Dhrstaketu who ruled Kekaya. His wife Srutakirti was most fortunate, and she was the sister of Vasudeva. This king had a charming daughter by the name of Bhadra. She was endowed with all good qualities. Although the king had been thinking of arranging his daughter's marriage, he had been unable to find a qualified husband for her for some time. After prolonged thought, he decided that Krishna, who carries a disc in His hand, was the perfect groom for his daughter. The king sent his son to Dwaraka and had Krishna brought to his capital, Avantipura. He arranged a grand festival in his capital to mark the auspicious ceremony of his daughter's marriage. The king greeted Krishna with great respect, and worshiped Him with various gifts and articles. He donated Lord Hari many jewels, horses, elephants, chariots, servants, and maidservants. Krishna became very pleased by the king's behavior. He took Bhadra with Him and returned to Dwaraka. In this way, Krishna married six queens. The inhabitants of the city were plunged into an ocean of happiness.

## The marriage of NagnajitI

The city of Kosala was situated in the middle of the earth planet and King Nagnajit was the ruler of this kingdom. The king was very pious and a great devotee of Visnu. He had a jewel-like daughter by the name of NagnajitI. Her beauty was matchless; she was like an incarnation of Laksmi. She was very talented. There was no one within the three worlds who could be compared to her. She enchanted everyone in the world by her beauty. The king was looking for a suitable groom for his daughter when suddenly he heard from the mouth of Narada Muni that the Supreme Lord had incarnated to diminish the burden of the earth. The king then made a promise, "Anyone who is able to tame seven bulls simultaneously can have my daughter as his wife. This will be the only way by which I will give my daughter in marriage."

When all the neighboring kings heard about this promise, they were amazed. Attracted by the girl's beauty, however, they went to the city of Kosala. The arrangement was that seven bulls were to be set loose within a fenced arena. Some of the kings tried to catch hold of the bulls, but they were unsuccessful, and left out of fear of being knocked down. Even the great, powerful kings were no match for these bulls. This turn of events made Nagnajiti a little bit worried. She thought to herself, "It seems no one will be able to marry me. The promise of my father is too difficult to follow. I think I will never obtain the favor of my Lord Gopala."

Thinking in this way, she daily worshiped goddess Durga and begged for her benediction. She prayed, "O maintainer of the creation! O destroyer of the distress! O wife of Siva! Please give me the benediction that I may have the Supreme Lord who holds a disc in His hand as my husband. O goddess of the three worlds! You bewilder everyone within the world. If you do not grant me my wish then you will be responsible for killing a virtuous woman." In this way, Nagnajiti daily prayed to the goddess Durga while fixing her mind on Krishna.

Meanwhile in Dwaraka, Krishna understood everything that was going on in the city of Kosala. He had also heard from different people about the conditions under which one might marry Nagnajiti. Thinking that taming seven bulls was a small task, Krishna immediately left for the city of Kosala. As soon as Krishna arrived at the palace, the king greeted Him respectfully and worshiped Him with padya and arghya. He took Krishna inside his palace and fed Him various sweets and drinks. The king then asked, "What brings You here?" Krishna replied, "O king! I have heard that you want to arrange for your daughter to be married. So, I have come here to marry your daughter." The king was extremely pleased by these words of Krishna. He folded his hands and said, "O Lord! I have the desire to hand over my daughter to You in marriage, but I have made a strong condition. It is my good fortune that you have arrived in my house at the right time. Now tame the seven bulls, and marry my daughter."

When Krishna heard about the condition, He said, "O king! Why did you make such a condition? What if some low-class person fulfills the condition and married your daughter? You and your fame will be ruined." But the king firmly replied, "O Narayana! What to speak of seven bulls, no one has so far been able to tame even one bull. I think no one other than You will be able to fulfill my condition. I knew this fact before, and that is why I purposely made such a condition. O Lord! Fulfill my condition, and marry my beautiful daughter, for she belongs only to You."

At this, Krishna simply smiled and prepared to tie all seven bulls simultaneously. Each of the bulls was gigantic and formidable. Krishna expanded Himself into seven forms and tied all of the seven bulls effortlessly. Seeing this, the king, his daughter, and the assembled spectators were struck with wonder. The king immediately brought his daughter and handed her over to Krishna. She was naturally beautiful, and she even looked more enchanting with all of the jewel-studded ornaments she was wearing. The king gave Krishna a substantial dowry comprised of a huge amount of wealth, as well as horses, elephants, servants and maidservants. Soon after marrying Nagnajiti, Krishna returned to Dwaraka. As usual the inhabitants of Dwaraka became jubilant on seeing Krishna and His new queen, Nagnajiti.

O human beings! Hear these pastimes of Krishna with full attention as I, Gunaraja Khan narrate them for your benefit.

## The marriage of Laksmana

There was a king named Madra near Dwaraka. He had a young daughter by the name Laksmana. As the daughter grew up, the king began to think about her marriage. He summoned all his friends and associates and requested them to find a suitable groom for his daughter. He sent messengers to different provinces for this purpose. In preparation for the marriage ceremony, the king had his men decorate the entire city beautifully. The city looked exactly like the city of Indra. In fact, at the advice of Indra, the king arranged for a contest. He placed a fish on a circling wheel high up near the ceiling as a target. Anyone who could successfully pierce the eye of the fish while looking at its reflection in the water on the ground would be given the hand of his daughter. The king made all the arrangements for the boarding and lodging of the guests. The professional dancers and singers displayed their respective skills. All the kings of the world arrived in the city of King Madra. On a fixed day, all the kings were to try their luck at the svayamvara ceremony. King Madra folded his hand and said, "O respected kings! I give my word that anyone who successfully hits the target will have my daughter."

One by one, the kings attempted to hit the target of the fish in the circling wheel on the ceiling by viewing its reflection in the water below. It was such a challenging feat, though, that some of them were not even able to pick up and string the bow, much less take aim and shoot at the spinning target. The king of Matsya, King Rukmi, the king of Vidarbha, and many other kings were unsuccessful. Duryodhana and his brothers too failed at their attempts. Salva, Sakuni, Bmadratha, Kasiraja, Nakula, and Sahadeva also failed one after another. Bhimasena somehow picked up the bow but could not shoot the target. Finally, it was the turn of Arjuna and he deftly strung the bow and swiftly shot the arrow. But despite his prowess, instead of hitting the target, the arrow hit the wheel, and crashed down to the ground. Arjuna became embarrassed and dejectedly left the arena. Krishna then fastened His waistband, and slowly walked up to the bow with a smile. He picked up the bow with His left hand, and shot an arrow with His right hand. As Krishna was not an ordinary person, but the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in one attempt, He successfully pierced the eye of the fish.

As soon as Krishna hit the target, King Madra came to worship Him with padya, arghya, and honey. Then Laksmana, who is the most beautiful within the three worlds, walked forward on to the stage with a flower garland in her hand. She was dressed in opulent garments. Her whole body was decorated with various ornaments. As she walked like an intoxicated elephant, her ankle bells made sweet sounds. She was expert in all artistic work. The whole assembly became illuminated by her effulgence. Laksmana walked up to Krishna and put the garland on His neck with great joy. As Krishna married Laksmana in this manner, a sound of jubilation vibrated all over the world. Thereafter, King Madra led the newlywed couple inside the palace and performed the required rituals according to the prescription of the scriptures. He gave six hundred chariots, six hundred thousand horses, one thousand elephants, six million soldiers, three hundred maidservants as well as a large amount of jewels to Lord Govinda. Krishna was very pleased with the king and his daughter Laksmana. After marrying Laksmana, Krishna left for Dwaraka. Out of frustration, though, the assembled kings who had gathered there tried to obstruct Krishna from proceeding and a battle ensued between Krishna and the rest of the kings. Krishna defeated all of them and then joyfully went off on His way to Dwaraka.

O human beings! Hear these enchanting topics of Krishna with joy. This is how Krishna married His eight principal queens. Anyone who hears these topics with faith will attain happiness in this life. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya.

## The mischief of Narakasura

The great king Naraka was the son of Mother Earth. He was a powerful warrior and lived in his kingdom situated in the middle of the earth. He was the emperor of the whole world. All the kings in the world were under his control. He even defeated Kuvera and took away his chariot, and then he captured a mountain of jewels and filled his kingdom with opulence. He had a desire to marry twenty thousand girls. For this scope, he used to kidnap worldly and celestial girls whenever he got the chance. His duty was to defeat different kings and then forcibly kidnap their daughters. He conquered the heavenly planets and brought many Apsaras to his own kingdom. Once he stole the earrings of Aditi, which made Indra furious. In order to take revenge, Indra fought with Naraka fiercely. Finally he had to leave the battlefield because he could not stand the prowess of Narakasura.

Being defeated by Naraka, Indra thought for a while about what to do and then went to Dwaraka to take advice from Krishna. As he arrived in Dwaraka, Krishna immediately greeted him with regard and made him sit comfortably. Krishna then inquired, "What brings you here?" Indra narrated the whole incident about Narakasura and also reminded Krishna that he had to face such humiliation, even in the presence of Krishna in this world. Indra said, "This Naraka has kidnapped thousands of beautiful girls and kept them confined in a secret place. He has decided to collect twenty thousand girls and marry them together. So far, he has collected sixteen thousand and one hundred girls. O Lord! This Naraka is very sinful and puffed up. He defeated Kuvera and took away his entire treasury. He forcibly took away my mother's earrings. My mother Aditi told me to come to You and ask for Your help. She told me to get help from Krishna, kill Naraka, and bring back her earrings."

On hearing these words of Indra, Krishna assured him that He would certainly kill Naraka. He then bade farewell to Indra and sat down with Balarama to discuss the ways and means to kill Naraka. He said to Balarama, "You take care of the citizens of Dwaraka while I am gone. I have to take care of this wicked Narakasura. Look after Vasudeva, Devaki, and Ugrasena nicely. I will ride on the back of Garuda to fight with Naraka while Daruka can follow Me in a chariot." Then Krishna, along with Satyabhama, mounted on the back of Garuda, and set out to kill Narakasura. As They were flying in the sky, a demon named Mura saw them from within the water.

## The killing of the Mura demon and his associates

As Krishna was passing through the sky, He suddenly saw a huge fiery palace within the water. This palace was not accessible to any outsider. This palace belonged to the great demon Mura who was a friend of Naraka. He had built his palace within the water just to remain safe. This demon had five heads and looked formidable. Even from within the water, he tried to rule the whole earth. He had seven sons who were as fierce as Yamaraja. As soon as Mura saw Krishna flying in the sky, he immediately came out from the water and challenged Him, saying, "O You! Where are You going? Why did You disturb me by flying over my head? Today Your life will be finished in my hands. Today I will send You to the house of Yamaraja."

After speaking these words, Mura released ten arrows simultaneously. Krishna quickly cut those arrows into pieces with His own cakra. Mura again released a fiery weapon at Krishna, but the Lord tactfully cut it into pieces. After that, Krishna swung His cakra towards the demon and cut his body into many pieces. As Krishna killed the demon Mura, all the demigods began to shower flowers on Him. Since then Krishna became known as Murari, or the killer of Mura.

When the demon's seven sons got the news of their father's death, they became very angry at Krishna. They came rushing to fight with Him, but all of them lost their lives in the hands of Krishna. In this way, after the killing of Mura and his whole family, Krishna continued His journey towards the palace of Narakasura.

## The killing of Narakasura in a fierce battle

Within a short time, Krishna arrived at the palace of Narakasura who, upon seeing Krishna, came out with a weapon in his hand to fight. He said, "You have killed my friend in a battle, but now you will be finished at my hands." They both fought fiercely with bows and arrows. There was a rain of arrows in that wonderful battle. All the captured princesses began to worship and pray to goddess Durga, "O Devi! O wife of Siva! Please save us from the ocean of distress. Make sure that the sinful Naraka does not marry us. O mother! Give us the boon that the Lord of the three worlds may become our protector."

While the princesses fixed their mind on Krishna with full attention, Krishna continued to fight a heavy battle with Naraka. Naraka picked up his bow and shot five arrows simultaneously. But Krishna is very expert. He cut off the arrows with His own cakra. King Naraka then released his brahmastra which flew towards Krishna like fire. The whole sky became illuminated by the effulgence of the weapon. Although Krishna tried to shoot arrows at the weapon, He could not check its movement. All the arrows shot by Krishna became useless. When the fiery weapon was about to hit Krishna, He quickly released His cakra and cut the weapon into pieces. Before Naraka could attack Him again, Krishna jumped over to him, grabbed him by the neck, and punched his head. The demon could not tolerate this powerful blow and lost his life. As soon as Narakasura fell down dead, the denizens of heaven chanted "Jaya! Jaya!" And showered flowers on Krishna.

## Mother earth prays to Krishna

Krishna and Satyabhama descended from the back of Garuda, and went inside the palace of Narakasura. As Krishna entered within the palace, Bhumi Devi, the predominating deity of the earth, respectfully greeted Him and offered Him her prayers with folded hands, "O Lord Narayana! O controller of the universe! You are the cause of the creation, maintenance and destruction. O Supreme Lord! All living entities, including the demons, are created by You. You had previously delivered me from the waters of Garbhodaka Ocean by picking me up in Your form of Varaha. By Your touch, I became pregnant, and in due course of time, I gave birth to this Narakasura. He was actually Your own son, and now You Yourself have killed him. Please be kind to me."

When Krishna heard this prayer of Bhumi, He felt compassion for her. Krishna spoke to her in a sweet voice, "O Devi! Once upon a time you cried in front of Me on the shore of the ocean of milk. You were accompanied by the demigods headed by Indra. At that time, I promised to incarnate and diminish your burden. Therefore I have come and killed your son in the course of completing My mission. There is no need to lament." When Krishna reminded the goddess of the earth in this way, she became embarrassed and said, "Whatever You have done is good." Thereafter, Bhumi handed over Aditi's earrings to Krishna. She fell down at the lotus feet of Krishna and cried profusely. Satyabhama, who was standing nearby, was amazed to see yet another wife of Krishna. Krishna then embraced Bhumi and satisfied her with pleasing words. After comforting Bhumi, Krishna went inside the palace with Satyabhama and Bhumi to meet the sixteen thousand and one hundred women confined by Narakasura.

## Krishna marries sixteen thousand and one hundred queens

Within Narakasura's palace, Krishna saw that all of the young women who had been kidnapped were absorbed in deep meditation. They were concentrating on Krishna's lotus feet with their bodies, minds, and words. As soon as Krishna arrived before them, they all stood up in awe and reverence, overwhelmed with transcendental passion. They folded their palms and requested Krishna to accept them as His maidservants. Krishna reciprocated by marrying all these beautiful young women. After that, Krishna took charge of all the possessions and wealth of Naraka and happily returned to Dwaraka. The people of Dwaraka were jubilant at seeing Krishna return. Krishna wasted no time in rendering the earrings unto Aditi. In this way, Krishna, who carries a cakra in His hand, enjoyed the company of sixteen thousand one hundred eight queens. The Lord expanded Himself into as many forms as there were queens and begot ten sons in the wombs of each of these queens. All of these sons were equally as powerful as Krishna. He also begot one daughter in the womb of each of His queens.

O human beings! Krishna's pastimes are simply wonderful. Gunaraja Khan says that one who hears these pastimes with undivided attention does not take birth again in this material world.

## The kidnapping of Pradyumna and the killing of Sambara

## Narada explains to Sambara about the presence of Kamadeva within the womb of Rukmini

Krishna lived in Dwaraka and spent His days and nights with joy. In due course of time queen Rukmini became pregnant. All the inhabitants of the city were very happy when they heard this auspicious news. When Narada Muni understood that Kamadeva was going to take birth from the womb of Rukmini, he went to the place of Sambara to inform him about it. As soon as Sambara saw Narada, who is highly enriched with penance, he immediately stood up with respect and offered Narada a place to sit. Narada then disclosed the fact of Kamadeva's birth to Sambara. Long ago, Mahadeva had cursed Kamadeva and burnt him to ashes. At that time, Kamadeva's wife Rati prayed to Siva, "O Lord! You have cursed my husband due to his fault. Now, please make some arrangement so that my husband becomes relieved of this curse. O husband of Uma! Please bring my husband back to life."

When the crest jewel among the demigods, Mahadeva, heard these words of Rati, he reassured her saying, "When the Supreme Lord appears in this world He will marry a beautiful girl by the name of Rukmini. The Lord will beget a son from the womb of Rukmini. This son is none other than your husband. He will be as powerful as his father, Krishna. He will kill the demon Sambara and become celebrated in this world as Sambarari."

After explaining this incident to Sambara, Narada reminded him that his very enemy was already in the womb of Rukmini at Dwaraka.

## Sambara kidnaps Pradyumna

After Narada Muni left, Sambara began to contemplate what to do to get rid of his enemy. He created an illusion and went to stay at the house of Krishna. The wicked Sambara was very expert in playing magical feats. Sambara remained in the palace of Krishna until Pradyumna was born. After the completion of ten months and ten days, Rukmirri gave birth to a baby boy at an auspicious moment. Immediately after the child was born, Sambara entered within the maternity house and stole the child. Unseen by others, he took away the child and threw him into the waters of ocean. As soon as the child was thrown into the ocean, a giant fish immediately swallowed him. By the arrangement of providence, some fishermen were catching fish just at that moment. Among those fishermen was one fortunate fisherman by the name Kaurava. Kaurava spread his net into the water and captured a large fish in his net. The fisherman slowly pulled his net and was amazed to see the extraordinarily large fish. Out of respect for King Sambara, the fisherman gave the fish to him. The king sent the fish to the kitchen to be cooked, but when the cook cut open the fish, he found an attractive child within the stomach of the fish. Rati was extremely pleased to see the child. The king, who was childless, also became very happy and came rushing to see the child. Sambara handed over the child to Rati and instructed her to take care of him.

## Narada instructs Rati

In the meantime, the great sage Narada went to the palace of Rati and spoke to her in a secluded place. "Listen O Ratidevi! Let me remind you something. When your husband was burnt to ashes you asked for a benediction. Today your husband has taken birth in this world. You have forgotten that you are the wife of Kamadeva. You are living happily here in the house of Sambara. Just like Sambara, you are also expert at magical feats, so you have expertly deceived king Sambara. This child is your husband Kamadeva. By the mercy of Mahadeva, he was born as Krishna's son, but your father threw him in the ocean considering him his enemy. This child was swallowed by a fish, and this is how he has now come to be in your house. You should serve your husband with faith. You have to somehow get rid of this wicked Sambara." After advising Rati in this way, Narada left, and Rati began to look after her husband who was still a child.

## Rati serves her husband and becomes glorious

Rati concentrated her mind on taking care of her husband in the house of Sambara. Day by day, Kamadeva grew up, and looked more and more attractive. Within a very short time, Kama became an adult. He was most handsome. He learned the science of releasing weapons. Gradually, he learned all the tricks and arts that he had possessed previously. One day, Rati approached Kamadeva in a conjugal mood. But Kamadeva,seeing Rati's change of heart, quickly remembered Lord Hari. He said to her, "Why all of a sudden are you acting in this way? So far you have been treating me like a son, and now you want treat me like your husband. Tell me what is going on? I think your character is not good. Tell me the truth." To this. Rati replied with a smile "I am neither the daughter of Sambara nor am I your mother. My name is Rati, and I was previously your wife. You were burnt to ashes by the curse of Mahadeva. But upon my request for blessings, Mahadeva gave me the benediction that when the Supreme Personality of Godhead appeared in this world, you would then take birth from the womb of the Lord's wife Rukmini. Till then, I was supposed to perform austerity on the banks of the Ganges."

## Sambara kidnaps Rati

Rati continued, "As I was performing austerities, King Sambara suddenly saw me and picked me up on his chariot. He brought me to his palace and tried to forcibly enjoy my company. At that time, I created an illusory form of mine and deceived Sambara. O husband! This that I have told you is the truth. Since then, I have been residing here and anxiously waiting all this time simply for you." Rati then showed Kamadeva the illusory Rati which she had created.

## Kamadeva goes to the house of Sambara

Rati continued, "When Sambara heard about your birth, he went to Dwaraka, stole you from your mother, and threw you into the ocean. You were saved by the creator. A giant fish swallowed you, and that fish was captured in a fisherman's net. Thereafter, the fisherman gave the fish to Sambara, and this is how I found you in the belly of that fish. When Sambara heard about it, not knowing your identity, he told me to look after you nicely since he has no issue. At that time, Narada Muni came and told me in a solitary place to serve you. O dear one! Rukmini is your mother, and Krishna is your father. Eliminate this wicked demon Sambara, and let us go back to your own house. "

## Narada gives instruction to kill Sambara

After the conversation between Kamadeva and Rati ended, Narada Muni suddenly appeared there. The great sage advised Kamadeva saying, "Kill Sambara as soon as possible and go back to Dwaraka with Rati." So Kamadeva then made plans with Rati to kill Sambara. Rati encouraged her husband saying, "O Lord of my life! You are a son of Krishna, therefore you possess immense power. You will have no difficulty destroying Sambara. Moreover, you possess the five divine arrows with which you can easily slay the demon. O Lord! Quickly accomplish the mission, and let us go back to Dwaraka."

## The battle between Sambara and Kamadeva

Being thus encouraged by Rati, Kamadeva left to fight with Sambara. He took various weapons with him and went before the demon. Sambara became somewhat puzzled. He thought, "He is my son, so why is he coming to fight with me?" Kamadeva challenged Sambara by saying, "Who is your son? O sinful demon! I am the son of Krishna and Rukmini. Don't you remember you threw me into the ocean? I am Krishna's son, and Krishna protected me at that time. Now I will send you to the abode of Yamaraja." These words of Kamadeva opened Sambara's eyes. He immediately remembered everything. He jumped up in anger and picked up his favorite weapons. Pradyumna and Sambara fought with each other vigorously, but neither one was able to defeat his opponent. Sambara knew many tricks and used them against Kamadeva. He released the Gandharva weapon against Kamadeva who counteracted the weapons skillfully. One by one, Kamadeva cut the weapons of Sambara into pieces. Sambara could not tolerate this insult, so he picked up his powerful club and challenged Kamadeva.

## Kamadeva prays to the Goddess to be able to counteract Sambara's club

The demon Sambara had received the club weapon from the goddess. This club is more effulgent and powerful than the brahmastra, and it shone brightly. When Sambara was about to release his club, Kamadeva became somewhat frightened. The demigods began to watch this fierce battle between the two powerful personalities. Just in that moment, Narada Muni appeared on the scene and advised Kamadeva, "Do not try to counteract this club. Pacify your mind. This club of Sambara is invincible within the three worlds by the grace of the goddess. Do as I say. Worship the goddess with full attention and pray to her. Then the club will no longer have any effect." After advising him like this, Narada left, and Kamadeva put down his bow and arrow and began to meditate on the goddess.

He prayed, "You are the material nature and the maintainer of the material creation. You are the source of everything. You are the mother of the universe. You are the source of the rivers, mountains, sky, water, land, and all the other manifestations. O goddess! You are the destroyer of all dangers. You mitigate everyone's poverty. You are in charge of all types of weapons, and you are very dear to Lord Narayana as well. I beg you, please protect me from the wrath of this club." On hearing this prayer of Kamadeva, goddess Candika personally appeared before him and assured him as follows, "My dear son, I tell you that the club of Sambara will not show any effect on you. You take up your own weapon and kill Sambara. When Sambara throws his club at you, it will turn into a flower garland and adorn your neck." After speaking these words, the goddess disappeared, and Kamadeva went forward and challenged Sambara with more valor. Just then, Sambara threw his club at Kamadeva, but it turned into a garland and decorated the body of Kamadeva. Initially Kamadeva was very enchanting and now, with this garland, he looked even more attractive. Decorated with this garland, Kamadeva began to fight with Sambara even more powerfully.

## The killing of Sambara

Kamadeva then invoked his own brahmastra weapon and released it towards Sambara. The demon was half dead just at the sight of the powerful weapon. Within a moment, the brahmastra severed Sambara's head from his body. Immediately, the inhabitants of the three worlds began to exclaim, "Jaya! Jaya!" The happiness of the demigods knew no bounds as they showered flowers on Pradyumna. Thereafter, Pradyumna took up all the wealth of Sambara and returned happily to Dwaraka with Rati.

## Rati and Kamadeva return to Dwaraka

As Rati and Kamadeva arrived in the city of Dwaraka, people quickly gathered around to have a glimpse of them. At the appearance of Kamadeva, all the people of Dwaraka became restless. Rukmini had been lamenting her fate, "Such a nice son I had. Who was that wicked person who took away my son from me? He would have looked like Krishna. He would have resembled the full moon in the sky. If he had been alive, he would have looked like this." When Rukmini was lamenting in this way, Vasudeva and Devaki came there and tried to pacify her. Just then, Narada Muni appeared in the assembly and revealed the entire story about Kamadeva and Rati to all of them. Rukmini cried in joy and milk began to flow from her breasts on seeing her son. Pradyumna got down from the chariot and offered his obeisances to Krishna, Rukmini, Vasudeva, Devaki, Baladeva, Ugrasena, and other elderly personalities. He was very happy to be back at home among his own family members. Rukmini was so happy that she forgot her own self. She greeted her daughter-in-law and arranged a grand festival. She invited hundreds of chaste married women and took the newlywed bride home. All the people of Dwaraka enjoyed the festival to their full satisfaction.

## Krishna enjoys pastimes with Rukmini at the Raivataka mountain

One day, Sri Krishna took Rukmini to the Raivataka Mountain for a pleasure trip. This sacred spot is very enchanting. The mountain is very colorful and looks wonderful. As Krishna was enjoying His time with Rukmini, Narada Muni came there. Govinda respectfully greeted the sage and offered him a place to sit. After Narada Muni had been duly worshiped by Krishna and Rukmini, Krishna inquired about the cause of his arrival. The great sage replied with a smile. "O Lord! I have just been to the abode oflndra. I saw many beautiful parijata flowers there, so I brought a garland made of parijata flowers for you. Please accept it." Krishna immediately took the garland with respect and gave it to Rukmini. Queen Rukmini is an incarnation of Laksmi. When she wore this garland, she looked doubly beautiful. The specialty of parijata flower is that anyone who comes in contact with it does not suffer from any disease or lamentation. In this way, Krishna and Rukmini enjoyed blissfully on the Raivataka Mountain.

After offering the flower garland to Krishna, Narada went to Dwaraka and arrived at the house of Satyabhama. As soon as Satyabhama saw the sage, she worshiped him with all kinds of paraphernalia such as padya and arghya. Thereafter Narada sat down peacefully and spoke something to Satyabhama. "O Devi! Rukmini must be very fortunate. She has got a garland of parijata flowers from heaven. The daughter of Bhismaka is the crest jewel among all chaste women; more so because she got the garland directly from Krishna. I know you are very dear to Krishna, but still I don't understand why Krishna gave the parijata flower garland to Rukmini. I don't find any fault in you. Why then is Krishna angry with you? Moreover, this parijata flower is very rare in this world. The Lord of the universe gave it to Rukmini and not to you. You are the daughter of the renowned King Satrajit. You are also as beautiful as Parvati. Still, Krishna did not give the parijata flower to you. Why is the Lord of the three worlds so cruel? Can you tell me why Krishna is merciless to you?"

When Satyabhama heard these words of Narada, her heart trembled. She offered her obeisances to the sage and inquired very slowly, "O sage! Are you sure Krishna gave a parijata flower to Rukmini?" Narada replied, "Of course, I have seen with my own eyes. By giving the garland, Krishna has enhanced Rukmini's prestige. I am sure Krishna has neglected you." On hearing this, Satyabhama fell to the ground unconscious. All her companions immediately sprinkled water on her face. After she regained her external consciousness, she began to throw away all her ornaments. She looked red with so much anger. From that day onwards, Satyabhama became so upset that she gave up eating and drinking and slept only on the floor.

Meanwhile, the great sage Narada quickly went to Krishna and said, "O my Lord! Out of separation from You, Satyabhama has stopped eating and drinking. Her condition is pathetic. If you want to see her alive then quickly go to her palace." As soon as Krishna heard this news, He immediately went back to Dwaraka with Rukmini. Thereafter, Krishna solaced Rukmini with sweet words and sent her home to her palace while He Himself went straight to the palace of Satyabhama.

## Satyabhama's anger subsides

On arriving at the palace of Satyabhama, Krishna saw her lying down on the floor. She was breathing heavily, as she lay surrounded by her pale-looking companions. Krishna slowly went over to her side and made a gesture to her companions not to inform Satyabhama of His arrival. Satyabhama was lying down suffering in separation. Krishna told one of her companions who was braiding Satyabhama's hair to move aside, and He Himself began to braid her hair. Suddenly there was an enchantingly sweet smell in the room because of Krishna's presence. Satyabhama too smelled it and got up suddenly. She looked around and saw Krishna sitting behind her. Satyabhama glanced at Krishna out of the corner of her eye and spoke to Krishna in an angry mood while looking at one of her companions. She said, "Everyone in the world knows that Krishna is Rukmini's husband. Then why has He come here? Rukmini is beautiful and very dear to Him. Tell Him to go and enjoy the company of Rukmini in the Raivataka Mountain. My body is burning at the sight of Krishna. I will end my life today by entering into a blazing fire."

Satyabhama spoke like this in an angry mood, and then she began to cry profusely, and soon fell down to the ground unconscious. Her necklace burst apart, and her garments also scattered on the ground. Krishna could not tolerate this any longer. He immediately picked up Satyabhama and embraced her. The Lord wiped her face with His own hand and spoke to her in a sweet voice, "O dear, why are you angry at Me? There is no one dearer to Me in the world than you. Everyone knows that Krishna is a servant of Satyabhama. You are being unnecessarily angry at Me." When Krishna politely spoke these words, Satyabhama became somewhat pacified. She kept quiet for a while and then said, "I received Your association after worshiping goddess Parvati. O lotus-eyed Lord! Out of good fortune I have seen Your lotus feet. You have displayed compassion to me since our marriage. The whole world knows that I am very dear to You. So in spite of that, why are You so merciless to me now? Just now, I will end my life in front of You. O Lord of the universe! The parijata flower is very rare in this world, and You gave it to Rukmini and neglected me. I have also learnt from Narada Muni, You no longer have mercy on me. Therefore it is better for me to die, and to die before You."

When Lord Madhusudana heard these words, He solaced Satyabhama repeatedly saying, "I will tell you the truth. There is no one dearer to Me than you. I cannot tolerate it when you cry. Stop lamenting and pacify your mind. I gave Rukmini only a garland of parijata, but I will give you the whole tree." Satyabhama knew the prowess and glories of Krishna. She smiled and said, "As You wish. Make sure You do not break Your promise." Krishna again assured Satyabhama and hugged her. He wiped the dust from her body and made her sit on His left thigh. Satyabhama was very pleased by this behavior of Krishna's. Her anger subsided as she offered her obeisances to Krishna and made Him sit comfortably by her side.

## Satyabhama serves Krishna with love

Satyabhama quickly ordered her maidservants to bring water. When water was brought, she personally washed Krishna's lotus feet with that water. Then, she massaged Krishna's body with scented oil and gave Him a nice bath. She offered opulent clothes to Krishna, and decorated His body with fragrant sandalwood paste. After Krishna was seated comfortably, the chaste Satyabhama brought various foodstuffs like sweets, grains, vegetables, and drinks, and personally fed Krishna. After Krishna had finished His meal, Satyabhama made Him lie down on a couch while she herself began to massage Krishna's lotus feet. In this way, both Krishna and Satyabhama became very happy.

## Narada visits the palace of Indra

Lord Krishna enjoyed His transcendental pastimes in this world. One fine morning, Krishna called Narada Muni and told him to visit the abode of Indra. He said, "Go and tell Indra that I am his younger brother and that I have requested him to give Me a parijata plant. If he does not agree, then tell him firmly that if he refuses to give the parijata then he will not enjoy his position as the lord of the demigods for long. If he does not give you the plant, then he should be prepared to fight with Me. I will hit his chest with My club and forcibly bring the plant." Thereafter Narada Muni went to the abode of Indra and explained to him everything in detail. He then asked Indra, "Tell me what is your reply? What should I tell Krishna?" Indra became very angry at this and said to Narada, "O sage, Krishna does not know what He is talking about. He is on earth in a human body. He wants to fight with me only for the sake of the parijata flower? I have never heard of a battle between demigods and human beings. O sage! Go and pay my regards to Krishna. Let Him come here to fight with me."

Narada Muni returned to Dwaraka and related to Krishna what Indra had told him. Krishna simply smiled and said, "O great sage! You come to Indraloka to see the fighting. You should watch as I defeat Indra and snatch away the parijata tree." Saying this, Krishna and Satyabhama hopped on the back of Garuda and departed for heaven. Indra lives within a huge palace, and the parijata tree is nicely guarded by the Gandharvas so that nobody can take it away. Indra lives just by the side of the parijata tree and enjoys the sweet aroma of this flower with Saci. Krishna, the Lord of the world, went directly to the place where Indra was sitting. He saw the parijata tree next to the gate of Indra's palace.

## Krishna forcibly takes away the Parijata tree

Krishna got down from the back of Garuda and went straight to the parijata plant. He told the guards to go and inform Indra that Krishna was taking away his parijata tree. Saying this, Krishna uprooted the parijata tree with His left hand and put it on the back of Garuda. As soon as Indra heard from his guard the news of Krishna's stealing parijata tree, he came running with great anger. He was so furious that it seemed as if he was going to destroy the whole world. Indra sat down on the back of Airavata along with Saci and came out of his palace with his favorite thunderbolt weapon in his hand. He quickly went before Krishna and said, "You cannot run away like this." Krishna accepted the challenge and stood there bravely. The Lord of the demigods Indra showered many different weapons on Krishna in anger, but Krishna counteracted all of them by cutting them into pieces with His cakra. The more Indra shot arrows, the more Krishna destroyed them. Satyabhama was watching this with great fun, but Indra became angrier at this. Finally, he picked up his thunderbolt to strike Krishna, at which point Krishna picked up His cakra. Krishna thought that Indra's thunderbolt would never fail by the mercy of the great sages. If the thunderbolt failed, then it would be a violation of the sages' words. By the arrangement of providence, suddenly Garuda spread one of his wings in the way of Indra's weapon. Already in motion, the thunderbolt hit that wing and immediately became ineffective.

Krishna then chased Indra with His cakra, and Indra became very frightened and restless. At seeing this, Satyabhama ridiculed Indra and said, "The husband of Sac! should not have left the battle in this manner." After teaching Indra a lesson, Krishna took the parijata tree and happily returned to earth. In Dwaraka, Krishna planted the parijata tree in the flower garden of Satyabhama. Because of the beauty and sweet aroma of this flower, the whole place became enchanting. At night, Krishna enjoyed various pastimes under this tree. In this way, people of Dwaraka lived happily day and night. They danced, sang and chanted the glories of Krishna. O human beings! This topic of stealing the parijata tree is certainly wonderful. One who hears this story with faith and devotion will certainly go back to Godhead. Listen to the wonderful and nectarean topics of Krishna as Gunaraja Khan narrates them.

## Krishna deceitfully speaks to Rukmini

Krishna, who is nondifferent from Narayana, enjoyed His blissful pastimes in Dwaraka. All His associates and relatives lived with Him in great joy. HJ expanded Himself into sixteen thousand one hundred and eight forms, and enjoyed the company of the same number of His queens. One day, Krishna was sitting on a couch in Rukmini's palace in a happy mood. The maidservants were fanning Him with a whisk that had a golden handle. Realizing that Krishna was relishing the cool breeze produced by the whisk, Rukmini herself began to fan Him. Krishna suddenly began to speak to Rukmini in a joking manner. He said, "O Devi! There were many greatly powerful kings in this world. They were all great warriors and more powerful than Me. They were also more handsome than Me. They were expert in the art of fighting and releasing weapons. Their beauty easily defeated the beauty of Cupid. They had great wealth, horses, elephants, chariots, and above all, they were pious. Why then did you choose Me as your husband? I am a poor person. I have no money. Neither have I a kingdom, nor am I a king. I belong to a lower caste and live on the shore of the ocean. Actually I cheated you, and you fell into My trap and married Me. You are so beautiful and an incarnation of Laksmi. Moreover, I am a low-class person. Therefore, it was not proper for you to marry Me. O princess! Why did you reject the great kings and chose Me? Especially over Sisupala; he was a very powerful king."

When Krishna spoke in this deceitful manner, Rukmini lowered her head and began to write something on the ground with her toe. She thought to herself, "Why has Krishna spoken like this to me?" In fact, she became extremely frightened. Unable to bear the emotions that had overcome her, she fell unconscious to the ground just as a banana tree falls to the ground when it is cut. Krishna immediately picked her up and wiped her face with His own hands. He embraced Rukmini with love and affection. Krishna made her sit down on the couch and spoke to her in a sweet voice. He said, "O dear! Do not become frightened. I was only joking with you, but you have taken it seriously. Do not worry."

## Krishna solaces Rukmini

Krishna continued, "O Devi! Actually there is no one within the whole world who is dearer to Me than you. That is why I spoke to you in that manner. Do not maintain any doubts in your mind. I belong to you." When Krishna assured Rukmini like this, she became pacified and peaceful.

## Rukmini refutes Krishna's deceitful words

Rukmini folded her hands and spoke to Krishna with great love. "O Lord of the universe! You are not a poor person. Millions of Laksmis emanate from the dust of Your lotus feet. So how are You poor? Millions of goddesses of fortune faithfully serve Your lotus feet. Your lotus foot is the birth place of the sacred river Gaiiga. If You are poor, then who is rich? You said You are not qualified to become my husband, but O Lord, You are the proprietor of the whole material creation. You are the king of millions of universes. Indra who is the ruler of the three worlds is only a servant of Your lotus feet. Brahma, the lord of the demigods, is an insignificant servant of Your lotus feet. When I meditated on Your lotus feet, I saw all the other kings as tiny as blades of grass. You also said You are a low class person, but O my Lord! You are present everywhere. You are present in the beginning, at the middle and at the end of everything. You also said You are not powerful like the other kings. But I don't think so. You can destroy anyone at the blink of an eye. When You were a child, You killed so many powerful demons effortlessly. You also said You have no good qualities. O Lord! You are indeed free from all material qualities, which are supposed to be good in the eyes of the materialistic people. You are the essence of the universe, yet You incarnate in this world for the benefit of the living entities. Actually, You are full of transcendental qualities. Who can understand Your glories within the three worlds? After worshiping Siva and Parvati for many lifetimes, I have been blessed to have You as my husband. All the other kings are no better than animals before You. Why then did You deceive me in this way?"

After speaking these words, Rukmini became very emotional and began to cry profusely. Her clothes became soaked with her tears. Krishna felt very compassionate and immediately embraced and kissed her again and again. The Lord then enjoyed various pastimes with Rukmini.

O human beings! Hear these extraordinary characteristics of Krishna as Gunaraja Khan narrates them.

## The adventures of king Bana

Krishna, who loves to wear a garland of forest flowers, lived happily in Dwaraka in the association of His sons and grandsons. O readers! Now hear about the story of King Bana, who was the ruler of Sonitapura. The two doorkeepers of Vaikuntha namely Jaya and Vijaya took birth in this world due to being cursed by the four sons of Brahma headed by Sanaka. They took birth as the two infamous demons named Hiranyaksa and Hiranyakasipu. They were greatly powerful demons yet the Supreme Lord had killed them both. Thus they were relieved of their curse and returned to their original position in Vaikuntha.

The son of Hiranyakasipu was the exalted devotee Prahlada. Who can estimate the glories of Prahlada? Prahlada's son was the world famous Virocana who had a son by the name Bali, who had worshiped the Supreme Lord in His form as Vamanadeva. Bali gave the entire world to Lord Narayana in charity. He had one hundred sons and lives in Patalaloka. Among his one hundred sons, the eldest one was King Bana who underwent severe penances to please Siva. In due course of time, Siva appeared before him and gave him a benediction. The three-eyed Siva told him that he would have one thousand arms and that no one would be able to defeat him in the three worlds. With his immense power, Bana then conquered the three worlds and kept everyone under his sway. Even Siva and Parvati had to live in his palace. Karttikeya had to work as his bodyguard, with a trident in his hand.

One day, King Bana spoke to Siva with great pride. "O Sankara, by your grace I have become invincible in the three worlds. There is no one other than you who can compete with me. I have one thousand arms, but I don't find any suitable opponent to fight. Now my arms are just a burden to me as I can not utilize them for fighting." To this, Siva replied, "O king, do not worry, very soon you will be engaged in a fierce battle. Your chariot and flag will be broken to pieces, and you will have to ask for my help." After saying this, Lord Siva left. Bana became happy thinking how finally he would have a chance to fight.

## Lisa obtains a boon

King Bana had a beautiful daughter by the name of Usa. She was as enchanting as a vidvadhan. She used to worship goddess Durga every day and one day, the goddess appeared before Usa and ordered her to ask for any benediction she wished except immortality. Usa then prayed, "O goddess! I have no problems as yet. I am very happy in all respects. But now that I have grown up, I wish to know when will I meet my husband and will who he will be?" To this, Parvati replied, "O lady! Listen, you will have an excellent husband! A handsome personality will appear to you in a dream on the twelfth day of the waxing moon in the month of Vaisakha and accept you as his wife." Saying this, the goddess disappeared in the sky. Usa was stunned to hear this auspicious news. She began to count the days with eagerness.

## Aniruddha meets Usa in a dream

The arrangement of the Supreme Lord cannot be overruled. One day, Usa was sleeping happily on a couch. A handsome personality suddenly appeared in her dream and began to arouse her passion in various ways. Usa woke up but could not see anyone by her side. She became so frightened that she lost her external consciousness. Her maidservants revived her by sprinkling water on her face. Although her maids tried to pacify her, she could not remain calm and instead began to cry profusely. She spent the entire night like that. In the morning, one of her companions, Citralekha. came to see her. Citralekha solaced Usa with sweet words. Citralekha inquired, "Please do not feel aggrieved. Tell me what has happened. Why is your condition like this?"

With great difficulty, Usa controlled herself and narrated the incident which had taken place the previous night. She said, "at around midnight, when I was fast asleep on my couch in my bedroom, a blackish personality suddenly appeared before me. I could not understand who he was, whether he was a demigod, a Kinnara, or a Gandharva. He enjoyed conjugal pastimes with me in various ways. Look here, I still have the marks and signs of that on my body. When I woke up, I could not see anyone in my bedroom. He must be the lord of my heart. I feel uneasy not seeing him. My entire body is burning with the unbearable fire of lust. Even though I try to cool off my body by lying down on the floor, I cannot find any peace. O friend! What should I do now? Where can I go so that I can see him? He looked more enchanting than Cupid. He had lotus-like eyes and his face defeated the beauty of the moon. His eyebrows were just like the bow of Cupid."

As Usa spoke like this, she cried profusely again. Then the daughter of Kumbhanda solaced Usa by saying, "O dear one! Please pacify your mind and stop crying. Don't you remember what the goddess had once told you? The personality who will enjoy with you in a dream will be your husband. Whatever the goddess predicted has come true. See for yourself these marks of conjugal pastimes." On hearing these words of Citralekha, Usa remembered the previous incident. Usa then said, "My dear Citralekha! How can I meet that Lord of my life? I am very young and have already tasted the intimate association of my beloved. He has captured my heart and as a matter of fact, I cannot think of anyone else as my beloved Lord.

O sakhi! I fall down at your feet. Please save my life by arranging our meeting. I cannot wait any longer."

## Citralekha draws sketches

Feeling sad about Usa's predicament, Citralekha then said to her, "O friend! Stop weeping. I will certainly do something so that you can find your lover. By the mercy of a great sage, I can travel throughout the three worlds. I am also capable of drawing sketches of each and every person I see. In three days, I will travel throughout the three worlds and draw sketches of all of the inhabitants of the three worlds that I encounter in my travels. Please wait for three days till I come back."

After speaking in this manner to Usa, Citralekha first went to the heavenly planets and sketched everyone's features just as they were. She then traveled to the lower planets like Patalaloka and Nagaloka and drew their portraits as well. Finally she had made the sketches of all human beings on this earth. She completed the entire list of demigods, Yaksas, Kinnaras, human beings, and other creatures in three days and then returned. She then placed the sketches before U§a and asked her to identify her husband.

## Usa recognizes her husband

Princess Usa immediately got up with joy and began to search for her husband without feeling any shame. In one large stack of drawings, she saw the paintings of the demigods and Kinnaras, but could not find the one who had stolen her heart. She then checked the stack of paintings of the inhabitants of the Patala and Nagaloka. Among these too she could not find the thief. Hence she felt very sad. Finally Usa observed the sketches of earthly residents. Once again, she was shocked not to find the lord of her life. She checked the east, west, and north sides of the list, but did not find the person who had appeared in her dream. At last, when she looked at the south of the list, to her astonishment, she found the thief who had stolen her heart.

Usa quickly called her companion and showed her the exact person. "This thief has taken away my chastity!" Usa exclaimed. "I want to meet him immediately. Whose son is he? Where does he live? O sakhi\] What is his family background?" "The Supreme Lord has descended in this world to diminish the burden of the earth," Citralekha replied with a smile. "Your beloved lord is the grandson of that Supreme Lord Krishna. He is the son of Pradyumna, and his name is Aniruddha. He belongs to the ksatriya dynasty and lives in Dwaraka. You are very fortunate to have him as your husband." Usa then requested her friend, "O sakhil You are expert in all types of art. Please do not waste any time. Bring me my beloved Lord. My body is burning continuously by the fire of passion. Go quickly; if I die now all your attempts will be futile."

## Citralekha goes to Dwaraka

Citralekha immediately went to Dwaraka lest Usa gives up her life. Meanwhile, Aniruddha the son of Pradyumna could not remain peaceful after enjoying the intimate association of Usa in the dream. He continued to feel restless all the time and kept on thinking about how to come closer to Usa. Aniruddha lost all taste for opulent life and constantly stayed in a somber mood. When he was in such a mood, Citralekha arrived there and met him in a solitary place.

## Citralekha introduces herself and explains the condition of Usa toAniruddha

When he saw Citralekha, Aniruddha could not understand who she was. He inquired, "O lady! Who are you? Who is your father? How did you come here in spite of so many guards?" Citralekha, who was a Vidyadhari, replied, "I have come here as a messenger. You must have heard about the powerful King Bana. He has a daughter by the name of Usa, whose beauty is compared only to Parvati. I am her companion and my name is Citralekha. By the grace of a sage, I have access to anywhere and everywhere. That is why I have been able to come here despite the guards. I know you went to Usa in a dream and enjoyed with her in various ways. When she got up in the morning, she could not find anyone by her side. She lost consciousness thinking of you. I helped her regain consciousness somehow or the other. She told me everything you have done with her in detail. She is young and innocent, and now it is very difficult for her to live without you. Although we tried to find her a suitable husband, but she became very angry to this proposal."

She said to me, 'O friend, do you want me to break my chastity? My husband is he who appeared to me in a dream and enjoyed with me. I accept him as the Lord of my life. Please bring him from wherever you can; otherwise you will be responsible for killing a woman.' Thereafter, I made drawings of all the inhabitants of the three planetary systems and asked her to recognize her husband. After examining the drawings, she finally recognized you and requested me to bring you back to her, otherwise she will give up her life."

After hearing what had happened to Usa, Aniruddha became very restless. Somehow, he pacified his mind and spoke to Citralekha in a sweet voice, "O Citralekha! I have nothing to hide. Let me tell you the truth. Actually Usa deceived me in the dream. Since then I have become indifferent. I have given up eating and drinking and simply think about her. I have stopped my opulent way of life and constantly remember her day and night. I too beg you to kindly let me see Usa immediately."

## Aniruddha accompanies Citralekha to the residence of Usa.

Aniruddha had no control over himself. He was extremely afflicted due to separation from Usa. When Citralekha asked him to get on the chariot to go see Usa, Aniruddha quickly followed her. He did not inform anyone in the palace. He followed Citralekha like a puppet. Within a short time, they both reached the palace of Usa. It was nighttime, and they slowly entered within the room of Usa. To their surprise, they saw Usa was lying on the floor and breathing heavily. Citralekha went near her and whispered, "Look, I have brought your husband!"

## The marriage of Aniruddha and U§a

Usa got up hurriedly, and saw before her standing the Cupid-like form of her enchanting husband. She became struck with wonder and worshiped him with pady a and arghya. Her companions tried to pacify her as she was unable to control herself due to being afflicted by lust. Thereafter, Aniruddha and Usa were married according to the Gandharva custom. After the marriage ceremony was over, they both went to the bedroom and enjoyed conjugal pastimes. They were so attached to each other that they forgot to follow the worldly etiquette. Both of them were perfect matches for each other. They enjoyed amorous pastimes day and night. In this way, the young couple of Aniruddha and Usa enjoyed their married life for sometime. In due course of time, Osa became pregnant.

When this news spread, King Bana also came to know about it from his spy. The spy said to the king, "O king! A handsome person has come to the house of Usa from the sky. He is a young fellow and his bodily color is blackish. He enjoys the intimate company of Usa day and night. Your daughter must be lucky to have such a personality at her disposal. They do not care for anyone nor are they afraid of anyone."

As soon as King Bana heard these words, he became furious with anger and immediately sent five guards to arrest the thief. He ordered them to go and catch the thief, beat him up, and arrest him. When the guards went to Usa's room, they saw U§a and Aniruddha playing chess in a joyful mood.

## Aniruddha kills the guards of Bana

As the guards surrounded Aniruddha from all sides, he immediately stood up and prepared to fight with them. He slapped a guard and snatched away his weapon. Then, he used that weapon and bravely fought with all the guards. One by one, he cut off their heads. After destroying them, Aniruddha continued to enjoy playing chess with Usa. When King Bana heard that his five soldiers had been killed by the stranger, he immediately summoned four of his powerful commanders in chief and said, "Take elephants and horses and go arrest that fellow. If you are unable to arrest him, then just kill him. Don't come back empty handed. This is my order." The four chiefs quickly went to the residence of Usa. Aniruddha jumped down from the couch and picked up his weapon to fight with them. The brave son of Pradyumna fought expertly like a great hero and chopped their hands and legs. The soldiers of Bana screamed like lions. Aniruddha killed all the four chiefs one by one. This made King Bana very angry, so much so that he began to tremble. He could not wait any more. He picked up a weapon and ran to Usa's room.

## The battle between Aniruddha and Bana

From a distance, Usa saw her father coming in an angry mood. She began to shiver out of fear thinking how could her father possibly kill her own husband? She caught hold of Aniruddha's garment, and began to weep. She repeatedly requested her husband not to fight with her father, but Aniruddha was adamant. He said, "Do not weep. O Usa! I am not afraid of anyone. I am the grandson of Krishna and the son of Pradyumna. No one within the three worlds can defeat me. Do not worry. Just sit down on the throne and watch me fight. I will kill everyone alone." King Bana arrived there and challenged Aniruddha with a grave voice, "Hey You! You are only a child before me. You are too young to compete with me. You have come here only to be killed by me." Saying this, Bana released a host of weapons at Aniruddha, who skillfully broke all those weapons into pieces. Aniruddha, with his bow and arrow attacked Bana severely. A formidable battle took place between the two equally powerful opponents. Aniruddha slayed the commanders in chief who came forward to fight with him. The king became more disturbed and angry when his chiefs began to die. King Bana picked up a trident and charged Aniruddha forcefully. The trident was accompanied by a body of fire. Usa became almost half dead on seeing the fiery trident. However, Aniruddha neutralized the weapon by breaking it into pieces with his arrows. The son of Bali became even more furious when his trident was rendered ineffective. Bana used all his one thousand hands and shot arrows at Aniruddha who counteracted them very easily. Bana did not know what to do and now he was very frightened.

## Usa laments seeing Aniruddha bound by a snake

Bana thought, "My weapons are infallible, so how is this fellow still competing with me? He is only a young boy." With renewed vigor, Bana attacked Aniruddha. This time he tied Aniruddha with a rope that was actually a snake. Aniruddha did not know the secret of untying himself from that bondage. He had no option but to be arrested by the rope of Bana. The king put him in the prison house in that condition and went home. Aniruddha was bound so tightly that he felt suffocated and breathed heavily. Usa slowly went to his side and began to cry pathetically. She tore off her necklaces and garments, and rolled on the ground. She picked up her husband on her lap and wept incessantly. She lamented, "O Lord! I told you before to not get into a fight with my father. You did not pay attention to my words. My father is invincible in the three worlds by the grace of Siva, yet you went to fight with him alone. You had no support, hence Bana defeated you with his tricks and bound you up. No one from your family knows about this. You have such ill luck. Bana is my own father, yet he has cheated me in this way. I will certainly enter into fire cursing him all the while."

As Usa lamented like this, she rolled on the ground and cried profusely. She lamented further, "I worshiped Siva and Durga with faith, and as a result, they gave me the boon to have an excellent husband. I did get a qualified husband who is as attractive as Cupid. But now, why has this happened to me?"

## Aniruddha prays to Goddess Candika

When Usa was crying pathetically like this, the great sage Narada arrived there. He tried to pacify U?a with the following words. "Do not cry, keep your head cool. Your husband will regain consciousness very soon." Narada Muni then turned towards Aniruddha and said softly, "O son of Pradyumna! Why do you forget yourself? Be steady and pray to goddess Candika. You will see that the snake rope will soon become ineffective." Being thus advised by Narada, Aniruddha fixed his mind on Candika and prayed to her. "O goddess! O NarayanI! O Bhavani! You are the maintainer of the whole creation. You are the water, the land, the air, the fire, the Mandara mountain, the Kailasa mountain, the sun, the moon, the day, and the night. You are the cause of everything, and the destroyer of all miseries. You annihilated the demons and protected the demigods. You are the friend of those who are in danger. My body is burning by the poisonous effect of the snake. Kindly save me from this situation. O mother Candika! I take shelter of you."

As Aniruddha offered prayers like this, the goddess personally appeared before him with a smiling face. She said, "Ask me for any benediction. O Aniruddha! There is nothing to be afraid of. The Supreme Personality of Godhead will soon come and deliver you." When Candika reassured him in this way, Aniruddha became pacified as if he had regained his lost life. He again folded his hands and prayed to the goddess to do something about the burning effect of the poison. Candika blessed him by saying that the poisonous effect will no longer have any impact on him. After saying this, the goddess disappeared.

Meanwhile, back in Dwaraka everyone was puzzled as to what had happened to Pradyumna's son. He was sleeping happily in his room and suddenly vanished. No one knew where he had gone and who had taken him away. Pradyumna became worried and so he informed his father, Krishna, as follows, "O father! O Lord of the three worlds! Somebody has come to our palace and kidnapped my son." At this, Krishna thought for a while and understood that Aniruddha had been taken away from there by Usa's companion and that at present, he was in the city of King Bana. He had secretly married Usa, the daughter of King Bana. He told Pradyumna that Aniruddha was also in trouble right now as he had been bound by Bana. Krishna then sent His spy to gather more information about Aniruddha's condition.

## Krishna prepares to fight with Bana

Just then, Narada Muni arrived at the residence of Krishna, who immediately stood up and greeted the sage respectfully. After the sage had been properly worshiped, he said, "My dear Lord! Call all Your principle associates." Krishna smiled, and called Balarama to His side. Narada then disclosed the entire story of how King Bana had bound up Aniruddha. He also informed Them of the battle between Bana and Aniruddha, and how Bana had bound up Aniruddha with a snake rope. He also said that Aniruddha had fought alone with Bana bravely, but Bana had finally managed to tie him up with a rope. As soon as Krishna heard this news, He immediately declared a war against Bana. Krishna kept Ugrasena in Dwaraka while He along with many soldiers set out for the city of Bana. Krishna surrounded the palace of Bana with His soldiers. The palace of Bana is very difficult to conquer because it is surrounded by fire and ditch. No human beings, demigods, or any other living entities can enter that palace. Krishna stopped for a moment after seeing the blazing fire. He then ordered Varuna to extinguish the blazing fire. Being ordered by Krishna, Varuna immediately showered torrents of rain. Within a very short time, the blazing fire came to an end. The Lord was very happy with Varuna. He took His army with Him and entered the palace of King Bana along with Balarama.

## The battle between Krishna and Siva

By nature, King Banasura was very puffed up with pride. When he got the news of Krishna's arrival in his palace from his spy, he bragged, "This son of a cowherd has come here only to die. I have got one thousand hands. I will behead Him and His army. Today my dream will be fulfilled. Today Siva's benediction will bear fruit. Finally, I have someone to fight with " Saying this, Bana raised his thousand hands up to the sky. Krishna had brought with Him twelve aksauhim soldiers. King Bana prepared to fight with Krishna led by none other than his master Siva. Siva had promised to fight on behalf of Bana, so he took up his trident and began to fight with Krishna along with his own son Karttikeya. Krishna with His Sudarsana cakra and Siva with his trident engaged in a fierce battle that created a frightening situation. It appeared like the destruction of the material creation. All the demigods began to shiver.

King Bana fought with Satyaki while Karttikeya fought with Pradyumna. Balarama fought with the two brothers, Kumbhanda and Kupakarna. Other warriors like Gada and Samva fought with the opposing kings. But the best and the most thrilling encounter of all was between Siva and Krishna. Their fight reminded all present of the annihilation of the universe. A blazing fire erupted in the course of their fight. It was so powerful that Siva could no longer tolerate the heat and had to flee. Krishna then turned His attention to Bana who was fighting with Satyaki. When Krishna was about to kill Bana with His Sudarsana cakra, the wife of Siva immediately appeared before Krishna and stood there naked. She did so just to protect her son. Krishna felt embarrassed at this and quit fighting with Bana temporarily. This time Bana survived by the mercy of the goddess. Siva then released his powerful weapon called Siva-jvara targeting Krishna. It came and surrounded Krishna from all sides. Krishna felt uneasy due to its unbearable heat and became almost senseless. Krishna then hurled His own celebrated and more powerful weapon called Visnu-jvara towards Siva. The two weapons Siva-jvara and Visnu-jvara had a fierce battle till Siva-jvara surrendered to Visnu-jvara and prayed for His mercy, "O Lord of the three worlds, please save my life. You are Brahma, Visnu and Siva. You are the leader of the human beings. You are Indra and all the other creators. You have created everything by Your own power. Kindly spare my life as I am dependent on You. Who can match Your prowess?"

When Krishna heard such prayers by the Siva-jvara, He just smiled and replied. "Do not be afraid. You will be spared. Any person who either hears or narrates these topics of the battle between Siva-jvara and Visnujvara will never be subject to material suffering." Siva-jvara then offered his obeisances to Krishna and returned to King Bana. Bana became red with anger on learning that Siva-jvara had failed to defeat Visnu-jvara. He took his trident and charged Krishna. He began to release various weapons by his thousand hands, but Krishna expertly destroyed them with His cakra. Being baffled repeatedly in his attempts, Bana could no longer stand Krishna. He became like a mad man. He took his trident to strike a powerful blow to Krishna who also held up His cakra in anticipation. Krishna's cakra shone brightly and illuminated the entire sky.

## Bana's hands are cut off by Krishna

At that moment, Mahadeva went ahead of Bana and began to offer prayers to Krishna, "O Lord! It is by Your mercy only that people of the three worlds worship me as the lord of lords. King Bana is like my son. If you kill him, my prestige will be hampered. O my Lord! Please forgive him this time, and Your glories will certainly be spread throughout the world." After hearing this prayer of Siva, Krishna laughed and reassured him. "I will spare Bana's life. Besides, I have given My word to Prahlada that I will not kill anyone in his family. Moreover, you gave him a benediction; therefore, I will not take his life. But since he is greatly puffed up because of his one thousand hands, I will certainly cut off his hands from his body. I will spare only four hands for your sake and the rest of the hands will be cut off by Me." Saying this, Krishna gradually cut off the hands of Banasura with His own cakra. Mahadeva felt compassion for his devotee Banasura. Therefore, he brought Bana before Krishna and requested the Lord to touch Banasura with His lotus hands so that he could be relieved of the pain inflicted by the cakra. Krishna obliged Siva's request, and Banasura appeared beautiful with his four hands.

After the whole ordeal, King Bana heaved a sigh of relief. He offered his obeisances to Krishna, and then invited Him and Mahadeva to his own palace. He offered Them a nice place to sit and worshiped Them with various ingredients. He also offered various gifts to Krishna and Mahadeva.
