He then quickly went over to Usa's palace and released Aniruddha. He brought him and Usa along and presented them before Krishna. He formally handed over the hands of his daaghter Usa to Aniruddha. The king donated various jewels, ornaments, elephants, horses, chariots, servants, and maidservants to the newly wedded couple. He satisfied everyone present there. Thereafter Krishna, along with Usa, Aniruddha, and other members of the Yadu dynasty, triumphantly returned to Dwaraka. Krishna arranged the grand festival to mark the auspicious marriage of Aniruddha and Usa. The inhabitants of Dwaraka were jubilant.

O human beings! Hear these wonderful pastimes with full attention. Anyone who hears these pastimes of Krishna undoubtedly achieves liberation. This is the conviction of Gunaraja Khan.

## King Nrga becomes a lizard

Krishna and His sons headed by Pradyumna once went out of the city on a pleasure trip. They went to a beautiful forest situated near Prabhasa Ksetra and began to enjoy various sporting activities. As they were playing in the sun, after some time, they all became tired and thirsty. They searched the forest for water, but could not find any. Finally they located a well. The Yadu boys quickly approached the well, but to their utter surprise, they saw a large lizard within the well. It was lying there with its head resting on the bottom. The lizard was so large and the well so deep that it could not come out on its own endeavor. The boys of the Yadu dynasty first tried their best to pull him out, but failed repeatedly, so they quickly went to Krishna and told Him about their adventure.

They said, "O Govinda! Listen to this amazing story. We saw a giant lizard inside a well. Surely he had gone to drink water in that well. As there is no water within that well, the lizard is certainly dying out of starvation and without water. We tried our best to save him but could not do much as he is so heavy and huge." When Krishna heard this news, He immediately understood the real story of the lizard, so He followed the boys to the well. Therein, He saw the big lizard, and with the two fingers of His left hand, Krishna easily lifted the lizard out of the well. As soon as Krishna touched the lizard, he left his lizard body and transformed into a beautiful Vidyadhara. The Vidyadhara stood up with folded hands and began to offer prayers to the Supreme Lord Krishna.

"O Supreme Personality of Godhead! By Your mercy, I have become relieved from a curse. You are Lord Narayana. You are the reality and the essence of the material creation. You are the cause of creation, maintenance and annihilation of the universe. Simply by remembering You, people can get liberation from material life. O husband of Laksmi! I am indeed fortunate for You have touched me today with Your own hands. Now kindly instruct me about my duty."

After hearing his prayer, Sri Krishna smiled, and though He knew everything, He asked the lizard-now-turned-demigod, "Which caste do you belong to? What is your name? Why have you been suffering in this condition? You are handsome and a demigod, so how have you taken birth as a lizard?"

At this, the Vidvadhara folded his hands and replied, "O Supreme Lord of the universe! I am ashamed to tell you my story. Since you have asked me I must follow Your order. I am the son of Iksvaku, and my name is King Nrga. O Hari! I was the emperor. On my own strength, I conquered the three worlds. After defeating all the other kings, I became the undisputed king of the whole universe. I began to conduct sacrifices and give in charity. O Narayana! No one can estimate the amount of charity I gave just as no one estimate the drops of rain, the stars in the sky or the particles of dust on earth. I personally selected countless cows which were equal to surabhi cows \[of the spiritual world\] and daily gave them away to the qualified brahmanas after decorating their horns with gold, their hooves with silver, and their necks with jeweled necklaces. All these cows gave an abundance of milk and were free from disease. I used to donate innumerable cows like this everyday. One day, by chance a great brahmana lost one of his cows. By the arrangement of providence, that cow somehow returned to my own cowshed. As usual, on the next day I donated that same cow to another brahmana. I was completely unaware of this fact. As this brahmana was returning home with his cows, the previous brahmana recognized his cow and demanded it back from the second brahmana.

## The story of king Nrga

"The first brahmana said, 'Only yesterday I received this cow in charity. How dare you steal my cow? You are illegally taking away my cow along with your own cows.' Saying this, the first brahmana forcibly took away the cow in an angry mood and was about to return home. Then the second brahmana said, 'Only today I received this cow in charity. There is no doubt about it.' The two brahmanas began to quarrel pulling the cow to their respective sides. Neither of them was ready to surrender.

Finally, they came to me with the cow to find out the truth. They spoke to me harshly. 'O best of kings! How dare you give the same cow to both of us? None of us are ready to let it go.'

Although I tried my best, I could not persuade them to resolve the matter amicably. None of them was prepared to sacrifice. I pleaded with them again and again, but in vain. I even fell down at their feet and offered to donate ten thousand cows in exchange of that one cow as compensation for that mistake.

When they did not budge an inch from their respective positions, I again requested them and agreed to offer one hundred thousand cows in exchange for that one cow. But O Lord Gadadhara! Neither brahmana agreed to my proposal. Finally, the stronger brahmana among the two took away the cow to his own house. Some days after this incident, I left my body, and the Yamadutas took me to the abode of their master.

Yamaraja, the authority on religious principles, said to me. 'You have performed unlimited pious activities. I am unable to estimate them. O king, you have performed many sacrifices, given charity, and maintained your subjects properly. You earned a lot of fame in this world because you never indulged in impious activities. But O king, you have unknowingly committed one sin. When the two brahmanas who were quarrelling over the ownership of a cow, approached you for a solution, you did not solve their dispute. This lone sin is present in your body. You have got an unlimited amount of piety to enjoy and a tiny amount impiety to endure. Now tell me which of the two do you wish to satisfy first?'

On hearing these words of Yamaraja I decided to endure my sinful reaction first. Then Yamaraja told me, 'Go and live in a forest as a lizard. You will have to live in a well that has no water in it and you will be hanging there upside down.' O Lord Gadadhara! Since then I have been living in that condition as a result of my own misdeed. O lotus-eyed Lord! Out of my good fortune, You have touched me with Your own hand. As a result, all my sinful reactions have become completely vanquished."

As King Nrga finished narrating his story, a chariot sent by Indra arrived from the sky and took him to heaven.

## Krishna's instructions to the princes of the Yadu dynasty.

When the sons of Krishna heard this thrilling story, they became struck with wonder. They became frightened as well. Krishna then called everyone together and said. "Did you hear the story of King Nrga? The curse of a brahmana is more dangerous than poison. Poison kills only one person, but the curse of a brahmana can destroy the entire family. If one steals the possession of a brahmana unintentionally, his three generations becomes destroyed, and if one steals the possession of a brahmana intentionally, his twenty-one generations are ruined. Anyone who steals a brahmana's possessions somehow or other suffers in hell for millions of lifetimes. Therefore My dear sons! Be very careful while dealing with brahmanas." All the Yadus then returned home. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya.

## The marriage of Duryodhanas daughter Laksmana with Samba

O human beings! Listen with attention as I narrate the marriage of Duryodhana's daughter Laksmana with Samba.

One day, Duryodhana began to contemplate whom he should find for his daughter to marry. His daughter was already at marriageable age. She was very beautiful and looked more or less like Laksmi. He discussed this matter with his friends and ministers and decided to find a husband for his daughter by arranging a svayamvara.

Soon, many messengers were sent to different parts of the country to invite the different kings. As they slowly arrived in the capital of Duryodhana, they found the mood of the whole city to be very festive. The city was nicely decorated, and everyone was in a festive mood. Many kings participated in that ceremony, especially because the bride was exquisitely beautiful.

Meanwhile, Krishna and Jambavati's son Samba too arrived there to see the fun. When all the assembled kings had taken their seats, the bride came out with a garland in her hand to choose her husband. Her complexion was fair, she had an attractive hairstyle, and her breasts were round and large. The beauty of her face defeated the beauty of the moon. There was no comparison to her beauty. Her throat resembled a conch, her waist was thin, and her hips were large. As she entered within the assembly of the selected kings, everyone became struck with wonder seeing her charming beauty. They almost lost their external consciousness. Taking this opportunity, Samba quickly grabbed the bride, put her on his chariot, and drove her straight out of the city.

Realizing what had happened, all the assembled kings said, "Alas! Alas!" They then took up their weapons and called after Samba in anger with many bad words, "How dare you take away the bride? You are a thief. Don't you feel ashamed of stealing another's daughter! You belong to a family of thieves, hence it is your habit to steal."

Meanwhile, Duryodhana and his one hundred brothers too had taken up their weapons and challenged Samba. Even Yudhisthira, Bhima, Arjuna, Nakula, Sahadeva, Bhisma, Drona, Krpa, Karna, and others too came running to the spot. They all surrounded Samba and challenged him to fight. Samba alone fought bravely with all those powerful kings. He fought relentlessly with them as a powerful lion fights in the midst of elephants. The great archer Samba counteracted all the arrows shot at him by the powerful archers. Unable to defeat him in any way, the kings united to make a plan.

Out of frustration, Duryodhana and some great warriors defeated the great hero Samba in an unlawful fight and arrested him. Duryodhana took him to his palace and put him behind bars after binding his legs with iron chains. Krishna heard all the news in Dwaraka and quickly began to make arrangements for a battle. He took four kinds of soldiers with Him. As Krishna was about to proceed in anger, an embarrassed Balarama tried to pacify Him.

Balarama said, "King Duryodhana is a respectable relative. Why are You so angry with him? Samba is still young and childish. He tried to forcibly take away Duryodhana's beautiful young daughter. The king has suitably punished him according to his fault. O Gadadhara! I think it is not proper for You to be angry with the king. If You order Me, I can go there Myself and bring the great hero Samba and his wife Laksmana to Dwaraka."

By speaking in this way, Balarama stopped Krishna from going, and He Himself got on a chariot and started for Hastinapura. Upon arriving in Hastinapura, Baladeva waited in a place and sent His messenger to inform King Duryodhana of His arrival. As soon as Duryodhana heard, he came with his friends and relatives and greeted Baladeva with great respect. After duly worshiping Balarama, Duryodhana invited Him to his palace.

Duryodhana inquired, "O Lord of the Yadu dynasty, What brings You here?" Balarama replied, "I have come here with a message from King Ugrasena. You arranged for a svayamvara of your daughter and Samba came here to participate in it. He invoked My power within himself and kidnapped your daughter from the assembly. You are a ksatriya yet you did not follow the etiquette of a ksatriya. You have arrested him illegally. There is no fighter who can defeat him in a legal fight. You are bragging after arresting him in an illegal fight. O friend! You have actually made a mistake and this time I am going to forgive you. Now quickly hand over Samba and your daughter to Me otherwise be prepared for a battle. The king will come with his army and destroy you. You had better obey the king's order. Bring your daughter quickly and let Me return."

Balarama's words made King Duryodhana breathe heavily and shiver in anger. He repeatedly looked at Balarama's strong body and hissed like a python. He remarked, "Just because You are Balarama, I tolerate this insult. If it was anyone else, I would have sent him to the house of Yamaraja. Ugrasena calls himself an emperor, but actually who knows him anyway? He too belittles me. I can't take it anymore. Moreover it's my bad luck that You are involved in this matter and have come to me personally. Considering You my superior, I restrain myself. Now go back home, and tell Ugrasena to come and fight with me."

These statements of Duryodhana made Baladeva furious. He said, "I alone can defeat all of you. Ugrasena is respected by all the great kings of the world and you treat him lightly."

Duryodhana became angrier after hearing these words. As he left the spot in an angry mood, he began to verbally abuse King Ugrasena.

Baladeva had remained patient so far, but when Duryodhana used filthy language to speak about Ugrasena, He could not control His anger any more. Realizing that Duryodhana had committed a serious insult to His king, Baladeva took up His plough and decided to throw the entire Hastinapura into the water of the Ganges. He exhibited His supernatural power which resembles the fury of annihilation and struck the southern side of the ground of Hastinapura with His plough. By the influence of His immense power, the whole earth began to shake. The entire tract of land of Hastinapura, along with the palace and the inhabitants, was about to submerge in the waters of the Ganges.

Everyone in Hastinapura, old and young, men and women, all became frightened. They soon realized that the impending destruction was an act of Baladeva, and they had no hope of living through it. All the inhabitants ran here and there in utter confusion, and screamed in fear. There was a huge commotion in the city as people ran for their lives.

All the prominent personalities like Bhisma, Drona, Dhrtarastra, and Krpacarya gathered together and began to offer prayers to Balarama in order to subside His anger.

They prayed, "You are Lord Narayana. You are the controller of the universe. You alone are the cause of creation, maintenance and annihilation of the material world. There is nothing but You within the entire creation. You have incarnated to diminish the burden of the earth. You are the Lord of millions of universes. So what is the big deal for You to destroy a tiny city like Hastinapura? Duryodhana has certainly misbehaved with You and aroused Your anger. Please be pacified and be merciful to all of us. The whole earth can be vanquished simply by a fraction of Your anger. So what is this King Duryodhana before You?"

When Baladeva heard these prayers from the leaders of Hastinapura, He smiled and withdrew His plough. In this way, the city of Hastinapura was saved from being ruined by the wrath of Baladeva. Even today one can see that this city is situated on the bank of Ganges tilted towards north. Since Balarama inserted His plough from the south, the entire tract of land leaned towards the north. One can also see today the split mark on the ground created by Balarama's plough.

Thereafter King Duryodhana invited Baladeva to his palace with great respect and offered obeisances to Him. He had Him bathed with scented water and gave Him a comfortable place to sit. He offered Balarama a nice feast and drinks. When Lord Balarama was thus worshiped and satisfied, Duryodhana released Samba and brought him before Balarama.

On the advice of Baladeva, Duryodhana let his daughter marry Samba. As a matter of gratitude, Duryodhana donated many elephants, horses, servants, and maidservants to the newlywed couple. He also gave two hundred girls, all well dressed and decorated. Then Baladeva along with Samba and Laksmana mounted on a chariot and left for Dwaraka, the king saw them off as they drove away from Hastinapura. Duryodhana could not hide his tears of separation as he watched his daughter leave.

In the due course of time, Baladeva reached the city of Dwaraka. All the inhabitants became jubilant and shouted, "Jaya! Jaya!" Baladeva handed over the new bride and groom to Govinda and Jambavati, who became very happy in their company.

O readers! Just go on hearing the topics of the Supreme Lord. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates tales of Balarama's extraordinary power.

## The Rasa-lila pastimes of Baladeva and the killing of Dvivida gorilla.

Krishna, who wears a garland of forest flowers, lived joyfully in Dwaraka. He enjoyed many pastimes in the company of His friends and relatives. Suddenly, one day Balarama remembered the forests of Vrndavana and decided to visit there. He mounted on a chariot and went to Vrndavana.

Upon arriving in Vrndavana, He first offered His obeisances to Nanda and Yasoda. All the inhabitants of Gokula gathered together with great eagerness to see Him. Baladeva spent some time in Vrndavana where He enjoyed rasa-lila pastimes with the young cowherd girls of Vraja. One day while enjoying rasa-lila pastimes in an intoxicated mood, He felt thirsty, so He ordered the river Yamuna to come nearer so He could quench His thirst. But seeing Baladeva in that condition, Yamuna refused to come any closer to Him. This made Baladeva furious with anger. He picked up His plough and pulled the river forcibly to Him with His plough. The bank of Yamuna was broken and split as to make the water flow freely by the spot where Baladeva was enjoying the rasa dance. The personified Yamuna trembled in fear seeing Baladeva's anger. Since then, the river Yamuna has flowed towards Vrndavana. Baladeva quenched His thirst by drinking her waters and enjoyed various water sports in the association of the gopis.

Another day, Baladeva was busy relishing different pastimes with His cowherd girl friends in a forest. By chance, a gorilla called Dvivida used to live there. He was very mischievous and always disturbed the penances of the great sages. He created havoc in that forest. Dvivida gorilla came before Baladeva and His girl friends and ridiculed them by showing his red anus. Baladeva, who was already intoxicated with pride, became very angry at the gorilla. He took up His weapon and chased the gorilla within the forest. Dvivida Vanara uprooted a big tree and challenged Baladeva to a fight. A fierce battle ensued. Finally Baladeva hit him so hard that the gorilla fell flat to the ground unconscious. In this way, Baladeva killed the naughty and sinful Dvivida gorilla. The denizens of heaven and the sages glorified Him with lavish prayers.

## Narada witnesses the extraordinary pastimes of Krishna

Krishna continued to enjoy various pastimes in Dwaraka with His children and grandchildren. One day, Narada Muni visited the city of Dwaraka and was struck with wonder seeing Krishna engaging in different activities in His different palaces. In one palace, he saw Krishna sitting with Rukmini and engaging in deep meditation. Narada then went to the house of Satyabhama where he saw Krishna placing one of His children on His own lap. Then Narada went to the house of Jambavati where he saw Krishna taking His meal. From there, he went to the palace of Kalindi. Lord Krishna was sleeping on a couch there. Thereafter, Narada went to the house of Mitravinda and was surprised to see Krishna playing chess with His queen. Narada felt very joyful seeing these different pastimes of the Lord simultaneously taking place. Then, he went to the palace of Bhadravati where he saw Krishna along with His sons and grandsons watching the dancing and singing of professional dancers.

Narada then visited the house of Laksmana and saw Laksmana applying sandalwood pulp on the body of Krishna. From there, the great sage went to the house of Nagnajiti where he found Gadadhara taking rest on a couch. One by one, Narada visited all the houses of the sixteen thousand one hundred and eight queens. He was simply amazed to see Krishna's opulence. He took pleasure seeing the Lord in different positions at different houses. He considered himself fortunate to be able to witness such astonishing pastimes of Krishna. He realized how Krishna had expanded Himself into sixteen thousand one hundred and eight forms. His whole body became filled with symptoms of ecstatic love. Tears flowed down from his eyes as he considered that today his life had become successful.

## The killing of king Srgala Vasudeva

One day, Sri Krishna was sitting along with King Ugrasena and others in the assembly hall known as Sudharma Sabha. The doorkeeper entered the assembly and announced that a Srgala Vasudeva had sent a messenger. Krishna smiled and ordered the doorkeeper to bring the messenger in. The messenger came in, stood before everyone with folded hands, and read out the message he had brought from his king.

"I am known by all people as Vasudeva. I am always adorned with a conch, a disc, a club, and a lotus. I am the undisputed king of the whole world. Conch, disc, club, and lotus are my ornaments. How dare You put on my symbols? You belong to a low-caste and live on the bank of the ocean. You enjoy life with another's possessions. Think for a moment who You are, and give up my symbols. Surrender my weapons and hand them over to my messenger. If You fail to follow my order, then I will personally come and kill You."

Hearing this silly message, Krishna and everyone else could not hide their laughter. Krishna said to the messenger, "Go and tell your master to come quickly. I have put on his symbols out of fun, and I will relinquish them one by one in front of him."

The messenger left Dwaraka and returned to Paundraka Nagara and narrated everything that Krishna had said to the king. The king was furious with anger and he decided to attack Dwaraka with his demoniac friend Kasiraja. They took many weapons, horses, elephants, and chariots with them. Paundraka Vasudeva took up his symbols as well conch, disc, club, and lotus and started for Dwaraka.

As soon as the news of their arrival in Dwaraka reached Krishna, Krishna immediately took a chariot and went to challenge the impostor god. A formidable battle erupted between them. Krishna said to the Paundraka, "You have asked Me to give up your weapons. Here you go, take this. It's your weapon." Saying this, Krishna released His Sudarsana cakra. The infallible cakra swung into action and severed the head of Paundraka from his body. The king immediately fell to the ground dead. Then Kasiraja came forward to fight with Krishna, so Krishna decided to make fun of him. He decided to kill Kasiraja in an unusual manner.

The Supreme Personality of Godhead Krishna picked up His cakra and cut the entire body of Kasiraja into pieces. The shoulder fell to the ground; the lower body fell onto the body of Paundraka, while the head fell right in front of the king's wife and son. Previously they were laughing and joking, but suddenly the king's head fell right before them. They were horrified to see this. They could not believe their eyes. A tumultuous noise of crying filled all the direction. Kasiraja's wife and son plunged into a pool of lamentation. Realizing his position, the son quickly shook off his grief, equipped himself with weapons, and left for Dwaraka to seek revenge on his father's death.

But as he entered Dwaraka, he saw Krishna coming towards him with a cakra in His hand. The son of Kasiraja ran away in fear. He then made a plan to perform severe penance to please Siva. He did exactly that and satisfied Mahadeva. Mahadeva appeared before him, and said, "O king, I am pleased by your austerity. Now ask me for any benediction you wish." The prince then folded his hands and spoke. "O my Lord! Please tell me the way by which I can defeat my father's killer. Please give me some powerful fiery weapon so that I can destroy Dwaraka and avenge my father's death." Siva who is easily pleased by his devotees agreed and gave him his desired boon.

When Kasiraja's son invoked the new weapon, a giant fiery personality appeared from a blazing fire with a trident in his hand. As the giant moved towards Dwaraka, all the inhabitants began to feel the heat. They ran here and there and shouted, "O Krishna! Please protect us!" On hearing their pathetic cry, Krishna immediately assured them of safety. He took up His cakra and ran towards that fiery creature. Krishna released His cakra on that creature. Unable to bear the scorching heat of the cakra, the fiery creature returned to Kasi. Since he could not use his fire on Dwaraka, he had to burn something, so he had no option but to burn the city of Kasi to ashes. Along with Kasi, the king of Kasi was burnt to ashes, too. The residents of Dwaraka glorified Krishna and worshiped Him with love. They were amazed to see this wonderful act of Krishna.

## Krishnas pastime as an ideal king

In Dwaraka, Krishna lived joyfully in the company of His friends, children, grandchildren, and His queens. Although He is the Supreme Lord, He set the example of a perfect householder. Every morning, Krishna would get up early and practice meditation. He followed all the duties of a householder as prescribed in the Vedic literatures. In the early morning, He would put on a fresh set of clothes and sit down to meditate on His own self. Thereafter, He brushed His teeth and took a bath nicely, followed by offering oblations to the ancestors. After taking His bath, He would offer respects to His elders. In this way, He satisfied everyone by His exemplary behavior. After this, Krishna would sit down in a chariot arranged by His servant Daruka, and go out for inspection of the city. As He left, professional dancers would dance, singers would sing, and musicians would play different musical instruments. Professional reciters would chant Vedic mantras and other glorifications. "Jaya! Jaya!" Would be heard in the four directions. As Krishna left His palace and went to the assembly hall, many beautiful women would throw flowers all over His body.

Sitting on His chariot, Krishna would come directly at the gate of the Sudharma assembly hall accompanied by His well-wishers. After taking His seat, Krishna would please everyone in various ways. Thereafter, He will take up the day's agenda item by item. He would discuss religious matters, royal matters, and other subject matters.

One day, when He was sitting in the assembly hall, a messenger arrived. He offered his obeisances to Krishna and said, "O Govinda! When Jarasandha came to fight with You, there were some other kings who refused to join Jarasandha in his endeavor to fight You. As a result of this, Jarasandha became angry with them and arrested them. Now these kings are suffering behind bars under the care of Jarasandha. O Krishna! These kings have no hope for life. Therefore they are constantly thinking about Your lotus feet. Please deliver them. If You do not deliver them, then who will?"

## The conversation between Narada and Yudhisthira

Just then, the great sage Narada arrived at the royal court of Krishna, who along with others, immediately stood up respectfully. Krishna washed the lotus feet of Narada and offered him padya and arghya. With folded hands, Krishna then asked, "O sage! What brings you here? If you do not mind, please tell Me, what is the matter?" Being asked by Krishna, Narada who is enriched with penance replied, "I came to Your place as a messenger. I saw King Pandu sitting outside the gate of Indra's palace. When I asked him why he was sitting outside and not inside the assembly of the demigods, he replied with humility, 'I am not qualified to enter within the assembly because I have not performed enough pious activities. It's good that I have met you. O sage! You can convey my message to my children. Although each of my sons is able to conquer the whole earth, I am sitting here at the doorstep of heaven unable to enter inside. Only if my sons perform a Rajasuya sacrifice will I be able to enter the gates of heaven and sit in the company of Indra.'

"Thereafter I went to Hastinapura and conveyed this message of Pandu to his sons headed by Yudhisthira. Yudhisthira was so unhappy at hearing this shocking news that he fell to the ground unconscious. After some time, when he regained consciousness, he asked me how he could perform a Rajasuya sacrifice and repay his debts to his father. I was very sad to see the condition of both Pandu and Yudhisthira, so I advised Yudhisthira to make arrangement for the Rajasuya sacrifice.

## Yudhisthira begs for Krishna's mercy in his Rajasuya Sacrifice

"I told him, 'The lotus-eyed Sri Krishna is the Lord of the universe. At present, He has descended in this world to diminish the burden of the earth. He is very kind to you, so much so that He considers you as one of His relatives. If somehow or other you can obtain His support, then you can undoubtedly conquer the three worlds.' When I advised Yudhisthira like this, he came to his senses and immediately sent me to You as a messenger. O Krishna! You know everything. Please see that the Rajasuya sacrifice is conducted without any impediment. Do not delay any more. Come quickly to Hastinapura."

When Krishna heard these words, He quickly called Uddhava and discussed this matter with him. When Krishna asked, "What is the best course of action in this situation?" Uddhava replied, "O Lord of the universe! By the performance of Rajasuya sacrifice, two missions can be accomplished. The kings who are confined in the prison of Jarasandha will be released, and King Jarasandha will be eliminated. Therefore You should immediately proceed towards Hastinapura. Let me tell You another secret as well. Jarasandha is a powerful king and is always surrounded by his countless soldiers. Indeed, he is not to be killed by You. So, You, Bhima, and Arjuna should meet him in the guise of sannyasis and, with the pretext of begging alms, should beg to fight with him. This is the perfect way of killing Jarasandha." On hearing this plan, Krishna became so pleased that He embraced Uddhava with great affection.

## Krishna goes to Hastinapura and meets Yudhisthira

Krishna made an announcement to the whole city of Dwaraka that He was leaving for Hastinapura for some time. He called Baladeva to His side and asked Him to take care of Dwaraka and her residents in His absence. Krishna then boarded a chariot along with His eight principal queens and started for Hastinapura. He did not forget to take the great sage Narada with Him. After passing many kingdoms, rivers, and fields, Krishna arrived in Hastinapura at the end of the day.

As soon as King Yudhisthira heard the news of Krishna's arrival, he immediately came out of the palace and greeted Krishna respectfully. Anticipating the visit. He had built a special palace for Krishna to stay in. This palace was decorated beautifully with golden pitchers. As Krishna entered the city, the ladies showered flowers on Him and made auspicious sounds. Krishna offered His respectful obeisances to King Yudhisthira and Bhima. He embraced Arjuna, and blessed Nakula and Sahadeva. Within the palace Krishna offered obeisances to Kunti and blessed Draupadi. Kunti was especially pleased to see her brother's son. Tears of affection flowed down from her eyes. All the eight queens headed by Rukmini and Satyabhama also offered their obeisances to Kunti and Draupadi. After the greetings were over, Kunti took all of Krishna's queens into her chamber. She had them take bath and then fed them a sumptuous meal. She offered various gifts and ornaments to Krishna's queens and entertained them by arranging a party of singers and dancers.

## The arrangement for the Rajasuya Sacrifice

Next morning, King Yudhisthira spoke to Krishna and narrated to Him all different tales in great unhappiness. He mentioned that it was Krishna's mercy by which they were surviving despite having to face all kinds of disturbances. He said, "O Lord! If I can perform a Rajasuya sacrifice, my father will be delivered. Therefore, I seek Your assistance in this regard. O Lord of the three worlds, if You help me then I can certainly perform the sacrifice successfully. If I cannot do this for the benefit of my father then it is better for me to die."

When Yudhisthira spoke like this in great humility, Krishna solaced him by saying, "O dear king! Why do you speak like that? All of you five brothers are no ordinary persons. Each one of you is capable of conquering the whole world. I assure you that the Rajasuya sacrifice will be performed successfully and without any hindrance. You should send your four brothers to the four directions and let them conquer everyone they meet in the course of their journeys. In the meantime, you can arrange for the necessary wealth, manpower, and other items required for conducting the sacrifice. O crest jewel among all kings, I will be glad to help you in every possible way."

According to the instructions of Krishna, Yudhisthira sent Bhima along with some soldiers towards the west. He sent Arjuna to the north, Sahadeva to the east, and Nakula to the southern direction. These four brothers conquered all the kings in their respective directions, and brought back huge amount of wealth and followers to Hastinapura. King Yudhisthira was very pleased by the heroic activities of his brothers.

## Krishna makes a plan to deliver the arrested kings

On another day, Krishna took Yudhisthira to a solitary place and discussed with him how to bring back those kings who had been forcibly imprisoned by Jarasandha. Krishna said, "O king, it does not look good to perform a Rajasuya sacrifice without the presence of these noble and great kings. We have to devise a plan to somehow deliver about twenty thousand eight hundred kings who were arrested by the king of Magadha. If we can kill Jarasandha then all these kings will be ours. In fact, they will be subordinate to you and serve you in all respects. However, Jarasandha has got a vast army, and no one is able to defeat him directly. I do have a plan to eliminate Jarasandha, though. For this I will need two of your brothers, Bhima and Arjuna. The three of us will defeat Jarasandha and bring back all the arrested kings."

After hearing these words, Yudhisthira replied, "My dear Krishna! I have always regarded Your words like God. Bhima and Arjuna are as good as my own life. I am a king on the strength of these two brothers of mine. These two brothers always protect me. If they fall into danger then I will certainly give up my body. Besides Jarasandha is a powerful king. How can anyone defeat him alone? People are afraid to even enter his city. I have many doubts in my mind, and also fear that all three of you are going together to face such dangers. Anyway, what more can I say? You do whatever You feel is right."

To this, Krishna said, "O king! There is nothing to fear as long as I am there. Just give Me your two brothers and do not worry about them. We will kill Jarasandha and return to Hastinapura quickly." Very soon, Krishna, Bhima, and Arjuna took permission from the king and left for Magadha after circumambulating Yudhisthira. All three of them discarded their royal dress and put on a loin cloth and chadar. They carried sticks and waterpots in their hands and looked exactly like sannyasis. They held broken umbrellas over their heads and wore old slippers on their feet. As the three ksatriyas disguised as sannyasis walked slowly towards Magadha, Bhima suddenly asked Krishna, "Why this fellow is called Jarasandha?"

## The tales of Jarasandha's birth

In reply to Bhima's inquiry, Krishna said, "Now listen as I describe the reason behind the name Jarasandha. Bmadratha was a great king of Magadha. He had no son though he had been married for a long time. The great king performed many sacrifices and gave huge amounts of wealth in charity, But still he had no issue. He was very sad. One day, the great sage Durvasa came to his house. As usual, the pious king worshiped the sage with proper etiquette by offering padya and arghya. Being satisfied by the king's hospitality, the sage said, 'O king! I wish to give you a benediction. Ask me for any boon you want from me.' The king folded his hands and replied, 'O lord! I have everything by your mercy. But I have only one distress, and that is I have no son.' Saying this, King Bmadratha fell at the feet of Durvasa and begged for a son.

The kindhearted sage comforted the king, saying, 'O king! Do not lament. You will have a son. Do as I say. Perform a sacrifice, while controlling your senses and the mind, and very soon you will be blessed with a powerful son.'

Being advised by the sage, the king immediately called for the qualified brahmanas and began the sacrifice. On the day when the sacrifice was completed, the king offered oblations with great respect. He satisfied everyone present in the sacrificial arena with appropriate gifts. Finally, the sage gave him a fruit and instructed the king to feed it to his good wife. With the fruit in his hand, the king immediately went entered his palace, but while entering he thought, 'how can I give one fruit to my two wives? If I give the fruit to one of them, then the other one will die with envy.' Thinking in this way, the king cut the fruit into two pieces and gave one piece each to his wives. His wives joyfully followed the order of their husband.

By the arrangement of providence, the two wives became pregnant in due course of time. On an auspicious day, after the period of ten months and ten days, they each gave birth to a child. But alas! The child that they gave birth to were only half the normal size and looked extremely ugly. Each part of the half-child had only one eye, half a nose, one hand, and one leg. Both of the parts of the bodies on each half-baby looked similar. When the king of Magadha heard this strange news, he became disgusted and immediately ordered his men to throw away this child who had been born split in half into the jungle. By the king's order, the two separate parts of the child were placed in a basket and thrown under a bamboo bush. Even though the child had been rejected and thrown into a bush, he was protected by the Lord.

There lived a witch by the name Jara in that city. Whenever there was a miscarriage in the city, this witch Jara would make a feast on the remains. This time too she came quickly to eat the embryo, but when she saw the strange-looking child in two parts, she thought, 'Someone must have cut this newborn baby into two pieces.' So she took the half-child's pieces in her hands, and looked top and bottom, but could not find any mark of cutting. Suddenly, she had an inspiration, and joined the two split parts of the body together, and to her surprise, a beautiful baby was formed. The baby began to cry. Jara was perplexed to see this wonder as she had never seen such a thing before. Although she had eaten thousands of dead babies, this time, she decided not to eat the baby. Recognizing the baby as the son of her king, she thought, 'the king must be very unlucky. After such a hard labor, he finally got a baby boy, but somehow he could not keep him. Therefore I will not eat this fortunate boy for because of me, he has been revived.'

Thinking like this, Jara took the child to the king's palace and narrated the whole incidence to the king. She said, 'O king! My only business is to eat babies who die after death. When I heard that there was a miscarriage in your palace, I came in search of the dead child. I found the child in the bush but was surprised to see the baby split in half. As soon as I joined the two parts together, the baby came back to life. I took pity on the child, and decided not to eat him. O king! I spared the life of your son. Now take him back.'

When King Bmadratha heard these words, he was amazed and could not believe his own eyes. He rewarded Jara with a hefty amount of wealth. He fed her sumptuously and gave her many gifts. He also said, 'As long as you are alive and you live in this city, you should come to my palace and eat whatever you wish.' The inhabitants of Magadha became filled with joy by this news. The two qualified mothers were given the responsibility of looking after the child together. It was the arrangement of the providence that the child will have two mothers and that a witch named Jara would join the two parts of the body and thus give the child new life. For this reason, the child was addressed as Jarasandha. Now he is a powerful king and easily defeats the other kings. This is the story behind the name Jarasandha."

## Krishna deceives Jarasandha in the guise of a sannyasI

By the time Krishna finished narrating the story, the three warriors had arrived at the city of Jarasandha. In this way, while discussing the tales of Jarasandha's birth, Krishna, Bhima and Arjuna arrived at the city of Magadha. For a few days, all three of them lived in the city like mendicants so people could treat them just like saints. Understanding that the king was somewhat kind to the brahmanas, Krishna, Bhima, and Arjuna approached the king in the guise of saintly persons to beg for alms. It was the day after Ekadasi, so they thought it would be a good idea to beg for alms.

They entered within the king's palace through the main gate and stood before the king. As soon as the king saw these saintly persons at his residence, he quickly stood up with reverence and offered them due respects. The king worshiped them properly by offering padya and arghya. Then he offered them a nice place to sit. After they were seated comfortably the king inquired, "O best of brahmanas! What brings you here? Tell me what can I do for you?"

To this, Krishna deceitfully replied, "We have heard that you are a noble and charitable person. Hence we have come here to beg something from you. We belong to a foreign country and are suffering from poverty. Everyone in the world glorifies you as a liberal-minded person. People say that King Jarasandha gives charity without any hesitation. He fulfills everyone's desires quickly. That is why we have come here. O king! If you have no objection then we may ask you for something. Moreover, previously the King Avanti gave his entire kingdom the whole earth in charity and thus his fame remained immortal even today."

The king was really surprised to hear these statements, and looked at the bodies of the sannyasis with some confusion. He thought, "These men are dressed as brahmanas though they have bodies similar to that of ksatriyas. Besides I can see some marks of injury caused by weapons on their bodies. Are they really sannyasis? Or are they three great warriors? It seems I have seen them before? I have fought with them somewhere before? I am quite sure they are not sannyasis. They may have come here like this to deceive me. Anyhow, whether they are ksatriyas or brahmanas, whether they ask for my kingdom or my life, I will satisfy them. All those kings who had previously given charities are glorified in this world, even today. Bali was a famous king. Lord Visnu deceived him in the guise of a dwarf and took away everything from him. Even though his spiritual master Sukracarya forbade him to do so, he gave the Lord the three worlds in charity. As a result of that, he now lives happily in the Patalaloka, and people even today sing of his good deeds."

After contemplating in this way, King Jarasandha said, "I will gladly give anything you ask me." To this, Krishna smiled and said, "Will you fight with us alone?" King Jarasandha stood up in anger and said, "Certainly, but who are you all? Tell me the truth." Krishna again replied, "O king! This is Bhimasena, and this is the great soul Arjuna. They are My cousin brothers. My name is Krishna, your branded enemy. Do you remember Me now?"

When Jarasandha heard this, he began to laugh sarcastically. He shouted, "O coward! Why have you come here again as a sannyasV. Don't you remember how You ran away from the battlefield? Don't You have any shame? How dare You want to fight with a ksatriya? Only a degraded ksatriya will want to fight with a cowherd like You. Look at this Arjuna! He is still young and immature. I cannot fight with someone who is not equal to me. If Bhima is ready, then I can fight with him. He looks somewhat equal to me. Therefore I advise all of you to go back home and not try to show your valor. Besides, what will I gain by killing innocent boys like you?"

## The fight between Bhima and Jarasandha

In reply to these foolish words of Jarasandha, Krishna could not hide His smile. He said, "Do not worry about us. Bhima will fight with you alone." Jarasandha then entered into the room where his weapons were kept, and brought out two powerful clubs. One he took himself and the other one he gave to Bhima. They all went outside the palace and selected a spot for the battle. The two great heroes took up their respective clubs and began to strike each other. People of the whole city gathered there to witness this wonderful fight. Even the demigods secretly began to watch this fight from the sky. Bhima and Jarasandha attacked each other from all directions, circling back around as they fought. Each hit the other hundreds of times with their clubs. They kicked and punched each other powerfully. Sometimes they would charge and crash into each other with their strong and broad chests. Even after fighting for a long Lime, neither one could defeat his opponent. Again they picked up their clubs and started the fight anew with even greater prowess. As the battle waged on, the two expert fighters strictly followed the rule of club fighting that one can only hit the opponent above the navel and never below.

## The killing of Jarasandha

After some time, Krishna tactfully called Bhima over to His side and said, "How is it that you have forgotten why the king was named Jarasandha? Don't you remember how the body of Jarasandha was joined together by a witch named Jara?" Although Krishna gave this hint to Bhima, Bhima could not understand it, as his mind was too absorbed in the spirit of fighting. Then, Krishna took a blade of grass and split it into two pieces in front of Bhima. It was then that Bhima finally understood the mystery and the way to kill Jarasandha. Suddenly, Bhima dropped his club on the ground and grabbing hold of Jarasandha's feet, he pulled him down. Unable to stay balanced, Jarasandha fell flat to the ground. Bhima took this opportunity to tightly catch hold of Jarasandha's two legs. With his powerful bare hands, he tore Jarasandha's legs apart just as one tears a piece of cloth. Thus the body of the king of Magadha split open into two pieces. As soon as this happened, the whole city of Magadha plunged into confusion and lamentation. There was a terrible noise which made everyone scared. Krishna began to dance in ecstasy, and the demigods began to shower flowers on Bhima. All the pious people chanted "Jaya! Jaya!" because of the death of Jarasandha.

The demigods returned to their respective residences after being fully satisfied. By nature, Bhima was very powerful, and he became invincible because of the presence of Krishna. Since Jarasandha left his body in the presence of Krishna, he got a four-armed spiritual body and returned to Vaikuntha. After that, Krishna called Jarasandha's son and solaced him in various ways. He made him the king of Magadha and gave him the name Sahadeva. Krishna then released all the kings who had been illegally arrested by Jarasandha. The kings were extremely grateful to Krishna for delivering them. They consider themselves fortunate to be able to see Krishna who was decorated with a club, a disc, a lotus, and a conch. With gratitude, the kings folded their hands and prayed to Krishna.

"O Supreme Lord! It is actually very good that Jarasandha arrested us and put us in the prison. That is why today we have been able to see Your lotus feet. O Lord! You are very kind, but we could not recognize You being maddened by the pride of our positions. We must have performed some pious activities in our previous lives for now we see You face to face. Now our material bondage has been destroyed. Please deliver us; we surrender unto You." Krishna then ordered the young prince Sahadeva to take care of these kings properly. The prince quickly arranged for their baths and satisfied all of them by offering sandalwood pulp, flower garlands, and jewels. Thereafter, Krishna put each of the kings on a separate chariot and sent them off to participate in the Rajasuya sacrifice that was to be performed by King Yudhisthira. After the kings had left Magadha, Krishna sat down on the chariot of Jarasandha and He too departed for Hastinapura. But before leaving, Krishna carefully admonished young prince Sahadeva, "Now you are the king of Magadha. Be responsible and maintain your subjects properly. I invite you to attend the Rajasuya sacrifice of Yudhisthira."

Hearing these divine instructions, Sahadeva fell at the feet of Krishna and offered his obeisances to Him. Krishna, Bhima, and Arjuna then moved towards Hastinapura. As they approached the city, they roared like lions and that sound destroyed all inauspiciousness. Yudhisthira's happiness knew no bounds. He came out of the palace and blessed all the three of them. He embraced them with affection. Krishna, Bhima, and Arjuna offered their obeisances to the king. Krishna explained the whole incident of how they had killed Jarasandha and delivered all the kings. King Yudhisthira was very pleased by this and thought that it was a good beginning to his Rajasuya sacrifice. O human beings! Just hear these wonderful tales, and you will be relieved of all lamentation and distress. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates the accounts of killing of Jarasandha.

## The commencement of the Rajasuya Sacrifice

On an auspicious day and at an auspicious time, King Yudhisthira along with Krishna, Bhima, Arjuna, and others began to perform the sacrifice. First of all, Yudhisthira invited the Maya demon and worshiped him because it was Maya who had built an excellent palace and royal assembly for Yudhisthira long ago. It so happened that the palace built by Maya was in fact more beautiful and opulent than the palace of Indra. Once, when Krishna, Yudhisthira, and others were sitting in that house, Duryodhana came there. The palace was filled with illusions, and had been built in such a way that upon entering, Duryodhana became completely perplexed. As he made his way into the courtyard, he stepped forward and fell into some water, thinking it to be land. Then after climbing out of the illusory pool, he walked further into the garden, but was deceived again. This time, he mistook the land to be water and thus lifted his cloth to keep it dry. His blundering made Draupadi and other ladies who had been secretly watching him laugh loudly and reveal themselves while poor Duryodhana had imagined himself almost dead from the confusion and the fall. Tactful Yudhisthira however rushed to greet his guest and gently picked him up. He made him sit comfortably and called his servants to help him change his clothes and put on jeweled ornaments.

King Yudhisthira called his astrologer to find out the most auspicious time for the commencement of the sacrifice. When all the great sages and the demigods along with their disciples and grand disciples assembled in the sacrificial arena, King Yudhisthira greeted and worshiped each of them one by one. He worshiped Parasara, Suka, Vyasa, Agastya, Vasistha, Dhaumya, the son of Renuka, Durvasa, Kaundilya, the great sage Narada, Atreya, Suta, and many other renowned personalities. He also worshiped Bhisma, Drona, Krpa, Dhrtarastra and his one hundred sons, Sisupala, Salva, Sailya, the king of Karusa, Kasiraja, Matsyaraja, and Karna as well as many other top class, middle class, and low class kings. He worshiped each of them according to their social and spiritual status. As Yudhisthira sat down to begin the sacrifice, all the assembled kings and sages blessed him. King Duryodhana was given the responsibility of looking after the treasury, while Kama was given the charge of giving charity. Bhimasena was given the responsibility of cooking, and Sahadeva was told to welcome all the invited guests. In this way, everyone was given some kind of responsibility. At the auspicious time, King Yudhisthira began to offer oblations to the sacrificial fire after worshiping all the brahmanas properly. Everyone who participated in the sacrifice was given many jewels and other ornaments as gifts.

## Choosing the suitable candidate for worship

King Yudhisthira had his servants bring all the jeweled ornaments and dresses to be offered to the guests. But he could not decide to whom the first gifts should be offered. He respectfully asked for the opinion of the assembled personalities, but everyone kept silent. Finally, Sahadeva stood up and spoke without any partiality, "There is one suitable candidate for receiving the adoration and gifts first." Sahadeva said. "He is the Lord of the three worlds. He is very much present here. He is the Supreme Lord, Sri Krishna. By His mercy, living entities become delivered from the material existence. Who else can be more qualified than Him? O king! Do not feel astonished. Please take padva and arghya and worship Krishna with full attention."

When Sahadeva spoke like this, Bhima also got up and said, "I totally agree with whatever Sahadeva has just said. We meditate on the Supreme Lord daily and that Supreme Lord is directly present before us. Therefore, He must be worshiped first by all means. He has incarnated in this world to decrease the burden of the earth. He is the Lord and master of the whole material creation. It is by His mercy that we can become delivered. So, how can one worship anyone else when He is present personally? O king! You are indeed fortunate. There is no limit to your good fortune. By your mercy, the whole city of Hastinapura is going to be delivered. This place is non-different from Vaikuntha because Lord Hari is present here."

After hearing these words of Bhima, Yudhisthira stood up with padya and arghya, and offered them at the lotus feet of Sri Krishna. He also offered the Lord fine yellow silk cloth and various ornaments. He decorated the entire body of Krishna with many valuable ornaments. After washing Krishna's lotus feet with padya and arghya, the king sprinkled some of that water on his own head and considered his life to be successful. All his family members also sprinkled that water on their heads.

## Sisupala blasphemes Krishna

When Sisupala saw Krishna's being given such worship, he could not control his anger. He became envious of Krishna. He stood up in an angry mood and spoke harshly about Krishna in the assembly. "This King Yudhisthira is performing the sacrifice uselessly," Sisupala said. "He has been influenced by this fellow Krishna, who is like a eunuch. Instead of worshiping many other qualified kings, Yudhisthira has chosen this Krishna as the recipient of the first worship. How can we tolerate the worship of an inferior person before superior persons? It is hard to tell whether Krishna is a cowherd or a ksatriya. Who knows which caste He belongs to? Yet, Yudhisthira is offering adoration to Him first. This fellow left His own residence and went to the shore of the ocean to live. We all know that only the outcasts live on the seashore. He has many bad habits. Since His childhood, He has been a thief. He used to steal His friend's wife. He killed many kings deceitfully."

Sisupala continued, "There was a king named Naraka in this world, and this fellow killed him unlawfully. Everyone knows about this incident. Naraka gathered many princesses to marry them together, and this Krishna killed Naraka and took away all those girls for Himself. Just recently, He went to the palace of Jarasandha in the guise of a sannyasi and killed him deceitfully. He could not stand before Jarasandha during many previous encounters. He even left Mathura out of fear of Jarasandha. Once, He was fighting with Kalayavana, but He was so afraid of being defeated that He left the battlefield like a jackal. He tactfully led Kalayavana to a cave where Mucukunda was sleeping. In this way, He disturbed the sleep of Mucukunda, who out of anger burnt Kalayavana into ashes. This Krishna cannot do anything Himself. He always takes the help of others and gets His job done. Not only that, He is supposed to be a subject of King kamsa, yet He took away kamsa's life. kamsa was unprotected, for he had no idea that he would be killed by his own subject. I am not telling any lie, for everyone knows the real truth. How dare Yudhisthira offer his first worship to a eunuch!"

Sisupala continued, "Listen! O Salva! O Dantavakra! O Kasiraja! Let us go immediately. We have been insulted by King Yudhisthira." After speaking these words, Sisupala breathed heavily and burned with anger. As soon as Bhima and Arjuna heard these blasphemous words, they immediately picked up their weapons. Nakula, Sahadeva, and all the other supporter of Yudhisthira were ready to take Sisupala's life. Soon Sisupala and his supporters also picked up their weapons and began to fight. Krishna watched the fight for a while, and then personally intervened. He pacified both the parties by speaking politely to them.

## The secret behind Krishna's forgiving one hundred faults of Sisupala

"O Bhima and Arjuna!" Krishna said, "Remain calm and control your anger. Do not fight and listen carefully to what I say. This Sisupala is meant to be killed by Me, and I will kill him. You don't need to fight with him. I have to tolerate his criticism because I am duty bound to do so. I gave a word to his mother. When he was born, he had four arms. His parents and everyone else was frightened to see his extra limbs. At that time, Narada Muni came there and pacified his father by saying, 'O king! Do not lament. He will be a powerful demon and a powerful king in this world. Your son will cease to have four arms as soon as he meets his enemy who will kill him.' Speaking like this, Narada left."

"Sisupala's parents decided to invite all their friends and well-wishers to show their sons and thereby recognize their son's enemy. They sent their messenger and invited everyone for a party. I too went there with My parents. Sisupala's mother is My father's sister, hence we were invited. But as soon as he saw Me, he lost two of his four hands. When his mother saw this, she began to hate Me. She said, 'Today I have understood the real meaning of Narada's words. My son took birth as Your enemy, but despite this situation, I still have one request for You. Forgive one hundred faults committed by him.' I gave her the assurance that I would follow what she said. That is why I am quietly tolerating all his blasphemous words. Now that he has crossed the prescribed number of one hundred, its time for Me to destroy him."

## The killing of Sisupala

After speaking these words, Krishna released His Sudarsana cakra. The cakra immediately went up in the sky, and appeared more effulgent than millions of suns. The most powerful cakra then swiftly descended and cut off Sisupala's head in no time. All the assembled kings began to lament, "Alas! Alas!" The demigods began to shower flowers from the sky. Everyone watched as the soul of Sisupala left his body and entered the body of Krishna. After eliminating Sisupala, the cakra came back to Krishna. All the people who had gathered there were astonished to see this. When everyone inquired about this to Narada, he replied as follows.

## The birth of Sisupala

The great sage Narada narrated, "O king! There were two doorkeepers named Jaya and Vijaya in Vaikuntha. Once, when the great sages headed by Sanaka went to see Narayana in Vaikuntha, these two doorkeepers stopped and mistreated them. As a result, the sages cursed them, 'You two will have to take birth as human beings in the mortal world.' When Jaya and Vijaya wanted to know how they could return to Vaikuntha again, the sages told them, 'if you take birth as enemies of Visnu, then it will take fewer births than if you take birth as devotees of Visnu'. Because of that curse that same Jaya and Vijaya first took birth as Hiranyaksa and Hiranyakasipu. In that birth, Hiranyaksa was killed by Lord Varaha when He incarnated to deliver the earth. Hiranyakasipu was killed by Lord Nrsimhadeva. In their second birth, they came as brothers named Ravana and Kumbhakarna. Their father was Visvasrava and their mother was Nikasa. The Supreme Lord killed them in His form as Lord Ramacandra. In their last and final birth, they appeared as Sisupala and Dantavakra. Now by the cakra of the Lord, Sisupala has been killed. In their three births, the Supreme Lord has personally killed them and taken them back to their original place in Vaikuntha. You have seen with your own eyes how the soul of Sisupala entered the body of Krishna. O king! You are very fortunate, for Krishna considers you as His relative." After hearing these statements of Narada, Yudhisthira and his family members became so overwhelmed with emotion that they immediately fell down at the feet of Krishna and offered their respectful obeisances.

O human beings! The tales of sri Krishna Vijaya are simply wonderful and anyone who hears them certainly goes to Vaikuntha. Even by thinking about these pastimes, one is sure to achieve liberation. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates sri Krishna Vijaya.

## Salva fought with Lord Krishna

O readers! Now hear about the wonderful tales of the battle between Salva and Krishna. When the svayamvara ceremony of Rukmini took place, this King Salva was defeated by Krishna. Being humiliated by Krishna, Salva did not return to his residence, but went to the forest to perform penance. He wanted to please Siva by his penance and get the benediction from him that he would be able to kill Krishna. In the forest, he performed penance for twelve years. He stood on his head and did not eat anything. In this way, he worshiped Siva with his body, mind, and speech.

By nature, Siva becomes pleased very easily, so he appeared before Salva. King Salva suddenly regained his consciousness and immediately offered his obeisances to his master Siva. Siva was already satisfied by the prayer of Salva, so he requested Salva to ask for any benediction other than immortality. Salva was very happy by this gesture of Siva. He fell down at the feet of Siva and said, "O Lord! Give me the boon that no one will be able to defeat me in this world. Please also give me the benediction that I will build a castle in the sky and live there peacefully. If I am attacked, I should be able to defend myself from there."

The three-eyed Siva granted him his desired benediction. Salva constructed a huge illusory palace in the sky, and accumulated various powerful weapons for his own defense. Thereafter, Salva went to Dwaraka and began to shoot arrows and other weapons towards the city while remaining above the city in the sky, unseen by those below. People became confused because they could not understand who was shooting all these arrows at them and breaking their houses. There were not many people in Dwaraka at that time because most of them had gone to participate in the Rajasuya sacrifice of King Yudhisthira. Both Krishna and Balarama were not present in Dwaraka, hence all the inhabitants became more frightened.

When the great hero Pradyumna heard this commotion, he mounted a chariot, equipped himself with weapons, and went ahead to fight with Salva. He was supported by many other princes such as Samba, Aniruddha, Gada, Satyaki, and so on. When Salva saw these young princes, he proudly spoke to them, "O young boys! Why do you come to die here? The insects rush towards the blazing fire only to die. What will I gain by killing young and immature boys like you? Send your guardian Krishna here to fight with me. It is more profitable and worthwhile to kill Him."

When Pradyumna heard these words of Salva, he was filled with rage. In an angry mood, he said to Salva, "With my arrow, I will send you today to the house of Yamaraja. Krishna would not like to fight with a low creature like you." Thereafter, a fierce battle ensued between the two which went on for many days. Neither of them was able to defeat his opponent. Everyday, they would fight with each other.

Meanwhile, Krishna continued to help Yudhisthira in his endeavor to peacefully conduct the Rajasuya sacrifice. Suddenly Krishna saw a bad omen, and from this omen. He instantly realized that something inauspicious had taken place in Dwaraka. He understood that the sinful king Salva had attacked Dwaraka and that he was still there creating havoc. He informed Yudhisthira that somebody was trying to destroy Dwaraka, and that He had to go there immediately. Krishna then took leave of the king, boarded a chariot along with His eight queens, and departed for Dwaraka.

In the meantime, Pradyumna continued to fight with Salva with great enthusiasm. King Salva had a dear friend named Dyuman who came to help Salva in his fight. Dyuman came before Pradyumna and started fighting with him. He shot countless arrows at Pradyumna, so many that the entire sky became filled with his arrows. Pradyumna was not one to give up, though. He picked up his bow and arrow, and with his accurate shots, cut all the arrows of Dyuman into pieces. In response, Dyuman again shot a bunch of arrows at Pradyumna, who effortlessly destroyed all of them at once. Dyuman turned and picked up a fiery club and, rushing towards him, powerfully hit the chest of Pradyumna. This time Pradyumna was injured. He lost his consciousness and fell back on his chariot. His charioteer Daruka immediately took him away from the battlefield.

Pradyumna, however, after only a short rest, regained consciousness, and looked around to find himself away from the battleground. He angrily turned to Daruka and said, "Why did you do that? You are a disgrace to the family. You made me a coward. Anyone who runs away from the battlefield gets a bad name and becomes infamous. This is the first time such a thing has happened in our Yadu dynasty." To this, Daruka folded his hands and replied, "O Master! Please listen, I have not done anything against the rules prescribed in the scripture. It is said that if a warrior loses consciousness, it is the duty of the charioteer to take him away to a safe place and then, when the warrior regains consciousness, he can return to fight again. Please control your anger, and go back to fight once more."

## The killing of Dyuman

Pradyumna was placated and, taking a drink made of honey, he resumed his fighting. He roared like a lion, and began to shower arrows on Dyuman. Dyuman counteracted by shooting many arrows back at him. Pradyumna was very expert and powerful as well. He easily nullified all the weapons released by Dyuman. Finally, Pradyumna remembered his father Krishna, and released his cakra aiming at the head of Dyuman. The cakra was as brilliant as millions of suns. The cakra went up in the sky and then severed the head of Dyuman before returning to Pradyumna. Pradyumna became jubilant at seeing Dyuman's head roll on the ground. He roared in ecstasy. The death of Dyuman made King Salva furious. In an angry mood, the king himself began to shoot arrows at Pradyumna.

## Salva fights with Krishna.

At that moment, Lord Krishna arrived in Dwaraka in the company of His eight queens. He quickly sent all the queens home and personally went ahead to fight with Salva. Krishna began to shoot arrows at Salva so fiercely that Salva found it very difficult to counteract. Finding no other way to survive, Salva flew up in the sky and took shelter of his illusory palace. He began to release different weapons at Krishna while remaining invisible to everyone. Krishna was somewhat afflicted by these weapons. Taking his opportunity, Salva used magic to make it appear that he was attacking the chariot of Vasudeva, Krishna's father. He caught hold of illusory Vasudeva's hair and screamed, "O Krishna! Just see what I am going to do now to your father. You are very puffed up. Now I will send your father to the abode of Yamaraja right before you!"

Speaking these words, Salva cut off Vasudeva's head. The body and head of Vasudeva fell down to the ground. As soon as this happened, Devaki who had heard the news came rushing to that spot and began to cry loudly as her hair loosened. She picked up the dead body of her husband on her lap and cried pathetically. She lamented profusely, and said a few words to Krishna, "O child! How did this happen? How could You let anyone kill Your father in front of You? Arrange a funeral pyre so that I, along with Your dead father will enter into the fire. I have no desire to live anymore." Krishna too, became very emotional seeing His dead father and crying mother. He dropped His weapon in disgust. He could not understand how this had happened even though He was personally present there!

In the meantime, Salva roared like a lion and said, "You have been cheating many kings by fighting from Your palace in Dwaraka. But today, You are destined to die by my hands. Today I will destroy the whole city of Dwaraka by burning it to ashes. Today I will offer oblations with your own blood to all my relatives who had been unlawfully killed by You." Krishna did not say anything, but simply kept His head low. Suddenly, He remembered that this Salva has some mystic power by which he was able to create illusions. "His fighting is illusory," Krishna thought. "My father is not dead and that lady who is crying is not My mother Devaki. I am certain that all these incidents are illusory."

Krishna then destroyed the illusion created by Salva and prepared to kill him. Krishna washed His hands, feet, and mouth, took off His weapon, and mounted His chariot. He shouted at Salva, "Listen, O wicked king! Actually, you have no power of your own. You are simply making an illusion to deceive us. But now, I have understood your evil plan. For Me it will take only one arrow to send you to the house of Yamaraja." Saying this, Krishna simultaneously released ten arrows at Salva. In this way, He cut Salva's head into pieces. Krishna destroyed Salva's illusory palace in the sky and also killed all his soldiers. As soon as Salva was dead, the denizens of heaven showered flowers on Krishna and chanted, "Jaya! Jaya!" This is how Krishna killed Salva and returned home with great pride. Krishna's fighting with Salva was very extraordinary because Salva had the power to bewilder anyone.

## The marriage of Aniruddha

Krishna lived joyfully in Dwaraka in the company of His queens, sons, and grandsons. He was especially very fond of His grandson Aniruddha. One day, Rukmini spoke to Krishna in a humble manner, "My dear Lord, kindly pay attention to my words. I know my brother Rukmi had offended You. O lotus-eyed! Please forgive his offense. He has a desire to arrange for his granddaughter to marry Your grandson Aniruddha. If You give permission, and agree to this proposal, then kindly proceed to his house along with the groom." When Rukmini requested like this Krishna agreed instantly. In due course of time, Krishna went to the palace of Rukmi who lived in the kingdom called Bhojakata. Baladeva, Pradyumna and Rukmini also accompanied Krishna.

King Rukmi was delighted to see Krishna and others. He quickly greeted them respectfully and took them inside his palace. He exchanged greetings and offered them sweets and drinks. After they were comfortably seated, Rukmi folded his hands and disclosed his plan of having his granddaughter marry Aniruddha. Krishna was pleased by the humble behavior of Rukmi. He agreed to Rukmi's proposal. A grand festival was arranged for the marriage. There were many professional dancers and singers. The sound of various musical instruments filled the whole sky. All the assembled ladies began to make auspicious sounds. At an auspicious moment, Aniruddha was married to the beautiful bride named Carumati. All the assembled kings headed by Dantavakra enjoyed this occasion.

After a few days, Rukmi called Dantavakra by his side and asked, "My dear friend! Can you tell me how we can defeat Krishna?" Dantavakra replied, "Listen, my dear sir! Actually Balarama is more powerful than Krishna, and He is Krishna's elder brother. It is very difficult to defeat Him. He has been living in Gokula, and therefore, He does not know how to play the royal game of chess. So we will put a bet on Him on the pretext of playing chess and defeat Him easily." Making a plan like this Rukmi went to Krishna's place. He pretended to be a Krishna's relative. He smiled, joked and spoke with everyone in a joyful mood. He especially cut many jokes with Baladeva. He asked, "My dear sir, do You know how to play chess? I know You have been tending cows in the forest of Vrndavana for Your whole life. So it is not surprising that You are ignorant about the royal game."

As soon as Rukmi uttered these words Baladeva became was filled with anger. He shouted, "Yes, I know how to play all games. I also know how to play the royal game of chess." Then Rukmi and Baladeva sat down face to face and began to play chess. In the first turn, Rukmi placed a bet of one thousand coins. Baladeva was defeated, and Rukmi teased Him by making nasty remarks. When it was His turn, Baladeva placed a bet of ten thousand coins. This time too Baladeva was defeated and lost His bet. In the next round, Baladeva bet one hundred thousand coins and this time. He was victorious. Baladeva laughed loudly and embarrassed Rukmi. Thus far Dantavakra had been sitting quietly watching the game, but when Rukmi was defeated by Baladeva, Dantavakra became restless. He was very sad in his mind.

## The killing of Rukmi

Being an envious demon, Dantavakra could not tolerate Baladeva's victory. He falsely announced that Balarama was defeated, and his friend Rukmi had become victorious. Saying this, he began to laugh loudly exposing all his teeth. Baladeva not only refuted his false claim but to substantiate His own victory, He made a voice vibrate from the sky.

"There is no doubt that Baladeva has won the game and that Dantavakra's statement is wrong."

As soon as the voice from the sky made its proclamation, Baladeva immediately got up and powerfully punched Dantavakra in the mouth. As a result of the blow, Dantavakra's teeth fell out. This made Rukmi extremely angry. He grabbed hold of Baladeva in a wrestling manner, but Baladeva effortlessly freed Himself from his clutch. Baladeva then gave him a strong punch. He picked Rukmi up and smashed him to the ground. Baladeva then sat down on his chest and, with His left hand, He squeezed Rukmi's throat while punching him in the face with His right hand. Blood spurted from Rukmi's nose and mouth. Unable to tolerate Balarama's powerful blows, Rukmi lost his life. Seeing this incident, all the kings began to lament in distress. Although Balarama had killed Krishna's wife's brother, Krishna did not say anything to Balarama. Rukmini too kept quiet out of awe and reverence for her husband's elder brother.

Krishna then called Rukml's son, Krtavarma, and instructed him to rule the kingdom, after solacing him with sweet words. After that, Krishna along with all His relatives, returned to Dwaraka and also brought the newly married bride and groom with them. When the inhabitants of Dwaraka heard this news, they immediately came out of their houses with joy and went ahead to greet them. They became very pleased and felt secure by the arrival of Krishna. Krishna and Balarama were their life and soul. They lived happily again in Dwaraka while fixing their minds on the Supreme Lord Govinda.

## The killing of Dantavakra

When Dantavakra heard that Balarama had killed his friend Rukmi, he became filled with anger. He could not bear the news of his dear friend's demise. He quickly summoned his army and started for Dwaraka to take revenge by killing Krishna. With a club, he ran swiftly towards Dwaraka. As he entered the city, he called the name "Krishna, Krishna" loudly. Soon a messenger of Krishna informed Him that Dantavakra had surrounded Dwaraka with his vast army, and he was marching towards the palace.

Krishna picked up His cakra and conch and went to counteract the challenge. At the sight of Krishna, Dantavakra angrily said, "It's good that I found You quickly. Today I will kill You and, with Your blood, I will offer oblations to Rukmi." Speaking thus, he roared like a lion. All the residents of Dwaraka were frightened not knowing what was in store for Dantavakra. Krishna simply smiled and replied, "I will soon send you to meet and talk to your friend Rukmi. O sinful person! Let Me face your weapon. Release your weapon now. I will first tolerate the blows of your weapon and then send you to Yamaraja."

Dantavakra then hurled his club at Krishna. It sounded like the thundering of the clouds. As the club came flying towards Krishna, Krishna simply smiled and released His own cakra. Krishna's cakra cut the club into pieces. Krishna then took up His own club and hit Dantavakra on his chest. Dantavakra could not bear the blow and fell to the ground making a huge sound. In this way, Krishna relieved Dantavakra of the sages' curse. He became liberated and went back to Vaikuntha. Thus after three births, Jaya and Vijaya became liberated and returned to their original place in Vaikuntha. Dantavakra's brother Viduratha arrived in Dwaraka with his army and fought with Krishna to avenge the killing of his brother, but he and his army met the same fate as Dantavakra.

Krishna's pastimes are just like nectar and one who does not relish them simply suffers the pangs of death. This is the opinion of Gunaraja Khan.

## The story of Vajranabha

By hearing such narrations, people can forget the ocean of material distress. At the eastern foot of Sumeru Mountain was the city of Vajranabha. This kingdom was inaccessible by the people of the whole world. The rooms of its palace were all made of gold, and the walls were bedecked with jewels. This city was inhabited by the people of all castes, and it was situated on the bank of Narmada. King Vajranabha was the son of Diti. He was the master of Vajrapuri. By nature he was an arrogant and sinful king.

## Vajranabha performs penance and obtains benediction

In his early life, Vajranabha desired to conquer the three worlds. The evil-minded king thus went to Sumeru Mountain and began to perform penance to please Brahma. He practiced severe penances and almost dried up his own body. He performed austerities for one thousand celestial years. Being satisfied by his penances, Brahma appeared before him and asked him to take any boon he liked. Vajranabha folded his hands and prayed, "O my Lord!
