Please grant me that no one including Candra, Surya, Vayu or any living entities can approach or conquer my kingdom. Let me become indestructible even by the demigods." Brahma gladly granted him these benedictions.

Having received Brahma's benediction, Vajranabha returned to his own capital Vajrapuri and lived happily after conquering the three worlds. The king of demons Vajranabha received a beautiful daughter by the grace of Mahadeva, whom he pleased by his service. She was unique in beauty and qualities within the whole world. Who can describe her good fortune? Vajranabha spent his days happily in his kingdom. One day, he thought that he should take over the abode of the demigods. To follow it up he sent a messenger to heaven with a message for Indra. The message said, "You have been enjoying heavenly pleasures for so long as the king of heaven. Just as you are a son of Kasyapa, I too am a son of Kasyapa. Therefore quit the heavenly kingdom at once and let me rule and enjoy it."

Hearing the message, Indra smiled, but in his mind he knew that none of the demigods were capable of killing Vajranabha. Indra invited Bmaspati to his palace and discussed what to do with Vajranabha. Bmaspati advised him that in such conditions, the only alternative left was to take shelter of Hari. Indra should speak to the messenger carefully and should immediately go to Dwaraka to seek Krishna's assistance in getting rid of the demon.

Indra then sent his messenger with a reply to Vajranabha that went as follows, "Our father Kasyapa has started a sacrifice. When he completes it, then we will approach him and ask him what to do. I will abide by whatever he orders." Indra instructed the messenger in this way and fared him goodbye. Indra wasted no more time and immediately started for Dwaraka. He met Krishna and explained everything about the message of Vajranabha to Him in detail. Hearing these words of Indra, Krishna smiled, thought for a while, and then began to speak to Indra.

## Krishna reveals how to eliminate Vajranabha

Krishna revealed, "O king of the demigods! You have come to Me just in time. I have to make a plan of how to kill the demon Vajranabha. This demon has obtained a benediction from Brahma that he will not be killed by the hands of any demigods. That is why no one has been able to conquer him and his kingdom. Therefore, I will send My son Pradyumna to his place. I will give him a special strategy by which we will be able to defeat the demon. I will also send Gada and Samba to assist Pradyumna. Although it is very difficult to approach his kingdom, I will make some arrangement so that they can conquer the city easily. I will bring the female swans to help in this matter. You should send Brahma's carrier, the swan, to meet Vajranabha's daughter named Prabhavati. This daughter is incomparably beautiful and talented, by the grace of Mahadeva. The swan should approach Prabhavati and attract her mind to Pradyumna by glorifying Pradyumna's qualities to her. Somehow Pradyumna has to make his way into the palace and meet her so that they are together. Therefore, take My word and send the swan immediately."

## Prabhavati meets a female swan

When Krishna assured Indra and explained to him what exactly was to be done, Indra returned home and called the female swans to his place. He explained to them about their duty. These swans belong to the swan family of Brahma's carrier. All of them are very beautiful and have golden wings. Their eyes and legs are made of coral. They can speak like the human beings. By the order of Indra, these swans flew to the city of Vajranabha and took shelter in a lake near the capital. The lake was filled with fully blossomed lotus flowers, and its water was full of aroma. Many aquatics and water born animals enjoyed the crystal clear water of that lake. The swans also joined them and began to enjoy various sporting activities. The whole sight was very pleasant. Whoever saw them became amazed. Soon the maidservants of Prabhavati saw the swans and were astonished. They quickly went and informed Prabhavati about these beautiful swans.

On hearing their words, Prabhavati, accompanied by her maidservants, went to the lake to see the swans. As she watched with joy, the swans sometimes swam across the lake, and sometimes came out of the water and walked around on the banks. The innocent Prabhavati became so moved that she wanted to catch hold of these swans. The swans began to play in different ways just to please the mind of Prabhavati. Prabhavati became restless and could not wait anymore. She got up and tried to catch hold of the swans. The swans too allowed themselves to be caught by her. In this way, the swans led Prabhavati to a secluded place and, finding the opportunity, they spoke to her in sweet voices.

"We usually fly in the sky. No human beings can catch us. As a matter of fact, you too have no power to catch us. Moreover, you are still young and innocent. Your intelligence is not mature. We have come here intentionally to be caught by you. Now it's your duty to take care of us nicely." When they spoke like this, Prabhavati assured them that she will look after them nicely and that no harm will come to them. After that, Prabhavati caught hold of one of the female swans and was amazed to see its beauty. She caressed its whole body with her own hand and glorified its beauty. Sometimes she would put it on her shoulders and sometimes she would put it on her own dress. She loved the swan so much that she would not put her on the ground. She named the swan Sucimukhi. This particular swan remained with Prabhavati while the other swans returned to heaven.

The swan developed a nice relationship with Prabhavati and spent her time in a joyful manner. She would always please the mind of the princess in different ways. Very soon, Sucimukhi became the best of Prabhavati's friends. The princess would sit down and chat with her best friend about many different tales. They would discuss different cities, great personalities, and great kings. In this way, Sucimukhi would enchant Prabhavati's mind.

## The glorification of Pradyumna before Prabhavati

One day, as they were sitting together, the swan realized that it was the right moment and began to speak in a grave manner, "I belong to the same swan family who is the carrier of Lord Brahma. By his mercy, we are able to move anywhere within the three worlds without any obstacles. I have visited everyone including Indra, Varuna, Kuvera, Mahadeva, Nairta, Sungod, Yamaraja, the Dikpalas, Brahma, Ananta, and all the other demigods. I also traveled to all the heavenly planets, earthly planets, and hellish planets. While traveling to different planets, I suddenly came across a beautiful city situated in the middle of the ocean. I had never seen such a beautiful place within the three worlds. The more I watched it, the more my mind became attracted to it. It was a magnificent city made of jewels, gold, and silver. There is no tinge of clay anywhere within that city. This city is known as Dwaraka and is rare within the three worlds. It is said to be the second Vaikuntha. The Lord and master of this city is Krishna, Who is the proprietor of the three worlds. All the demigods are under His subordination. His powerful arms are just like the punishing rod for the demons. He is the illumination of the three worlds and His prowess is incomparable."

The swan continued, "I love to stay in that city so that I can relish its beauty and particularly the beauty of Krishna. He has an attractive son by the name Pradyumna who is as powerful as His father. He is a great hero, and his beauty cannot be matched with anyone. He is said to be the incarnation of Kamadeva. When Kamadeva was burnt to ashes by the fire of anger created by Siva, his wife Rati prayed to Siva for her husband's wellbeing. Siva gave her a benediction that her husband would take birth from the womb of Rukmini. Thus, Kamadeva took birth as the son of Krishna and Rukmini. He is the eldest among Krishna's sons, and possesses unlimited qualities. When I saw him, I forgot everything else. I never saw anyone more handsome than he, even in the assembly of Indra. What more can I say about his beauty and qualities; even the celestial ladies daily come to serve him, so great is their desire to have his association."

In this way, the female swan daily discussed similar tales with Prabhavati just to understand her intention. The swan bewildered everyone and continued to live there as a dear friend of Prabhavati.

## Prabhavati requests the swan to bring Pradyumna

After hearing about Pradyumna daily from the swan, Prabhavati developed some attraction for him. She was in the prime of youth. She thought that Krishna's son has all good qualities, and therefore felt it to be an arrangement of the providence that she had developed a liking for him. According to karma, a living entity faces different situations in life. Sometimes, one gets a rare thing in his life, and sometimes some extraordinary things happen to him. The more Prabhavati heard about Pradyumna, the more she became anxious to hear about him. She would request and even force the swan to speak about the qualities of Pradyumna again and again.

Prabhavati would say to the swan, "You are very expert at talking. Actually, you have satisfied me by your sweet words. I have heard and learnt many things from your good self. I really appreciate your behavior. I am lucky that I met you. Although you have got a swan's body, you speak like a learned scholar. It is the arrangement of God that I have met you. I do not treat you as a stranger, rather I consider you as my dearmost friend. Now please, bring the prince of Dwaraka and save my life."

When Sucimukhi heard this, she thought that the mission of Indra was about to be fulfilled. She thought it wise to glorify Pradyumna more. So, she said, "This prince is a great personality. He could easily be the saving grace of both families. His father is the Lord of the three worlds. People of the three worlds are attracted by his beauty and qualities. Who has the power to bring him here? The prince is the incarnation of Cupid. He is the life and soul of his parents, so they never allow him to go out of their sight. He is a great minister, a learned scholar, and is equal in prowess to his father. He is always guarded by powerful guards. Still, I will try my best to bring him here. If you are fortunate enough, then he may agree to come here. But remember, there is the problem of entering your father's kingdom. Your father is the king of the demons, and is very sinful in nature. No one is allowed to enter his kingdom."

After hearing these statements of the swan, the princess replied, "I know you can do it. There is nothing which is impossible for you. I wish to marry Pradyumna without my father knowing. I will marry him in the Gandharva custom. Now, stop juggling words, and go to Dwaraka to bring the prince as soon as possible. I can no longer tolerate the arrow of lust. Bring him at once and revive my life." Sucimukhi then pacified Prabhavati by saying, "Do not worry. Keep your mind cool. I will remove all your distress by bringing the lord of your life. I am sure you and the prince will make a perfect match as the ideal husband and wife."

## The swan returns to heaven

After solacing Prabhavati, Sucimukhi the swan flew up into the sky with great enthusiasm. She could not understand whether the whole incident was a dream or reality, but she was sure that her mission would be successful. Meanwhile, Prabhavati gradually gave up eating and sleeping. Her only engagement was to constantly remember the tales of her beloved lord as described by her friend Sucimukhi. She spent her days and nights waiting anxiously for Sucimukhi's return.

In the meantime, Sucimukhi arrived in the abode of Indra and narrated everything that had taken place at the palace of Vajranabha. Indra became very happy and praised her for her good work. Thereafter, she went to Dwaraka and told everything to Krishna who immediately understood that His plan had worked. Krishna then called Pradyumna and spoke to him, "My dear son!" He said, "There is a powerful demon named Vajranabha, who has a wicked motive of capturing the abode of Indra. He is invincible by the demigods. Both his city and palace are unapproachable by anyone. He is destined to be killed by you. Do not feel surprised. By killing him, your fame will increase unlimitedly." After explaining this to Pradyumna, Krishna asked Sucimukhi to go and fetch Bhadranata. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sn Krishna Vijaya while fixing his mind on Him.

## The tales of Bhadranata

Once, the great sage Kasyapa performed a great sacrifice at a place called Prabhasa. All the demigods, sages, human beings, Daityas and Asuras participated in it. Many ascetics and sages also came there. At that time, a person by the name of Bhadranata also went there. He was an expert dancer, as his name suggests. He began to dance beautifully and sing melodiously near the sacrificial arena. As he followed the proper steps and musical notes, all the assembled personalities became struck with wonder. Being satisfied by his performance, Kasyapa gave him many benedictions. He said, "You will master all the arts of dancing. You will get whatever you desire. Your movement on earth will be unrestricted. You will be able to captivate anyone you meet."

Krishna further said to Sucimukhl, "You go to the place of Kasyapa, and you will see Bhadranata there. Give My reference to him, and bring him here quickly. I will send Pradyumna in the guise of a dancer along with him to the place of Vajranabha. In this way, I will have Vajranabha killed by Pradyumna."

Without wasting any time, Sucimukhl then went to Kasyapa's place and brought Bhadranata to Dwaraka as instructed by Krishna. On reaching Dwaraka, Bhadranata pleased Krishna by his artistic dancing and singing. Krishna too satisfied him by giving him sufficient wealth. Krishna then explained to him, "The demon Vajranabha wants to take over the heavenly kingdom in an illegal way. Indra came and informed Me of this plan. That's why I have brought you here. That demon is destined to be killed by My son, Pradyumna. No one else can harm him or enter into his city. My son will dress as a dancer like you and enter his palace along with you. He will be accompanied by Gada and Samba. They will kill Vajranabha and thus remove Indra's distress and enhance your glories."

## Krishna instructs Bhadranata and Pradyumna

After imparting instructions to Bhadranata, Krishna asked Gada, Samba, and Pradyumna to accompany Him. He further instructed them by saying, "O sons! It is the duty of a ksatriya to give protection to a distressed person and maintain the citizens. Indra is in trouble, and he has come to take shelter of us. Therefore, make an all-out effort to help him. It is the duty of all living entities to execute their own occupational duties and give protection to their fellow citizens. This practice will make them happy and prosperous, and will rid the world of miscreants, and do good to others. Do what you have been told with faith and care. Please go all of you, and live in the palace of Vajranabha as professional dancers and singers; then you will be able to get your job done as soon as you find an opportunity. But remember to do everything without the demon's knowledge."

Krishna further said, "Vajranabha's daughter Prabhavati has been convinced by Sucimukhi, the swan, about Pradyumna, and she is already attracted to him. Her beauty is famous throughout the three worlds. Pradyumna has to secretly meet her and live happily with her after marrying her. You should all keep Me informed from time to time with the help of Sucimukhi. Vajranabha's brother Sunabha has two charming daughters called Candraprabha and Gunavati. Gada and Samba should marry them. All these activities should be done very carefully and intelligently. Now move quickly, and do not keep any anxiety in your mind."

Having received Krishna's instruction in this way, Pradyumna offered His obeisances to his father, and assu.ed Him that he would try his best. Thereafter, Gada, Samba, and Pradyumna stayed with Bhadranata for sometime and learned the art of acting and dancing. Very soon, they mastered all the arts and felt ready to proceed with their mission. Sucimukhi became very pleased to see this development, and thought that the mission would be a success.

Finally, Krishna bid them goodbye and wished them good luck. He also advised them to keep their plans secret. Bhadranata and the three others offered their obeisances to the Lord and departed at an auspicious time. Krishna blessed them affectionately as the sound of "Jaya! Jaya!" resounded from all quarters. Sucimukhi was already sent ahead to meet and inform Prabhavati. Bhadranata and the three princes soon arrived near the city of the demon and waited outside. They could not enter without Vajranabha's permission, so they waited outside the city as per the advice of Sucimukhi.

Meanwhile, Sucimukhi went to heaven and explained the entire plan chalked out by Krishna to Indra. Indra was very satisfied with it, and quickly sent Sucimukhi to Vajrapuri. Upon arriving in the city, she approached a lake where she saw some of Prabhavati's companions. One of the companions rushed inside the palace and informed Prabhavati that Sucimukhi had returned. Prabha looked up in the sky with utmost eagerness just as a farmer looks up in the sky for rain whenever there is a drought. She felt ecstasy and her whole body became filled with symptoms of love. She became so emotional that she could not speak any more. Sucimukhi then said to her, "But how will the Lord of your life enter within the palace? No one can come in without your father's permission. I have a plan, but I need your help to meet your father. I will speak to him in such a way that his mind will become joyful. Then I will devise a plan to bring the prince in."

## Sucimukhl meets Vajranabha

In a joyful state of mind, Prabhavati along with her friends and Sucimukhi, went to meet her father. Prabhavati offered her respects to her father and stood by his side. The king of demons Vajranabha started laughing when he saw the beautiful swan. The demon asked the swan, "Who gave you these golden wings?" Prabhavati replied, "She got them by your mercy. This swan is from the family of the swan-carriers of Brahma and is full of good qualities. She is known for her attractiveness in all the three worlds. What's more, she can speak just like human beings. She has come here to serve you. I have been taking care of her for a long time, but now I felt I should introduce her to you."

At this, the demon turned to the swan and spoke in a sweet voice, "You have been here so long, but did not ever come to speak to me. I am very impressed by your qualities and beauty. Tell me, how can I make you happy? What can I give to you?"

Upon hearing Vajranabha's words, Sucimukhi went a little closer to him and gladly replied, "I live in the house of Brahma and travel throughout the universe whenever I feel like it. I know what's going on all over the three worlds, and I hear your good name wherever I go. Your fame is spread throughout the universe. I have had a great desire to see you, but how could I have come? Even the demigods long for your lotus feet; in fact, not only that but once the demigods approached Brahma and told him about your glories. Brahma too regards you in high esteem. I have not seen any king like you anywhere. Now, after seeing you, my mind has become peaceful. I have served your lovely daughter Prabhavati so that one day I would get an opportunity to see you. O noble one! Today my life has become successful. Kindly give me your leave so that I can return to Brahma. Please, do let me know if you want to convey any message to Brahma."

After hearing these statements of Sucimukhi, the king of demons requested her in a soft tone. "I have not seen such a charming bird like you within the three worlds. Even though you are a bird, you have captivated my heart. Now it will be difficult for me to tolerate your separation. Why don't you stay instead? I am sure all your desires will be fulfilled. I can give you whatever you want. I will take care of your hunger and thirst. Moreover, I wish to hear tales of the three worlds as well as of all the great personalities from you."

After that, Sucimukhi lived happily in the palace and glorified the demon king daily. She narrated different tales to the king and kept him happy. One day, she intentionally glorified Bhadranata before the king. She told him how she saw Bhadranatha's dancing in the court of Brahma, how great he was and how talented he was. She praised Bhadranata so heavily that the king finally said, "Yes, bring him here; I want to see him."

## Sucimukhi goes to bring Bhadranata

The more Vajranabha heard the characteristics of Bhadranata the more he developed an eagerness to meet him, so he sent Sucimukhi to bring Bhadranata to his palace. The king gave her many gifts and told her to depart immediately. Sucimukhi became very happy because things were happening according to Krishna's plan. She went to Prabhavati and said, "O dear friend! You are very fortunate. Whatever we planned has become successful. According to our previous plan, the prince will soon come within the palace along with Bhadranata. Your desire to meet the prince will be fulfilled. Do not feel sad anymore. Now I am going to bring the dancer as requested by your father."

After speaking these words, Sucimukhi went to Bhadranata and told him to come inside the palace along with the other dancers. Sucimukhi spoke something about Prabhavati to Pradyumna. She said, "The daughter of Vajranabha is very sad due to separation from you. Her beauty is rare in the whole world. She will be a suitable match for you." Thereafter, all the dancers joyfully proceeded towards the palace in order to accomplish their mission. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya while fixing his mind on His lotus feet.

## The dancers begin to dance in front of Vajranabha

Keeping Sucimukhi in front, all the dancers happily went inside the city. They were greeted respectfully by everyone. Every day, they would dance and sing at different places in the city. Soon they pleased the minds of all the friends of Vajranabha by displaying various artistic skills. All the inhabitants of Vajrapuri became captivated by their qualities. They rewarded the dancers with various gifts and wealth. Soon the news of their extraordinary qualities reached the king. With a desire to meet the dancer the king called Sucimukhi and inquired, "Tell me the truth where is your renowned dancer? Have you brought him here?"

Realizing that the king was very eager to meet the dancers, Sucimukhi spoke to him in a sweet voice, "O king, I looked around the whole world and finally I found him in a place called Prabhasa. He was performing there in front of many demigods, sages, and human beings in the sacrificial arena of Kasyapa. He satisfied everyone assembled there and was suitably rewarded. I explained to him about your glories and somehow brought him here. O king! He is already in your city. If you wish to meet him and watch his dancing, then send your man to bring him here."

King Vajranabha sent a servant to bring the dancers into his court. Within a short time, all the dancers arrived before the king and offered their obeisances to him. Meanwhile, Sucimukhi quietly went to Prabhavati and informed her that the prince had indeed come to her palace. As soon as she heard this, Prabhavati embraced Sucimukhi with affection. Her mind became pacified and her passion for meeting the lord of her life increased. Meanwhile, in the court of the king, all the dancers began to perform their show. Pradyumna played the hero, Gada was the comedian, and Samba played the role of Bmannala.

Badranata assumed different forms as he was blessed by sage Kasyapa to do so. Simply by watching these dancers, all the demons and their family members became overwhelmed. They completely forgot themselves. The king was so satisfied that he requested the dancers to depict the pastimes of Ramacandra. Remembering the lotus feet of Govinda, Gunaraja Khan narrates His pastimes.

## The dancers enact a drama about Ramayana

A stage was erected in the courtyard of the king. The play of Ramayana was about to begin. Everyone gathered around the stage with great eagerness. First, an artist entered the scene dressed as Dasaratha. He was accompanied by his three wives Kausalya, KaikeyT, and Sumitra. Since the king had no son, he performed a sacrifice on the stage. Lord Visnu appeared and gave the king four pots. He instructed the king to feed this to his three queens. The three queens ate it with great respect. As a result, Kausalya got a son by the name of Rama, who was full of transcendental qualities. His beauty was matchless. KaikeyT got a son named Bharata, who was a noble person. Sumitra got two sons known as Laksmana and Satrughna. All the four brothers were incarnations of Lord Visnu.

After that, the four players in the dress of Rama, Laksmana, Bharata, and Satrughna came on stage followed by another player dressed as Visvamitra. The sage took Rama and Laksmana to the forest where the two brothers then killed Subahu and Tadaka. In this way, Rama helped protect the sacrificial performance of the sages. Later, in the courtyard of King Janaka, Rama broke the bow of Siva and married Sita. His three brothers also married the three sisters of Sita, namely Urmila, Mandavi, and Srutikirti As they were returning home, they met Parasurama. Although Rama was only a young boy. He defeated Parasurama very easily. After defeating Parasurama, they returned to Ayodhya. At that time, Dasaratha tried to hand over the kingdom to Rama. He even made all the necessary arrangements for the coronation ceremony of Rama, but KaikeyT, on the ill advice of her maidservant, created an impediment in this regard. Dasaratha was bound by the promise he had made, and so, instead of Rama, he made Bharata the king of Ayodhya.

The play continued. Rama, Laksmana, and Sita then departed for the forest. They dressed in tree bark, kept matted hair and walked off into the forest. Rama and Laksmana carried bows and arrows in their hands. On the way, they met Guhak candala, who became friends with Rama. Rama embraced him affectionately. Guhak helped the three to reach the forest of Dandakaranya. At one point, Sita felt tired and could not walk any further. Her feet started to bleed. She asked Rama how far the forest was. Rama cried on seeing Sita's bleeding feet.

Meanwhile, Dasaratha became so afflicted due to separation from Rama that he left his body. The king died only because of separation from his beloved son. Then someone entered the stage dressed as Bharata, and chastised his own mother. He then went into the forest to bring Rama back to Ayodhya. He met Rama in the forest and fell at His feet, crying profusely. Bharata informed Rama about the death of their father. As soon as the three exiles heard this news, they fell to the ground unconscious. After some time, Ramacandra performed the sraddha ceremony of His father in the forest according to rules and regulations prescribed in the scriptures. Rama then pacified Bharata and told him to go back to Ayodhya. Bharata caught hold of Rama's feet and begged Him to return. Rama did not change His mind about the exile, but allowed Bharata to take His shoes back to Ayodhya instead.

One day, as Laksmana and Sita were wandering in the forest of Dandakaranya, Ravana's sister Surpanakha approached them. After some discussion, Laksmana cut off her nose and ears. Then someone entered the stage dressed as Kharadusana. A fierce battle took place, and in that battle, Sri Rama killed fourteen thousand demons. Suddenly, Laksmana heard a noise made by Marica, "O brother Laksmana! Please help me and save My life." Laksmana immediately went out keeping Sita alone in the cottage. At that moment, Ravana came to the cottage in the guise of an ascetic. He kidnapped Sita and took her away in his chariot. Meanwhile, Rama killed Marica and returned to the cottage with Laksmana. But, when they arrived, they did not find Sita there. Rama became overwhelmed with separation from Sita and cried profusely. He grew restless, and sometimes He would sit down, while at other times, He would lie on the floor. He felt that the three worlds were all empty due to separation from Sita. He searched for Sita everywhere, inquiring from each and every tree, creeper and mountain about the whereabouts of Sita. He walked and walked until He could walk no further. All He could think about was how to get His beloved Sita back. Although Laksmana tried to solace Rama, He did not succeed.

In this way, the two brothers wandered about the forest. In their meanderings, suddenly they met Jatayu, the king of birds. Jatayu had seen Ravana forcibly taking away Sita, and had even tried to stop him. He fought with Ravana, but he could not kill him because Ravana was not to be killed even by the demigods. After defeating Jatayu, Ravana took Sita to the Asoka forest and kept her there. The two brothers met Jatayu who told Them the news about Sita and then gave up his body. Rama performed a sraddha ceremony of Jatayu in the forest, just as He had done for His own father, and as a result, Jatayu attained liberation.

After the departure of Jatayu, the two brothers proceeded towards Lanka. They both had Their bows and arrows with Them as They walked through the dense forest. After walking for a short while, They arrived at the Rsyamuka Mountain. They slowly climbed to the top of the mountain, and found Hanuman there. Hanuman respectfully offered obeisances to Rama, and introduced Sugriva to Him. Some time before, Bali and Sugriva had been the two monkey kings who ruled the kingdom called Kiskindhya together. At one point, Bali drove Sugriva out of the kingdom and took power as the only king himself. He also stole Sugriva's wife. Out of fear of Bali, Sugriva along with five monkeys lived on the top of Rsyamuka Mountain. Rama and Sugriva became good friends, as they had both lost their wives. Rama promised Sugriva to bring back his wife after killing Bali, while Sugriva also promised Rama to help Him get Sita back.

Rama, the descendent of Raghu dynasty, then pierced seven palm fruits just by shooting one single arrow. Rama killed Bali with one of His arrows as well, and made Sugriva the rightful king of Kiskindhya. After the four months of the rainy reason, Rama sent His monkey soldiers off into the four directions to look for Sita. Prince Angada went towards the ocean in the south. Rama came up with a plan to send a messenger to Lanka, and decided to give this responsibility to Hanuman. In order to cross the ocean, Hanuman, the son of Pavana (the wind god), climbed up on the peak of a mountain, and jumped off it with great power. He swiftly and effortlessly crossed hundreds of yojanas to land safely in Ravana's capital city. He entered Asokavana and found Sita in her prison garden. After speaking with her, Hanuman turned the whole forest upside down. He also killed many demons like Aksaya-kumar. After a valiant struggle, Indrajit ultimately arrested Hanuman and brought him before Ravana. In an angry mood, Ravana ordered his servants to set fire to Hanuman's tail. Hanuman escaped from Ravana's men, burned the city of Lanka and then returned to Rama by jumping over the ocean. He narrated the whole incident to Rama, especially the news of Sita. He also told how Ravana's maidservants had insulted Sita and how he had killed the demon Aksaya-kumara and burned Lanka into ashes. As proof of his having been in Sita's presence, Hanuman then handed over to Rama the jewel given to him by Sita.

At the sight of the precious jewel, Sri Rama began to cry profusely in His great separation from Sita. He held the jewel to His chest, and breathed heavily. Despite their anguish at hearing of her condition in captivity, they rejoiced to learn of the whereabouts of Sita. Rama was very pleased with Hanuman's prowess. In this way, the three artists perfectly enacted the pastimes of Ramacandra. In the next scene, Rama was preparing to go to Lanka. Someone dressed as Vibhisana, the brother of Ravana, and tried to explain to Ravana the principles of religion. As expected, Ravana paid no attention to his brother's words, and instead banished him from his kingdom after insulting him. Vibhisana went to Rama's camp and took shelter of Him. Rama in turn gave him all protection. Soon Rama gathered all the monkey soldiers from different parts of the country in order to invade Lanka. Led by Rama and Laksmana, They went to the southern seashore. Among the monkey soldiers were Nala, NTla, Angada, Susena, Jambavan, Sarabha, Gavaksa, Gaya, Hanuman, Mainda, Dvivida, Kumudha, Kesari, and others. They were of different bodily shapes and strength. Sugriva was the leader of all these monkeys. There were millions of monkeys and thousands of commanders-in-chief. After arriving at the seashore, Rama and Laksmana spoke to Vibhisana and Sugriva in this way, "This ocean seems insurmountable and vast. Therefore, how are we going to cross over it?" The two monkey leaders thought for a while, and finally suggested that they build a bridge across the ocean. Soon all the monkeys set out in different directions to fetch stones for a bridge. After the bridge was built, all of them crossed the ocean and entered the city of Lanka. The monkeys uprooted trees and hit the demon soldiers with them. Many sons and grandsons of Ravana were killed in this battle.

Indrajit then came forward in an angry mood to fight. He created an illusion and arrested Rama and Laksmana binding them with a snake rope. After this heinous act, Indrajit went home amidst the resounding glorification of his followers. Meanwhile, Sugriva, Arigada, Jambavan, and Hanuman sat down around Rama and Laksmana. Hanuman then whispered something into the ear of Rama. Rama immediately remembered Garuda who in no time appeared before Rama. As soon as Garuda appeared, the snakes with which Rama and Laksmana were bound ran away in fear of Garuda. In this way, Rama and Laksmana freed Themselves and returned to the battle. All the monkey soldiers embraced each other and jumped with joy. Meanwhile, Ravana's anxiety increased moment by moment. He sent Kumbhakarna to fight with Rama. The powerful Kumbhakarna went to the battlefield and started to eat the monkey soldiers. He scratched at them with his nails and thus wounded many monkeys. He punched some and squeezed others. Soon the monkeys were running away from the fight out of fear of him.

Then Sugriva the king of the monkeys came to fight. Kumbhakarna grabbed hold of Sugriva's throat tightly, thus rendering him unconscious. After defeating Sugriva, Kumbhakarna started returning to the palace of Ravana. But despite his obvious prowess, while fighting with Sugriva, he lost his ears and nose when Sugriva bit them off. Kumbhakarna felt shy to meet Ravana in that condition, so he changed his mind, and went back to the battle field to fight again. Meanwhile, the monkey soldiers who had run away out of fear of Kumbhakarna met Rama and told Him everything. Rama picked up His bow and joined the fight to kill Kumbhakarna. One by one, Rama cut off the seemingly invincible warrior's hands and legs. Finally, Rama shot another arrow and cut off Kumbhakarna's head.

Unable to tolerate such humiliation, Indrajit came forward himself to battle with Rama. He shot a fiery arrow at the royal brothers, and promptly took away Laksmana's life. On seeing what had happened, the son of Pavana, Hanuman immediately flew off to the Gandhamadana Mountain to bring some special medicine to bring Him back to life. There, Hanuman killed a formidable female crocodile called Gandhakali and alone defeated thirty million Gandharvas. Finally Hanuman decided to bring the whole mountain with him back to Lanka and give the necessary medicinal herbs to Susena who then revived Laksmana with the medicine. Seeing Laksmana regain His consciousness, all the monkeys chanted, "Jaya! Jaya!" and the demigods showered flowers on Laksmana.

Laksmana then went to the sacrificial arena of Indrajit with Hanuman and Vibhisana. A fierce battle commenced between Laksmana and Indrajit

in which Laksmana killed Indrajit. All the demigods headed by Indra began to dance with joy and showered flowers on Laksmana. Finally Ravana came to fight as he was still greatly afflicted by lamentation due to losing his son. Rama and Ravana fought bravely and fiercely. At the end, Rama killed Ravana with the help of a brahmastra. The inhabitants of the three world chanted, "Jaya! Jaya!" After killing Ravana, Rama installed Vibhisana as the king of Lanka. After that, Rama delivered Sita from the Asokavana, at which time He tested Sita to prove her chastity. The demigods personally appeared to offer lavish prayers to Rama. Then by the order of Rama, Indra let loose showers of nectar upon the dead monkey soldiers to bring them back to life. After killing Ravana and delivering Sita, Rama boarded an airplane made of flowers and returned to Ayodhya.

As soon as Bharata heard the news of Rama's arrival, he took Rama's shoes on his head and went out to greet Him along with his subjects. He offered Rama His shoes and paid obeisances to Him like an ordinary servant. Rama took over the responsibility of ruling Ayodhya. There was no disease, no lamentation, no old age or even death during Ramarajya. Soon, however, due to some unfortunate circumstances, people began to doubt Sita's chastity, and Rama had to banish her to the forest. He cried profusely because of the pain of separation from Sita. In the forest, pregnant Sita gave birth to a set of twins whom she called Lava and Kusa. Their father Rama, unaware of their true identity, fought with His sons Lava and Kusa. Thereafter, Satrughna killed Lavanasura. Sita was again put to another test, but this time. She could not tolerate the insult and therefore entered the earth for good. Rama, the beloved son of Raghu dynasty, lamented profusely. He gave a huge amount of wealth in charity and handed over the kingdom to Lava and Kusa. Laksmana gave up His body in the waters of the river Sarayu. Rama was even more overwhelmed with grief because of Laksmana's demise. None of the residents of Ayodhya were able to pacify Rama. A despondent Rama too gave up His life by entering into the waters of Sarayu. One by one Bharata, Satrughna, and all the relatives and followers of the Ayodhya dynasty also gave up their lives in the same way. The queens followed the same path as well being unable to tolerate the fire of grief. Soon all the inhabitants of Ayodhya gave up their lives in a similar fashion. Rama, along with His entourage, then returned to His original celestial abode.

In this way, the three artists enacted the pastimes of Rama. All the assembled demons became fully captivated by their play. O readers! The characteristics of Rama are really wonderful. Simply by remembering His pastimes, a person can achieve liberation. In this way, the artists performed different pastimes like the tales of Aja and Indumati, the tales of the appearance of Gariga in this world, and the tales of King Draupada and his daughter Draupadi. I am unable to estimate the number of pastimes these three enacted. Thus the dancers lived in the city of Vajranabha by bewildering them with their artistic skill. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrated Sri Krishna Vijaya at the feet of Govinda.

## Pradyumna assumes the form of a bumblebee

And so the three disguised dancers continued to stay in the city of Vajrapuri with great delight. Sucimukhi met Prabhavati one day and explained to her how the prince had gained entry to the palace by adopting the dress of an artist. The demon's daughter became jubilant to hear that news, and she thanked Sucimukhi again and again.

She said, "I can no longer wait. Go and somehow bring him here. I had been suffering the pangs of separation from him. O dear friend! Now that he has come here how can I live without seeing him? Please go quickly and bring him here so that my life will be saved by your mercy."

Sucimukhi soon went to the dancers' quarters and spoke to Krishna's son Pradyumna about the pathetic condition of his lover.

Pradyumna thought for a while and asked, "But how will I go inside the residential quarter of the demon king?" Sucimukhi replied, "You are the master of illusion. Assume the form of a bumblebee and enter the palace by sitting on a flower kept in a basket carried by the king's florist. The female florist will take the flowers to the princess and give them to her. " After advising the prince, Sucimukhl quickly departed.

Pradyumna waited patiently for the right opportunity. The sun slowly moved towards the west in the direction of Varuna's country. The whole sky slowly turned red and soon after, darkness fell upon the earth. The sky became filled with countless stars which looked like flowers. The moon looked like a big ripe orange. Just at that time the florist passed by while carrying flowers for the princess. Humming bees followed her from behind. Pradyumna, as planned before, took the form of a bumblebee and slowly followed them. Meanwhile Sucimukhi met Prabhavati and told her that the prince would come to her place in some form or the other. "Just be ready to welcome him. Make arrangements for your Gandharva marriage as soon as possible." On hearing Sucimukhi's words, Prabhavati quickly called all her companions and instructed them in the following manner.

## Prabhavati gets ready for Gandharva marriage

"Today a young demigod will come here to meet me." Sucimukhi instructed her friend. "But be careful; do not disclose this to anyone. Anyone who reveals this secret will be burnt to ashes by the prince's wrath. Therefore perform your duty accordingly, and do not embarrass me."

When her friends heard this, they became frightened. They followed their respective duties entrusted to them. They brought flowers and decorated Prabhavati. Many bumblebees along with the bumblebee in the form of Pradyumna hovered around the flowers. In the evening, all the other bumblebees went back to their own place except one. Thus Pradyumnaturned-bumblebee secretly entered that flower which Prabhavati was using to decorate her ear and remained there. In this way, Kamadeva lived in the flower on Prabhavati's ears as a small bumblebee. Who can understand the mystery of Cupid himself? He was very much in the presence of Prabhavati, yet continued to give her immense distress.

## Prabhavati's intense eagerness to see Pradyumna

Prabhavati remained absorbed in intense anxiety day and night as she waited to meet Pradyumna. She kept her eyes constantly fixed on the doorway in anticipation of her beloved's arrival. She contemplated, "Just now the prince will come, and I will see him. I will serve him this way and that way. I will speak to him like this when I meet him." Prabhavati floated on the mental plane. Sometimes she stood up, sometimes she sat down, sometimes she went outside, and sometimes she came inside. She was completely restless. The small bumblebee on her ear watched all this agitation, and smiled. Pradyumna took pleasure in watching her restless behavior. All that he had heard from Sucimukhi about the princess could now be viewed with his own eyes.

At one point, Sucimukhi came to see her, and Prabhavati fell at her feet and says, "O dear friend. Do not deceive me. Tell me, will he really come here today? Touch my head and swear that he is going to come today. If I am so fortunate by the grace of providence as you have said, then why am I bereft of his association? I am so restless that I cannot wait any more. If you are trying to deceive me, then better you kill me."

## Pradyumna meets Prabhavati and marries her

Krishna's son Pradyumna could not tolerate Prabhavati's intense eagerness anymore, so he gave up his disguised form of a bumblebee and assumed his original form. As soon as the girl saw the prince, she lowered her head in shyness. She did not know what to say. Sucimukhi then introduced the prince to her, "This is the prince I have been telling you about." Sucimukhi said to Prabhavati, "He is the son of Rukmini and Krishna. He is a great hero and the illumination of the Yadu dynasty. Even celestial women faint on seeing his beauty. On the strength of your good fortune, I have been able to bring him here. Now take good care of him, and keep him very carefully."

All the friends of the princess quickly began to make arrangements for the marriage. The bride and groom were made to sit on a golden throne. Ghee lamps made of jewels were lit. First, the couple was given a cool bath with scented water. Then they were dressed in opulent dresses. They were decorated with sandalwood pulp and various ornaments made of jewels and so on. Then they were seated on a jewel-studded throne. Prabhavati offered a garland to Pradyumna, and placing it round his neck, she said, "I surrender myself to you. I promise in front of the fire and all the demigods that from today onwards, you alone will have the right to enjoy my youthful body. From today on, you are the lord of my life."

Pradyumna and Prabhavati became very joyful and lived happily. During the day, Pradyumna would remain in the guise of an artist, and at night he would assume his real form and come back to his beloved wife to enjoy various conjugal pastimes. His attachment for Prabhavati increased day by day. A long time passed in this way. After some time, the symptoms of a conjugal affair began to show on Prabhavati's body. One day, Sunabha's two daughters Gunavati and Candraprabha came to see her. When they saw her, they said, "O sister! What has happened to you? We see the marks and symptoms of conjugal affairs on your body. You look lazy, and your eyes are almost closed. Your eyes are reddish as well. Tell us the truth."

Prabhavati answered in a clever way, "You know, one sage suddenly came to my room yesterday. I served him faithfully with my body, mind, and speech. Being satisfied by my service, the sage gave me a mantra and said that if you invoke this mantra, you can call any demigod. After that, the sage left my place. In order to test the mantra, I invoked it, and suddenly, a prince from heaven appeared before me and made me completely bewildered with passion. He was very attractive and young. He enjoyed conjugal affairs with me. It is my good fortune that I had the rare association of a celestial being. I have been thinking about both of you since then because I want to give that mantra to you two. It is good that you have come here today. It is far better to marry some worthwhile superior entity than marry people from your own community. Besides, the demons are by nature very envious. They always disturb the demigods, the performance of sacrifice, and saintly persons. I also think that the demons will eventually be wiped out from the world."

In this way, Prabhavati tactfully tricked her two sisters who became very happy to hear such a proposal. Prabhavati sent them home after asking them to return the next day to take the mantra. When that night Kamadeva again came to her place, Prabhavati told him everything about her two sisters. Pradyumna said, "You are very wise. Do not worry. The two demigods who are supposed to be the hero of your mantra will be Gada and Samba. I will convince them and bring them here tomorrow."

After speaking to her like this, Pradyumna returned and spoke to Gada and Samba about the whole story. He said, "I go to Prabhavati's room every night in the form of a bumble bee. Her two sisters want to marry both of you. She has already made the arrangement for your meeting with your brides. Tonight, three of us will go to Prabhavati's room in the form of bumblebees and destroy the distress of everyone."

## The marriage of the two daughters of Sunabha

Meanwhile, Prabhavati used her skills and made an arrangement for invoking the three princes. The three princes immediately appeared before her. All of them looked attractive. Pradyumna went by the side of Prabhavati, while Gada and Samba stood by the side of Gunavati and Candraprabha. Gada and Samba married their respective bride in the custom followed by the Gandharvas. They exchanged flower garlands with each other. In this way, the three great heroes received their respective queens in the city of Vajrapuri. Sucimukhi then went to Dwaraka and informed Krishna about the marriage of these three princes.

## The great sage instructs Vajranabha

At the completion of Kasyapa's sacrifice, all the demigods headed by Indra arrived there. Vajranabha, the king of the Daityas, also went there. When he saw Indra he said, "I sent a messenger to your place asking you to hand over your kingdom to me. You borrowed some time from me till the completion of Kasyapa's sacrifice. Now that everything is over, do as you yourself have agreed." At this, Kasyapa, the great sage, replied to Vajranabha, "The kingdom of Indra is not meant for you, O king of Daityas! It is better to be happy with whatever you are qualified for. Indra is a religious minded person. He maintains all the inhabitants of heaven. He gives protection to the yaksas, raksasas, sages, and the devotees of Krishna, whereas you know your own character very well. Therefore, go back to your own kingdom and live happily."

Speaking to Vajranabha this way, the sage Kasyapa saw him off. Indra offered his obeisances to Kasyapa, and went to his abode in heaven. Meanwhile, the three princes continued to reside in the palace of Vajranabha and keep everyone enchanted by their artistic skills. They stayed there during the whole rainy season and into the autumn season as well. They were so happy that they totally forgot about the passage of time. In due course of time, the three girls became pregnant. Sucimukhi quickly went to Dwaraka and informed Krishna of everything.

Vajranabha returned to his palace after being insulted at the place of Kasyapa and decided to wage a war against Indra. Indra, understanding his demon mentality, quickly went to Dwaraka and told Krishna what was about to occur. He requested Krishna to help him out. Krishna then sent Sucimukhi to Vajrapuri to inform the three princes to kill Vajranabha as soon as possible. "The three pregnant princesses will give birth to three great sons by the grace of the demigods." Krishna told Sucimukhi, "These three sons will be transformed into powerful princes right after their birth. They will be expert in the art of fighting and will help their fathers. I will also come there to watch them fight and I will send Jayanta to help them." Sucimukhl wasted no time and flew instantly to the city of the demon to meet the three princes and their queens. She explained everything that

Krishna had said to them. Very soon, the three queens gave birth to three beautiful sons who all became powerful warriors immediately after their birth. They were especially empowered by the demigods. No one could defeat them in battle. They were great fighters. The names of these three sons were Candraprabha, Gunavanta, and Hamsaketu. They were as powerful and handsome as their fathers.

Meanwhile, Vajranabha gathered his army in order to fight with Indra. He took many elephants, horses, infantry, chariots, and charioteers. There were so many soldiers that one could not finish counting them even in one year. One day, the three queens' bodyguards saw them with their newborn babies. The guards were perplexed by the sight. They ran to the king and informed him about this. The king understood that his daughter had not been honest and that the artists were in fact princes. He ordered that the princes Pradyumna, Gada and Samba, be killed at once.

## The fight between Talajahgha and the princes

King Vajranabha ordered his commander in chief Talajahgha to go and arrest the princes. He also told him to kill the princes if he could not arrest them. Talajahgha went inside the palace with some soldiers and surrounded the ladies' quarters. When the three daughters saw this, they became so afraid that they almost lost consciousness. Prabhavati somehow controlled herself, and quickly sent Sucimukhi to bring Pradyumna. Sucimukhi immediately went to the place of the artists and brought the three heroes Pradyumna, Gada, and Samba back with her. They came quickly and solaced the queens along with their three sons.

Thereafter, the six heroes went out of the palace in order to fight with the enemies. They began to kill the army of Talajahgha mercilessly. They fought so bravely that some of the soldiers ran in retreat and some of them died instantly. Soon all the soldiers left fearing for their lives. Finally, Talajahgha came forward and started fighting alone. He shot countless arrows at the six fighters and almost covered the whole atmosphere, but Pradyumna expertly cut all the arrows to pieces with a sharp trident. Not only Pradyumna, but the others also counteracted Talajangha's arrows. The fight was formidable. There were so many dead horses, elephants, and charioteers lying everywhere.

## The killing of Talajangha

Pradyumna then dropped his trident and grabbed hold of Talajangha tightly. The two wrestled fiercely. They fought by hitting each other with their hands, heads, legs, fists, and chests. Talajangha then gave a powerful blow to Pradyumna from which Pradyumna fell to the ground unconscious. In a short while, though, Pradyumna regained consciousness and stood up with renewed power and enthusiasm. He caught hold of Talajangha's leg and, lifting him high in the air, he then dashed him to the ground. He sat down on his chest and punched his face. At the end, he pressed Talajangha's throat with his own knee so tightly that the demon lost his life. As soon as Vajranabha heard about the death of his commander in chief, he felt his body swell with rage. He equipped himself with weapons and soldiers and went forward to fight.

## The fight between Vajranabha and the Demigods

The king of Daityas could not understand how the three princes had entered into his palace and ruined everything. He thought, "I can fight with Indra later, but first of all I have to get rid of the six princes." So whatever army and ammunition he had taken to fight with Indra, he now decided to use to kill the six warriors. Many inauspicious symptoms could be seen throughout the city. The king had only one intention and that was to kill those princes. Meanwhile Sucimukhi went to the place of Indra and Krishna and told them about the killing of Talajangha as well as about how Vajranabha was about to declare a war against the six princes.

Krishna climbed on the back of Garuda and immediately left for Vajrapuri, and Indra too went there accompanied by all the demigods. The thirty-three million demigods remained up in the sky above the city of Vajrapuri to witness the battle. The eight Lokapalas also joined them. Indra sent his son Jayanta and a Purava brahmana to the princes to assist them. It is said that whenever there is a decline in religious principles and an increase in irreligious activities, the godly personalities appear and destroy the miscreants. When Jayanta arrived near the palace, Sucimukhi went to Pradyumna and told him about this. She said, "O prince! O son of Krishna! Do not be afraid." Sucimukhi told Pradyumna, "Fight bravely. Indra, Krishna, and many others have arrived here to assist you. Just look up in the sky and see for yourself. Jayanta and the Purava brahmana are just about to arrive here. Take their help and kill all the demons."

Meanwhile, the soldiers of the king surrounded the whole palace and came marching shouting, "Kill them! Kill them!" They showered many weapons such as clubs, fireballs, and arrows on the princes. The whole palace became covered with arrows. The three queens began to tremble out of fear. Their three sons also fought with great vigor and killed many demoniac people. Pradyumna, Gada, Samva, Jayanta, and Purava—all these five great warriors displayed their expertise in the art of fighting. They killed many demons with their arrows. They also destroyed many elephants and horses of the enemy camp. The demons were no match for these great fighters. There was so much blood on the battlefield that it appeared as if a river of blood was flowing. Many heads and bodies could be seen floating into that river. No one could stand before these five grave fighters. They filled the whole sky with arrows. The demons became greatly frightened by seeing their prowess. In no time, the soldiers of the demons dwindled. The chariot drivers fled leaving behind their chariots, and the elephant keeper too ran for his life leaving behind the elephant. They were completely confused and scattered all over the place. When the rest of the army and their leaders ran for their lives, Vajranabha and his brother Sunabha came forward to fight.

Samba began to fight with Sunabha, while Gada fought with Vajradanta who had a strong body. Purava fought with Durmukha, Jayanta fought with Dirghadantha and Pradyumna personally fought with Vajranabha. Such a fight had never been seen before. Even the battle between Rama and Ravana seemed less formidable than this one, such was the power of Krishna's son Pradyumna. However, the king of the demons was still at large. He was immensely powerful and almost invincible. All the five great warriors fought with their counterparts in a brave manner. The demigods glorified the princes and encouraged them on. After a great fierce battle, Samba released his special weapon which resembled a half moon, and severed the head of Sunabha. The demigods rejoiced at seeing this turn of events. In the same way, Gada fought with Vajradanta for a long time and eventually killed him by shooting his Pasupati arrow. Jayanta and DTrgadantha engaged in a fearful battle. Jayanta finally eliminated DTrghadantha by releasing the arrow known as Varuna. The great fighter Purava fought with Durmukha with great power. In the long run, Purava killed him by shooting him with the arrow known as Agni. In this way, these four demons that had been rendered practically invincible by the demigods were thus killed. The king of demons could not bear the separation from his relatives and friends. His distress and anger increased constantly as he shot unlimited arrows at Pradyumna. Pradyumna was equally expert, and counteracted them all effortlessly. Then Vajranabha released ten arrows simultaneously which turned into ten poisonous snakes before reaching the target. In response, Kamadeva shot twenty arrows and cut the snakes into pieces. When the demon tried to shoot twenty arrows at him again, Pradyumna expertly cut his bow into pieces. Every time the demon tried to pick up and use a bow, Pradyumna would cut it into pieces.

Thereafter the demon released his fiery weapon with which he had conquered the three worlds. The special feature of this weapon is that whoever it hits will be killed for certain. However Pradyumna, unperturbed, jumped in the air and caught hold of the weapon. The demigods exclaimed, "Well done! Well done!" The demon then decided to use a divine weapon. Pradyumna became a little worried at the sight of the weapon. He too invoked his divine weapon and released it to neutralize the demon's weapon. There was a fierce battle between the divine weapons of Pradyumna and the demon. When nothing seemed to be working, the demon took help of his illusory magical power for which he was well known. He went up in the sky with his chariot and began showering arrows. It was so thick that the sun and the moon became totally covered.

The chariot of Pradyumna was smashed and he was left standing on the ground without a chariot. To nullify the demon's illusion, Kamadeva too created his own illusion and destroyed all the arrows. The demon was thus forced to come down to earth. He then took up a fiery weapon and hit Pradyumna's chest. Although Jayanta tried to save him, Pradyumna fell to the ground unconscious. Krishna and Indra then revived him by showering nectar on him.

When Pradyumna opened his eyes, he saw Krishna and Indra in the sky. In order to encourage Pradyumna, Krishna said to him, "Kill the king of demons. You are able to conquer the three worlds. Who is this tiny demon compared to you?"

Being encouraged in this way, Pradyumna remembered Krishna's lotus feet and released his divine arrow in the shape of a half moon at the demon. The whole sky became illuminated by the fire emanating from that arrow. The arrow shot by Kamadeva cut off Vajranabha's head from his body and the head rolled to the ground. As soon as all the other demons saw that Vajranabha had been killed, they ran away in retreat. They hid themselves in the mountain caves, in the forests, and even in Patalaloka. The demigods played drums in joy and showered flowers on Pradyumna. Soon the wives of the demon came running to the battleground.

## The wives of Vajranabha lament

As soon as the wives of the demon heard about their husband's death, they rushed to the battlefield and began to cry pathetically. They rolled on the ground and lamented profusely. Their hair loosened and they broke their bangles by hitting them against their heads. They beat their chests repeatedly and their faces dried up completely. Their hearts were broken. Tears flowed down from their eyes incessantly. They became almost mad seeing their dead husband lying on the battlefield. The head of their husband was rolling on the ground while his body lay motionless to one side. The queens joined the head and the body of their husband together and lamented.

"O dear one! How can you leave us like this? Our hearts break when we see you lying here on the ground instead of lying on the golden couch. You look like the moon and even the celestial women desire your company. The same body which we used to decorate with sandalwood and perfume is now lying on the floor. To whom should we narrate our distress? Your chest used to be decorated with flower garlands, and now that same chest is bearing the flow of your own blood. You are very cruel hence you are leaving us like this. Just open your eyes and look at us only once. If we have committed any mistake then punish us. Why don't you speak to us anymore?"

As the wives of Vajranabha cried pathetically like this, suddenly many vultures, foxes, and jackals came there, and to the utter disbelief of the wives, these animals began to eat up the head and body of the king right in front of their eyes. The jackals chewed at the king's head and made him unrecognizable. Other animals tore apart the king's body, and had a delightful feast on it.

The wives continued, "Look, O dear! Your brother Sunabha is also lying here dead. Now what will happen to us? We know now you don't require us any more as you have got the lap of a young girl in the form of the earth. Why didn't anyone tell him that one should not neglect the love and affection of women?" As Vajranabha's wives were crying like this, Krishna and Indra, who were watching and hearing all these talks thought it wise to pacify them.

## Krishna pacifies the wives of the demon

Krishna and Indra boarded a chariot and arrived before the queens. Krishna said to the queens, "Do not lament and cry like this. What was destined to happen has happened. No one can reverse the decision of providence. Your husband was too independent, and thus never bothered to hear the good advice given by saintly persons. He gave trouble to the inhabitants of the three worlds. So there is no point lamenting. You go and arrange for his funeral ceremony."

Then the funeral of the demon was performed by the demon's queens. Krishna then collected the huge amount of wealth stored in the king's treasury, loaded it on bullock carts and sent it to Dwaraka. He then divided Vajranabha's kingdom into four parts and gave one part each to Hamsaketu, Gunavanta, Vijaya who was the son of Jayanta, and Candraprabha. The Lord then left for Dwaraka along with His three sons and daughters-in-law.

Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna's blissful pastimes while engaging his mind on His lotus feet.

## Sudama Brahmana

O human beings! Just hear the glorious pastimes of Lord Krishna. Now I will describe how the brahmana Sudama was blessed by his childhood friend Krishna with material opulence and liberation.

There was a poor brahmana named Sudama who lived in the city of Avantipura. He was very unhappy because of his poverty-stricken life. He was living out his meager life with his wife, however, his mind was fixed on Krishna, for his goal of life was the lotus feet of Krishna, and he thought only of Krishna while begging alms to sustain himself and his wife. His attention was never diverted to any other object except Krishna. The great brahmana and his wife tolerated many difficulties and went on with their lives. They did not engage in any impious activities and always remembered Lord Hari.

One day, Sudama's wife spoke softly to her husband expressing the great unhappiness she was holding in her heart. "My dear Lord, you once told me that you have a friend named Krishna, who is the Lord of the three worlds. At present He is the king of Dwaraka, and everyone respects Him very much. He seems so opulent that even Indra the king of heaven worships Him. I think that you should go to Him and ask for some financial help. Even if he helps a little, all our poverty will be removed. Please approach Him. I am a woman, how much distress can I bear? If I die out of distress and poverty, your reputation will be hampered."

Speaking in this way, Sudama's wife cried out of lamentation. Sudama thought for a while and decided to go to Dwaraka to meet his childhood friend Krishna. Krishna is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. He is the essence of the entire universe. He appeared in this world to diminish the burden of the earth. Sudama was excited when he thought of meeting his old dear friend.

"Listen I have made up my mind to go to Dwaraka." Sudama said to his wife. "In Krishna's association, I will make my life successful. But before I go just see what we have in our house that I can take for Him as a gift. It will be nice if I can take some sweets for Him." On hearing her husbands words the wife looked everywhere in her house but could not find anything worthwhile to present to Krishna. Finally she saw some broken puffed rice lying in a container in the corner of her room. She took it out and tied it in an old piece of cloth, which had turned black due to not being used for a long time.

Sudama took that insignificant gift and departed for Dwaraka. As he walked, he kept on remembering his childhood encounters with Krishna. Gradually Sudama arrived in the city after surpassing many barren lands, hills, and rivers. Since he was a brahmana, nobody checked him or stopped him from going straight to Krishna's palace. As soon as Sudama entered Krishna's palace, seeing him from a close distance, Krishna immediately stood up with respect and greeted Sudama with affection. At that time, Krishna was sitting with Rukmini on a couch. Krishna asked Rukmini to bring some water quickly to wash Sudama's feet. When Rukmini fetched some water, Krishna personally washed Sudama's feet with great reverence. After washing His friend's feet with water, Krishna sprinkled a few drops of the water on His own head and on Rukmini's head. Thereafter Krishna massaged scented oil on Sudama's body and bathed him. Krishna arranged for various types of sweets and cool drinks to be brought for His dear friend Sudama.

The Lord then guided Sudama to the couch and requested him to take some rest. Krishna Himself stood down by the side of Sudama's feet and began to speak, "My dear friend Sudama, do you remember when we used to study together in the city of Avantipura? Do you still remember how you used to study all the scriptures in the gurukulal Can you recollect how one day the wife of our teacher told all of us to bring some wood from the forest? We all set out in search of wood within the dense forest. After collecting a large amount of wood and tying it together, when we were about to return, there was a hailstorm accompanied by heavy rain. The clouds thundered loudly, and the whole atmosphere became dark. It rained heavily so much that we could not see each other. Some of us began to cry and prayed to the Lord for safety. It was late at night, and we could not return to the gurukula. We spent that night in the forest. Next morning, when our teacher came to know that we had been sent to the forest by his wife to bring some wood , and we had still not returned home, he became very angry at his wife and chastised her. The teacher then went out alone to search for all of us. He wandered about here and there within the forest and called us loudly by our names. When he finally found us, he felt a great sigh of relief. He embraced all of us with great affection and took us back home. He fed us nice foodstuffs with love and affection. O friend! Do you still remember all these things?"

As Krishna narrated this incident, both He and Sudama began to cry. They embraced each other with affection and exchanged many other words. Krishna asked Sudama if he had married, and how his wife was. The brahmana Sudama, however did not give any direct reply to Krishna out of shyness.

## Krishna eats the rice particles offered by Sudama with love

Sudama wa s struck with wonder at seeing Krishna's opulence. He thought how he could give his insignificant food offering of broken rice to the supreme controller? Thinking like this, he hid the rice particles, which he had brought fo r Krishna, under his armpit. But Krishna the Supreme Personality of Godhead understood everything. He joked with Sudama by saying, "How com e you did not bring any milk sweets for Me from your home? You have come to see Me empty handed? I have a feeling that you must have brought some sweets for Me. Why are you hiding them from Me? O friend! Even if someone gives very little, but with love and devotion, I relish it like nectar. But if someone gives Me a lot, without any devotion or love, I become offended."

Speaking like this, Krishna forcibly snatched the packet of rice particles from Sudama and began to eat a morsel of it. As He took one more palmful of that rice Rukmini immediately grabbed His hand and took away the rice particles. She folded her hands and requested Krishna in this way. "O Lord of the three worlds, You ate the insignificant food given by the brahmana. You have deceived us for a long time, for now we know; there are others who m You love more than us."

On that night, Krishna and Sudama slept together and discussed various joyful pastimes. The next morning, Krishna bid farewell to His friend Sudama. They wer e overwhelmed with affection for each other. On the way back home, Sudama thought this way, "I met my friend who is the Lord of lords. He respected and treated me like His elder brother, but did not give me any wealth. Now how will I solace my dear wife?" Then again he thought, "It is good that Krishna did not give me any wealth, otherwise I would forget His lotus feet while being puffed up by pride of wealth." While contemplating in this way, the brahmana Sudama finally arrived on the outskirts of his village Avantipura.

## Sudama becomes astonished to see the jewel-studded palace

As he approached the place where his house had always stood, Sudama was surprised not to find his own house there. "What has happened to my house?" He thought. "Who tore it down? Where is my wife?" As he thought like this, he grew frightened and hopeless. Looking up, to his utter surprise, he saw a huge palace standing exactly where his house used to be. The palace was decorated with golden pitchers; all the rooms were made of jewels. The boundary wall almost touched the sky. Everything was decorated with crystals. There was a beautiful courtyard, and the rooftops were decorated with coral and pearls. On the four sides of the palace, there were nice lakes. It was nothing less than the palace of Indra. In the gardens were varieties of flower plants and trees. Within the palace, there was a throne studded with jewels, and the rooms in the palace were decorated with jewels and other precious stones. The soft beds were adorned with diamonds, jewels and so on. Sudama also saw many maidservants who were all decorated with golden ornaments. There were many elephants and horses in that palace. Sudama was completely bewildered. He thought, "Where am I? Where have I come? Whose palace is this? It seems some king has suddenly built a huge palace, or maybe Indra has constructed a garden palace for himself."

Suddenly Sudama saw a group of beautiful ladies decorated with ornaments coming out from the palace. To his complete surprise, Sudama saw his own wife in the midst of them. As soon as the wife saw her husband Sudama, she immediately worshiped him with padya and arghya and welcomed him to the palace. She gave him a nice bath and dressed him with divine cloth. She fed him palatable foodstuffs and made him lie down on a soft bed. When Sudama saw all these opulence, he gradually understood that it was Lord Narayana who had given him all of it. He also understood that the Supreme Lord had deceived him in a way. Although Krishna had given Sudama everything he and his wife could possibly have wanted, Sudama decided not to fall prey to mundane temptation. Rather, he concentrated his mind on the lotus feet of his dear friend Krishna, and because of his unflinching faith and devotion, at the end, Krishna awarded liberation to Sudama.

## Krishna and His associates go to Prabhasa Tirtha

Lord Hari enjoyed His transcendental pastimes at Dwaraka. He diminished the burden of the earth by performing various wonderful pastimes. In due course of time, the day of the solar eclipse arrived. All the inhabitants of Dwaraka departed for Prabhasa to take a holy dip. This place is very sacred especially during the time of solar eclipse. It is said that Lord Parasurama had previously performed great austerities here. Krishna along with His family members and associates also departed for Prabhasa. All men and women of Dwaraka went to Prabhasa with great enthusiasm. All the elderly people headed by Yudhisthira went there, along with their wives and children. The cowherd men of Vraja headed by Nanda also went there in the company of the gopis. Great kings who ruled vast kingdoms like Anga, Banga, and Kalinga also came along with their respective entourages and worshiped that holy place. They took a holy dip in the sacred water, offered oblations to their respective forefathers, and gave a huge amount of wealth in charity. There were waves of joy everywhere. Because of this auspicious occasion, Kunti and Vasudeva got an opportunity to meet each other after a long time. Kunti cried profusely on seeing her brother Vasudeva. She breathed heavily as soon as she saw Krishna and Balarama. She said to Vasudeva, "O brother! I have gone through a great deal of harassment at the hands of the Kauravas. I was particularly disturbed when I had to live in exile with my five sons. By your mercy and by the mercy of the Supreme Lord, we were saved."

Vasudeva tried to pacify Kunti by saying, "My dear sister! I have heard about all the trouble that the wicked Duryodhana was giving you. The sinful Kamsa had arrested me and kept me in the jail. That is why I could not help you in any way. Although Kamsa and his associates were finally eliminated by Krishna, still the trouble was far from over. Then it was the turn of Jarasandha to give us trouble. Out of fear of him, we had to leave Mathura and go to Dwaraka. Krishna is very kind. He built a palace in Dwaraka and has given us protection."

Speaking in this way, brother and sister embraced each other and cried so much. Nanda, Yasoda, and the gopis were standing there as well. They approached Krishna and Balarama, but could not check their tears of love and affection. Mother Yasoda tightly hugged Krishna, and spoke to Him in an emotional voice, "My dear Krishna! How did You forget Vrndavana? How did You forget all the cowherd boys and girls?" How did You forget the city of Gokula? How did You forget the Govardhana Hill?" Speaking in this way, Yasoda cried profusely as she embraced Krishna with affection. The whole body of Krishna became soaked with Mother Yasoda's tears.

Thereafter, the gopis slowly came before their beloved Lord Krishna. They simply kept on looking at Krishna's face without blinking their eyes. Their eyes and hearts could not get enough of the sight of Krishna's beauty. Krishna on the other hand created an illusion and pacified all of them with sweet nectarean words, "O friends! I am not the controller. Everything is being done by the arrangement of providence. It is the Supreme Lord who brings all of us together and then separates us as well."

In this way, Krishna and the residents of Vrndavana exchanged various tales and were jubilant to meet each other. Meanwhile Rukmini met Draupadi and exchanged pleasantries. They sat down together with the other chaste ladies and began to discuss various topics.

## Draupadi narrates the story of her marriage

Rukmini was very glad to meet Draupadi. In a lighter vein, she said to Draupadi, "My dear Draupadi, you are one woman, but you have got five husbands. How do you manage them? How do you please all of them? Tell me everything. How was your marriage arranged? When I heard about you and your marriage, I was very surprised. I had a great desire to meet you personally and talk to you."

When Draupadi was asked in this way, she gave up her shyness and began to speak. "My father arranged a svayamvara ceremony for my marriage. Many powerful kings assembled in that ceremony. It was decided that anyone who could pierce the eye of a fish placed on a moving wheel would become by husband. Many kings came forward but failed in their attempts. Finally, the great Arjuna arrived there in the dress of an ascetic and effortlessly pierced the eye of the fish with his sharp arrow. At this, all the kings charged him with their swords, but Arjuna defeated all of them, and took me away to the forest where he and his brothers were living. Arjuna and his four brothers went to their mother Kunti and said that they had brought a wonderful gift. To this, Mother Kunti said, 'Whatever it is, you must equally share.' When she came to know that the wonderful gift was in fact a young girl, Kunti became little bit perplexed. Nevertheless, the five brothers could not go against the words of their mother, and in any case, by then all of their eyes were transfixed on me. Just then, the great sage Vyasadeva arrived there and revealed the details of my identity. He said that my name was Pancali and that this was my destiny. Thereafter, the five brothers married me under the supervision of great sage Narada. I have served and satisfied each of them to my best capacity and without any discrimination."

After Draupadi narrated the story of her marriage, she inquired from Rukmini about how Krishna who carries a cakra had married her.

## The story of the marriage of Rukmini

When the beautiful Rukmini heard about Draupadi's marriage, she was very happy. She then began to narrate to Draupadi, the story of her own marriage.
