"Lord Krishna personally kidnapped me from the svayamvara arena. Although my father had a desire to get me married to Krishna, my brother Rukmi protested it. He made a conspiracy and decided to give my hand to Sisupala. He induced my father in so many ways. When I heard about it, I lost consciousness. Very soon I secretly sent a brahmana messenger to Dvaraka with a message for Krishna that He should come immediately and kidnap me. Govinda did come and kidnapped me during my svayamvara ceremony. All the assembled kings became furious, and they began to fight with Krishna, but Krishna easily defeated all of them single-handedly. He then brought me to Dwaraka and married me according to the proper rules and regulations. My father came to my marriage ceremony and gave my hand to Krishna. After this, my father returned home, and since then I have been serving the lotus feet of Govinda just like a maidservant."

After hearing this wonderful story of Rukmini's marriage to Krishna, Draupadi turned to Satyabhama and asked, "How did Govinda marry you?" Satyabhama smiled and replied, "My uncle died in the forest, but my father thought that it was Krishna who had killed him. He always blamed Krishna for his brother's death. Realizing the situation, Krishna personally went to Patalaloka and vigorously fought with Jambavan and took away the Syamantaka jewel from him. Krishna then handed that jewel to my father, who immediately returned it to Krishna, and also handed me over to Krishna in marriage. I always meditate on that Lord Narayana and pray that I may remain under His lotus feet birth after birth."

With great enthusiasm, Draupadi then turned to Jambavati and asked, "How did the husband of Laksmi marry you?" Jambavati replied, "Listen with care how the carrier of the cakra got my hand in marriage. In order to retrieve the Syamantaka jewel, Krishna entered into Patalaloka and took away the jewel from my father Jambavan after a fierce battle which lasted twenty-seven days. After defeating my father, Krishna compassionately displayed His incarnation as Lord Ramacandra to my father. My father felt embarrassed for fighting with his Lord. He quickly brought Him home and worshiped Him with faith and devotion. He not only returned the jewel to Him, but also gave my hand to Krishna in marriage. From that day onward, I have been serving and meditating on His lotus feet. May I always be fortunate enough to serve His lotus feet birth after birth."

Draupadi then asked Kalindi how she got married to Krishna. To this, she folded her hands and began to speak. "The Lord of the universe once saw me and became attracted to my beauty. He said to my father, 'Your daughter is very beautiful and young. Lord Hari has descended into this world to diminish the burden of the earth and I think He is the most suitable husband for your daughter within the three worlds. If your daughter performs some penance, then she can easily get hold of Lord Narayana.' When my father told me about this, I went to Hastinapura and performed severe penance on the bank of the Ganges. The Supreme Lord is seated in the hearts of all living entities. He understood my intention and came there with His friend Arjuna. He brought me to the city. King Yudhisthira arranged a grand festival in honor of our marriage. I constantly think about Lord Narayana's lotus feet. I pray that I may always remain a soul surrendered to Him."

Draupadi then asked Mitravinda how she got Madhusudana as her husband. Mitravinda replied, "Listen, O Pancali! I must have undergone severe penance for many lifetimes, which is why I was blessed with this boon. My father is a great devotee and his heart is always fixed on Krishna's lotus feet. He always wanted me to marry Krishna. However, my two brothers Vinda and Anuvinda were enemies of Krishna. Therefore they opposed my father and arranged a svayamvara for my marriage. When I came to know about it, I began to worship the goddess Gauri. I worshiped her with fixed determination for a long time. Finally, realizing my intentions, Lord Hari came on a chariot and took me away after defeating everyone else. In this way, He married me, and I have been serving His lotus feet ever since. Please bless me so that I may remain His eternal maidservant birth after birth."

When Draupadi asked Bhadra how she got married to Krishna, she replied. "O beautiful Draupadi! Krishna is actually my maternal brother in relation. My father is a great devotee of the Supreme Lord. He knew that the Lord has made His appearance in this world to deliver the pious and annihilate the miscreants. He sent my brother to Dwaraka and invited Krishna to his own house. When the lotus-eyed Lord came, my father duly married me to Him and gave Him wealth, gifts, and maidservants. He instructed me to serve Krishna with the utmost faith and love. O daughter of Draupada, it is my good fortune that I have got a husband like the Supreme Lord, who holds a cakra in His hand."

Draupadi then asked Nagnajiti, "How did Krishna marry you? Tell me, I am very curious to know." Nagnajiti replied, "O princess! I must have had heaps of piety to be able to get a husband like Lord Hari. My fortunate father made a severe promise after consulting his ministers that anyone who could bring seven bulls with sharp horns under his control and tie them up would marry me. As a matter of fact, no one was able to control even one single bull. When the great hero Krishna heard about the test, He immediately came, passed it, and won my hand. Lord Madhusudana married me and took me to Dwaraka. I have worshiped His lotus feet life after life, as a result I now have Him as my husband."

Then Draupadi turned to Laksmana and requested her to narrate her story of marrying Krishna. Laksmana said, "My father promised to give my hand in marriage to anyone who could pierce the eyes of a fish placed by him on a moving wheel. It was a similar condition that was put forth by your father for your marriage. No warrior was able to pass the tough test. Even Arjuna failed. He could only manage to touch the bow and nothing else. Indeed, he left the arena feeling quite embarrassed. Then Krishna arrived, and effortlessly pierced the eyes of the fish in front of everyone. My parents were happy. They immediately invited Krishna to their home and performed our marriage with great pomp. My father gave many gifts in charity. Since then, I have always held the lotus feet of my husband within my heart and served Him with all humility."

Draupadi then asked the sixteen thousand queens with folded hands, "Now tell me, how did Krishna marry all of you?" The queens replied, "Narakasura was a sinful king who conquered the entire world with his immense bodily strength. He kidnapped all of us one by one and kept us confined in a cave. We became extremely frightened, and we simply prayed to Krishna for deliverance. Krishna being situated in the hearts of all realized our plight and immediately came on the back of Garuda and rescued us. Lord Govinda killed Naraka and his associates and delivered all of us from the clutches of that sinful demon. All :\>f us had already accepted Krishna as our husband in our hearts. None of us could have ever refused to marry Him. Krishna fulfilled our desires and treated all of us equally. In this way, our lives have become successful by Krishna's mercy."

Krishna's pastimes are extraordinary and wonderful. All the queens of Krishna forgot themselves completely as they narrated their respective stories. Draupadi too became astonished to hear such enchanting pastimes. She said to the queens, "You are all greatly fortunate for you have gotten Krishna as your husband. No one can properly glorify your good fortune." After exchanging words with relatives and family members, and enjoying a happy stay at Prabhasa, everyone returned to their respective homes.

The pastimes of Krishna are full of nectar, and one who hears them becomes immortal. Thus Gunaraja Khan prays at the lotus feet of Krishna to be able to remember Him constantly.

## Vasudeva performs a fire sacrifice

…peformance of a fire sacrifice. Lord Krishna was personally present at that sacrifice. All those great sages who came to Prabhasa went to the house of Vasudeva to see Krishna. Setting an ideal example for others, Vasudeva duly worshiped all the sages with padva and arghya. They all sat down peacefully, and were especially happy to see Krishna and Balarama. Their hearts were filled with love and devotion. Taking this opportunity, Vasudeva inquired from them about the principles of religion. "Which religious principles should I follow? How can a householder be delivered from material life?" Vasudeva asked the sages.

The sages were perplexed to hear these questions of Vasudeva. They thought, "He is asking these questions when, in his own house the Supreme Personality of Godhead is personally present? He always sees that Supreme Lord by whose remembrance people get liberation and become supremely religious. Yet he is asking about religious principles. We do not understand this. It is said that if one is too close to an object he often forgets about it. One may not have intense devotion all the time, if one is always with the Supreme Lord. A person who lives by the side of the Ganges often goes to take bath in a holy place far away from his home."

All the assembled sages then requested Narada to speak about the principles of religion to Vasudeva. Narada then said, "O Vasudeva! It is good that you have rightly inquired about one's prescribed duties. The Supreme Personality of Godhead is personally present in your house. Great personalities chant mantras, practice penance, offer worship while following the rules and regulations, control the mind and senses, meditate on Absolute Truth, just to attain the shelter of the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord. Great devotees like Sanaka, Sanatana, Siva, and Karttika always meditate on Him through yoga. Although they tried their best to understand the Supreme Lord, they were unsuccessful. The illusory energy of the Supreme Lord is very difficult to overcome. In order to display mercy on His devotees, the Supreme Lord has now appeared as your son. O Vasudeva! There is no one in the whole world more fortunate than you. The Lord of the universe Himself is present in your house. Behold Him, worship Him, and love Him. Simply by serving Him you will attain liberation. Your two sons Krishna and Balarama are the Supreme Brahman, yet you are inquiring about the principles of religion? This is very surprising."

"Nevertheless, I will explain to you in brief about the duties of the human beings. It is the duty of a householder to perform sacrifices. Brahma created the human beings so that they can perform sacrifices. If this system is checked then the demigods will not be pleased at all. Those who do not perform sacrifices are at fault. This is the instruction of the Supreme Lord." When Vasudeva heard these words, he became very satisfied. He looked upon his two sons with love and devotion. Krishna too gave Vasudeva immense pleasure by serving him with a smile.

One day not long afterwards, Krishna spoke to His father in a playful manner. "My dear father, I think whatever Narada has said is correct. Every householder is duty-bound to perform sacrifices. Besides, there are ample ingredients for conducting a sacrifice. You should arrange a sacrifice at this place because this place is sacred and all the sages are already here." Saying this, Krishna invited all the assembled sages to be present in the sacrificial arena.

On the day of the sacrifice, everything was arranged perfectly. All the ingredients for sacrifice as well as the foodstuffs had been collected sufficiently. Krishna personally looked after all the sages and other guests. He greeted them with respect and worshiped them by offering ghee, honey, five kinds of grains, flowers, and fruits. A spot was selected for performing the sacrifice. First, the spot was cleansed and then tilled with a golden plough. The sages dug a sacrificial pit in the middle of that spot. By and by many great sages and personalities like Vyasa, Vasistha, Parasara, Dhaumya, Visvamitra, and Bhrgu arrived there along with their disciples. There were debates and counter-debates as is customary among the sages, nevertheless the whole function proceeded smoothly. Everyone received sufficient amounts of wealth in charity. These personalities were pure-hearted and expert in all fields of activities. The whole tract of land became purified by their presence. They were learned, pious, and full of effulgence. They spoke sweetly and conclusively. There were the five Pandava brothers, Duryodhana and his one hundred brothers, Bhisma, Drona, Krpa, Kama, Jayadratha, Kratha, Kausika, Satyaki, Mahasatya, Satanlka, Bmadratha, Dhrstadyumna, Dhrstaketu, Vidura, Ketu, Candraketu, and many others. They all sat down around the sacrificial arena to watch the sacrificial performance. Krishna duly worshiped every one of them in a manner befitting their position. They were offered various gifts, a golden throne to sit on, opulent dress, ornaments, and jewels. The demigods also arrived in the outer space and began to watch the ceremony. King Vasudeva then personally handed different gifts to all the assembled sages. Many personalities from the Yadu dynasty like Akrura and Uddhava were also present.

## Vasudeva begins the sacrifice

At an auspicious moment, Vasudeva began his sacrifice. All the assembled sages chanted the invocation mantras. All the containers for performing sacrifice were made of gold. There were different types of jewels and other precious stones. It appeared as if the peak of the Himagiri Mountain had come on earth. Vasudeva first worshiped all the brahmana priests by offering them each some sandalwood paste, a flower garland, and various ornaments. After taking permission from all the assembled sages, Vasudeva lighted the sacrificial fire while chanting the appropriate mantras. All the assembled sages also joined Vasudeva in chanting mantras and pouring ghee in the fire. Everyone poured ghee in the fire according to his own desire. Thereafter Krishna personally fed all the sages and the brahmanas. There were four kinds of eatables: those which are chewed, drunk, sucked, and licked. Everyone had a sumptuous meal. Thereafter, all the poor people present were fed to their full satisfaction. They were also given various gifts. Vasudeva gave away innumerable elephants, horses, servants, and maidservants. The demigods headed by Brahma personally appeared in that sacrificial arena and accepted the oblations of ghee poured in the fire. Thereafter the demigods offered their respectable obeisances to Lord Govinda and praised Him and Vasudeva for conducting this great sacrifice. Vasudeva gave offerings to all the assembled sages, demigods and brahmanas. The whole earth became surcharged with the sweet aroma of the sacrifice. Thus all the demigods and the human beings could not stop glorifying Vasudeva's sacrifice.

## The completion of Vasudeva's sacrifice

After satisfying everyone, Vasudeva offered his final oblations in the sacrificial fire. He gave gifts to all the assembled personalities according to their respective positions. All the sages and demigods were fully satisfied, and thus they took leave from Vasudeva and departed to their own abodes. All the brahmanas and kings were also given sufficient gifts before they departed. No one in the past had performed such a great sacrifice. Everyone began to glorify this good act of Vasudeva. In this way, it was actually Krishna who fulfilled the desires of everyone. All the people of the world began to talk about this wonderful sacrifice.

## The story of the great sage Bhrgu

One day, all the exalted sages headed by Vasistha and Bhrgu were sitting in the sacred place of Naimisaranya. The Supreme Personality of Godhead had assumed the forms of Brahma, Visnu, and Siva in order to take charge of the three material qualities of passion, goodness, and ignorance. But the sages inquired among themselves, "Who among these three personalities is the greatest of all?" The sages could not find an answer to this. They debated for a long time, but could not come to any conclusion. Finally they decided to send Bhrgu to test these three personalities and find out who among the three was the greatest. Sent by the sages, Bhrgu first went to the Kailasa mountain. He saw Siva was sitting alongside Parvati. As soon as Mahadeva saw Bhrgu, he immediately stood up with respect. As he was about to embrace Bhrgu with affection, Bhrgu said to Lord Siva, in a rather harsh manner, "Do not touch me." Bhrgu further rebuked Lord Siva, "You always mingle with ghosts, hobgoblins, and other abominable creatures. How dare you want to touch a brahmana'.?"

When Siva heard these blasphemous words of Bhrgu, he became fiery with anger. He picked up his trident and came charging towards Bhrgu who ran away out of fear. Thereafter Bhrgu went to the abode of Brahma who was sitting in the assembly of demigods. Because Brahma was preoccupied with the meeting he could not display proper respect to Bhrgu. As a result, Bhrgu became very angry and said, "I am a guest at your house. You have not treated me with proper etiquette. You are too much puffed up of your position. Therefore your worship is not worth accepting. Besides you have a bad reputation of running after your own daughter." When Brahma heard these insulting words of Bhrgu, he immediately chased Bhrgu to kill him. Bhrgu quickly ran away from there to save his life.

Finally Bhrgu went to the abode of Lord Narayana in Vaikuntha. At that time, the lotus-eyed Lord was taking rest being served by Laksmi. The great sage thought for a while and entered straight into the room where the Lord was resting. Bhrgu kicked hard on the chest of Narayana and woke Him up. The Lord woke up from His rest and folded His hands with respect.

The Lord said to Bhrgu, "Please forgive My offense. You have come here as My guest, but I could not understand this. I was sleeping when I should have taken care of My guest. You have hit your soft feet on My hard chest, which must have caused you so much pain. Please forgive Me for that. Besides, by your kick, all the contamination from My heart has now vanished."

The Lord offered His repeated obeisances to the great sage Bhrgu and stood there with His folded hands. It took no time for Bhrgu to realize who among the three was the topmost. He then returned to Naimisaranya and explained everything that had happened to all the assembled sages and demigods. They then concluded that the Supreme Lord Visnu who is in charge of the mode of goodness is the greatest of all. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates sri Krishna Vijaya.

## The killing of Vrkasura

O human beings! Pay full attention to the wonderful pastimes of Lord Krishna. There was a formidable demon named Vrkasura who was the son of Sakuni. He was infamous for his demoniac behavior. He conquered the whole world and even the heavenly planets. One day, he went to the hermitage of the sages headed by Bhrgu who were sitting there chanting mantras peacefully. He fell down at the feet of the sages and asked, "Please tell me one thing for sure. I wish to get a boon immediately. Please tell me who among Brahma, Visnu and Siva should I worship?" The sages thought for a while and replied, "You should worship the threeeyed Siva to get a boon quickly."

Thereafter, Vrkasura began to perform severe penance and worshiped Siva with determination. He created a sacrificial pit and daily performed sacrifice by offering various ingredients into the fire. He even slaughtered cows and offered their flesh into the sacrificial fire along with ghee, but even after all of these offerings, still he could not please Siva. When he failed in all his attempts to satisfy Siva, he picked up his own trident and was about to cut off his own head. Just then Siva appeared from the sacrificial fire and stopped him from committing suicide. He grabbed his hands and said, "O Vrka! You may ask for a boon." As soon as Vrka saw his master, he immediately began to offer prayers with folded hands. After Siva was sufficiently pleased with Vrka's prayers, he asked him to take a benediction. Vrka folded his hands and said, "O lord of the universe! If you are pleased with me then give me a benediction that if I place my hand on the head of anyone, his head will be burnt to ashes." Siva consented and awarded Vrka his desired benediction.

When Vrkasura was thus blessed by Siva in this way, he wanted to test the effect of the benediction. So he asked Siva, "Since you have awarded me this benediction, I want to test if it works. I would like to test it first by touching your head." Siva became afraid of this, and he ran away from Vrkasura. The demon was also very clever and powerful so he chased Siva. Siva decided to go back inside his own palace, but Vrka followed him there as well. When Siva saw Vrka chasing him, he went to the abode of Indra, but Vrka also arrived there in no time. Siva kept running away from Vrka, and finally arrived in Dwaraka. Krishna, the Supersoul of all living entities, understood Siva's awkward situation. So the Lord assumed the form of a small boy and came out of His palace. Krishna slowly went before Vrka and spoke to him in a deceptive manner.

"O son of Sakuni! Where are you going? Why are you in a hurry? What happened?" Vrka became very pleased to hear these sweet words of the small boy. He wondered how a small boy could know the name of his father. Vrka sat down before Krishna as he was very tired from chasing Siva. When Krishna again asked him about the reason of his running, he replied, "Siva gave me a benediction. But I think he has cheated me. I wanted to test the effect of his boon by touching his head with my own hand but I could not get hold of him." On hearing his words, Krishna smiled and spoke to Vrka politely. "You are an intelligent person. Why do you trust the words of a madman?"

## Vrkasura was burnt to ashes

"If you really believe his words, then why don't you touch your own head?" the young boy said to Vrka. The demon thought for a while and concluded, "This small boy has actually opened my eyes. Siva's benediction must be false, otherwise why is he running away? The wicked sages have deceived me and made me go through this hard labor. I am so foolish that I never thought about it before."

After speaking in this way Vrkasura placed his hand on his own head. To everyone's surprise, the whole body of Vrkasura was immediately burnt into ashes. The child who was standing there assumed His original form as Krishna and stood there smiling. Siva, who was thus saved from imminent danger, began to offer prayers to Krishna with folded hands.

## Siva prays to Krishna

"O Supreme Lord Narayana! You are the cause of the universe. It is You who creates, maintains, and annihilates the whole material manifestation. Today I am in trouble because of my own foolishness. You have saved me by killing that demon in no time. It is very difficult to understand Your illusory energy. O Lord Hari! Please remove Your energy so that I can understand You."

After hearing Siva's humble words, Krishna embraced Siva with affection. O human beings! There is no difference between Krishna and Siva. Anyone who discriminates between them suffers profusely. After this incident, both Krishna and Siva returned to their respective residences.

## Krishna delivers the dead sons of a brahmana of Dwaraka

The Supreme Lord Krishna who wears a garland of forest flowers enjoyed His life in the city of Dwaraka. He was surrounded by His sons and grandsons. In the city of Dwaraka was a brahmana named Vipradeva who lived with his wife in a happy way. In due course of time, his wife gave birth to a baby boy, but immediately after the child was bom, he died. The brahmana couple cried profusely and lamented greatly. The brahmana blamed his wife for the death of his son, "Because of your sin, I have lost my son," He said to his wife. "That is not true; I have not committed any sin since my birth." His wife replied to him.

The wise brahmana thought for a while and took his dead son to the palace of Krishna. Upon reaching there, he said to Krishna, "O controller of the universe! O Govinda! Just look how I have lost my child. I think it must be because of Your fault and Your sin that my child has died untimely."

The brahmana was about to leave the dead child behind on the doorstep of Krishna's palace, when Krishna hurriedly came before him and said, "O great brahmana\\ Why do you behave like this? Your son did not die because of My sin. If your wife becomes pregnant again, then My son Pradyumna will personally protect your child." After solacing the brahmana, Krishna sent him home. In due course of time, his wife gave birth to yet another son, but again this time the son died immediately after his birth right in front of Pradyumna's eyes. The brahmana lamented profusely and said, "Shame on you, O Pradyumna! How could this happen in front of you? You should be ashamed of yourself because you gave me your word that you would protect my son." As before, the brahmana picked up his dead son and went to Krishna. He said to the Lord, "Listen, O Krishna! This is the second time I have lost my son. You will be responsible for killing two brahmana sons."

Krishna pacified the brahmana in a sweet manner and said that next time the great hero Samba will give protection to his son. But once more for the third time this new son died soon after his birth. The brahmana became furious and scolded Samba saying, "You are a disgrace to your family. Why have you taken birth in this world?" Again the brahmana took his dead son and placed him on the doorstep of Krishna's palace.

Krishna immediately called Satyaki by His side and said to the brahmana "Please do not feel sad. This time Satyaki will protect your son." But to everyone's surprise, the fourth child of the brahmana died soon after his birth. The brahmana chastised Satyaki and went before Krishna and criticized Him, too. Krishna stood up with reverence and then fell down at the feet of the brahmana. The Lord pacified him and said that next time He would send Aniruddha to protect his son. The fifth time also the brahmana's son died soon after his birth. The brahmana couple lamented unlimitedly and accused Aniruddha fo r negligence. He took the dead son and went to Krishna. He complained repeatedly and blasphemed Krishna.

Krishna tried His best to pacify the brahmana. He said to him, "Take My word, next time the great warrior Gada will give protection to your son." The brahmana took Gada home. In due course of time, his wife gave birth to a male child, but as usual, the child died immediately after his birth. The brahmana could not control himself any longer. He condemned Gada severely and went to Krishna with his dead son.

The brahmana said to Krishna, "One by one I have now lost six sons. Now people of the world will call You the killer of brahmanas." Krishna folded His hands and humbly said to the brahmana, "Kindly forgive My offense. Take Uddhava with you this time and I am sure he will protect your son." In due course of time, the brahmana had a son who also died in front of Uddhava. Like before the brahmana lamented so much and went to Krishna with his dead son. Krishna again pacified the brahmana and assured him that next time King Ugrasena himself would give protection to his son. Although a king, Ugrasena personally went to the brahmana's house. This time again, to the couple's surprise and dismay, their eighth son too died soon after his birth. The couple became red with anger and chastised Ugrasena profusely. "You are useless," The brahmana angrily said to King Ugrasena, "You do not know how to rule a kingdom. Under your rule, I have lost so many of my sons. Because of your sins and improper behavior, my sons have died. Listen, O sinful king! I will not stay in this city any longer. You have ruined the whole city of Dwaraka." After speaking these words, the brahmana went to Krishna and placed his eighth dead son before Him. Just then the great warrior Arjuna arrived there. The brahmana was so disgusted that he had decided to quit home and live in the forest as a sannyasl. Krishna folded His hands and pacified the brahmana in various ways.

… arjuna…brahmana. It's a shame that many powerful archers could not protect your sons. There is a reason why you have lost many sons untimely. I make a promise to you that if your wife gives birth again, I will personally stay in your house and protect your son. I will protect your entire house by spreading a network of my arrows." When the brahmana heard this promise, he laughed in disbelief. He replied in an angry mood, "I have seen many powerful warriors and personalities who also made similar promises. But all of them failed to keep up their promises. Therefore do not make false promises and expose yourself." To this, Arjuna said, "O brahmana! You do not know who I am. My fame is spread within the three worlds. I am not a child like Kamadeva or a less intelligent Satyaki or Gada or Uddhava or Ugrasena. People of the whole world know about the strength of my Gandiva bow. If need arises, I will conquer Yamaraja and bring back your dead son." Still the brahmana could not keep full faith in the words of Arjuna. "You are too proud; you had better give up your false ego." he told Arjuna. "I think you have no power to save the son of a brahmana. It is only Krishna who is capable of protecting my son." To this, Arjuna replied, "My dear brahmana, I swear in front of all of these people that if I fail to give protection to your son, then I will give up my weapons and enter into a pyre of blazing fire."

In due course of time, the brahmanas wife became pregnant and as promised arjuna took his weapons and went to the brahmana's house to protect his child. At first, Arjuna sealed the whole house with a network of arrows. But despite Arjuna's many precautions, as usual the wife gave birth to a male child who died immediately after his birth. The death happened right in front of Arjuna. The spirit soul left the body of the male child and the body kept lying there. Arjuna was perplexed and angry. He looked around, but could not find anyone who could have taken away the child's life. Arjuna took his weapons and went directly to the abode of Yamaraja. But he did not find the brahmana's son there. From there he went to the abode of Varuna, to the abode of Kuvera, to the abode of Brahma and to the abode of Indra. Still he could not find a son of the brahmana. He searched as far as the sunlight and moonlight could reach, but to no avail. Finally he returned to Dwaraka. He went to the brahmana's residence and admitted his inability to protect his son.

As he had promised earlier, Arjuna then decided to enter into a blazing fire to give up his life. Just as Arjuna was about to enter into the blazing fire to end his life, Krishna arrived there. The Lord smiled and said, "O Arjuna! There is no need to give up your life. Come with Me, and we will bring back the dead sons of the brahmana."

Then Krishna and Arjuna mounted on a chariot and went towards the north. They crossed beyond the seven islands and seven seas. They passed through many planets, and ultimately arrived in a city known as Kancana-nagara. The place was so dark that their chariot was unable to move further. The almighty Krishna left His chariot behind and walked with Arjuna. He used the effulgence of His Sudarsana cakra to remove the darkness. In a short time, they found a beautiful palace where an attractive lotus-eyed personality was sitting down. He had four hands in which He held a conch, a disc, a club and a lotus. He was decorated with deer musk. He had one thousand heads which were decorated with jewel studded crowns. As soon as that great personality saw Krishna, he immediately stood up in awe and reverence and offered many prayers. He worshiped Krishna by offering Him padya and arghya, and when Krishna and Arjuna had been thus greeted and worshiped properly, they began to look around. They saw the nine dead sons of the brahmana were there in that place.

Krishna then asked the mysterious personality, "Why did you bring these sons of the brahmana here?" That personality folded his hands and said, "O Lord Hari! I live at the edge of the seven islands. I was worried about how my country would be delivered. Thinking like this, I decided to steal the sons of the brahmana so that I would be able to see Your lotus feet when You come here in search of those boys. I had heard that You had descended to diminish the burden of the earth. I had an intense desire to see Your beautiful transcendental form. I could not think of any other way to make You come here, hence I stole the sons of the brahmana. Now by Your mercy I have been blessed by the dust of Your lotus feet. Today my life has become successful. I consider myself truly fortunate to be able to see Your lotus feet. My Lord! Now please take these sons of the brahmana back with You."

Krishna and arjuna were very happy. They took the sons on a chariot and left that place. Soon they arrived in the city of Dwaraka and blew their conch shells. The inhabitants of Dwaraka came rushing to see Krishna. Krishna handed over the nine sons to their brahmana father. Arjuna had personally witnessed this wonderful feat, and so he began to describe Krishna's extraordinary activities to all the residents of Dwaraka led by Ugrasena. Everyone talked about this incident.

O human beings! Krishna's characteristics are wonderful to hear. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya.

## Krishna brings back the six dead sons of Devaki

One day, Krishna was sitting with His mother Devaki joyfully discussing various anecdotes with her. For Mother Devaki, Krishna was still a child. She thought to herself, "My child Krishna is performing many wonderful activities." Then Mother Devaki said to Krishna in a sad voice, "O Krishna! I have seen and heard about Your glories so much. You are not an ordinary child I feel. You brought back the nine dead sons of a brahmana. This feat is not possible for any ordinary person. There is no doubt in my mind that You are a great personality. Although I am Your mother, I have a request for You. I beg You to bring back to life my six sons who were mercilessly killed one after another by kamsa. My whole body is burning with lamentation and grief. I will be indescribably happy if You can kindly bring back my six dead sons."

After Devaki spoke like this, Krishna just smiled and left the palace after offering obeisances to His mother. The lotus-eyed Krishna mounted on His chariot and went off to bring back His six brothers. The Lord went directly to the planet called Patalaloka which is ruled by Bali Maharaja. The abode of Bali is situated in the Rasatala planet. As soon as Bali saw Krishna, he immediately stood up with respect and offered his humble obeisances at the Lord's lotus feet. He seated Krishna on a throne and worshiped Him by offering padya and arghya.

"O Supreme Lord! I know You have made your appearance on the earthly planet." Bali offered prayers to the Lord. "It is my good fortune that I am able to see and touch Your lotus feet. Today my entire abode, my family and myself have become purified by Your presence. Now, please tell me, what brings You here?"

Krishna replied with a smile, "O great king! You have the six sons of My mother with you. Mahamaya had arranged these six sons to take birth from the womb of My mother Devaki. When kamsa killed all of them one after another, they came here. My mother has a great desire to see her sons, therefore she ordered Me to bring them back to her. This is the reason I am here. Now, please give Me those six sons and let Me return." On hearing these words of Krishna, Bali immediately brought the six sons and handed them over to Krishna who promptly took them and returned to Dwaraka.

Krishna returned the six sons to Mother Devaki in the same position as they had been when they were killed by Kamsa. Devaki's happiness knew no bounds. As soon as she saw her sons, the milk began to flow from her breasts. The six sons began to drink her breast milk to their satisfaction. In this way, Krishna delivered His paternal dynasty. All the senior members of the Yadu dynasty headed by Vasudeva came to see the six sons and glorified Krishna unlimitedly. Just then, six chariots arrived on that spot from the outer space.

The six sons assumed their original forms and began to offer prayers to Krishna. "O Lord! You are the sum and substance of the whole cosmic manifestation. We have no power to sing Your glories. Today by Your touch, we have become liberated. We have become relieved from our curse by Your mercy. Now please give us permission so that we can go back to our own abode."

Krishna, although He knows everything, pretended as if He did not know anything. He asked the six personalities, "Who are you all?" Where are you going?" The six personalities folded their hands and replied, "O Hari! We are the sons of Marici. The name of our mother is Urna. We were very restless and did not fear and respect the sages. One day, we happen to meet the great sage Angira, but we failed to show him proper respect. As a result of our impudence, he became very angry and said, 'You are the sons of a sage and yet you did not offer respects to me. For this reason, you will have to take birth in the family of a demon as brothers.' When we heard this curse, we became greatly frightened and we tried to pacify him by offering him prayers. The sage became somewhat compassionate and assured us, 'When Lord Hari will incarnate in this world you will become delivered by His touch.' Since then, we took birth in the dynasty of Hiranyakasipu as the sons of Bali. Thereafter, goddess Mahamaya, by Your order, put us within the womb of Mother Devaki and had us killed by Kamsa. As we were killed by Kamsa one after the other, we again returned to the family of Bali and lived together. Now, by Your mercy we have been delivered. We wish to go back to our own abode."

Speaking like this, they offered obeisances to Krishna and sat down on a divine chariot. Everyone was astonished to see this extraordinary incident. They could not stop talking about Krishna's wonderful glories and activities. Gunaraja Khan joyfully narrates these pastimes and assures that anyone who hears them will have no fear from Yamaraja.

## Arjuna kidnaps Subhadra

O human beings! Now I will describe how Arjuna went to Dwaraka and kidnapped Subhadra. The great sage Narada had one day visited Hastinapura and instructed all the five Pandava brothers headed by Yudhisthira in this way. "Draupadi is one wife and you all are her five husbands. Therefore, you must show restraint and follow a particular rule. You must each associate with Draupadi individually only for a year at a time. If it so happens that anyone of you breaks this rule, that brother will have to go live in exile for one year." After explaining this procedure to all the brothers, Narada Muni left. In this way, the five brothers lived happily with their common wife Draupadi in Hastinapura.

One night, everyone was sleeping peacefully when all of a sudden, a thief entered the house of a brahmana and stole all his wealth. The brahmana screamed loudly for help. He called out, "O arjuna! Please save me." The great hero Arjuna woke up from his sleep and quickly went to help the brahmana. As he entered the room to take his weapons, he saw King Yudhisthira in the association of Draupadi. Yudhisthira said, "Why did you come here at this hour? Where are you going with weapons in your hand?"

Arjuna did not reply but continued rushing off. In a short while, Arjuna caught hold of the thief and returned all the wealth to the brahmana. Arjuna felt embarrassed that he had broken the rule. Therefore, early the next morning he went to Yudhisthira and said, "I have made a mistake, and I want to rectify it. Let me go into exile for one year. If a ksatriya breaks his promise, his life becomes ruined." However Yudhisthira pacified Arjuna and said, "You need not go live in exile. It was an accident on your part." But Arjuna was adamant. He said, "I beg at your feet that it is not proper for a ksatriya to break his promise."

Because of this incident, Arjuna voluntarily lived in a dense forest for one full year. While in exile, Arjuna happened to visit Dwaraka and met Krishna and Balarama there. Krishna became very glad to meet Arjuna, and they both spent happy days together. One day, Arjuna suddenly saw a beautiful young girl. He asked Krishna, "Who is this girl? What is the name of her father? She is very beautiful within the three worlds and looks exactly like a vidyadhari. She is young and possesses all auspicious symptoms. I think she is not married as yet."

On hearing his words, Krishna smiled and replied, "Her name is Subhadra, and she is my sister. She stays with Me until we find a suitable husband for her." arjuna could not say anything more on this matter, but kept on looking at Krishna's face. Krishna understood Arjuna's intention and said to him, "I think you are attracted to Subhadra's beauty. Tell Me the truth. Are you interested in her? It takes a lot of piety to get a talented girl in marriage."

Arjuna replied, "I have no objection provided You agree to this proposal." Krishna then said, "My dear Arjuna! There is one problem. My elder brother Balarama does not want her to marry you. No one has the courage to do anything against His will. But I have an idea. If you do it, your fame will be spread all over the three worlds. O Arjuna, you take My chariot and driver. The moment you get an opportunity, kidnap My sister Subhadra and run away from here."

Being instructed by Krishna in this way, Arjuna kept himself on guard all the time. He always thought of Subhadra day and night. One day, by the arrangement of providence, the beautiful Subhadra went to take bath alone. Taking this opportunity, Arjuna forcibly picked her up and put her on the chariot. He instructed Daruka to drive the chariot as fast as he could. Soon the inhabitants of Dwaraka informed Baladeva about this incident. Baladeva became furious with anger.

He shouted, "How dare he do this? There is no one within the three worlds who can even think of doing such a cowardly act. Even the demigods headed by Indra have no power to disturb us. This fellow Arjuna is only a child. Today I will send him to the abode of Yamaraja with the help of My club." Saying this, Balarama took His club and ran after Arjuna. He was followed by the great warriors of the Yadu dynasty. At the end of all the warriors was Lord Krishna who wore a garland of forest flowers.

In an angry mood, Balarama drove His chariot very fast. As He was about to come in contact with Arjuna, He suddenly saw Krishna's chariot and His charioteer Daruka. He looked behind with surprise and saw Krishna coming at a distance. Balarama had no difficulty understanding that the whole kidnapping incident was arranged by the will of Krishna. When Krishna came closer to Him, Balarama said in an angry mood, "You helped Arjuna to kidnap Subhadra. You even gave him Your own chariot." Krishna folded His hands and said in a humbled tone to Balarama,

"I do not know anything. Please do not be angry with Me. It is good that Subhadra got a suitable husband. Besides she is young, and she has to get married soon. This is the reason I did not stop Arjuna. There is no one within the three worlds who is as powerful as Arjuna. He tops everyone in the field of behavior, nature, character, qualities, and beauty. Our grandfather had a great respect for King Pandu. Therefore, he arranged for Kunti's marriage to him. The great hero Arjuna is the light of the Candra dynasty. He is well-versed in all scriptures. He strictly follows his occupational duties. When I heard that Arjuna ran away after kidnapping Subhadra, I did not feel any distress in My heart. Besides, Arjuna is just the suitable groom for Subhadra. I am speaking these words from My heart. Now if You wish to do anything about this, You are most welcome."

When Krishna spoke like this, Balarama became pacified. He gave up his anger and smiled. He understood that it was Krishna's own plan, and He could say nothing. Thereafter, Balarama and all the others returned home. Meanwhile, Arjuna went back to Hastinapura and explained the whole incident to King Yudhisthira. Everyone became very joyful at the news. They celebrated Arjuna's marriage with Subhadra in a great pomp. They sang, danced, and played musical instruments. Krishna then formally got Subhadra married to Arjuna in Hastinapura.

The narrative of Arjuna's kidnapping of Subhadra is most wonderful. Thus Gunaraja Khan gladly narrates Sri Krishna Vijaya.

## The saga of Ajamila

human being! Listen carefully as I narrate the glorious anecdotes of the benefit of chanting Lord Narayana's holy name. There was a brahmana named Ajamila who lived in the country of Kanyakubja. He was an obedient son, and he served his parents while observing the vow of celibacy. He would collect flowers from the forest in his village everyday. In fact, he helped his father in various ways. His only concern was to serve and satisfy his parents. In due course of time, he got married to a beautiful girl and spent his time joyfully. Suddenly, one day as he was plucking flowers in the garden, he saw a prostitute. Ajamila became captivated by her beauty. Soon he began to associate with that lady and neglect his duties towards his family and parents.

Ajamila said to that wicked lady, "O beautiful one! I cannot live without you, so please stay with me and make me happy." The prostitute replied, "I am very sinful and low class whereas you are the son of a brahmana. It is not good for you to act in this way. I am very afraid of this. You better go home and leave me alone. Moreover, you have got a beautiful wife at home. Be happy with your own wife and forget a sinful woman like me. I pray at your feet please go back home."

Yet Ajamila had no control over himself. He was completely captivated by lust. He continuously enjoyed the intimate relation with the prostitute. He forgot his parents, wife and household. He eventually built a house outside the village and lived there in the company of the prostitute. Gradually, he begot seven sons from her womb. He spent the rest of his life in a sinful manner. When the duration of his life was about to end, Ajamila became greatly frightened. At the time of his death, he called all his seven sons by his side. Everyone except the youngest one came by his side. Ajamila was very anxious to see his youngest son whose name was Narayana.

He called out for his youngest son, "O Narayana! Where are you?" At that time, the Yamadutas arrived there with ropes made of iron in their hands. They wanted to take away the spirit soul of Ajamila. Just before Ajamila breathed his last, he loudly called out the name of his youngest son, Narayana. As soon as the soul of Ajamila was preparing to leave his body while chanting the name of Narayana, four Visnudutas suddenly arrived there. They all had four hands in which they carried club, lotus, conch and disc. There was a great fight between the Yamadutas and the Visnudutas. The Visnudutas defeated the Yamadutas and untied Ajamila from the clutches of the Yamadutas, chastising the messengers of Yamaraja profusely. They told them, "This brahmana remembered the name of our Lord at the time of his death. Therefore all his sinful reactions accumulated since millions of lifetimes have become destroyed." The Visnudutas delivered Ajamila and went back to Vaikuntha.

In the meantime, the Yamadutas returned to their master, Yamaraja, and complained heavily against the behavior of the Visnudutas. They told their master, "Listen to this incident. We have never seen this before. One brahmana spent his entire life in the association of a prostitute, so we went to bring him here. He was very sinful and committed irreligious activities. We wanted to put him in the hellish life and make him suffer for his sinful acts. We had even arrested him and bound him with ropes. But suddenly, four Visnudutas came there and forcibly took away that brahmana from us. Not only that, they also beat us up severely. You can see the marks of beating on our body. I think you do not have authority over everyone."

Speaking in this way, the Yamadutas all began to cry. Yamaraja became red with anger and retorted, "Tell me correctly why the Visnudutas took such a sinful person away?" The Yamadutas replied, "O king! Although the brahmana committed many sinful activities throughout his life, at the time of his death he chanted the name Narayana and attracted the attention of the Visnudutas. The Visnudutas arrived there and took him away from us. Now it is up to you to do whatever you wish."

After hearing this, Yamaraja instructed his messengers as follows, "I have no right to punish any person who chants the holy name of the Supreme Lord, especially at the time of death. Do not lament. Try to pacify your mind. Do not try to bring such fortunate persons to me in future. The Supreme Lord is so glorious that simply by chanting His holy name, one becomes delivered. No one in the three worlds has the power to realize that Supreme Lord, although He is present everywhere. Just by chanting His holy name and remembering Him at the time of death, one is certain to become delivered. The twelve mahajanas, including myself, know the science of the Absolute Truth. Apart from me, the others are Brahma, Siva, Bali, Narada, Prahlada, Janaka, Manu, Bhisma, Sanat-kumara, Kapila, and Sukadeva; therefore, how can you possibly know the glories of the Supreme Lord? Please do not lament. Be happy and show due respect to the Vaisnavas in future."

Meanwhile Ajamila returned to Vaikuntha and assumed a four-armed transcendental form. O human beings! Try to understand the glories of the Lord's holy name. Fix your mind constantly in thinking of the Supreme Lord and always sing the holy names of Hari. Thus Gunaraja Khan narrates sri Krishna Vijaya.

## Krishna instructs Uddhava

Lord…the demons. He maintained all that had been created and established the principles of religion on the earth. The Lord enjoyed His pastimes with His wives and children in Dwaraka. He gave away charity and performed many sacrifices as an ideal householder. He served His parents and set the ideal example. The abode of Dwaraka was no less than Vaikuntha, and Lord Murari lived there for one hundred and twenty-five years.

Meanwhile, one day in the heavenly planets, Brahma began to contemplate, "The Supreme Lord Hari has descended on earth to decrease her burden. He has killed many demons and delivered many saintly persons. Let me go to Dwaraka and inquire about His next activity."

Thinking in this way, Brahma along with the other demigods went to Dwaraka. Upon reaching the beautiful city, he saw Krishna fully absorbed in blissful pastimes in the company of His wives, sons, and grandsons. Brahma offered his obeisances to the Lord and prayed with folded hands. "O Lord Narayana! Kindly pay attention to my words. You are Brahma, You are Visnu, and You are Narayana. You are Candra, Surya, the demigods, the earth, the sky, the fire, the water, the air, and everything that is. You are the source of everything. You are the cause of the creation, maintenance, and destruction. You are completely unaffected and unchanged. You are the shelter of all living entities. All the conditioned souls are bewildered by Your illusory energy. No one properly knows Your glories. You give happiness and distress to the conditioned souls according to their karma. O Lord Hari! Although You make everything happen, no one can see You.

"When Mother Earth felt distressed and approached us for relief, we the demigods went to the shore of the ocean of milk and prayed to You with faith and devotion. Being compassionate to the living entities of this world. You have appeared and fulfilled Your mission. You have annihilated many demons, and delivered many devotees. You have reestablished the religious principles. Although You are personally present here in this earth, no one knows You. The inhabitants of Vaikuntha are anxiously awaiting Your return. I wonder if You have forgotten all about that. Please, my Lord, tell us about Your intention."

After hearing these words of Brahma, the Supreme Lord smiled and gave them seats to sit down. The Lord then told the demigods, "Whatever you have said is correct. I have also made up My mind to return to Vaikuntha soon. Although I have eliminated many powerful and proud demons, there are some more elements in My own family who will create a burden on earth. The earth is shaking, and I have got to do something about it. I have decided to destroy them with the use of a brahmana'% curse. Thereafter I will return to Vaikuntha. Now you all may go back to your own abode and be happy."

The demigods headed by Brahma became satisfied with the Lord's words, and they all returned to their respective planets. Lord Krishna began to contemplate how to wind up His mission in this world and return to Vaikuntha.

## The sages go to Dwaraka to see Lord Krishna

While Krishna was contemplating in this way, some sages arrived there to meet Him. Upon arriving near the gate of the palace, the sages saw Pradyumna and asked him to inform Krishna that they had come to see Him. As all the sages were sitting comfortably in the courtyard, Samba, dressed as a woman, came before the sages. He artificially placed an iron container in his belly and pretended to be a pregnant woman.

In a spirit of disrespect to the sages, Samba then spoke them as follows, "Tell me, O sages! What kind of creature will I give birth to?" On hearing these words, the sages, especially Durvasa Muni, became very angry. Durvasa Muni then told Samba, "You will soon give birth to a club. This club will be the cause of the destruction of the entire Yadu dynasty." Just as the great sage Durvasa finished speaking, a beautiful club appeared in front of everyone. The members of the Yadu dynasty became particularly frightened on seeing the club, because they knew that this club would be the cause of their ruination. Soon this news reached Krishna who immediately came before the sages to greet them, but when He arrived there, He could not see the sages. Then all the members of the Yadu dynasty cried and fell at the feet of Krishna.

"O Krishna! The sages have cursed us improperly. We made a small mistake but got a huge punishment. Please tell us what we should do at this time of the crisis." Krishna said to them in a deceptive manner, "I have no power to reverse the curse of a brahmana. O sons! Why did you make fun of the sages? Anyway, I have an idea. You take this club to Prabhasa and finish it off by rubbing it against the stone. If you can neutralize it in this way you will be saved."

## Krishna creates an illusion to destroy the Yadu dynasty

When Krishna advised all the Yadavas in this way, they went to Prabhasa with the club. According to Krishna's order, they vigorously ground the club and turned it into powder. When there was a little portion of it left, they threw it into the ocean, but no one can change the plan of the Supreme Lord. The tiny pieces of the remaining ground iron rod that were thrown into the ocean grew to be a forest of tall Eraka trees. Then, another small piece was swallowed by a giant fish, which was caught by fishermen in their net. When the fishermen cut open the fish, they found within its belly a small piece of iron. A hunter named Jara bought that piece of iron and made an arrow with it. He kept it carefully in order to use it for his hunting.

Meanwhile, Krishna continued to pretend that He knew nothing about what was soon to happen. At that time, Uddhava also came to see Krishna. Uddhava thought, "The Supreme Personality of Godhead Krishna is the Lord of the three worlds. He is the proprietor of the whole universe. He has descended to remove the distress of the earth. He has created an illusion on the pretext of a brahmana's curse in order to destroy the Yadu dynasty. I believe that the time has come for my Lord to return to His own abode. Everyone knows me as a dear servant of Krishna, but He has deceived me as well." Thinking like this Uddhava approached Krishna, fell at His lotus feet, and began to cry. Krishna became compassionate upon Uddhava, and spoke to him with a smiling face.

## Krishna reveals His intention to Uddhava

"My dear Uddhava, I appeared in this world to diminish the burden of the earth!" Krishna said. "I have accomplished My mission by killing the demons and delivering the saintly persons. In the course of doing this, I have reestablished the principles of religion. I had a desire to stay in this world for some more time, but the demigods have requested Me to return to Vaikuntha. I was thinking that I had come to this world and annihilated many ksatriyas, but now I can see there are more ksatriyas in Dwaraka. The members of the Yadu dynasty are all great warriors. As long as they remain in this world. Mother Earth will have to bear their burden. Therefore, I wish to finish them all on the pretext of the brahmana's curse which has just taken place. Try to understand the situation and do what is best for you."

After hearing these words of Krishna, Uddhava could not control his tears. On being asked how he would be delivered, Krishna sat down with Uddhava in a solitary place and instructed him about the Supreme Absolute Truth.

"My dear Uddhava, listen carefully to what I say. Give up all illusion and fix your mind on the truth. Whatever you see in this world is all temporary and useless. Everything including the wealth, followers, sons, grandsons, and friends are all temporary. Give up all material activities and think of Narayana all the time. The Supreme Lord is the only essence within the whole material creation. He is completely aloof from material contamination. He is within the hearts of all living entities and within every atom, yet no one knows Him. He is the creator, maintainer, and annihilator of the universe. Anyone who meditates on that Supreme Lord also becomes free from the contamination of material existence. Do not be surprised by seeing all this. Fix your mind only on that Absolute Truth."

## Uddhava makes further inquiry

Uddhava folded his hands and asked, "O Lord of the universe! How will I understand the Supreme Absolute Truth? Who is a bona fide spiritual master? Where can one find such a spiritual master? What is the process by which one can meditate on You? My mind is not as steady. O Krishna! Kindly remove Your illusion and deliver me. I have no other engagement other than serving Your lotus feet. Please impart spiritual knowledge unto me and save me." Speaking these words, Uddhava cried out of love and affection for Krishna.

## Krishna narrates the stories of king Nimi and the Navayogendras.

On being thus requested by His friend Uddhava, Krishna began instructing him. "Long ago, there was a king named Nimi who lived in the country of Mithila. The king was very pious and always performed many sacrifices. Suddenly, the Navayogendras who were perfect souls arrived in his kingdom while traveling from place to place. The king immediately stood up with awe and reverence, and worshiped the great yogis. He offered his obeisances again and again with folded hands, and said, 'O great sages! What brings you here?' He asked the sages, "I know you all are exalted devotees. Kindly instruct me how to serve Lord Narayana." To this, the Navayogendras replied with a smile, "O king! We are very happy by your hospitality. Now hear with full attention as we describe the pastimes of the Supreme Lord to you for your benefit."

## The three kinds of vaisnavas

"O king! You are indeed fortunate." The nine sages instructed the king. "You have molded your life in such a way that you have achieved the perfection of life. There are three kinds of devotees in this world namely uttama-adhikan, madhyama-adhikari, and kanistha-adhikari. Those who treat all living entities equally, who reward people according to their degree of service, who display compassion to the conditioned souls, who do not discriminate between stool and sandalwood paste, and who do not feel unhappy when they are insulted and happy when they are respected are known as uttama-adhikan Vaisnavas or first class devotees. Those who think of Lord Hari all the time, who associate with devotees of the Lord, who know the material world to be a place of miseries, know that everything in this world belongs to Lord Hari, who serve Hari rather than engaging in sense gratification, always engage in the worship of the Supreme Lord whether in happiness, distress, prestige or pleasure, and who surrender themselves to Lord Hari are considered madhyama-adhikari Vaisnavas or second class devotees. Those who worship Lord Hari, who do not worship the demigods, who know this material world to be a useless place but are not fully convinced that Lord Hari is present within their hearts, who install deities of the Lord and worship Him, who do not make a distinction between gross and subtle objects, who do not become very happy when tl.ey meet the Vaisnavas and, who chant the name of Hari and think about Hari, but still possess material desires in their hearts are considered to be kanistha-adhikariVaisnavas or third class devotees.'

Those who always live peacefully, sometimes acting like a mad man, who are never overcome by lust, anger, greed or illusion, who wander about everywhere fully self-controlled and see all living entities of this world as devotees of Lord Hari certainly belong to the category of uttamaadhikari.'

After instructing King Nimi in this way, the nine perfect Yogendras departed. O Uddhava! The great sage Narada came to Dwaraka and narrated thus to My father Vasudeva. Now hear from Me with attention, as I describe to you the accounts of a bona fide spiritual master.

## The twenty-four spiritual masters

O Uddhava! Long ago, there was a king named Yadu. One day a great sage who was an avadhuta a wandering mendicant, arrived in his palace. The king immediately greeted the sage with respect and made him sit down comfortably. The king worshiped the great yogi with six kinds of articles. He also fed him various sweets, grains and so on.

The king then asked the sage, 'O my lord, what brings you here?' The avadhuta smiled and replied, i travel from place to place out of my own will. The whole earth is my house. I wander about everywhere and meet people who are dear to Lord Hari.'

The king then said, 'O lord, I am very glad and fortunate to meet you. I accept you as my spiritual master. Kindly deliver me from this ocean of material existence.'

The avadhuta replied, "No one is anyone's spiritual master. Listen to this. I was traveling all over the country as a brahmacari, searching for a spiritual master who could deliver me from the insurmountable material existence. In due course of time, while serving the lotus feet of Lord Narayana, I accepted twenty-four spiritual masters.

First, I accepted the earth as my spiritual master. I have learned from the earth that one has to tolerate everything that comes one's way without any retribution. By following her example, I have become successful in controlling my anger. Since then I treat honor and dishonor equally.

My second spiritual master is the air. The lesson I have learned from it is that although the air travels everywhere, it remains unaffected. That is why I too travel everywhere without being influenced and attached to anything. I always remain indifferent.

I then accepted the sky as my third spiritual master. The sky is everywhere, yet it is aloof from everything. In the same way, I think of Lord Hari without being involved in mundane activities. Although I travel everywhere, I do not become influenced.

My fourth spiritual master is the water. By nature water is pure and is very dear to everyone. Observing this quality of the water, my heart has also become purified. My life has become successful by remembering the lotus feet of Hari.

I accepted fire as my fifth spiritual master. Fire does not discriminate between good or bad. It burns everything into ashes. From fire, I have learned the lesson not to discriminate between objects of different qualities. I consider stool and sandalwood pulp to be equal.

My sixth spiritual master is the moon who does not die himself, but becomes thinner day by day. From the moon, I got the knowledge that although the material body dies after a certain time, the spirit soul eternally remains.

I accepted the sun as my seventh spiritual master. The sun is reflected in the water, on land and everywhere in this world. The reflected sun ray is not the original sun. Similarly I have understood that the spirit soul is aloof from everything and untouched by matter. He suffers in this world due to his desire for sense gratification.

My eighth spiritual master is the pigeon. The male pigeon lives happily in the forest with the female pigeon. Once I watched how in due course of time, the female pigeon became pregnant and soon laid four eggs. After some time, four baby pigeons took birth and the pigeon couple looked after them with great affection. One day the pigeon couple went out to bring some food grains leaving their baby pigeons alone. Taking advantage of this opportunity, a hunter spread his net under the pigeon's nest and threw some rice particles to tempt the baby pigeons. The hunter's plot worked, and the baby pigeons were thus captured by the hunter. When the pigeon couple returned to their nest with food, they could not find their children. They looked around the forest, but could not see them anywhere. The female pigeon was so sad that she lost consciousness. She was so overwhelmed that she forgot about everything. Finally she saw her children in the hunter's net and out of intense attachment for her children, sat down near the net and screamed, 'O dear sons!' In this way, she too was captured by the hunter. The male pigeon saw everything from the tree branch and lamented profusely about his wife's tragic end. 'O dear wife! You are dearer to me than my own life, yet now not only you, but even our four children are deceiving me. I find the whole world void because of your absence. You are chaste and pious. I will not see you again. I will not hear the sweet voice of my children anymore. My heart is breaking. I do not know why I am still alive.' Lamenting in this way, the male pigeon slowly approached his wife and children not knowing that his death was inevitable. Lamentation is the greatest enemy of the living entities. Because of this, people die and suffer in this world. The male pigeon was soon captured by the hunter, who became very happy to capture six birds in one attempt. I have understood from this that lamentation certainly kills one, and therefore, I have learned to give up lamentation by all means.

I found my ninth spiritual master in a python in the forest. The python lies on the ground happily in the forest all the time while keeping his mouth open. He gets his food automatically by the arrangements of providence. He eats only when something comes to his mouth of its own accord. He does not personally endeavor for anything. I picked up this quality of the python and gave up all endeavors for food. I am convinced that He who created me will also feed me. Therefore I do not try hard to accumulate wealth.

My tenth spiritual master is the ocean. I used to sit down on the shore of the ocean and watch. I never found that the ocean was increasing or decreasing. In the rainy season many rivers and reservoirs of water pour their water into the ocean; still the water level of the ocean remains the same. It does not increase even an inch. In the summer season, the water of the ocean evaporates; still the level of the water does not change. So I have learned from the ocean not to become influenced whether in happiness, distress, joy or lamentation.

I accepted the butterfly as my eleventh spiritual master. I saw how she rushed into the fire and was swiftly burnt to ashes, all because of her temptation for palatable food. In the same way, I do not keep any attachment for anything in this world. One who does so certainly loses his life.

My twelfth spiritual master is the bumblebee. The bumblebee takes the essence of honey from flowers and flies away. So I have understood that this material world is temporary and everything here has an end. Only Lord Narayana is the essence and the only reality.

My thirteenth spiritual master is the honeybee. The honeybees usually collect honey from different flowers and save it without enjoying it themselves. Later on, a honey-collector comes and takes away all the honey after killing the honeybees. The lesson I have learnt from the honeybee is that it is dangerous to accumulate in this world. One who saves for the future loses his prowess and intelligence.

I have accepted the elephant as my fourteenth spiritual master. A male elephant is very attached to a female elephant. The hunter tempts an elephant by digging a hole and keeping a female elephant in front to lure the male elephant. Being intoxicated by lust, the male elephant falls into the trap. So I have understood that women are the form of maya in this world. It is very difficult to overcome their attraction. Even the mind of a great sage can be bewildered by the attraction of a woman. I gained this knowledge from the elephant. I carefully avoid the association of women.

My fifteenth spiritual master is the deer. Everyone knows the deer are very fond of listening to musical sounds. Being captivated by the sweet sound, the deer loses her life. Therefore I have stopped listening to worldly songs sung by worldly people. They only create more attachment and nothing else. I have adopted the habit of listening only to the narrations of Lord Narayana.

I accepted the fish as my sixteenth spiritual master. I noticed how a fish lost its life in trying to enjoy the bait. In the same way, I have given up my greed and hankering after material pleasure. Whatever I can collect easily such as fruits, roots or water, I fill my belly with. I do not hanker after varieties of material pleasure.

My seventeenth spiritual master is a prostitute called Pingala. Listen carefully as I narrate her story. This prostitute lived in a city and earned wealth through her body. She had a number of customers from whom she collected a lot of wealth, and thus she enjoyed life. One day, a merchant came to her place and said, T will give you whatever wealth you ask but you must come with me and stay at my place.' Pingala could not check her greed, so she went to the residence of the merchant and lived there. By the arrangement of providence, she happened to see a saint and wanted to speak with him, but the saint tried to avoid her association. The prostitute became very restless. She waited patiently till midnight, yet there was still no trace of the saint. She became very disappointed. She waited and waited until three quarters of the night had passed by, but still the saint did not come. Pingala then lost all hope and sat down on the ground. She thought,

'I am very sinful and ambitious. What will I do with all this wealth when I die? Wealth will not give me protection. I have already committed so many sinful activities in this life. Although many people come here, I cannot claim any of them as my own. This wealth, and my followers, gifts, and decorations are all useless. When I die I will have to suffer severely in hell. Therefore I should give up all ambitions and travel to the holy places.' Being completely disgusted with her life, Pingala gave up all desires and slept peacefully. She fixed her mind on Hari and thus got relief from all distress. From this story, I have learned that freedom from material desires is the source of great happiness.

My eighteenth spiritual master is the Kurara bird. It so happened that this bird had a piece of meat in her beak. When the other birds of the forest saw this, they attacked her to take the meat away from her. The Kurara bird was very clever, and intelligent. She immediately dropped the piece of meat from her mouth and flew away. In this way, she avoided imminent death. O king, in the same way, those who are poor have no fear in this world. I always remember this teaching in the course of my life.

I have accepted the child as my nineteenth spiritual master. By nature a child has no fear. In his childhood, he knows nothing except enjoying happiness. In the same way I simply think of Lord Narayana and spend my time without any worry just like a child.

My twentieth spiritual master is the unmarried girl. Once the parents of a young girl made plans for her marriage and invited a suitable groom to their home. The girl was engaged in separating husk from the rice paddy in the next room. She had many bangles on both of her hands, and as she beat the rice paddy, the bangles made a loud noise. She felt shy of this, so she removed some of the bangles from her hands. Still the sound did not stop so she again removed all the bangles and left only one bangle on each hand. This time there was no sound and she was pleased. The point to be noted here is that association with worldly people will always invite trouble. The young girl kept only one bangle on each hand so that there would be no noisly conflict among the bangles. Similarly, one should give up the association of those who are not devoted to the Supreme Lord. Therefore I gave up all worldly association and became happy. Material duality is the cause of disturbance. Therefore, one should give up all bad associations and fix his mind on the unlimited Supreme Lord.

My twenty-first spiritual master is a blacksmith. The blacksmith was making a sharp arrow without diverting his attention to anything else. He was so fully focused on his job that he did not see nor recognize any passersby, even when the king was passing along with his vast army. Therefore, one who meditates on a particular aim in life, without distracting his mind, is sure to achieve perfection. Taking a cue from the blacksmith, I have also firmly fixed my mind on the lotus feet of Lord Hari. I have engaged my body and speech in the service of the Supreme Lord.

My twenty-second spiritual master is the serpent. A serpent has no home. It lives and enjoys life in a home built by others. Actually there is no need to build a house and increase one's attachment and thus suffer distress. I simply live under the shade of a tree and remain joyful.

My twenty-third spiritual master is the spider. A spider keeps a lot of web in her stomach. But when she dies, there is no more web. In the same way, this material creation is made in such a way that everyone becomes attracted to it. Actually no one is anyone's friend. Therefore I simply remain alone while always thinking about the Lord's lotus feet.

My last and final spiritual master is an insect. I have accepted it as my twenty-fourth spiritual master. Once a tiny insect was captured by a wasp and kept captive in a hive. Out of great fear, that tiny insect meditated deeply upon its captor, so that it changed its form to that of the wasp. In the same way, I constantly think about Lord Madhusudana, so that I may attain His association.'

This is how the avadhuta attained freedom and returned to the supreme abode. O Uddhava! No one is anyone's spiritual master. One has to learn by himself. O human beings! Give up all attachments and engage your mind at the lotus feet of Lord Hari. This is also the desire of Gunaraja Khan.

**SANKHYA YOGA**

Uddhava folded his hands and said to Krishna, "My dear Lord! Your illusory energy is very difficult to overcome. Kindly instruct me so that I will not be bound by the ropes of illusion. I do not want to go on to another mother's womb."

On hearing Uddhava's words, Krishna smiled and began to speak, "When the spirit soul transmigrates from one body to another, he first enters into the womb of his mother through the semen of the father. In the beginning before developing the body, the spirit soul remains along with the father's semen in the form of a bubble. O Uddhava, the situation within the mother's womb is extremely awkward for a spirit soul. He is not able to tolerate the distress. In the first month after being conceived the body develops just like a green pea. After two months, it develops like a bilva fruit. In the third and fourth month, it takes the shape of a body. In the fifth month, the body develops fully within the womb. In the sixth and seventh months, he remains in a posture of yogasana with his head bent downwards. At this time, he always finds a way to get out. The entire body of the spirit soul becomes smeared with stool and urine. He eats whatever the mother eats.

"At this time he remembers all his pious and sinful activities that he has previously performed. He also remembers his previous enjoyment and sufferings. O Uddhava, the poor and hopeless spirit soul within the womb becomes frightened by this memory. The misery within the womb of the mother is much more severe than the misery awarded by Yamaraja. The soul does not like to remain in the womb, but he has no choice. He thinks,

'This is my last time, and I will try everything not to enter the womb again.'

The suffering within the womb of a mother is hellish. The ten months appears to be longer than ten yugas. Maladhara Basu requests everyone to think of Narayana so that they do not have to again enter the womb of a mother.

"The situation in the womb is so awkward and severe that the spirit soul prays to Lord Hari for relief, and promises to think of Him when he comes out. But alas! As soon as he comes out of the womb, he forgets everything. He cries and wants to drink his mother's breast milk. He forgets the promise he made within the womb, and also the severe suffering. Everything is being carried out by the will of Lord Hari. The illusory energy of Lord Hari is very powerful. Soon the embodied soul begins to grow day by day under the care and guidance of his parents. When he grows further and becomes a young boy, he always thinks about how to enjoy sense gratification. The same material body which gives you so much distress also gives you the opportunity to enjoy material happiness. The conditioned soul becomes proud and forgets completely about the Supreme Lord. His material body is made up of stool, urine, flesh, blood, pus, and bones, yet he thinks that his body is beautiful. In due course of time, his youthfulness ends, and he enters into the phase of old age. But still he does not want to chant the holy name of Hari at all. By now he has a wife, children, and grandchildren. He enjoys blissful moments in the company of all of them. He still hankers for happiness without knowing that his days are numbered. O Uddhava! Try to understand this, and do not neglect your duty towards the Supreme Lord. There is no boat other than the lotus feet of Lord Hari to cross over the ocean of material existence."

Maladhara Basu appeals to everyone to always think of the lotus feet of Lord Narayana and be delivered.

## Karma yoga and the process of meditation

… great humility, "O Krishna! I have heard whatever You have said so far. But still my mind is not steady. Now I wish to hear from You on the subject of karma-yoga."
