Krishna then began to explain as follows, "First of all, you have to withdraw your mind from the temporary material objects and fix it on your constitutional position. The body is the chariot, the mind is the charioteer, and the spirit soul is the rider. You should always engage your mind in thinking of Me, and then only your bondage to material existence will be cut into pieces. There are three cakras within the body. One is in the middle and it is called Susumna, and the other two are on the either side, and they are called Ida and Pingala. You should meditate on Me by placing Me on a lotus flower present within your heart and in this way think of Me all the time. Thus, you will be able to control your restless mind. However, if you try to think of something void, you will not succeed in controlling your mind."

"The lotus-eyed Hari should be the object of your meditation. It is not easy for everyone to practice this process of controlling the mind. One has to practice continuously. You have to teach yourself. You should treat all living beings equally. If you control your mind, then the mind is your friend, and if you do not control it, then it becomes your enemy. It is the mind which determines what is good and what is bad. The spirit soul wanders about in different species of life on account of his karma. He is taken on a chariot in the form of the material body by the uncontrolled mind. Although the soul has nothing to do with the material body, but because of wrong identification, he suffers material happiness and distress. He becomes entangled in material life, household, wife, children, and enjoyment. No one is anyone's friend, yet people are full of ambitions.

The soul remains confined within the material body, which has got nine holes. He enjoys this world with the help of his mind and senses. He never thinks that by doing so he is killing himself. Therefore, O Uddhava, you should control your mind by giving up the path of sense gratification and cultivate self-realization. It is due to ignorance that a spirit soul suffers in this world. The soul is the helpless witness of all material activities. The Supersoul has nothing to do with the happiness and distress of this world. Only when the spirit soul accepts the Supersoul as the essence of the world does he become delivered from material existence."

## A description of the Lord's opulence

Uddhava further inquired, "O Lord of Laksmi! Kindly explain to me the process by which I can understand You."

To this, Krishna continued, "O intelligent Uddhava, everything that you see in this world is a part of My opulence. I dwell within the hearts of all living entities, yet I am separate from them. I am all pervading, and everything emanates from Me. I am the original cause of the material creation. The whole cosmic manifestation is created, maintained, and annihilated by My plenary portions. Among the gross and subtle elements, I am false ego, and among the senses I am the mind. I am Visnu among the Adityas, among the Rudras I am Siva, among the demigods I am Indra, and among the animals, I am the lion. Among the demigods I am the great sage Narada, among the demons I am Prahlada, among the introspective sages I am Vyasa, and among the attractive beings I am Cupid. Among the celestial beings I am Varuna, among the perfect beings I am Bhrgu, among the mountains I am Sumeru Mountain, and among the Vedas I am Sama Veda."

"Among the sound vibrations I am omkara. I am the prowess of the powerful, among the alphabets I am A, among the illuminating agents I am the sun, among the Maruts I am Pavana, and among the ancestors I am Aryama. Among wisdom I am knowledge, among the Yaksas I am Kuvera, among the trees I am the desire tree, among the reservoirs of water I am the ocean, among Gandharvas I am Citraratha, among the stationary objects I am Himalaya, among trees I am Asvattha, among horses I am Uccaiusrava, among elephants I am Airavata, among birds I am Garuda, among serpents I am Ananta, among rivers I am Ganga, among fishes I am the shark, among human beings I am the king, among weapons I am the arrow, among stars I am the moon, and among snakes I am Vasukl. I am also the beginning, the middle, and the end of everything. I am the cause of the creation and destruction. There is nothing in the three worlds which is separate from Me. I am the source of everything. O Uddhava! Just behold My opulence. Among the four varnas, I am the brahmana. Among intelligent persons, I am Bmaspati. I am forgiveness, compassion, perseverance, fame, glory, speech, and wealth. Among women I am Laksmi. One who worships Me with faith and devotion knows Me in truth. Just as the waves of the ocean merge into the ocean, similarly the material creation ultimately merges in Me. There is nothing beyond me. One who knows Me knows everything. As the sky is spread everywhere, in the same way I am spread throughout the universe. This material world is the perverted reflection of the spiritual world."

## Krishna displays His universal form to Uddhava

After hearing these descriptions, Uddhava became struck with wonder. With great devotion and humility, he again asked Lord Krishna, "My dear Lord, "Whatever You have said will be greatly beneficial to me. If You are really concerned about me, then please show me Your universal form. You are very affectionate to Your devotees."

Krishna was pleased with Uddhava and displayed His universal form. The universal form of the Lord consists of millions of suns, moons, stars, and so on. The head is the heavenly planets, and the earth is the body of the universal form. The diamond-studded crown pierced the heavenly planets and went upwards and surpassed Satyaloka and Tapaloka. The sun and the moon are the two eyes of the universal form. The sky is His ears, the celestial river is His tongue, Pavana is His breathing, the ocean is His belly, and the rivers are His navel. Sumeru Mountain is His playing stick, Surabhi is the fragrant cosmetic, the reservoirs of water are the pores of His bodily hairs, and the trees are His bodily hairs. A lotus has sprouted from His navel on which the four-headed Brahma is sitting and offering prayers to Him. In the mouth of the Lord's universal form is the residence of the four Vedas along with SarasvatI. Visnu is in His heart, Rudra is in the throat, and in between, Brahma resides. The lower portion of His body consists of the seven lower planetary systems.

## The behavior of the members of the four asramas

After seeing the Lord's universal form, Uddhava folded his hands and prayed, "My dear Lord Krishna! My illusion is now removed by Your divine instructions. The more I hear from You, the more I relish it. Who does not want to drink such nectar? Now kindly tell me by which actions one can achieve Your lotus feet."

Being asked in this way, Krishna smiled and replied, "O Uddhava! Engage your mind in thinking of Me and execute devotional service unto Me. Perform all your prescribed duties but without any sense of attachment.

One's prescribed duties are arranged by the creator. Therefore, one is duty-bound to follow them. One should not deviate from his prescribed duties. By strictly following them, one will gradually make advancement in spiritual life. The four social orders, namely brahmana, ksatriya, vaisya, and sudra, are considered My mouth, arms, thighs, and legs respectively. There are six prescribed duties by which the brahmanas are known. These are: worshiping the Lord and engaging others in the worship of the Lord, studying the Vedas and teaching others the Vedas, and accepting charity and giving charity to others.

A brahmana should serve the saintly persons, should not take any donation from the royal people, and should always remain satisfied with whatever he can collect by begging alms. The duties of the ksatriyas are protection of the subjects, accumulating wealth, not running away from the battlefield, never being afraid to fight, and performing sacrifice. They should also worship the Supreme Lord, study Vedic literature, and give charity. The duties of the vaisyas are farming, cow protection, and trade. They should also worship the Supreme Lord, study Vedic literature, and give charity. The duty of the sudras is to simply serve the members of the other three varnas. If everyone follows these duties strictly, then the whole society will remain peaceful and happy. I have thus explained to you in brief about the duties of the four social orders, which were created by Me." "There are four asramas or the spiritual orders of life: brahmacarya, gmastha, vanaprastha, and sannyasa. On the day of the sacred thread ceremony, a brahmacan should approach his spiritual master, control his senses and study Vedic literatures under his guidance. He should serve the spiritual master and his wife with humility and faith. He should faithfully obey the orders of his spiritual master. He should take bath three times a day and eat only when his spiritual master orders. In this way, a brahmacan should train himself under the care of his spiritual master. After completing his studies, he should give daksina to his spiritual master and return home.

"After returning home, if he so desires, the brahmacan cm enter into family life. In that case, he should marry a girl from his own caste. The girl should be well behaved, faultless, and talented. A person who has taken to gmastha life should properly follow all the rules and regulations laid down by the scriptures. He should perform five kinds of sacrifices in order to become free from the five kinds of debts. He should offer oblations to the ancestors on time. He should help other living entities, perform sacrifice, and worship the demigods, the brahmanas, and the sages. He should treat guests by giving them food, drink and shelter. By pleasing everyone in this way, a gmastha will be freed from his debts. If a guest remains without food in someone's house, then that person will have to live in hell for millions of years. If one turns away a guest, he loses all his religious principles and incurs sin. Therefore O Uddhava! You should worship the guest for I become pleased with a person who takes care of his guests. One who worships salagrama-sila and tulasi achieves great piety. Such a person becomes liberated from all sinful reactions. A person who daily takes caranamrta of salagrama-sila, offers ghee lamp to the tulasi in the evening, cleans the cowshed, and serves the brahmanas is very dear to Me.

"Those who happily worship the demigods become relieved from the debts to Lord Brahma. By begetting children in the womb of one's legitimate wife, one becomes free from the debt to Prajapati. One can accomplish his mission of attaining the goal of life by living in the gmastha asrama. In this asrama, one gets ample opportunity to serve and please everyone. A gmastha should be faithful, truthful, and a benefactor of all, and then only will he attain liberation. After gmastha life, one is supposed to enter vanaprastha life. At that time, one gives up his wife and children and goes to the forest. Sometimes he may take his wife with him and go with her to the forest to perform austerity. He should maintain his life by eating fruits and roots. He should cover his body with tree bark and drink water from the river. These are the rules and regulations to be followed by the vanaprasthas.

"A sannyasi should sleep on the floor, and maintain his livelihood either by collecting grains that are lying on the field, or by begging. He should give up hankering and illusion. He should carry a sannyasa danda and a waterpot while traveling door to door and begging alms. He should not stay at one place for a long time; rather he should travel from country to country. He should always be satisfied and give instructions to others on spiritual subjects. He should never think about wife or children. He should travel alone and always meditate on the Absolute Truth.

O Uddhava! I have thus explained to you in brief about the duties of the four asramas. If everyone follows their own prescribed duties, they will quickly attain their goal of life. Those who follow proper behavior live a longer life and attain happiness and wealth. One should conquer greed, lust, anger and illusion. They should engage in hearing the pastimes of Hari. Wealth is not forever, rather it creates many problems. Besides, there is so much hassle in accumulating wealth. Even the minds of the rich are never steady. The mind always hankers after wealth and when it does not get it, it laments profusely. A true hero is he who can give up the temptation of wealth. Such a person has nothing to lament for and nothing to worry about. He remains fearless all the time. One may desire many things, but he only gets what he is destined to get according to his karma.

O Uddhava! Try to understand this and fix your mind on the Supreme Lord. Do not worry; I will supply you with food. This material world is useless, but no one understands this. The parents become bound by the affection of their children. But when they die, they are forcibly separated from their so-called loving children. This is all illusion. The more one becomes attached the more he has to lament."

Krishna continued, "Due to lamentation for children and wealth, people often die. One's intelligence and strength become exhausted due to illusion. No one is anyone's friend. Wife, children, and household are simply a network of illusion. The life of one who is entangled in this network is ruined. Consider these facts carefully and give up illusion and attachment. Then only will you be able to swim in the ocean of transcendental bliss. Now listen to the way by which one can control his lust. Everyone has the power of discrimination. Lust or Kama was once burnt to ashes by the wrath of Mahadeva. Lust agitates the mind. The creator has invented the body of a woman by stuffing it with flesh, blood, pus, stool, urine, and bones, yet she looks very attractive. Even the minds of the great sages are distracted by the beautiful body of a woman.

Anger is considered a great enemy of a person. It ruins one's mental balance and austerity. Forgiveness counteracts anger. Therefore, one should practice forgiveness. Due to anger, a person does not hesitate to kill a brahmana, a woman, or a cow or even insult his spiritual master. One's whole life becomes ruined by being angry. One should treat all living entities equally because the Supersoul is situated in the hearts of all living entities. One who tortures another living entity goes to hell. Therefore, one must learn to control his anger by developing the quality of forgiveness.

"O Uddhava! You should live joyfully and do not become influenced by the three material qualities of goodness, passion and ignorance. All the conditioned souls are under the clutches of these three qualities. All living entities are like puppets in the hands of these material modes of nature. By nature, I am always transcendental to the material qualities. The spirit soul is one. According to his karma, a spirit soul takes up varieties of material bodies."

## Astanga Yoga

"All the great yogis have mastered the art of astanga yoga." The Lord further explained to Uddhava. "Astanga yoga consists of eight disciplines: yama, niyama, asana, pranayama, pratyahara, dhyana, nyasa, and samadhi. Now let me explain this to you in detail. One should practice controlling his mind and become fearless. He should practice satisfaction, forgiveness, purity, tolerance, compassion, giving charity, and displaying mercy. One should see all living entities with equal vision, become patient, speak the truth, and keep firm devotion unto Me. One should give up pride, false ego, and tolerate adverse situations. One should not hanker after another's wife, blaspheme others, or desire another's wealth. One should give up envy, hatred, cruelty, harshness, idle talk, and blasphemy. One should avoid cheating others and acting unlawfully. One should not harm others, but should always remain humble. One should fix his mind in the association of saintly persons."

The Lord continued, "One should purify his body by traveling to the holy places. One should perform various penances like candrayana, fasting completely, drinking only water and eating only fruits. One should spend his time in thinking about Me. One should not eat or collect too much, nor should one stop eating. One should practice asanas like padmasana and svastikasana. By controlling the mind in this way, one can achieve the eight mystic perfections. As a result of practicing pranayama one gets a long duration of life and become free from old age and death. There are innumerable nerves and veins within the body. Among them three are considered prominent. These are susumna, ida and pingala. Within susumna is the fine nerve known as citra. One has to practice raising his life air from the abdomen up to the top of his head. One should practice the breathing exercise known as puraka, kumbhaka and recaka. Breathing through the nose is called puraka, holding the inhaled hair within the body is called kumbhaka and letting the air out slowly is called recaka. One who daily practices this pranayama will gradually achieve perfection.

One has to control the life air within the body by closing the nine holes in the body. If one can achieve perfection in this system of yoga, he can float in the ocean of happiness and become liberated from old age, disease, lamentation and death. By the practice of pranayama, one purifies his body and lives a long life. He attains divine knowledge, divine vision, unrestricted movement and divine form. After controlling the mind by the process of pranayama, one has to withdraw his senses from their objects. If the mind becomes controlled, the senses automatically become controlled. When the mind is withdrawn from contemplating on sense objects, then none of the senses can enjoy their respective objects. One has to sit down quietly and meditate while fixing his eyes on the tip of his nose. As soon as the mind becomes steady, the heart becomes joyful. The lotus in the heart normally remains closed and is downwards. By the process of pranayama one has to turn it upwards and help it blossom by the power of omkara. Then one should place a jewel-studded throne on the middle of that lotus and meditate on the lotus-eyed Supreme Lord sitting on that throne. O Uddhava! It is very difficult to practice \\h\\syoga. Therefore you should meditate on My four-armed form which is eternal, full of knowledge and bliss."

## Krishna displays His four-armed form

"Now hear the description of My four-armed form. This four-armed form of Mine is as effulgent as millions of suns. His bodily complexion is blackish. His lotus-like face is enchanting. His head is adorned with a crown studded with various jewels. His two ears are decorated with fishshaped earrings. His face is shining like a moon. His sweet smile is just like the foam emanating from the waves of the ocean of milk. His four hands are like the four lotus stems, and His palms resemble lotus flower. He is decorated with armlets and bracelets. On His chest hangs a necklace of pearls. He is dressed in yellow garments. He appears like a lightning has struck a dark cloud. His four hands are adorned with a conch, a disc, a club and a lotus. His lotus-like navel is the birthplace of Brahma. His waist is decorated with a waist band. His toenails shine like jewels. His lotus feet are the decoration on the heads of the demigods like Brahma. On His left is goddess LaksmI whose complexion is just like the golden campaka flower. On His right is the personified earth whose bodily complexion is just like the tender durva grass. On His back the great sages are engaged in meditation on Him. In front of the Lord is Garuda who is offering prayers with folded hands. All the associates of the four-armed form of the Supreme Lord are standing around Him and watching His lotus feet.

"O Uddhava! If you meditate on this form of Mine you will surely achieve perfection. To meditate on this form, one should start from My lotus feet and gradually go upwards to My face. One should meditate on each and every limb of My body with full attention. By doing so one is sure to feel transcendental bliss. When one achieves perfection in this art, he will develop love for Me. Tears will flow from his eyes, and he will be fully absorbed in thoughts of Me. He will not hear any external noise, even if someone beats a drum near his ears. Even if a celestial prostitute comes and embraces him, he will not be distracted. Even if palatable food stuffs are placed in his mouth, he will not notice whether the food is sweet or bitter. If a parijata flower is held in front of his nose, he will not notice its fragrance. Actually he will remain indifferent to all external things. By controlling the mind and the senses one can gradually attain samadhi. A perfect yogi whose mind is fixed on Brahman can live anywhere just as a madman, a deaf man or a tree.

"O Uddhava! Give up worldly topics and engage your mind in the process of yoga. Try to understand this truth and follow with faith. You can preach this truth to the faithful people, but never reveal these teachings to the atheists, to those who are envious of Me, to the nondevotees and to the sinful people. You may explain this to My devotees only. O Uddhava! Now you may go back to your residence."

## Uddhava develops detachment

After speaking to Uddhava in this way, the Supreme Lord went into His palace. Uddhava became so purified by the Lord's association and the spiritual topics that the Lord explained to him, that he gave up everything including his family and opulence. He decided to reside in Dwaraka as long the Lord remained there.

## The glories of Dwaraka

.. inhabitants. There were parijata flowers brought from heaven. All kinds of opulences were fully manifest in Dwaraka. The people of Dwaraka had no worry, no lamentation, no fear of death, and no fear of defeat from anyone. Who has the power to describe the glories of Dwaraka? Dwaraka became more glorious because Krishna performed many uncommon pastimes there. Krishna had innumerable sons and grandsons. There were thirtyeight million tutors for teaching these children. They were very powerful, compassionate, and full of good qualities. The inhabitants of Dwaraka became immortal and were free from old age.

The Supreme Lord lived in this world for one hundred and twenty-five years. O human beings, hear always about the pastimes of Krishna's different incarnations; then you will easily be delivered from this miserable material existence. The Supreme Lord is always favorable to His devotees. He accepted the human form of body only for their sake. The Lord is always transcendental and unchangeable. In order to teach the people of this world, the Lord incarnates from time to time.

## The destruction of the Yadu dynasty on the pretext of a brahmana's curse

Although the Supreme Lord appeared in this world to diminish the burden of the earth, this burden had apparently increased due to the vast Yadu dynasty. The demigods headed by Lord Brahma went to the Lord and reminded Him about the accomplishment of His mission on earth.

The Lord told them, "Because of My influence, no one can destroy the Yadu dynasty." However, the time had come to wind up His mission, and so He had to find a way to destroy the Yadu dynasty. The Lord therefore arranged a curse by the brahmanas as part of His plan. Although He had the power to reverse the curse, He did not do so. As a result of the curse, many inauspicious symptoms gradually began to manifest in Dwaraka. There were disturbances everywhere. The hearts of the people were filled with fear and lamentation. The planet Rahu appeared to swallow the sun and the moon. There were earthquakes, flags falling off from the rooftops of the palatial buildings and blazing fire could be seen in the outer space.

There were loud noises all over, comets came rushing towards the earth, and different planets were found crashing into each other. People of Dwaraka suddenly became idle and fond of sleeping. Deities made of wood and stone began to crack. Some of the Deities would laugh loudly, and then the temples would collapse without any visible reason. Pigeons, owls, and doves made their nests in each and every house. The dogs howled vociferously, and the jackals ran with their mouths upwards. The demigods seeing the situation would cry. Tears would automatically just flow from people's eyes. The women would walk around with strange ornaments. When these inauspicious symptoms became prevalent, the whole city of Dwaraka began to tremble. Soon a vast body of water began to inundate the entire city of Dwaraka. All the devotees and the Yadavas, fearing for their lives, left Dwaraka and went to Prabhasa.

## The Yadavas go to Prabhasa

One day, Krishna called everyone and said to them, "There are so many disturbances in Dwaraka. We should move out of this place immediately. Let us all go to the holy place of Prabhasa. We will take bath and give charity at that place. In this way we can counteract the inauspicious happenings. Let our old parents and king Ugrasena stay here. Let Aniruddha's son and my great-grandson Vajranabha stay here and look after them. All the Yadavas should leave behind their wives and mothers and quickly go to Prabhasa."

After speaking like this, the Lord went to Vasudeva and Devaki and pacified them with sweet words. He said, "O mother and father! Give up family life and fix your mind on the Supreme Lord. I am neither your son nor are you My father. According to one's karma one takes a particular type of body and develops a particular type of relationship. Actually nobody is related to anybody. The whole world is temporary. Only the Supreme Lord is eternal. No one has the power to realize Him by his own endeavor. Only when the Lord manifests Himself can one know Him. Until one's mind is engaged in the Supreme Lord, one remains restless. We are going to Prabhasa. You should stay here and cultivate God consciousness."

After solacing His parents in this way, the Lord told Daruka to bring His chariot. He gave King Ugrasena the responsibility of ruling the kingdom and left for Prabhasa.

## A secret conversation between Krishna and Balarama

Krishna went to Balarama and discussed Their future plans with Him. He said, "We both appeared in this world to reduce the burden of the earth. We have killed many demons for that purpose. But now, the Yadu dynasty has created more burdens on the earth. Moreover, the members of the Yadu dynasty are invincible due to Our influence. The situation is almost unbearable now. What is the use of Our advent in this world if We cannot do what is required? Let us do something now to finish off the Yadavas." After discussing the situation in this way, the two Lords mounted a chariot and departed for Prabhasa. They were followed by the members of Yadu dynasty. Only the ladies stayed behind at Dwaraka. On reaching the holy place of Prabhasa, everyone went to take a holy dip and to give charity.

## The destruction of the Yadu dynasty at Prabhasa

After their oblations, the Yadavas became completely intoxicated from drinking a honey-liquor beverage. They became bewildered by the illusory energy of the Supreme Lord; so much so that they began to quarrel among themselves. Conflicts of interest developed among the family members. The quarrels gradually escalated due to the effects of the liquor. No one was ready to listen or tolerate anyone else. They began to push and pull at one another, and then they used their hands to beat each other up. A hellish fight then broke out at Prabhasa among the princes. It was so fierce that soon they had exhausted almost all their weapons. By the touch of the Eraka forest, which was created with the help of the club produced by the brahmanas'' curse, the entire Yadu dynasty was destroyed. Only a handful of warriors like Pradyumna, Aniruddha, Gada, and Krtavarma were spared, but in the wake of such a devastating event, they became perplexed and restless. They did not know what to do.

After some time, they came under some wicked spell, and decided to attack the Supreme Lord Krishna. They picked up whatever weapons they could find and threw them at Krishna. How powerful is the Lord's illusory energy! Although they released many weapons at the Lord's body, the Lord was unhurt. The Lord then decided that He had had enough, and that it was time to kill them all. Krishna took up a weapon, and killed all of them at once. When Krishna saw that there were none left, He took Daruka with Him and proceeded towards the ocean.

## The disappearance pastime of Balarama

There Lord Krishna saw Baladeva sitting in a yogasana posture under a tree on the seashore readying Himself to leave the earth. From the mouth of Balarama, a giant serpent came out. Its complexion was white and it had one thousand heads. He was Lord Ananta and He was being glorified by the perfect beings. He was surrounded by many other snakes like Vasuki. He was dressed in divine garments, and His bodily effulgence was equal to that of millions of suns. Lord Balarama sat on Lord Ananta Sesa who slowly went into the waters of the ocean and disappeared. When Krishna saw this incident with His own eyes, He took His charioteer Daruka and wandered about here and there. After a while, they stopped underneath a tree to take some rest.

## Krishna instructs Daruka

"My dear Daruka, do not waste any more time. Quickly go to Dwaraka and inform everybody about the destruction of the Yadu dynasty. You should also tell them about the disappearance of Balarama. The time has come for Me to leave here as well, and to return to My own abode. You should personally meet Vasudeva, Devaki, and the other senior members of Dwaraka and pacify them with sweet words. I know this news will be shocking and unbearable to them. But what can be done? This is the nature of the material world. Do not lament. For a person who has taken birth, death is certain. People cannot understand this because of My illusory energy. Tell them to remember what Narada Muni had previously predicted. They should give up all material attachments, and engage their minds in spiritual cultivation. After solacing them, you should quickly meet Arjuna. Tell him I will disappear from this world on the seventh day from today. After I leave, there will be devastation and the city of Dwaraka will be inundated. The parijata flower will go back to heaven, and the Age of Kali will enter this earth. You should bring Arjuna here quickly. Tell him to take care that everything is done as it should be. Install the great hero Vajranabha as the king of Mathura and make sure that Arjuna takes all the ladies to Hastinapura. After completing this task, you should give up your own body while fixing your mind on Me."

## Krishna leaves Earth

After sending Daruka to Dwaraka, Krishna climbed up a tree and sat down on a branch in order to leave the planet. He rested His head on another branch and kept one leg hanging while the other leg He placed on His own thigh. At that moment, the hunter named Jara arrived there. He had an arrow made from a piece of that club which had been produced by the curse of a brahmana. While walking here and there, the hunter suddenly saw Krishna's lotus foot hanging from a tree, and took it to be the ear of a deer. Taking aim, the hunter shot his arrow at that target. The arrow that had been created as a result of the brahmana''s curse pierced the Lord's lotus feet. The hunter quickly ran to capture the deer, but to his utter surprise, he did not find a deer there at all. Instead he saw a four-armed personality sitting there. This four-armed form was bluish in complexion. He was as effulgent as hundreds of suns. He was dressed in yellow garments. He had a crown on His head and His body was decorated with various ornaments like armlets and Kaustubha gem. His chest was adorned with the mark of Srivatsa. His eyes resembled a lotus flower. He carried a conch, a disc, a club, and a lotus in His four hands. He was decorated with a garland of forest flowers.

On seeing the Lord of the universe, the hunter offered his respectful obeisances. He folded his hands and begged pardon for his offense. He prayed, "I mistook Your lotus feet as a deer's ear. I have committed a grave offense. O master of the universe! You know everything. Now please do whatever You feel right."When the merciful Lord heard these words, He assured the hunter not to worry.

## The previous history of the hunter

Krishna explained to the hunter, "Listen, O hunter! In your previous birth, you were Bali's son. Your name was Angada. When I appeared as Ramacandra in this world, you helped Me to kill the demons and deliver Sita. Bali and Sugriva were two brothers. Once they had a great fight. Bali defeated Sugriva and took away his wife and wealth. Sugriva had to leave his kingdom and go to the forest. He took five monkey soldiers with him and went to the mountain called Visyamukha. When I was searching after Sita, I happened to go there and meet him. Both of us were suffering for the same reason, hence we forged a friendship. He promised to help Me kill Ravana and deliver Sita, and I promised to help him kill Bali and regain his lost kingdom. I kept My word and killed Bali without any fault of his. Now I have to face the consequences. Anyone who punishes someone without any fault will certainly face the consequences. You helped Me so much by delivering Sita that I was so pleased with you and wanted to give you a benediction. Under the influence of My illusory energy, you asked Me only for the benediction to take revenge on your father's killer. I granted you your desired boon, by saying, 'Yes, you will kill Me in the next life. I will appear as Krishna, and you will become a hunter.' Now all which was destined to happen has happened; you have seen My transcendental form with your own eyes, and have become relieved of all your sinful reactions."

When Krishna stopped speaking, flowers were showered on the hunter. A chariot came from the sky and took the hunter to Vaikuntha. In this way, Krishna left this world and entered into an effulgent atmosphere.

O human beings! Try to understand the nature of the material world. Nothing is permanent here. One who gives up attachment for women and wealth is actually sober. There is nothing worthwhile in this world except Lord Hari. Thus Gunaraja Khan requests everyone to fix his mind on the lotus feet of Lord Krishna.

## Daruka goes to Dwaraka

Daruka was thoughtful and deeply aggrieved when he saw the destruction of the Yadu dynasty with his own eyes. The same Supreme Lord by whose glance the whole universe can be delivered had caused the destruction of the Yadu dynasty using a brahmana's curse. How could the curse of a brahmana destroy the family members of that Supreme Lord, whose holy names can destroy all sinful reactions including that of killing a brahmana? The pastimes of the Supreme Lord are very difficult to understand. This material world is useless and temporary. It is covered with illusion. It is the perverted reflection of the spiritual world. Since everything is born of ignorance in the material world, there is always constant lamentation.

Thinking in this way, Daruka quickly went to Dwaraka and was surprised to see the changes in the city. It was not at all like before anymore. There were inauspicious symptoms everywhere. Daruka felt so sad that finally he went to Ugrasena and informed him about the destruction of the Yadu dynasty. He then met Vasudeva, Devaki, and Rohini, and told them about the Yadu dynasty and the instructions of Krishna.

When the remaining residents of Dwaraka heard this news, they felt as if they had been struck by a thunderbolt. They could not move and became speechless. Krishna was their life and soul, and now He had gone. They lost external consciousness and fell to the ground. Some of them closed their eyes and lamented profusely. Some of them fell forcefully to the ground near Daruka's feet. Some of the inhabitants of Dwaraka beat their chests and heads, and some of them fell flat on the ground just like banana trees. Some of them rolled on the ground and cried profusely. They were almost dead. Daruka became shocked and remembered the lotus feet of Govinda. He wasted no time. He quickly went to Hastinapura and brought Arjuna to Dwaraka. Arjuna used all his patience and maturity to keep himself calm. He pacified his own mind and decided to carry out the orders of his dear friend Krishna. He picked everyone up with great care, and solaced them by speaking to them on the basis of the scripture.

## Arjuna performs the funeral rites

Arjuna went to Prabhasa with everyone and performed the funeral rites according to the rules and regulations prescribed in the sastras. Revati entered within the funeral pyre of an illusory form of Baladeva and returned to Vaikuntha. The eight principal queens headed by Rukmini also entered into the fire. In this way, all the wives of different personalities ended their lives by entering into the funeral pyres of their respective husbands. Vasudeva, Devaki, and Rohini also gave up their bodies by entering into the blazing fire. Arjuna performed all the required rituals for all these personalities. He performed sraddha and gave charity according to the prescribed custom. After completing the responsibilities entrusted to him by Krishna, arjuna left. He went to Mathura and installed Vajranabha as the king of Mathura. Meanwhile Daruka, according to the order of Krishna, departed towards the north to perform penance. Arjuna took the rest of the ladies of Dwaraka and departed. As soon as Arjuna left Dwaraka, a vast body of ocean water inundated the whole city except the palace of Krishna. This palace can be seen even today. Those who visit this palace achieve the Lord's favor. The Lord eternally resides in this palace along with Laksmi. Arjuna carefully took the ladies to Hastinapura with a bow and arrows in his hand.

## The defeat of Arjuna

On the way to Hastinapura, Arjuna met with some demoniac cowherd men. These wicked men were surprised to see how one single person was taking so many ladies with him. They had some doubt, and so they surrounded arjuna from all sides. The demons began to tease and misbehave themselves with the ladies. Some of them pulled the ladies' hands, some fell on their bodies, and others pulled at their saris. They did all of these actions right in front of arjuna who became furious with anger. He decided to teach them a lesson. Arjuna took up his Gandiva bow and tried to string it. But alas! He could not succeed in his attempt. The same Arjuna who had previously shot millions of arrows effortlessly with this bow was now helpless. The demons ridiculed him and began to laugh sarcastically. Some how or the other Arjuna succeeded in stringing his bow, but he had no power to shoot arrows at the enemy. He felt embarrassed for he was a renowned warrior. With great difficulty, arjuna finally managed to shoot all his powerful arrows, but the arrows fell near the feet of the cowherd men. None of his arrows could hit the target. The more he failed in his attempt, the more he felt insulted and his pride broke into pieces. The same Arjuna who had previously pleased Siva by his power in archery, killed Lavanasura, defeated Indra, Bhisma, Drona, Kama, Salya, and many other powerful warriors was now impotent and powerless. All his weapons had become impotent.

It is said that the quiver of arjuna never becomes empty. But this time, he lost all his arrows, and his quiver became empty. All his divine weapons were scattered here and there. It was because of these powerful weapons that Arjuna was able to conquer and control the three worlds. Arjuna was shocked and confused. He thought, "Who has taken away all my power?" Arjuna then began to beat up those demons who were misbehaving with the ladies with his bow. The bodies of the ladies then turned to stone as soon as they were touched by the demons.

## Arjuna is surprised

Arjuna somehow managed to get rid of the demons. He contemplated, "I defeated many powerful kings and won the hand of Draupadi. I defeated Indra during the burning of Khandava forest. I satisfied Mahadeva by fighting with him. I have always protected the demigods by fighting with the demons on their behalf. I alone conquered the entire Gandharva community. I defeated Duryodhana, the king of the Kauravas. I defeated Bhisma and others, and returned the cow to Virata. In the course of my fighting throughout my life, I have never faced defeat. I believe that it is the will of the Supreme Lord that now everything is changing. I am the same person, my weapons, bow, arrows everything is the same. But alas! The Supreme Lord has withdrawn my prowess. Without Krishna, my life is empty. I have now lost all my energy and bodily luster. That is why even my inferiors are defeating me. Now what should I do?"

Thinking in this way, Arjuna went to the hermitage of the great sage Vyasadeva. Arjuna offered his respectful obeisances to Vyasa who blessed him with affection. When Vyasadeva saw Arjuna devoid of luster, impotent, and pale, he inquired from him as follows.

## The conversation between Vyasa and Arjuna

"O Arjuna! Why do I see you different today? Why do you look exhausted, restless and overwhelmed with lamentation? Did you kill a brahmana today? Did you help sinful people or blaspheme saintly persons? Did you fail to protect the surrendered souls? Did you turn away your guest? Did you secretly enjoy the company of another man's wife? Did you fail to fulfill your promise made to a brahmana? Did you fail to serve your spiritual master? Did you commit some sinful activities? Did you criticize others? Did you brag yourself? Did you fail to keep your promise? Did you hanker after another's wealth? Did you give false witness? Did you converse with atheists? Did you forget the Supreme Lord?

O Arjuna! What happened to you today? Have your inferiors defeated you? O Pandava! I find you absent-minded and completely lost."

When Vyasadeva inquired from Arjuna in this way, Arjuna replied with sorrow, "I now understand whatever you told me. The Lord of the three worlds, Lord Hari, has left this world. It is because of His mercy that I was invincible. Because of Him, no one was able to defeat me. The demigods, the demons, the Gandharvas and the human beings were afraid of me. Krishna was everything to me. It was He who protected me in every step of my life. And now He has gone for good. Alas! Why am I still alive?

I used to use my Gandiva bow by His order, conquered the three worlds by His mercy. But now I have lost all my power to such a degree that even those who are junior to me are defeating me in fights. Not only that, but even insignificant plunderers almost succeeded to kidnap the sages and wives of Krishna in front of me. I do not know what I should do now? O Lord! Kindly instruct me."

## Vyasa instructs Arjuna

Vyasadeva replied, "My dear Arjuna! Do not lament. Pacify your mind. Lord Hari dwells in the hearts of all living entities. He is the creator of the supreme religious principles. He is the life and soul of all. He is the cause of creation and destruction. He is the source of all power, all strength, all prowess, and the life air of all living entities. He is the shelter of everyone. He is transcendental and free from material contamination. He is inexhaustible and unlimited. He possesses a beautiful form which has no beginning or end. He is the cause of the material existence. At the end, the whole material existence merges into His body. He controls the whole material creation through the wheel of time. It is His prerogative to kill someone or to save someone or to glorify someone. In the form of time, He neither helps someone nor ruins someone.

The whole material creation is conditioned by His illusory energy. Sinful people are not interested in the Supreme Lord. Their only aim is to work hard and enjoy the result of their works to their full extent. Krishna appeared in this world by the request of the demigods headed by Brahma and you are part and parcel of Him. It was Krishna who killed all the sinful kings using you as an instrument. He reduced the burden of the earth and did a great benefit for the people of the world. After accomplishing His mission, He returned to His own abode. Actually He is the power, strength, and intelligence behind all that be within the three worlds. When He returned to His abode, He took all His paraphernalia along with Him. You did not defeat anyone; you did riot kill anyone. You were simply a puppet in the hands of Krishna. You simply danced as He made you dance. Therefore do not lament. Engage your mind in thoughts of that Supreme Personality of Godhead and thus deliver yourself.

## The reason behind the kidnapping of Krishna's wives

"Now listen carefully as I tell you the reason why Krishna's wives were mistreated by the demons. Long ago, Brahma ordered all the celestial women to go to the earth planet and take birth there. The Supreme Lord was about to incarnate on earth. The ladies then departed to the earth. At that time the great sage Astavakra went to take bath in the celestial Ganges called Mandakini. When the ladies saw the great sage, they pleased him by their humble service. The sage became very satisfied and gave them the benediction that on earth they would receive the Supreme Lord as their husband. Having received this wonderful benediction, when they were about to leave, the sage came out of the water. The ladies suddenly saw the crooked body of the sage. His body was twisted in eight places, such as in the waist, thighs, in the back, on the shoulder, at the feet, on the head and in the knees. Women are by nature very irreverent. When they saw the deformed body of the sage, they began to laugh and make fun of him.

"This laughter made the sage furious. He immediately cursed them, 'Though you will have Krishna as your husband, you will be kidnapped by the demons.' When the ladies heard this curse, they immediately fell at the feet of the sage and spoke to him with humility. 'O lord! We are irreverent by nature. Please reconsider your decision and save our prestige. Such a harsh curse is not proper for us. Please forgive us. We take shelter at your lotus feet.'

Then the kindhearted sage said, 'My words are infallible. You will certainly be kidnapped by the demons, but the moment they touch you, you will beome stone and then return to your own abode.'

After displaying compassion to them, the sage began to perform his daily duties. The ladies departed after circumambulating the sage. Thus, they took birth in royal families and became wives of Krishna. O Arjuna! You did not know all these facts. So do not lament and be peaceful."

## A description of the future pathetic condition of Kali-yuga

"In Kali-yuga, there will be darkness of ignorance everywhere. Strength, intelligence, power, and duration of life will gradually decrease. There will be fewer food grains, and people will be of less intelligence and strength. Only one quarter of the religious principles will be left. There will be a prominence of irreligious activities. People will give up truthfulness, purity, austerity, and charity. They will be interested only in sinful activities. The brahmanas will give up the study of the Vedas and take up the professions of sudras. There will be no respect for senior persons. People will misbehave with one another. The earth will not produce food grains, the clouds will not shower rain, there will be no fragrance in the ghee, the cows will not give milk, the weapons will no longer be effective, there will be no mantras left, and everyone will become full of anger and passion. Parents will criticize their children, younger brothers will criticize elder brothers, brahmanas will stop worshiping the Supreme Lord, everyone will glorify himself, the wife will not obey her husband but instead, she will commit sinful activities, and enjoy the company of other men. The earth will gradually be reduced, and there will be an increase in sinful activities. The fallen and low class people will become very rich. The saintly person will face difficulties, and the sinful people will become joyful. People will become opposed to religion because they will think it is a burden. The brahmanas will no longer perform any austerities nor will they speak the truth. They will not conduct any sacrifice but earn their livelihood by begging and or becoming businessmen.

Vyasadeva continued, "People will live only for twenty-five years. They will become fully grown up at the age of twelve. The women will become pregnant at the age of seven or eight. They will give birth to three or four children at a time. They will not listen or give respect to their fathersin-law and mothers-in-law. They will not be able to walk because of the burden of their young age. The ladies will be ugly and full of inauspicious symptoms. Hair will be their only ornament. They will not follow the orders of the seniors. They will marry without the desire of their in-laws. If a person has even one pot of wealth, he will be called a rich man. If someone gives a penny in charity, he will be glorified in public. People will buy and sell things on various pretexts. People will take to cheating at will. The king will be from a mleccha community. He will not take care of his subjects. In fact, he will plunder everyone's wealth and levy taxes on innocent people. He will give trouble to his subjects in order to gain wealth. There will be stealing and plundering in the broad daylight. No one will follow the rules and regulations laid down by the royal authority. There will be no superiority or inferiority in Kali-yuga. Anyone who has power, influence, and strength will become a king. People will be ugly and sinful.

## Chanting the Holy Name of Hari and bathing in the Ganges will be the supreme religious principles in Kali-yuga

"The same amount of piety achieved by performing austerity for thousand of years in Satya-yuga can be achieved in one day in Kali-yuga. In Kali-yuga, one can attain perfection simply by chanting the holy name of Hari. In Satya-yuga the process for God realization was meditation. In Treta-yuga, it was sacrifice, in Dvapara-yuga, it was temple worship and in Kali-yuga all perfection can be achieved simply by chanting the holy name. There are many faults in the age of Kali, as is said in the scriptures, but chanting the holy name of Hari and taking bath in the Ganges are the two most important religious principles of Kali-yuga. By nature, people of Kali-yuga will be less intelligent and less powerful. Their minds will be full of contamination. People will give up proper behavior and become wicked. There will be very little truthfulness. It will be difficult to perform austerity and sacrifice because of the nature of Kali. There will be decline in the religious principles. People will constantly harm others.

At the end of Kali-yuga, the Supreme Lord will mercifully incarnate as Kalki. He will preach the path of Vedic principles and proper codes of conduct. People will surrender at His lotus feet. The Lord will incarnate in the city of Kalapa, and He will be assisted by two great kings who belong to the Candra and Surya dynasties. The Lord will re-establish the principles of religion and take care of His subjects. In this way, the Lord will protect everyone. Again the work of charity and sacrifice will be effectively performed. The Supreme Lord decided to incarnate as Kalki so that He can remove the atrocities created by Kali. His body will be transcendental, and He will carry divine weapons. He will destroy all the mleccha-like people of this world."

Therefore, O Arjuna! Give up the path of sinful activities and worship Narayana. Abandon the processes of austerity, sacrifice, and charity, and simply meditate on the Supreme Lord. The holy name of Hari is superior to Lord Hari Himself in this age of Kali. Simply by adopting the process of chanting, one will become liberated from all sinful reactions."

## Arjuna returns to Hastinapura on the order of Vyasa

Vyasadeva then ordered Arjuna to go to Hastinapura and relate everything about the disappearance of Krishna and His associates to King Yudhisthira. "Tell him to hand over the responsibility of ruling the kingdom to Pariksit. Also tell him to go to the north and engage in spiritual cultivation."

Being thus ordered by Vyasadeva, Arjuna offered his obeisances and started for Hastinapura. Upon reaching there, Arjuna bowed down before Yudhisthira and told him everything with great sorrow. He informed the king that Krishna had left this world for good, and the whole city of Dwaraka had been inundated.

When the inhabitants of Hastinapura heard this news, they were shocked and plunged into a pool of lamentation. At that time, Vidura returned to Hastinapura to see Dhrtarastra. The king narrated everything that had happened to his dynasty and lamented profusely.

## Dhrtarastra and others go to heaven

Vidura became very compassionate on seeing the condition of Dhrtarastra. He tried to pacify him by explaining the spiritual science. After convincing him about the reality of life, he took Dhrtarastra and Yudhisthira to the forest. They were followed by Gandhari and Kunti. According to the instructions of the Supreme Lord, Dhrtarastra lived in the forest for some time before creating a yogic fire and giving up his body. Gandhari and Kunti also followed him. They assumed divine forms and went back to Godhead. Not seeing the old king, Gandhari, and Kunti, King Yudhisthira became very sad. He and his brothers were fully grief stricken. They even stopped eating and drinking.

At that time, the great sage Narada arrived and explained the reality of life to them. He said, "This material world is temporary and full of miseries. Give up your attachment for this world, and return home to Godhead." After giving this instruction, the great sage Narada disappeared. Yudhisthira along with his four brothers and Draupadi departed towards the north after installing Pariksit as the king of Hastinapura.

## The author instructs everyone to meditate on the lotus feet of Krishna

He by whose order Brahma creates, He by whose order Visnu maintains, He by whose order Siva destroys, He by whose order the sun gives light, He by whose order the moon shines, and He by whose order days, fortnights, months, and years roll on should be meditated upon at all time. He is present everywhere, even within every atom, and thus He inspires everyone to act. The Supreme Lord is situated in the hearts of all living entities, yet He is so subtle that no one can see Him with the naked eye. Those who meditate on the form of the Supreme Lord can easily and quickly attain his shelter. Therefore, fix your mind on the thoughts of the Supreme Lord. Know for sure that He is everywhere. Treat all living entities equally, then the Supreme Lord will never neglect you. As a boat needs a sailor, similarly the illusory energy of the Lord makes all conditioned souls wander about in the material world. Try to understand this fact, and steady your mind. Think about the lotus-eyed Lord with full concentration.

O readers! I have tried to describe Krishna's characteristics as far as my intelligence and power allows me to. The pastimes of Krishna are unlimited and even the four-headed Brahma is unable to find their end. The Supreme Lord descends in this world especially to shower mercy on His devotees. I have no knowledge, no intelligence, and no power, so how can I possibly describe the Lord's pastimes? The pastimes of the Lord are elaborately described in the Vedic scriptures. Since it is difficult for ordinary people to understand these vast scriptures, I have tried to explain those pastimes in a simple prose format.

## Result of hearing or reading this book

Almost everyone in this world is engrossed in enjoying sense gratification, but those who discuss Krishna's pastimes which are described in books like this one will become liberated from the clutches of Maya, the illusory energy of the Lord. One who keeps faith in these words will certainly go to Vaikuntha. Although people are busy day and night in useless activities, they should find some time to hear these pastimes. The more one hears these pastimes of the Lord, the more his heart will be purified. One can obtain the benefit of traveling to all the holy places simply by studying this book. Generally, sudras have no right to study the puranas, but they can be delivered by reading this book. However, one should discuss this literature only among devotees. One should not discuss it in the assembly of atheists and blasphemers. This is my heartiest request to all. O men, women, and children! Hear Sri Krishna Vijaya with faith and devotion. If a barren woman hears these pastimes, she will give birth to a son. A poor person can achieve wealth by hearing this book. People can be relieved from disease, lamentation, and distress by hearing this book.

This literature was started in the 1395 Saka era and completed in 1402 Saka era. I have no qualification. I am a fallen soul and have no knowl- edge, yet Lord Caitanya has awarded me the name Gunaraja Khan. The , name of my son is Satyaraja Khan. I request all the devotees to bless him. Taking a straw between my teeth, I beg pardon from everyone if there is any mistake or fault in this book. I was born in a kayastha family of Kulina-grama and Sri Vyasadeva has ordered me in a dream to compile this book. O human beings! Just chant the holy name of Hari to your full satisfaction. By hearing the Lord's holy name, you will attain liberation. Apart from this, there is no other wealth in this age of Kali. There is no need to perform penance, chant mantras, conduct sacrifices, or give char- ity; you can sit at home and chant the holy name of Hari and be happy. I appeal to all sections of people to faithfully hear Sri Krishna Vijaya.

***HARE KRISHNA HARE KRISHNA KRISHNA KRISHNA HARE HARE HARE RAMA HARE RAMA RAMA RAMA HARE HARE***
